# Reader v1.1 / v1.2 review: findings register

The consolidated findings register for the reader v1.1 / v1.2 review. Each entry merges one or more
verified findings (their ids are under "Source findings"). Where a verifier corrected a finding, the
corrected text is what is stated here; the "Not this" lines record details that verification walked back.
Severity: critical = broken or unreachable feature, data loss, likely learning harm, or a WCAG 2.2 A/AA
failure on a core path; high = fix before release; medium = soon after; low = polish.

The findings were written against a merge of main into v1.1. The recommended way forward (report section 9) merges no branch and ports each piece to main as its own PR, so read "the merge" as the PR that brings that code to main; report section 10 names it.

The report itself is [2026-10-15-reader-v1.1-v1.2-review.md](2026-10-15-reader-v1.1-v1.2-review.md).

## Pedagogy

### PED-01. Open call 3 (launch default): as built, v1.1 switches every existing profile to paged reading silently at deploy, while the paging evidence is null or weak and the measured costs fall hardest on phones.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** UI, Code

_Severity note:_ All three verifiers rated it high: whatever ships first reaches every existing profile at deploy and is then written into storage, so the call has to be made before release.

**Problem.** On v1.1, readingMode is a new settings key, so validateStoreData fills it from the default ('paged', settings.ts:77) and every existing profile switches to paged on its first visit after deploy, with no notice; the persist subscriber (:118-122) then writes the full state, so whatever ships first is frozen into every visiting profile and a later change of default reaches new profiles only (lineWidth shows the other case: it is an existing key that main already saves as 'medium', so P0.1's 'narrow' will reach first-time visitors only). The way out is the Lestrarstilling control inside Settings, below the first screen on a phone, and switching there loses the place (the visible figure ends up at y = 4738). The code comment calls paging 'the highest-evidence intervention in the reader plan', and plan:50 credits Salmerón 2024 with numbers that come from Clinton-Lisell & Litzinger 2026, but the direct evidence is null or weak: Salmerón 2024 found no paging/scrolling difference (p = .867, phones excluded), the closest phone test was null (Joshi 2025, 100 adults, narrative text), Haverkamp 2023 found a trend (p = .066) with more look-backs, and Sanchez & Wiley 2009 (scrolling hurt low-working-memory readers on desktops) argues for keeping paged mode available rather than imposing it, while defaults stick and read as an endorsement (d = 0.68). The measured costs fall hardest on phones: worked examples that scroll inside a page (PED-02), figures split from their citations (PED-03), one-block pages at 1280×800 (1.1 blocks per page, §1.2 is 34 pages, a page turn per paragraph on a projector at that resolution, not verified for Icelandic classrooms), screen-reader say-all stopping at every page (UI-05) and off-page blocks hidden from browser read-aloud (not verified on a device), eager figure downloads (median 1.91 MB, p90 7.9 MB, max 25.1 MB per pageable section; CODE-15), and an MT notice above the viewport on arrival (UI-09), whose fix shows the notice in view rather than undoing ruling 1.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/stores/settings.ts:73-77 (comment and default 'paged'), :118-122 (full state persisted on creation); v1.1:src/lib/utils/storeValidation.ts:45-58 (a missing key takes its default)
- ui-capture_v1.1 layout[13] (Lestrarstilling below the first phone screen), layout[14] (figure at y = 4738 after a mode switch), layout[2]-[3] (§1.2 is 34 pages, 1.1 blocks per page at 1280×800)
- R1 E04 (Salmerón 2024: paging −0.109 vs scrolling −0.149, p = .867, phones excluded), E13 (Haverkamp 2023), E14 (Joshi 2025: 14.8 vs 14.7 of 20, p = .93), E12 (Sanchez & Wiley 2009); R1 planCitationAudit[2] and [4]
- R6-23 (Jachimowicz 2019: defaults d = 0.68 over 58 studies); R6-22 (EPUB rendition:flow 'auto'; Kindle and Apple Books put the switch in the reading view); R1 designImplications[0] (treat the default as an experiment)
- health_v1.1 mutations[37]: a mutation of the readingMode default survives settings.test.ts

**Fix.** Open call 3 is [USER]'s to make before release; nothing here preselects it. If [USER] chooses paged as the default, ship it only after PED-02, PED-03 and the screen-reader stop (UI-05) pass their acceptance tests, and with a one-time, dismissible line on the first paged arrival of a profile that never chose (UI1-14's draft: 'Nýtt: kaflinn birtist nú í síðum sem passa á skjáinn. Flettu með Næsta, örvalyklum eða með því að strjúka til hliðar.' with [Allt í lagi] [Nota samfellt skrun]; drop 'Nýtt:' for brand-new profiles; native-speaker check). If [USER] chooses scrolled, offer paged through the same kind of one-time line in the reading view ('Prófaðu síðuskiptan lestur'). If [USER] wants a device rule, key it on viewport height or aspect, not width: at 1280×800 a page holds 9–11 lines, fewer than the phone's 12–14. Whatever the choice: (1) detect an existing profile in loadSettings() (a stored object without readingMode; main never wrote the key) before the persist subscriber writes the full state, never switch a reader's mode silently, and store a 'notice seen' or 'mode chosen' flag with a validator; (2) store readingMode only when the reader picks it, or store 'auto' and resolve it at runtime, so the default can be revisited; (3) put the mode switch in the reading view as well as in Settings, as Kindle and Apple Books do, and make it keep the first visible block in view (the mode-switch half of UI-11); (4) replace the 'highest-evidence' comment with 'Paged follows e-reader convention and the reader plan's hypothesis; direct evidence is limited (Salmerón 2024 null; Joshi 2025 null on phones).' and correct plan:50's attribution; (5) count switch-offs locally (PED-17) and, to revisit the default with data, run R1's counterbalanced two-section crossover in [USER]'s own classes (PED-35); none of this needs a backend. Reviewers' labelled recommendation, for [USER] to weigh: paged as the default only once the paged-mode defects are fixed and the one-time line and in-view switch ship with it; otherwise launch scrolled and offer paged.

**Acceptance test.** Unit (settings): a localStorage object written by main, without readingMode, loads with the mode the launch rule resolves (the expectation is read from defaultSettings or the resolver, never a literal), and the stored object gains no readingMode key until the reader picks a mode (today the first load writes the default into storage); changing the default in code then changes the mode such a profile gets; an explicit choice survives a reload and wins over any default; once [USER] has decided, settings.test.ts pins the decided value, so a mutation of the default (mutations[37], which survives today) fails. E2E at 390×844 and 1280×800: on a profile that never chose, the one-time line shows on the first section and not on the second; from an open section, switching mode takes at most 2 taps without opening Settings, and the block that was first on screen is inside the viewport afterwards (today the only switch is in Settings and the place is lost). `grep -r 'highest-evidence' src/` finds nothing.

**Decision for [USER].** Open call 3: what new and existing profiles get on deploy (paged, scrolled, or a rule keyed on viewport height or aspect), and whether a profile that never chose follows later changes of the default ('auto'). The evidence, the measured costs and R1's crossover are the inputs; the options are conditional and none is preselected.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: per-section image bytes of median 1.47 MB and p90 6.74 MB. That page set is unstated and was not reproduced; over the 136 pageable pages the median is 1.91 MB and the p90 7.9 MB (the max, 25.1 MB in §10.6, matches).
- NOT: Clinton-Lisell & Litzinger limit their findings to college students. They say the findings 'may be limited to' college students.
- NOT: P0.1's narrow default has already been frozen out of existing profiles ('as happened to P0.1'). It has not shipped; at deploy it will reach first-time visitors only, because main already saves lineWidth 'medium'.
- NOT: a width rule, 'paged at 768 px or wider, where fitted pages hold about 17–20 lines'. That holds only for the 768×1024 portrait tablet; 1280×800 pages hold 9–11 lines, and even at a 75% budget 1280×800 is no better than the phone.
- NOT: UI1-04 to UI1-12 are all verified paged-only defects. UI1-04 to UI1-10 were not re-verified, UI1-11's dead end is narrower than stated, and UI1-12's Settings-close jump also happens on main and in scrolled mode; only its mode-switch half is paged-specific.

**Source findings:** UI1-14, PED1-04, PED4-10

### PED-02. Worked examples are taller than any page, so the window scrolls inside a 'page' and the pager sits below the fold on every measured example page; how to page an oversized example is a design choice for [USER]'s QA.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** Code, UI

_Severity note:_ Both verifiers rated it high: the pager is below the fold on every measured worked-example page, and worked examples are the core problem-solving content (289 in 86 of 114 numbered sections).

**Problem.** On v1.1 a block taller than the page budget gets a page of its own with overflow-y visible and max-height none, so the window scrolls inside a 'page', a nested page-plus-scroll hybrid no study has examined (R1); the comments at paginate.ts:10-11 and :80-82 say such a block 'scrolls', and :93-94 calls the case rare. All 8 worked examples in §3.1 (85–178 words) exceed the budget at all three sizes, with outer heights of 1,055–1,913 px on the phone (budget 514 px), 835–1,566 px on the tablet (683 px) and 869–1,636 px at 1280×800 (427 px): the pager is below the fold on 8 of 8 example pages at every size, and the example itself crosses the fold on 8 of 8 on the phone and at 1280×800 and on 4 of 8 on the tablet, which is where the check-your-learning problem and its 'Sýna svar' can sit unseen (confirmed visually for Dæmi 3.1 on the phone). Space, PageDown and → turn the page with no check that the page bottom is in view (PagedReaderControls.svelte:287-302; Space is skipped only when a control has focus), so a keyboard reader can jump past that practice part, and on phones a horizontal swipe does the same. The book has 289 worked examples in 86 of 114 numbered sections; only §3.1's were measured, so that most of the others overflow is an inference from their length (median 145 words; 252 at least as long as the shortest measured example). CI cannot catch it, because pagination.spec.ts:102 accepts any single-block page, and the Settings help line 'Síðuskipt stilling sýnir efnið í síðum án skruns.' (SettingsModal.svelte:323) is contradicted on exactly these pages.

**Evidence.**

- ui-capture_v1.1 layout[1] (budgets 514/683/427 px) and layout[5] (oversized blocks: overflow-y visible, max-height none; §3.1 pages with the pager below the fold: phone 12 of 28, up to 3,013 px; tablet 9 of 23; 1280×800 10 of 28)
- measurements.json paged[] for paged-s3-1: 8 of 8 aside.example pages need window scroll to reach Fyrri/Næsta at every size (tablet pager 51–782 px below the fold)
- v1.1:src/lib/utils/paginate.ts:10-11, :80-91, :93-94; v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:148-168 (applyVisibility hides only top-level unit children), :287-302 (handleKeyDown); v1.1:e2e/pagination.spec.ts:102
- bookscan: 289 aside.example in 86 of 114 numbered sections, median 145 words; in every one the check-knowledge answer is the last child, and 3 hold two check-your-learning problems
- v1.1:docs/manual-qa-2026-06.md:216-218 (already asks: keep it, split long examples, or raise the page budget?); R1 designImplications[5]; R3 planCitationAudit[1] (the 'rare' rule rated wrong)

**Fix.** Ship now, whatever [USER] decides about splitting (the same fix as CODE-06, CODE1-02): while the bottom of the current page is below the viewport, Space and PageDown scroll the window by about 90% of a screen and turn the page only once the pager is in view; Shift+Space and PageUp do the same upwards when the page top is above the viewport; ←/→ and the buttons keep turning pages. The phone swipe stays as it is by design; note the gap for [USER]'s phone QA. Also correct the three paginate.ts comments, plan:165 and :222-223 and the Settings help line, and tighten pagination.spec.ts so an over-tall page passes only in the case the chosen option allows. How to page an oversized example is [USER]'s QA call, already asked in the v1.1 QA doc. (a) Look through an over-tall aside.example or aside.note and paginate its children, as sectionBlocks() already looks through a nested section, adding p.para-title and p.example-label to the keep-with-next set so 'Lausn' or 'DÆMI 3.1' never ends a page; the cost is that a problem statement and its 'Lausn' can land on different pages. (b) Render a unit that holds an oversized block as scrolled, with nothing hidden in that unit (a new rule; plan:229's fallback was specified for a layout pass that throws). (c) As a measured experiment only: one break before the first heading after 'Lausn', so the check-your-learning problem and 'Sýna svar' open the next page; on the phone the problem-and-solution part still overflows (Dæmi 3.1: 995 px against 514 px), and the break separates the problem from the solution students look back at. For (a) and (c), applyVisibility hides only top-level unit children, so they need a sub-block model; practiceReveal's injected toggle and assess row must travel with the answer; the partial aside box needs an app.css rule, not content.css; and a continuation label such as 'Dæmi 3.1 (frh.)' belongs in the pager chrome, never as text injected into the content (textAnchor offsets, bionicReading, search). Revealing an answer grows the page without a re-split (from code, not verified at runtime): re-split by hooking the practiceReveal toggle, not with a ResizeObserver on the content root, which fires on every page turn. A bigger budget is not a substitute at the phone and 1280×800, where every measured example is taller than the 800 px viewport; on the tablet an 85% budget would fit 2 of the 8 (an input to open call 1, UI-08, not a fix). Reviewers' labelled recommendation: (a) or (b), with (c) only as an experiment.

**Acceptance test.** E2E at 390×844 on §3.1: on the page holding Dæmi 3.1, pressing Space repeatedly from the page top brings that example's 'Sýna svar' button into the viewport before the page is left (today, from code, the first Space turns the page). The harness reports, per viewport, the §3.1 pages whose pager needs window scroll (today 12 of 28 on the phone, 9 of 23 on the tablet, 10 of 28 at 1280×800) before and after. If [USER] chooses (a), none remains except a page whose only block is one indivisible child taller than the budget (an image, a table or a single paragraph: the phone's 12 include 3 plain paragraphs of 618–700 px), and a unit test of the pure split step expands an item list holding an oversized example into its children; if (b), every unit that holds an oversized block renders with nothing hidden; if (c), a unit test on a synthetic example [label 30, h4 60, problem 200, Lausn 30, solution 500, CYL heading 30, CYL problem 150, toggle 40] with budget 514 splits only before the CYL heading and never between the problem and 'Lausn'.

**Decision for [USER].** A design choice for [USER]'s QA, already asked in the v1.1 QA doc (:216-218): (a) split an oversized example at safe boundaries, (b) let the unit that holds it scroll, or (c) a single break before the check-your-learning part, as an experiment. A larger page budget is open call 1 (UI-08) and does not remove the overflow at the phone or 1280×800. The Space/PageDown fix does not depend on the choice.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: 4 of 8 worked-example pages on the tablet force window scrolling. All 8 of 8 do (pager 51–782 px below the fold); 4 of 8 is where the example block itself crosses the fold.
- NOT: the 12 phone and 10 desktop over-tall §3.1 pages are the 8 examples plus the closing note. They also include 3 plain paragraphs of 618–700 px (phone) and one 647 px figure (1280×800); only the tablet's 9 are exactly the 8 examples plus the note.
- NOT: overflow is the measured normal case book-wide. Only §3.1's 8 examples were measured; 186 of the 289 examples have no image while every §3.1 example has 1 or 2, so the book-wide claim is an inference from word counts.
- NOT: the quoted block heights are element heights. They are outer heights including margins (element heights are 60 px less), which is what paginate compares.
- NOT: the check-your-learning part sits below the fold on every overflowing example page, as measured. That is derived from the layout and confirmed visually for Dæmi 3.1 on the phone only.
- NOT: plan:229 specifies a scrolled fallback for oversized content. It specifies one for a layout pass that throws; option (b) is a new rule borrowing that mechanism.
- NOT: split before the example's last p.para-title. In 5-2 Dæmi 5.3, 12-3 Dæmi 12.3 and 13-4 Dæmi 13.9 that leaves the first of two check-your-learning problems with the solution; split before the first heading after 'Lausn'.
- NOT: the check-your-learning split removes window scrolling and is a plain M. On the phone the problem-and-solution part still overflows (995 px against 514 px), and it needs a sub-block model; treat it as a measured experiment.
- NOT: inject a 'Dæmi 3.1 (frh.)' line into the content. It belongs in the pager chrome (textAnchor offsets, bionicReading, search).
- NOT: a ResizeObserver on the content root as the re-split trigger. It fires on every page turn, because hiding blocks changes the root's height; hook the practiceReveal toggle or watch only the current page's blocks.
- NOT: a bigger budget can never help. On the tablet an 85% budget would fit 2 of the 8 examples; at the phone and 1280×800 it cannot.

**Source findings:** PED1-02, PED3-01

### PED-03. Paged mode puts nearly every figure on a different page from the paragraph that first cites it, and a keep-figure-with-citation rule can rarely apply at today's page budget.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** UI, Code

_Severity note:_ Both verifiers rated it high; the learning cost is inferred from the spatial-contiguity literature, not measured in this reader.

**Problem.** In v1.1's paged mode a figure or table shares a page with its first in-text reference in 6 of 60 measured cases (§1.2 0/2/0 of 10, §3.1 1/2/1 of 6, §1.3 0/0/0 of 4 at 390×844, 768×1024 and 1280×800); in 52 of the 54 split pairs the figure is on the very next page, while in scrolled mode 5, 10 and 6 of the 10 §1.2 pairs fit one screen. It is the common case: in the 136 pageable pages, 388 of the 490 referenced top-level figures follow their citing block directly, as do 54 of 71 top-level tables, and paginate() simply breaks before any block that does not fit, with no relation between a figure and the paragraph that cites it (paginate.ts:95-113). At today's budget (514/683/427 px) a keep-with-citation rule that applies only when the pair fits barely helps on the phone and at 1280×800, taking each from 1 to about 2 of the 19 figures in the three sections, but it would lift the tablet from 4 to about 10 of 19. An in-text figure link in paged mode jumps to the figure's page, so the reader leaves the citing sentence (from code; not run in a browser). The case for keeping them together is spatial contiguity (Schroeder & Cenkci 2018, g = 0.63, grades 9–12 g = 0.82), which compares integrated with separated layouts and did not code separation across pages, so the cost of a page break is an inference, not a measurement in this reader.

**Evidence.**

- contiguity measurement over measurements.json paged[]: 6 of 60 figure/table targets on the page of their first reference; scrolled §1.2: 5/10, 10/10, 6/10 pairs fit one screen
- figure-pair simulation over the 19 figures of §1.2, §3.1 and §1.3 (a port of paginate() that reproduced all 9 measured page counts): same page 1/19, 4/19, 1/19 today; about 2/19, 10/19, 2/19 with a fit-only rule; 17–18 of 19 with an overrun rule
- bookscan: 388 of 490 referenced top-level figures follow their citing block (509 top-level figures); table scan: 54 of 71 top-level tables
- v1.1:src/lib/utils/paginate.ts:95-113 (fit-or-break), :104-110 (headings-only overrun); v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:64-68 (SCROLL_OFFSET 96, BOTTOM_CLEARANCE 76), :252-285 and :320-327 (a hash jumps to the element's page); v1.1:src/lib/actions/figureViewer.ts:26 (showLightbox takes one image and is module-private)
- R3-07 (Schroeder & Cenkci 2018: g = 0.63, grades 9–12 0.82; across-page separation not coded); R1 designImplications[4] and [6]; R3 designImplications[0]

**Fix.** Do first, whatever the budget: (1) in paged mode, make an in-text figure link open that figure in the existing lightbox over the current page, so a look costs one tap and no lost place. On phones it is the higher-yield fix, since no page rule keeps most figures with their text there. showLightbox already shows the figcaption but is module-private and takes one HTMLImageElement (figureViewer.ts:26), so it needs an exported entry point, and 'Tafla' links (1 of the 10 §1.2 targets) and multi-image figures need another overlay or the existing page jump plus a 'Til baka' that returns to the citing page (R1 designImplications[6]). (2) Add the keep-with-citation rule to paginate() under its own flag (for example PaginateItem.keepWithFigure), not keepWithNext, whose headings-only overrun (paginate.ts:104-110) would let a paragraph-plus-figure page overrun; it adds pages (tablet §1.2: 20 to 22). How far the rule reaches depends on open call 1 (UI-08) and on the variant [USER] picks. If [USER] keeps a fit-only rule at today's budget, the tablet gains (§1.2: 2 to 5 of 10) and the phone and 1280×800 stay at 0 of 10; with budgets of 644, 600 and 824 px (80% on the tablet) §1.2 reaches 2, 3 and 8 of 10, and 75% was not simulated. If [USER] lets a citing paragraph and its figure overrun together, like a heading and its block, contiguity reaches 17–18 of 19, but pages with the pager below the fold rise from 23 to 32 on the phone, 12 to 18 on the tablet and 19 to 29 at 1280×800. If [USER] trims the reserves, there is little room: the 56 px header leaves about 40 px of the 96 px offset, the 76 px bottom clearance keeps the floating chrome off Næsta (QA E18), and even +100 px brings only 3 of 18 separated pairs together on the phone and 2 of 18 at 1280×800. If [USER] wants figure and citation together without window scrolling, cap a figure's displayed height in paged mode to the space left under its citing paragraph, with tap-to-zoom through figureViewer, or use R3's thumbnail beside the citing sentence or its figure-first order. Report contiguity and window-scroll page counts side by side, over more than 3 sections; the numbers are an input to open calls 1 and 3, not a settlement of them. Reviewers' labelled recommendation: the lightbox first, then the dedicated flag, and a capped figure or thumbnail rather than an overrun.

**Acceptance test.** Unit: paginate([p 200, p 300 (keepWithFigure), fig 300], 650) returns pages [0,1) and [1,3); today it returns [0,2) and [2,3). E2E at 390×844: in paged mode, tapping an in-text 'Mynd' link opens the lightbox, and after closing it the pager label is unchanged (today the link jumps to the figure's page). Harness at 390×844, 768×1024 and 1280×800 over §1.2, §3.1 and §1.3: report the share of figure/table targets on the page of their first reference (today 6 of 60; for figures 1/19, 4/19, 1/19) next to the count of pages needing window scroll; the share at 768×1024 rises above today's and does not fall at the other two sizes. [USER] sets the target (R3 proposes at least 80%).

**Decision for [USER].** Which keep-together variant to adopt (fit-only, overrun with window scrolling, a capped figure height with zoom, or R3's thumbnail or figure-first order); its reach depends on open call 1 (page-height budget, UI-08). The lightbox and the dedicated flag do not depend on either choice.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the citing paragraphs say 'sjá mynd …'. They read '(Mynd 1.6)'; only 4 of the 22 links extracted from §1.2 and §3.1 say 'sjá Mynd'.
- NOT: paragraph plus figure exceeds any feasible phone page. The measured §1.2 phone pairs run 587–1,312 px and 2 of 10 fit a 644 px budget; say 'most'.
- NOT: a pair is split only when it exceeds the budget. The fit-or-break rule also separates pairs that would fit together whenever the page already holds content.
- NOT: at today's budget a fit-only rule lifts §1.2 to 2/10 on the phone and 3/10 at 1280×800. At today's budget both stay at 0/10; 2/10 and 3/10 need 644 and 600 px budgets, and the tablet's 8/10 needs 824 px (80% of the viewport); 75% was not simulated.
- NOT: a fit-only rule cannot change contiguity on any device. On the tablet it would bring about 10 of 19 figures together, up from 4.
- NOT: both research files propose only a fit-only rule. Only R1 does; R3 designImplications[0] already has a fallback for pairs that do not fit (figure first, then the citing paragraph, or a tappable thumbnail beside the citing sentence).
- NOT: g = 0.63 measures the cost of page breaks. Schroeder & Cenkci compare integrated with separated layouts and did not code separation across pages.
- NOT: the figure-link jump was observed in a browser. It is confirmed from code only.
- NOT: trimming the 96/76 px reserves gives the room needed. The offset has about 40 px to spare, the bottom clearance protects Næsta from the floating chrome (QA E18), and +100 px brings only 3 of 18 separated pairs together on the phone and 2 of 18 at 1280×800.
- NOT: the existing lightbox can open any in-text figure link as it is. showLightbox is module-private and takes one HTMLImageElement, so tables and multi-image figures need another path.
- NOT: the simulated counts are exact. They come from simulations on measured heights, may be off by about 1 where rounding moves a boundary (1280×800 base contiguity simulates 0 against 1 measured), and cover 3 sections only.

**Source findings:** PED1-01, PED3-06

### PED-04. The recall prompt has no page-type gate, so it fires on 103 of its 217 possible sites that are rollups, chapter introductions or the preface.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P0.2 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** UI, Code

_Severity note:_ Both verifiers rated it high: almost half of all recall prompts would land on pages that are not sections to recall, which teaches students to tap 'Sleppa'.

**Problem.** On v1.1, markAsRead opens RecallPrompt on the first completion of any page (+page.svelte:171-180), with no page-type gate, although ROLLUP_TYPES is defined in the same file (:38) and used only to decide paging. In the content copy's TOC that gives 217 prompt sites: 114 numbered sections, 21 chapter introductions, 81 rollups (21 key-terms, 21 summary, 21 exercise and 18 key-equation pages) and the Formáli, so 103 of 217 are not sections to recall; the capture shows 'Kafli lesinn — rifjaðu upp … úr kaflanum' under the Lykilhugtök and Samantekt lists. The cause is the missing gate alone, not ruling 2: both completion paths call markAsRead (paged oncomplete :442, scroll read detection :450), and v1.2's June copy (superseded) fires the same prompt with the same copy through its own paged rollups (+page.svelte:168-174, :452). The prompt also opens when 'Merkja kafla sem lesinn' is tapped on page 1 before any reading, and after 'Sleppa' it never returns. Prompt effects depend on compliance: about half of prompts are used as intended (R5-06), and the same prompt repeated over a term lost its advantage and lowered enjoyment and effort (R5-05); both studies are of university students, so the habituation argument is plausible for this audience but not demonstrated.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:38 (ROLLUP_TYPES), :171-180 (markAsRead, no type gate), :442 and :450 (both completion paths call it)
- toc.json recount of the content copy: 114 untyped + 21 introduction + 81 rollups (21 glossary, 21 summary, 21 exercises, 18 equations) + 1 front-matter entry = 217; the front-matter entry has no type key
- screenshots rollup-1-key-terms (recall prompt at the end) and recall-prompt via mark-read on page 1 ('Hluti 1 af 1 · Síða 1 af 11'); meas.rollup recallPromptOnScrollToEnd = true on 1-key-terms and 1-summary
- v1.2:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:168-174, :452; v1.2:src/lib/components/RecallPrompt.svelte:32, :65-68 (same trigger and copy)
- R5-05 (Nückles 2010: interest η² = .15, effort η² = .11 under permanent prompts), R5-06 (Sonnenberg & Bannert 2016: about half of prompts used as intended)

**Fix.** Add a data-driven gate next to ROLLUP_TYPES, in a pure, unit-testable function such as endOfUnitActivity(entry) that returns 'recall' or null, so later end-of-unit activities (for example a key-term deck on Lykilhugtök, PED-36) can slot in. It needs two inputs, not the toc type alone, because the Formáli's toc entry has no type either: return 'recall' only for a numbered module section (type absent and not front matter, or a section number such as '1.3'), and null for every ROLLUP_TYPES value, 'introduction' and front matter. Keep both completion paths and the three rulings as they are; the gate only filters by page type. Follow-on, not part of this S fix: PED2-03's 'only at the end' rule (an early manual 'Merkja kafla sem lesinn' marks the section read silently) needs the pager to expose whether the current page is the last one (only oncomplete is passed today, :442) and changes the path QA Batch D documents (v1.1 docs/manual-qa-2026-06.md:162-166), so update the QA doc with it. The heading's scope wording ('Kafli lesinn', '… úr kaflanum', and 'Merkja kafla sem lesinn' and 'þennan kafla' elsewhere on the page, while the app calls chapters 'Kafli 1') is a copy fix with a native-speaker check (PED-18); if it names the unit, it names whatever unit open call 2 settles on.

**Acceptance test.** Unit: endOfUnitActivity returns 'recall' for a numbered module section (no type, not front matter) and null for 'glossary', 'equations', 'summary', 'exercises', 'answer-key', 'introduction' and the front-matter entry. E2E on v1.1: scrolling 1-key-terms and 1-summary to the end shows no [role=region][aria-label=Upprifjun] (today it does); Næsta past the last page of 1-0-introduction shows none; Næsta past the last page of §1.3 shows it.

- NOT: it is a v1.1-only defect. v1.2's June copy has the same ungated trigger and the same copy.

### PED-05. The machine-translated content uses several Icelandic terms for one concept (in §1.5 'nákvæmni' even flips meaning) and varies its worked-example cues, so cards, cues and compare standards inherit whichever form a page uses; the fix is efni's terminology review.

**Severity:** critical · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** needs efni first · **Effort:** L · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ Critical per PED4-04's verifier, who raised it from the reviewer's high: likely learning harm on §1.5's own core concept if this content is synced. It is not live today (the live July MT is consistent), it is efni-side, and it confirms the existing chemistry sync hold (re-held 2026-10-10) rather than adding news; the other two members are medium.

**Problem.** In efni's ② preview (the review's content copy), §1.5, the section that teaches accuracy versus precision, cannot carry the contrast: across its title, objectives, `<dfn>`, key-terms rollup, glossary.json and body, accuracy is rendered {hittni, nákvæmni, réttleiki/réttur} and precision {nákvæmni, réttvísi, verkleg nákvæmni, genkvæmni}, and on one page, in the preview and on efni main alike, 'nákvæmni' means accuracy in the definition paragraph and the Mynd 1.27 caption, then precision in the Tafla 1.5 worked example ('nákvæmur … en ekki réttur'). This is a regression from efni's 2026-09-20 ch01 re-MT (df4ec4b99): at 485e266c1 and on the live site (fetched 2026-10-16) all surfaces agree (hittni = accuracy, nákvæmni = precision), while efni's term base approves 'nákvæmni' for both concepts. The drift runs wider: in the 115 numbered section files 94 of 465 in-text term forms differ in first-word stem from every glossary row with the same English term (§1.3's text says 'ómagnbundinn eiginleiki', glossary.json 'magnóháður eiginleiki', and §1.3's summary calls intensive properties 'Eðliseiginleikar'); the last p.para-title of the 289 worked examples has 16 distinct texts ('Kannaðu þekkingu þína' 98, 'Skoðaðu þekkingu þína' 64, 'Prófaðu þekkingu þína' 60, 'Spyrðu þig' 24, …); and the atomic mass unit is 'amu' 96 times and 'ame' 16 times, both inside the ch. 3 answer key. In vefur the tooltip resolves data-en 'precision' to 'verkleg nákvæmni' (code trace, not run in a browser), and any retrieval feature built on this text (P1.2 questions, P1.3 cloze, term cards, compare standards) would fix and grade against whichever form a page uses. All counts are on the ② preview, not on live content.

**Evidence.**

- content copy chapters/01/1-5 (title, page-data objectives, `<dfn data-en>`, definition paragraph, Mynd 1.27 caption, Tafla 1.5 worked example), 1-key-terms and glossary.json; efni main 98a5fe2ff 1-5-maeliovissa-nakvaemni-og-genkvaemni.html
- efni history: 485e266c1 (2026-07-10, consistent) against df4ec4b99 (2026-09-20 ch01 re-MT); live §1.5, toc.json and glossary.json fetched 2026-10-16 (consistent); efni glossary-unified.json concepts 2500 and 2501 both 'nákvæmni', approved
- term-consistency scan: 94 of 465 in-text forms in the 115 numbered section files differ in first-word stem from every same-English glossary row (98 under the reviewer's script)
- worked-example scan: 16 distinct last p.para-title texts over 289 aside.example; 'Lausn' uniform in 288 of 289; 'amu' 96 and 'ame' 16 after a digit (answer keys 14 and 4)
- v1.1:src/lib/actions/glossaryTerms.ts:199-207, :386-433 (tier 3 maps data-en 'precision' to 'verkleg nákvæmni'); main:static/styles/content.css:549-558 (one treatment for every aside.example .para-title); R3-08 (Alpizar 2020: signals vs none, d = 0.38)

**Fix.** This is efni's terminology review, not a vefur task, and it confirms the current sync hold; vefur adds no correction layer. Hand efni a note asking for the items in the efni-side note, §1.5 first. vefur's own part is small: give .term-en lang="en" (it has none today; showTermEnglish already defaults to true), keep selecting worked-example parts by class and never by label text, and mark machine-translated standards 'Vélþýtt', extending the 2026-10-03 figure ruling. Gate any glossary- or summary-based compare or grading step (PED-19, PED-23) on efni's terminology pass for the chapters in use, since the summaries are not yet a safe standard; vefur cannot refuse practice on failing terms unless efni ships the check result in content metadata, and P1.3 cloze cards are made by the student, so at most warn. content.css needs no change: main already gives every aside.example .para-title one treatment (:549-558); a distinct cue for the check-your-learning title would need efni to emit a class and register it through its css-contract test.

**Acceptance test.** Read-only from vefur, on the next efni sync candidate: a script prints, for each data-en, the Icelandic renderings across title, objectives, `<dfn>`, key terms and glossary.json; for 'precision' and 'accuracy' each set has exactly one member and the two are disjoint (today precision has four renderings and accuracy two), and the §1.5 definition paragraph, Mynd 1.27 caption and Tafla 1.5 worked example use the same pair; §1.3's `<dfn>`, glossary.json and summary use one term each for intensive and extensive property; the last p.para-title of every aside.example has one text (today 16); no digit is followed by whichever mass-unit spelling efni retires; and the term-consistency count falls below today's 94. In vefur, .term-en carries lang="en".

**efni side.** Ask efni for: (1) the previously published §1.5 pair restored (hittni = accuracy, nákvæmni = precision) across title, objectives, `<dfn>`, key terms, glossary.json and body, and the term base repaired, since glossary-unified.json approves 'nákvæmni' for both concepts and 'prentnákvæmni' is an MT artefact; (2) a regression gate that compares each term's rendering with the last published one, so any change to an approved pair becomes a review item; (3) a post-render check keyed on data-en across `<dfn>`, key terms, glossary.json and summaries, complementing tools/chapter-term-check.js (titles, objectives and body carry no data-en, so those need alignment with the English source); (4) an explicit §1.5 pass in [USER]'s terminology review; (5) one text for the check-your-learning heading and one abbreviation for the atomic mass unit across the book, the choice being the terminology review's. The vefur effort is S (lang on .term-en); the L is efni's whole-book pass. Not live today; it would ship with the held sync from efni main or the ② branch.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: content errors were a main driver of Korea's 2025 rollback (R6-13). R6-13 lists factual inaccuracies among the reported problems; the cited reasons were teacher resistance, screen time, literacy and budget.
- NOT: a check keyed on data-en alone catches the §1.5 problem. Titles, objectives and body text carry no data-en, and it would miss the worked-example inversion; add a regression gate against the last published rendering.
- NOT: vefur can withhold practice generated from failing terms. That needs the check result in content metadata, and P1.3 cloze cards are made by the student, so at most warn.
- NOT: 98 of 465 forms differ. Counted against every glossary row that shares the English term (6 keys have more than one), it is 94; 98 is the reviewer's script.
- NOT: the count covers the whole book. It covers the 115 numbered section files only; introductions, summaries, key terms and exercises are excluded.
- NOT: part of the mismatch is a stale glossary aggregate. The ② branch regenerated glossary and index, and for the five ch01 terms checked glossary.json matches the chapter's own key-terms page.
- NOT: the summaries are a safe standard for term cards or compare steps. §1.3's summary calls intensive properties 'Eðliseiginleikar', and §1.5's uses 'nákvæmni' for precision.
- NOT: Alpizar 2020 (d = 0.38) shows that signalling works when it is consistent. It is the effect of adding signals versus none; consistency of cue wording is not tested.
- NOT: at least six renderings of the check-your-learning heading (97/63/59/23/13/7). There are 16 distinct texts (98/64/60/24/13/7/6/5 and eight with 1–2 each); the earlier counts covered only exact ('Lausn', X) sequences.
- NOT: 'Spyrndu þig' is a verified corruption of 'Spyrðu þig'. Plausible, not verified.
- NOT: unit variance breaks answer checking today. No answer checking exists yet; the risk is prospective.
- NOT: vefur should give aside.example p.para-title one consistent treatment in content.css. main already does (:549-558); that half is a no-op.

### PED-06. Open call 4 (pre-question frequency): as written the card returns on every visit until the section is read, and it goes live as soon as P1.2 is ported and deployed.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P1.2 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.2, v1.1 · **Also in:** Code, UI

_Severity note:_ High per PED4-09's verifier, conditional on P1.2 being ported unchanged; the other three verifiers said medium (it is not on v1.1 today, and one counted on another fix capping it at about 60 sections). Live pages already carry objectives, so an unchanged port goes live on deploy; that is the case this issue covers, and high fits it.

**Problem.** On v1.2, P1.2 shows the card whenever the section is unread and has objectives (!isSectionRead(...) && objectives.length > 0, recomputed in the key-guarded afterNavigate, +page.svelte:103-107); dismissal only clears component state (:427) and nothing persists it, so the card returns on every visit until the section is read through a mark-read button, pager completion or read detection. That is the documented design (commit 9b74bb2: 'revisits pose the same question'; PR #127), and the comment at :103-104 ('first visits only') is what is wrong, an error R2 and the v1.2 code map repeat. A v1.2 build shows the card on 0 pages (process-content.js:130 writes objectives: []), but on main's pipeline it goes live as soon as P1.2 is ported and deployed: in the content copy 133 of 216 pages carry objectives (113 of 114 module pages, 20 of 21 chapter introductions, whose 'objectives' are outline titles such as 'Bohr-líkanið', and 0 of 81 rollups), and 26 of 36 live chemistry pages sampled on 2026-10-16 already do. The pick hashes the slug key (PreQuestionPrompt.svelte:22-29), so a rename can change the objective, and the gate and pick are inline and untested (mutants K2 and K3 survived). The frequency evidence is indirect and none of it is from 16–20-year-olds: a prompt repeated every week lost its advantage and lowered enjoyment and effort while faded prompts kept it (R5-05, university students), about half of prompts are used as intended (R5-06), pretests raised MOOC dropout (R2-08), a reflect-only prompt changed nothing for 1,005 seventh graders (R5-08), and the prequestion benefit is item-specific (g = 0.54 for the prequestioned item, 0.04 for the rest; R2-09, abstract only).

**Evidence.**

- v1.2:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:103-107 (gate), :427 (dismissal not persisted); v1.2:src/lib/components/PreQuestionPrompt.svelte:22-29 (slug-hash pick)
- commit 9b74bb2 message and PR #127 body (revisits re-ask until the section is read); health_v1.2 mutations[18] (K2) and [19] (K3) survived
- toc.json scan of the content copy: 133 of 216 pages with objectives (113/114 module pages, 20/21 introductions, 0/81 rollups); 36 live chemistry pages sampled 2026-10-16: 26 with objectives
- v1.2:scripts/process-content.js:130 (objectives: []) against main:scripts/process-content.js:116, :157; v1.1:src/lib/utils/contentLoader.ts:72-84 (moduleId parsed, not returned); analytics.ts:62 (ActivityType has no pre-question entry)
- R5-05, R5-06, R5-08, R2-08, R2-09; R5 designImplications[2], [8], [14]; R2 designImplications[7], [8]; v1.1:docs/manual-qa-2026-06.md:259-261 (QA call: first section per chapter, or per day)

**Fix.** Open call 4 is [USER]'s and must be decided before P1.2 is ported and deployed. Decision-independent work for the port: move the rule into src/lib/utils/preQuestion.ts as shouldShowPreQuestion(...) and pickPreQuestion(objectives, key) with unit tests; correct the :103-104 comment to whatever rule ships; key the pick, and any record of 'shown' or of the guess, on the page-data module id, which needs contentLoader (:72-84) to return it, because module ids survive renames and need no renameSections(); a slug-keyed record would need renameSections() and a line in migrateRenamedSections() (#258). Any type gate must test for an absent type, since module pages have no type key. Add a pre-question entry to ActivityType (analytics.ts:62), log shown, answered, 'Veit ekki' and skipped locally, and show the counts on /greining so classroom data can reach [USER] without a backend (PED-17). The options, none preselected: (a) every unread visit to a page with objectives, as built and documented (133 pages); (b) first visit per section, persisted; (c) the first unread section per chapter; (d) at most once per day or per study session, which needs a backend-free definition such as the calendar day or a sessionStorage session, and gives up the item-specific benefit for the sections it skips; (e) only sections with an authored or tagged question (a strict filter finds a conceptual answered exercise for 60 of 114 sections; authored questions would come from efni as metadata, R2 designImplications[8]). Sub-choices that belong to the same call: whether chapter introductions get a card (under (c) the introduction is the first section of each chapter, and its 'objectives' are outline titles, so a card there needs another source of question); whether arrival by deep link (#sub-…, #CNX_…) or from search skips the card; and whether Settings offers a choice (R5 designImplications[14]: 2–4 options with the recommended one preselected and on), in which case each option must name exactly one cadence. Reviewers' labelled recommendation, for [USER] to weigh: less often than every unread visit; three of the four suggest first visit per section, persisted; all four lean to numbered sections only, to be weighed against (c), where the introduction is the first section; three suggest local counters to revisit the call after a few weeks of classroom use. The question form is PED-07's, and the 'Talist upp' objectives in 19.2 and 21.5 are efni's to fix.

**Acceptance test.** Unit tests on preQuestion.ts that hold under every option: false for a read section and for a page without objectives; the same objective for the same module id across calls and after a slug rename (today the pick hashes the slug); and a truth table over page type, read state, stored record and date that matches the rule [USER] chose, written once as a config the tests read rather than as literals in the tests. E2E on a scripted visit sequence (two unread numbered sections on one day, a reload of the first, then 1-0-introduction): the number of cards shown equals what that rule predicts. The shown, answered and skipped counters increase in localStorage and appear on /greining (no pre-question event exists today).

**Decision for [USER].** Open call 4, before P1.2 is ported and deployed: how often the card appears (every unread visit, first visit per section, first unread section per chapter, once per day or study session, or only where a real question exists), whether chapter introductions get one, whether deep-link or search arrival skips it, and whether Settings offers a choice. Options and evidence are in the fix; none is preselected.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the commit message says 'first visits only'. Only the code comment at v1.2 +page.svelte:103-104 does; commit 9b74bb2 and PR #127 document re-asking until the section is read, so the comment is wrong, and R2's 'as now' and codemap_v1.2 gapsVsSpec[1] repeat it.
- NOT: as built on v1.2 the card shows on 133 pages. A v1.2 build shows it on 0 (process-content.js:130 writes objectives: []); 133 is the figure after the port onto main's objectives pipeline.
- NOT: 55 of 114 sections have a conceptual answered exercise. It is 60 under the stated strict filter; 55 was a regex artifact.
- NOT: more prequestions help more 'only if each is answered'. The condition is 'attempted, covertly or in writing, on a specific question' (Rivers 2026).
- NOT: keying the pick on the module id is a drop-in change. toc metadata has no module id, and contentLoader.ts:72-84 parses page-data moduleId but drops it, so it needs wiring first.
- NOT: 306 of 308 objectives read correctly as 'Geturðu …?' questions. Two of the 306 are MT errors ('Talist upp' in 19.2 and 21.5, efni's to fix) and 7 end in '.', so 304 of 308 read correctly once full stops are stripped; and 'Geturðu …?' is a yes/no judgment-of-knowing prompt, not a question with a committed answer.
- NOT: the card is 'the most frequent interruption in the reader', as a measured fact. Plausible, not measured.
- NOT: 'Stundum' is a ready option. It must name exactly one cadence, and 'study session' needs a definition without a backend.
- NOT: excluding rollups and front matter changes anything. They carry no objectives; excluding introductions is the only exclusion with an effect.
- NOT: at most one card per day keeps the benefit. It gives up the item-specific benefit (g = 0.54 only for prequestioned material) for most sections.
- NOT: local counters reach [USER] by themselves. They live on each student's device, so a pilot needs them shown somewhere a teacher can read (for example /greining) or checked in class.
- NOT: the frequency studies are of this audience. They are of university students, MOOC learners and seventh graders, and R2-09 is verified at abstract level only.

**Source findings:** CODE2-10, UI2-13, PED2-10, PED4-09

### PED-07. The pre-question poses a learning-objective statement, already listed on the page, and asks only for unspecified reflection, where the evidence supports a specific question that the section answers.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P1.2 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.2, v1.1 · **Also in:** UI, Code

_Severity note:_ Every verifier kept high, and it applies on port: on a v1.2 build the card never renders (v1.2 scripts/process-content.js:130 writes objectives: []). On main's pipeline it would go live on the first deploy after the port, because live chemistry pages already carry page-data objectives (26 of 36 sampled on 2026-10-16).

**Problem.** v1.2's P1.2 card (heading 'Áður en þú byrjar', aria-label 'Forspurning') shows one learning objective picked by a hash of the section key under 'Hugleiddu í smástund hvað þú veist nú þegar um eftirfarandi — það bætir námið þótt þú vitir lítið enn:', and its only dismissal is the button 'Ég hugleiddi þetta — byrja að lesa', which asserts a mental act. The objectives are skill statements, not questions: on the review content copy 0 of 308 numbered-section objectives end in '?' and 306 are supine fragments written to follow the content's own stem '…muntu geta:'; on chapter intros the 'objectives' are the chapter outline (83 of 111 items equal a same-chapter section title, and 1-0 also lists a leaked test-module title). The evidence favours a specific question that the section answers: objectives turned into pretest questions beat objective statements (R2-10, d = 0.84), and prequestions mainly help the asked item (R2-09: g = 0.54 vs 0.04; teenagers 0.51 vs 0.08, R3-13). On a v1.2 build the card never renders; after a port onto v1.1 it would show on 133 first reads (113 numbered sections and 20 intros) with the same objective also in the Námsmarkmið panel, and in paged mode (v1.1's current default; the launch default is open call 3) it sits above the arrival scroll target, off-screen on arrival. Live chemistry pages already carry page-data objectives, so whatever is ported ships on the first deploy after the port.

**Evidence.**

- v1.2:src/lib/components/PreQuestionPrompt.svelte:21-29 (hash pick of one objective), :45-57 (lead and the single button); v1.2:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:103-107 (gate: unread and objectives present, no section-type test), :422-429 (card above the {#key} content wrapper)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:170-174 and :427-431 (arrival scroll to the content wrapper), :121-125 (gapAbove measured from the wrapper top, so a card inside the wrapper shrinks every page)
- Objectives survey, review content copy: 133 pages with objectives (113 of 114 numbered sections, 20 of 21 intros); 308 numbered-section items, 0 ending in '?', 306 supine; 83 of 111 intro items equal a same-chapter section title
- End-of-chapter recount, review content copy: 1,736 exercises, 873 with an answer-key entry; all 114 sections have at least one (median 6); strict conceptual filter 92 in 60 sections; worked examples in 86 of 114 sections
- R2-10 (Sana 2020, fulltext), R2-11 (Richland 2009, abstract), R2-09 (St. Hilaire 2024), R3-13 (King-Shepard 2025), R3-14 (Rivers 2026: covert answers matched typed ones), R3-02 / R3-03 (worked-example order)

**Fix.** Rebuild the card around a specific question before porting it; the question source, the attempt form and the frequency are [USER]'s (see the decision). Whatever is chosen: (1) Pose a question, never an objective verbatim and never a section title, taken from the chosen source at build time (vefur's process-content.js into toc metadata, or efni page-data), and render its HTML, not text (Dæmi 3.1's problem contains C13H18O2). Avoid 'Geturðu …?' plus an objective, which invites a yes/no self-judgement. (2) Ask for a guess and keep every exit one tap; in the optional-field form (the attempt form is [USER]'s) that would be: heading 'Spurning áður en þú lest'; lead 'Giskaðu á svarið. Það kemur fram í kaflanum — rangt gisk hjálpar líka.'; the question; an optional one-line field 'Mitt gisk…'; buttons 'Vista gisk', 'Ég giskaði í huganum' and 'Veit ekki', each of which starts reading; no text asserts a mental act. (3) Show no answer before reading (R2-10 Exp 3: feedback right after the pretest lowered final scores; R3-13 found feedback strengthened prequestions, so reveal after reading, which fits both if its feedback came after the lesson, not verified). When the section is finished, ask the same question again, then show the saved guess and the answer: one re-ask per pre-question, whatever open call 2 decides for the recall prompt. Its Rétt / Að hluta / Rangt is a self-grade and must not feed Kvörðun as an outcome (PED-10). (4) Render the card inside the content wrapper at the top of page 1, below the arrival anchor, so ruling 1 stands and the card is on screen on arrival. The pager measures gapAbove from the wrapper top, so recompute pages when the card is dismissed; its height must stop counting against whatever page budget open call 1 sets. (5) Keep the guess in a section-keyed store with renameSections() and a line in migrateRenamedSections() (#258). (6) Replace the inline SVG with the registry Icon 'circle-help'. Intro pages: if [USER]'s frequency rule includes chapter intros, they need an authored question, because their page-data 'objectives' are the outline (an efni defect); if it excludes them, gate on the toc type 'introduction'. A classroom comparison (the chosen form against another or none, scored on a delayed quiz item matched to the prequestioned one) fits the spring 2027 term. All strings need a native-speaker check.

**Acceptance test.** Unit: the question picker, run over every page of the content copy, never returns a string equal to one of that page's objectives or to a TOC section title (on 1-0-introduction it returns nothing or an authored question, never 'Stærðfræðileg meðferð á niðurstöðum mælinga'), and each picked question has an answer for its section. String check: no text in PreQuestionPrompt contains 'Ég hugleiddi'. E2E on the v1.1 port with readingMode set explicitly, once paged and once scrolled: on a fresh profile open the first section for which the frequency gate returns true (taken from the gate, not hardcoded); on arrival the card intersects the viewport and sits inside the content wrapper; none of the answer's text is in the DOM before the section is finished; 'Veit ekki' starts reading in one tap; in paged mode, after dismissal the page count equals that of the same section opened with no card; after 'Næsta' on the last page the same question is asked again and the saved guess is shown. Unit: migrateRenamedSections() moves a saved guess to the renamed slug.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] chooses three linked things before P1.2 ships (live chemistry pages already carry objectives, so the card goes live on the first deploy after the port). (1) The question source: (a) each section's first answered end-of-chapter exercise, computed in vefur's process-content.js, with a human pass, because a filter admits non-questions (§10.4's answer '(a) (b) (c) (d) (e) vökvafasi (f) þurrgufun') and end-of-chapter answers are not always stated in the section; all 114 sections have an answered exercise, 60 a conceptual one; (b) an efni tag on one exercise per section; (c) an efni-authored conceptual multiple-choice question per section with misconception distractors (about 114 items); (d) the first worked example's problem (86 of 114 sections), a problem-first form that R3-02 and R3-03 argue against as a default, so better as a trial arm; or (e) an interim that keeps the objective but with the content's own stem 'Í lok þessa kafla muntu geta:' and an attempt prompt (grammatical for 306 of 308 objectives, but the untested broad-objective form). (2) The attempt: an optional typed guess with one-tap covert and 'Veit ekki' exits (R3-14: covert matched overt), or a required answer (R2-11), a trade-off between friction and effect. (3) Open call 4, the frequency, which interacts with (1) because a card can appear only where the source has a question, and which includes whether chapter intros get a card.

**efni side.** Ask efni to (1) stop emitting the chapter outline as intro-page objectives in page-data (83 of 111 intro items are section titles on the review content copy) and remove the leaked test-module item from 1-0-introduction ('Eining til að prófa virkni ýmissa atriða til að sjá hvernig þau myndast í PDF-skjali', also on efni origin/main 98a5fe2ff); main's own Námsmarkmið panel lists these items on intro pages (by code reading; the live site was not checked). (2) Put the two imperative objectives on 9-5 ('Tilgreindu …', 'Notaðu …') into the supine form the other 306 use. (3) If [USER] picks source (b) or (c): tag or author one question per numbered section in page-data, with its answer findable in that section; (c) is more than M of efni work.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: 82 conceptual exercises in 55 sections — the recount finds 92 in 60 of 114 under the same strict filter (the reviewer's regex dropped 10 items), and the filter is crude.
- NOT: the card is the control condition of the research it cites — neither the plan nor the component cites a specific study, and the card does ask for a covert attempt; it is nearer Sana 2020's objective-statement condition and Zengilowski 2025's reflect-only prompt (R5-08) than Richland 2009's Exp 5 control.
- NOT: prequestions need an overt attempt to work — R3-14 found specific 'you will learn' statements helped the same items nearly as much (g_av 0.57 vs 0.71–0.73) and covert answering matched overt; the attempt mainly adds a small benefit for related items.
- NOT: problem-first works mainly when designed as productive failure — Sinha & Kapur 2021 found problem-first beat instruction-first on average (g = 0.36), somewhat more with productive-failure fidelity (0.37–0.58).
- NOT: the card is off-screen in every mode — only in paged mode after a port onto v1.1; scrolled mode has no arrival scroll, and on a v1.2 build the card never renders.
- NOT: three copies of the objective, already on screen, after a port — on v1.1/main it is two (the content's own box is hidden), and v1.1's arrival scroll puts the panel above the viewport; three copies describes v1.2's layout, where nothing renders today.
- NOT: the button is the only way past the card — the content renders below it and can be scrolled to; the button is merely the sole way to dismiss it.
- NOT: every numbered-section pick is a supine fragment — 112 of 113 are; the 113th, 'Notaðu…' (9-5), is an imperative that parses on its own.
- NOT: about 40% of this app's readers read below baseline — that is PISA 2022's Icelandic 15-year-olds (R4-21); the share among upper-secondary chemistry readers is unknown.
- NOT: 'broken Icelandic' as a checked fact — plausible, but not checked by a native speaker.
- NOT: showing the guess again at the end turns it into retrieval — re-asking the question does; showing the guess is comparison and feedback.
- NOT: a new interim stem 'Í lok kaflans áttu að geta:' — reuse the content's own 'Í lok þessa kafla muntu geta:' rather than adding a third phrasing.
- NOT: Option B (an efni-authored multiple-choice question per section) is M of efni work — about 114 items with misconception distractors is more.
- NOT: a test that the card never appears on 3-0-introduction — that page has no objectives and no example, so the test cannot fail.

**Source findings:** PED2-06, UI2-12, PED3-07, PED4-08

### PED-08. The rating step asks how easy the recall was ('Hversu auðvelt var þetta?'), so an effortful correct recall rated 'Erfitt' counts as a lapse in SM-2 and as 'rangt' and 'Ofmat' in Kvörðun.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P0.3 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** UI, Code

_Severity note:_ High from UI2-06 (confirmed) and PED4-07: once P1.1 is ported, Kvörðun tells students that effortful successes were 'rangt'. On main and v1.1 alone the verifier rates it medium (PED2-02): the mapping is pre-existing, and the scheduling error is conservative (extra reviews and a lower ease, no lost retention). Hence stage 'port'.

**Problem.** After the answer is revealed, /minniskort and the /nam review phase both ask 'Hversu auðvelt var þetta?', and 'Erfitt' maps to SM-2 quality 2 (DIFFICULTY_TO_QUALITY), below MIN_CORRECT_QUALITY 3, so an honest 'Erfitt' after a correct but effortful recall is scored as a lapse: with the real srs.ts a card at 6 days drops to 1 day, its consecutive count resets, ease falls from 2.50 to 2.18, and rateCard records wasCorrect false. The question, labels and mapping are pre-existing on main, so v1.1 does not regress them, and the scheduling error is conservative; main's /greining success-rate ring also counts 'Erfitt' as wrong. P0.3's predictions make it matter: on v1.2's Kvörðun (P1.1) a learner who tapped 'Man það', recalled with effort and chose 'Erfitt' is shown under 'Hélt sig kunna — rangt' and counted in 'Ofmat — „man það“ en svarið var rangt'; replaying the tab's formulas, a strong discriminator who rates 15 effortful correct recalls 'Erfitt' goes from 10% to 40% 'Ofmat', with the amber tile and the guidance shown. Separately, the guidance's percentage is of 'Man það' answers, not of all predictions (45% = 5 of 11 in the seeded capture, against 5 of 16 = 31%). Every review entry keeps its `rating`, so wasCorrect can be recomputed for old entries after a fix.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/minniskort/+page.svelte:224, :240 and v1.1:src/lib/components/study/ReviewPhase.svelte:134, :150 ('Hversu auðvelt var þetta?', 'Erfitt'); the same on main (minniskort :214, ReviewPhase :118)
- v1.1:src/lib/types/flashcard.ts:29-34 (hard: 2), used by v1.1:src/lib/stores/flashcard.ts:282 and :299 (wasCorrect: quality >= 3; main :296); v1.1:src/lib/utils/srs.ts:18, :57-59 (quality below 3 resets the interval); srs.ts's own RATING_TO_QUALITY (:27-32) drives only the interval preview (:253)
- massed_srs measurement (real v1.1 srs.ts): after two 'Gott' 6 d; one 'Erfitt' then gives 1 d, consecutive 0, ease 2.18; the preview reads 'Erfitt 1 d', the same as 'Aftur 1 d'
- calib_metric measurement, student D (15 effortful correct recalls rated 'Erfitt'): 'Ofmat' 10% to 40%, amber and guidance shown; v1.2:src/lib/components/analytics/CalibrationTab.svelte:27-49 (matrix from wasCorrect; Ofmat over 'Man það' answers), :143-155 (labels), :159-163 (guidance)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/analytics/FlashcardStatsTab.svelte:68-89, :106 (success ring and rating breakdown); R2-18 (Lipko 2009, grade 8: wrong answers credited 73% without a standard, 44% with the definition shown)

**Fix.** Fix the rating step, not only the tab; it is [USER]'s call under CLAUDE.md's SRS caution, shown with the before/after interval table (massed_srs). Option A, relabel only (srs.ts untouched): hint 'Berðu saman við svarið: hafðirðu rétt fyrir þér?' and buttons 'Rangt' (again, 0) / 'Að hluta' (hard, 2) / 'Rétt' (good, 4) / 'Auðvelt' (easy, 5), so an effortful correct recall is 'Rétt' and passes while a partial recall stays a lapse, as in SM-2; it changes what past 'hard' ratings mean in FlashcardStatsTab. Option B, map hard to 3 in both DIFFICULTY_TO_QUALITY and RATING_TO_QUALITY (canonical SM-2: correct with serious difficulty): today's labels become honest, a pass with ease −0.14, but intervals change. Option C, ask correctness first ('Rétt' / 'Að hluta' / 'Rangt'), then ease only when correct, with an explicit, tested mapping to SM-2 quality (a deliberate SRS change). Whichever is chosen: serve /minniskort, ReviewPhase and FlashcardStatsTab from one shared label table, and pin the two quality tables together in a test. When porting P1.1, classify Kvörðun from the stored `rating`, not wasCorrect, so old entries are reclassified too: until the rating step asks correctness, give 'Erfitt' its own cell (e.g. 'Kunni — með erfiðismunum'; safer than counting it as recalled, since some students may use it for partial recall) or leave it out of the matrix, and let no CalibrationTab string say 'rangt' for a cell derived from an ease rating. Use the second person ('Hélst að þú kynnir það — rangt'). PED-09 replaces Kvörðun's percentages and guidance line, which also fixes the 5-of-11 denominator, and PED-10 the per-section block. All strings need a native-speaker check.

**Acceptance test.** All options: one shared rating table drives /minniskort, ReviewPhase and FlashcardStatsTab (grep finds no rating label hardcoded elsewhere), and a unit test pins DIFFICULTY_TO_QUALITY and srs.ts RATING_TO_QUALITY as equal so they cannot drift. If [USER] picks A: after a predict button the hint reads 'Berðu saman við svarið: hafðirðu rétt fyrir þér?', the buttons read Rangt / Að hluta / Rétt / Auðvelt, and rateCard(id, 'good', true) records wasCorrect true. If B: rateCard(id, 'hard', true) records wasCorrect true and does not reset the consecutive count. If C: a correct answer rated with effort is recorded as correct. P1.1 port: a seeded entry with predictedKnown true and rating 'hard', made before the fix, is not counted under 'Hélt sig kunna — rangt'. The option checks and the Kvörðun check fail today.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] picks A (relabel only, srs.ts untouched), B (hard to 3 in both tables; intervals change) or C (correctness first, then ease; a deliberate SRS change), after seeing the before/after interval table. CLAUDE.md asks for care with anything that changes the SRS.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: v1.1 or P1.1 introduces the mix-up — the question, the labels and hard = 2 with wasCorrect = quality >= 3 are all pre-existing on main (types/flashcard.ts:31, stores/flashcard.ts:296); P1.1 surfaces it.
- NOT: the mapping lowers retention (Dunlosky & Rawson 2012) — that study's mechanism is overconfidence ending study too early; this mapping errs the other way, so the scheduling error is conservative.
- NOT: srs.ts RATING_TO_QUALITY drives scheduling — it drives only the interval preview (srs.ts:253); rateCard uses DIFFICULTY_TO_QUALITY in types/flashcard.ts.
- NOT: Kvörðun ends in a diagnosis with no action — the per-section rows link to their sections (to re-read); only the flashcard half has no action.
- NOT: the 'bad' cells use the --accent-color token — they are Tailwind amber classes (only the headline % uses the token), the same hue.
- NOT: 'Gekk illa (Aftur/Erfitt)' as an honest interim label — it still files an effortful success under failure; classify on rating !== 'again' or give 'Erfitt' its own cell.
- NOT: '„Rétt hjá mér“ í 1 af 4 dæmum' as row text — it counts attempts as problems (one seeded problem with 4 attempts) and quotes only the inline button; say 'merkt rétt í 1 af 4 tilraunum'.
- NOT: drop 'það er algengt við skjálestur' because the screen link is baseless — R1 finds a small screen calibration gap (Clinton 2019 calibration g = 0.20; Ronconi 2022); drop it because it pins one learner's flashcard result on screen reading, which was never measured.

**Source findings:** PED2-02, UI2-06, PED4-07

### PED-09. Kvörðun's headline 'Raunsætt sjálfsmat' is plain prediction accuracy, so it tracks how often the learner recalls and rewards always tapping 'Man það'.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P1.1 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.2, v1.1 · **Also in:** Code, UI

_Severity note:_ Both verifiers kept high (CODE2-03 confirmed). It applies when P1.1 is ported; P1.1 exists only on v1.2.

**Problem.** On v1.2, Kvörðun's headline 'Raunsætt sjálfsmat' is (hits + correct rejections) / predictions (CalibrationTab.svelte:41-45), plain percent agreement, so it follows how often the learner recalls rather than whether the predictions discriminate. Through the component's own formulas, a learner who always taps 'Man það' and recalls 80% scores 80%, the same as one whose predictions separate known from unknown cards by 60 points; zero-discrimination predictions score 74% and coin-flip predictions 50%. The 'Ofmat' tile's amber colour (:131) fires with no minimum count while the guidance (:159) needs 5, the guidance 'Þú ofmetur kunnáttu þína í um …% tilvika — það er algengt við skjálestur.' pins one learner's flashcard result on screen reading, which was never measured, and the flashcard half lists no cards and offers no action; its 'Ofmat' figures also inherit the ease-as-correctness mapping (PED-08). It reads the whole reviewHistory with no book filter while review entries carry no book, so each book's /greining would pool every book's predictions (latent while one book is live). P1.1 is a design to port, and the evidence that aggregate self-assessment feedback or dashboards help is weak (R2-19, R2-20, R5-21, all abstract-level).

**Evidence.**

- v1.2:src/lib/components/analytics/CalibrationTab.svelte:41-45 (calibrationPct), :46-49 (Ofmat over 'Man það' answers), :125-127 (headline), :131 (amber with no minimum), :159-163 (guidance and its text), :23-25 (no book filter)
- calib-check measurement (the component's own lines): always 'Man það' at 80% recall gives 80%; a 60-point discriminator gives 80%; coin-flip predictions give 50%
- calib_metric measurement: always 'Man það' 80, strong discriminator 85, zero discrimination 74
- v1.2:src/lib/stores/flashcard.ts:41-49 (FlashcardReviewEntry keeps rating, has no book), :229 (startStudySession(deckId, mode)), :407-411 (currentCard looks only in currentDeck)
- R2-19 (Miller & Geraci 2011: concrete feedback improved low performers' predictions, not grades), R2-20 (Raaijmakers 2019: accuracy feedback did not help), R5-21 (Kaliisa 2024, 38 studies: dashboards not shown to raise achievement)

**Fix.** When porting P1.1, move the maths into src/lib/utils/calibration.ts as pure, unit-tested functions and change what the tab shows. (1) Lead with an action: heading 'Kort sem komu þér á óvart', lead 'Þú sagðir „Man það“ en svarið var rangt.', the card fronts, and a button 'Æfa þessi kort núna' that starts a session with exactly those card ids. That needs a new store API for a queue of card ids with a lookup across decks, because startStudySession takes a deckId and currentCard looks only in currentDeck. (2) Replace both percentage tiles with two self-referenced count lines, 'Þegar þú sagðir „Man það“ var svarið rétt í {x} af {n} skiptum.' and 'Þegar þú sagðir „Man það ekki“ var svarið rétt í {y} af {m} skiptum.', plus once 'Því stærra sem bilið er, þeim mun betur veistu hvað þú kannt.'; show a line, a colour or guidance only when its column reaches a minimum count held in one named constant. No peer comparison. (3) Delete 'það er algengt við skjálestur.' and use neutral data-viz colours, not the amber accent hue, for the 'wrong' cell (CLAUDE.md accent rule). (4) Classify from the stored rating as PED-08 settles it, and leave out off-schedule reviews once PED-11 marks them extra. (5) Book scope: label the flashcard half 'Öll minniskort' for now (FlashcardStatsTab is unscoped too). For per-book scoping the verifiers differ: stamping the route's bookSlug on new entries in rateCard survives card deletion but mislabels another book's card studied in a shared deck (decks have no book), while deriving the book from the reviewed card's source prefix (`<bookSlug> > …`) is right per card but cannot place reviews of deleted cards; stamping the card's own book, read from its source when it is reviewed, would keep both properties. All strings need a native-speaker check.

**Acceptance test.** Unit tests on calibration.ts: (a) 100 'Man það' reviews with 80 correct and 50/50 predictions with a 60-point gap no longer produce the same summary (today's headline gives 80% for both); (b) the first renders 'rétt í 80 af 100 skiptum' and no 'Man það ekki' line; (c) with fewer predictions in a column than the named minimum, no number, colour or guidance renders for that column (expectation read from the constant). Component: 'Æfa þessi kort núna' starts a session whose queue equals the listed card ids, including cards from two decks. String check: 'skjálestur' does not occur in CalibrationTab. If per-book scoping is built, another book's cards contribute nothing; otherwise the flashcard heading says 'Öll minniskort'.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the screen-reading attribution is unsupported — the R2 audit rates it 'partly' (a small screen calibration gap exists, Clinton 2019 g = .20); drop it because it cannot be attributed to one student's result.
- NOT: Kvörðun offers no action — the per-section rows link to their sections (that half cannot render on v1.2); the flashcard half has none.
- NOT: Miller & Geraci 2011 is negative evidence — it is mixed: concrete feedback improved low performers' predictions but not their grades.
- NOT: strong evidence that an item list beats aggregate feedback — R2's support is abstract-level and weak; the case rests on the base-rate flaw itself.

**Source findings:** CODE2-03, PED2-05

### PED-10. Kvörðun's per-section Ofmat/Vanmat verdicts compare one self-report (objective confidence) with another (self-graded practice) under copy that implies a measured result.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P1.1 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.2, v1.1, main · **Also in:** Code, UI

_Severity note:_ High per the pedagogy verifier (PED2-07): the verdict badges present one self-report against another as a measured result. The code verifier rated the same block medium (CODE2-12). On a v1.2 build the block never renders; it goes live on port.

**Problem.** Kvörðun's per-section block on v1.2 (CalibrationTab.svelte:61-103) maps mean objective confidence 1–5 to (avg − 1) / 4, subtracts the practice success rate and labels each section 'Ofmat', 'Vanmat' or 'Í jafnvægi' at ±20 points, under 'Sjálfsmat á markmiðum borið saman við árangur í æfingadæmum sömu kafla.' with rows reading 'Árangur í dæmum'. After a port both signals are self-reports: every practice writer on v1.1 and main records a self-grade after the answer is shown (practiceReveal's 'Rétt hjá mér' / 'Þarf að æfa meira', AdaptiveQuiz, PracticePhase and ReviewPhase), the condition in which students over-credit themselves even with the standard visible (R2-18: 73% falls to 44% but persists). The flashcard block's lead, 'Borið saman: spá þín áður en svar birtist og hvernig þér gekk í raun.', overclaims too, because its outcome is the learner's own rating after the reveal. The confidence scale is labelled two ways on main (/markmid 3 = 'Hlutlaus', 4 = 'Nokkuð viss'; ReflectPhase 3 = 'Nokkuð viss', 4 = 'Viss'), so a student who picks 'Nokkuð viss' in /nam is mapped to 50% and shown 'Vanmat' after self-grading 80%. On a v1.2 build the block stays empty (objectives: []); it goes live after a port, through ReflectPhase and /markmid.

**Evidence.**

- v1.2:src/lib/components/analytics/CalibrationTab.svelte:61-103 (sectionRows; :89 (avg − 1) / 4, :98 ±20 verdict), :110-113 (flashcard lead with 'í raun'), :173-175 (block lead), :196-206 (pills, 'Árangur í dæmum')
- main:src/lib/actions/practiceReveal.ts:162, :171 ('Rétt hjá mér' / 'Þarf að æfa meira'); the other markPracticeProblemAttempt callers on main, AdaptiveQuiz.svelte:69, study/PracticePhase.svelte:30 and study/ReviewPhase.svelte:51, are all self-grades after the reveal
- main:src/routes/[bookSlug]/markmid/+page.svelte:41-47 (3 'Hlutlaus', 4 'Nokkuð viss') against main:src/lib/components/study/ReflectPhase.svelte:23-29 (3 'Nokkuð viss', 4 'Viss')
- R2-18 (Lipko 2009: wrong answers credited 73% without a standard, 44% with it); R2-17 (Yang 2023: reading questions and answers without answering does not improve monitoring)

**Fix.** When porting P1.1, show no Ofmat / Vanmat / Í jafnvægi verdict computed from two self-reports. Recommended: port the per-section half relabelled as self-assessment, with heading 'Sjálfsmat á markmiðum og eigið mat á æfingadæmum', lead 'Hér berðu saman hversu örugg(ur) þú sagðist vera á markmiðum kaflans og hvernig þú mast eigin svör við æfingadæmum.', row text such as 'Sjálfsöryggi 100% · merkt rétt í 1 af 4 tilraunum' (attempts, not problems), and no verdict badges or 'Árangur' wording. The alternative is to hold the block back until a scored outcome exists; say plainly that this means indefinitely as built, because every practice and quiz writer self-grades, and the prequestion re-ask (PED-07) or student-graded Check-your-learning answers would still be self-grades, at best against a standard; only automatically checked answers would be scored. Drop 'í raun' from the flashcard lead as well. On main now, since both pages are live: give /markmid and ReflectPhase one shared 1–5 label table; it relabels values already stored from /markmid (3 was 'Hlutlaus'), so note or migrate them, and if a 1–5 to % mapping ever returns, the midpoint label must mean about 50%. The flashcard half's book scope is in PED-09. All strings need a native-speaker check.

**Acceptance test.** Component test on the ported tab with a fixture holding objective confidence and inline self-graded attempts: no 'Ofmat', 'Vanmat' or 'Í jafnvægi' badge renders, and no CalibrationTab string contains 'í raun' or 'Árangur í dæmum' (passes whether the block is relabelled or held back; fails on today's v1.2 code). Unit: /markmid and ReflectPhase import the same confidence-label table.

**Decision for [USER].** Whether the per-section half ships relabelled as self-assessment (recommended; it keeps the feature) or is held back until a scored outcome exists, which as built means indefinitely.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: 'hvernig þér gekk í raun' introduces the per-section list — it is the lead of the flashcard block (:110-113); the per-section block's own overclaim is 'árangur í æfingadæmum' (:174), 'Árangur í dæmum' (:206) and the verdict pills.
- NOT: the success rate comes only from practiceReveal's 'Rétt hjá mér' — /prof's AdaptiveQuiz and the /nam practice and review phases also record self-marked Rétt / Rangt after the reveal (four writers in all).
- NOT: record deckId on review entries and filter by the book's decks — decks have no book field, so that cannot be built as written (book scope is in PED-09).
- NOT: the cross-book counting is specific to Kvörðun — all flashcard state is house-wide (one store key, user-created decks with no book; /minniskort and FlashcardStatsTab are unscoped too).
