- NOT: Yang 2023 tested confidence ratings — its 'reading questions and answers' condition is an analogy for a confidence rating, not a test of one.
- NOT: the prequestion re-ask or committed Check-your-learning answers count as scored outcomes — they are still self-grades, at best against a standard; only automatically checked answers are free of self-report.
- NOT: a shared label table with 3 = 'Nokkuð viss' as the midpoint — if a 1–5 to % mapping returns, the midpoint label must mean about 50%.

**Source findings:** CODE2-12, PED2-07

### PED-11. 'Æfa' reviews every card each time and feeds those massed reviews to SM-2, so a few minutes of practice schedules cards weeks away (pre-existing on main).

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P0.3 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ The verifier kept high: it is live on main, and it undermines both spacing and the predictions that P0.3 and P1.1 rely on.

**Problem.** On main and both branches, the deck list's 'Æfa' and the completion screen's 'Æfa aftur' call startStudySession with its default 'all' mode (main flashcard.ts:226; v1.1 :229-244), whose queue is every card in the deck, due or not, and processReview never checks whether a card was due, so each 'Gott' compounds the interval: four same-day 'Gott' ratings give 1, 6, 15 and 38 days. The deck list's '{n} til endurtekningar' then means little, and /nam, which builds its review phase from getDueCards, will not show those cards for weeks. Spacing is among the best-supported effects (R2-21, Latimier 2021: spaced vs massed g = 0.74 after trim-and-fill) and learners rate massed study as more effective (R5-10, Kornell & Bjork 2008), so 'Æfa aftur' is likely to be used. Pre-existing on main; P0.3's predictions and, after a port, P1.1's data depend on it, because a recall minutes after the last exposure tracks short-term accessibility rather than retention days later, so those predictions discriminate little.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/minniskort/+page.svelte:43-45 (startStudy passes no mode), :129-133 ('Æfa'), :163-166 ('Æfa aftur'); v1.1:src/lib/stores/flashcard.ts:119-132 (buildStudyQueue supports 'due' and 'new'), :229-244 (default 'all'); main:src/lib/stores/flashcard.ts:226
- v1.1:src/lib/utils/srs.ts:76-105 (processReview has no due check), :129-155 (sortCardsByPriority keeps every card), :160 (getDueCards; isCardDue(undefined) is true, so every new card counts as due)
- massed_srs measurement, real v1.1 srs.ts: four same-day 'Gott' give 1, 6, 15, 38 days at ease 2.50
- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/nam/+page.svelte:51-53 (review phase from getDueCards); R2-21 (Latimier 2021, fulltext preprint), R5-10 (Kornell & Bjork 2008, abstract), R2-12 (Rhodes & Tauber 2011, abstract)

**Fix.** Fix on main, where it is live, and carry it into v1.1 with the merge. (1) Make 'Æfa' start a 'due' session (buildStudyQueue already supports it); getDueCards returns every new card, so also cap new cards per session with a named constant (the reviewer suggests 10). (2) Offer 'Aukaæfing (öll kort)' as a separate button on the completion screen and the deck list. In that mode rateCard records the review and the prediction with extra: true but skips the interval growth for a card whose nextReview is in the future, while still applying a lapse (quality below MIN_CORRECT_QUALITY), so a forgotten card does not keep its 38 days; today that covers 'again' and 'hard', and it changes if PED-08 maps hard to 3. (3) Give an empty due session its own empty state, and show 'Þú hefur lokið öllum kortum fyrir í dag.' only after a due session. (4) Kvörðun (PED-09) leaves out extra: true reviews. Show [USER] the 1, 6, 15, 38-day table when deciding (CLAUDE.md's SRS caution).

**Acceptance test.** Unit: (a) startStudySession from the deck list queues only due card ids plus at most the new-card cap (read from its constant); (b) in extra-practice mode, rating a not-due card 'good' leaves its studyRecords entry (nextReview, interval) unchanged and adds a reviewHistory entry with extra: true; (c) in extra mode a rating below MIN_CORRECT_QUALITY still reschedules the card; (d) a card rated 'Gott' four times on one day through the UI paths has an interval of 1 day, not 38 (fails today).

**Decision for [USER].** This changes how the SRS is applied (CLAUDE.md asks for care), so [USER] decides whether 'Æfa' becomes a due session, the cap on new cards per session, and whether extra practice records reviews without growing intervals (lapses still reschedule).

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: an 'Æfa aftur' prediction is an immediate judgment, the least diagnostic kind (Rhodes & Tauber 2011) — it is cue-only and comes after N−1 other cards, the delayed form in their sense, unless the deck has one or two cards; what massing spoils is that recall minutes after exposure tracks short-term accessibility, not retention.
- NOT: mode 'due' alone gives 'due cards plus up to 10 new' — getDueCards already returns every new card, so it needs a cap.
- NOT: extra practice should leave a not-due card's schedule alone after any rating — a lapse must still reschedule, or a forgotten card keeps its long interval.
- NOT: no owner decision is needed — it changes how the SRS is applied, so it is [USER]'s call.

**Source findings:** PED2-08

### PED-12. /prof and /nam store a Check-your-learning question by walking back over `<p>` siblings only, so 66 of 292 problems reach review with an empty or truncated question (live on main).

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** main, v1.1 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ Confirmed high: live on main, and 66 of 292 problems on the review content copy reach /prof and /nam without a usable question.

**Problem.** practiceReveal's questionText() (main practiceReveal.ts:34-42) builds the stored question by walking back from the answer over `<p>` siblings only and stops at the first non-`<p>`, so a Check-your-learning (CYL) problem that ends in a display equation, an image or a table is stored with an empty or truncated question. Example: §4.4's 'Hver er prósentunýtni hvarfs sem framleiðir 12,5 g af gasinu Freon …' is followed by its reaction equation (div.equation), so /prof and /nam show 'Dæmi 1' with no problem and 'Sýna svar' reveals 'Svar: 48,3%'. On the review content copy, 47 of 292 CYL problems are stored with an empty question and 19 more are truncated (siblings skipped: div.equation 47, div.media-inline 23, tables 5), and the stored answer repeats its 'Svar:' heading under the quiz's own 'Svar' title. Live on main (the inline reveal itself renders correctly); v1.1 inherits it unchanged, and v1.2 still has the older practiceProblems.ts. Because markPracticeProblemViewed returns early when the id exists (quiz.ts:342), snapshots already saved in students' browsers never heal.

**Evidence.**

- main:src/lib/actions/practiceReveal.ts:34-42 (questionText walks `<p>` siblings only), :177 (question captured), :185-193 (stored question and answer textContent)
- main:src/lib/stores/quiz.ts:342 (markPracticeProblemViewed keeps the first snapshot)
- profTextAll measurement, review content copy: 292 CYL problems, 47 empty questions, 19 truncated; skipped div.equation 47, div.media-inline 23, tables 5
- Real-action run over §4.4's HTML (p + div.equation + aside.check-knowledge-answer): stored content '' and answer 'Svar: 48,3%'

**Fix.** S, in practiceReveal.ts on main (v1.1 picks it up with the merge): walk back to the NEAREST preceding p.para-title (the CYL heading) or a previous check-knowledge answer, and collect every sibling in between (paragraphs, equations, figures, tables), not only `<p>`; 3 examples hold two CYL problems (5-2, 12-3, 13-4), so stopping at the example's last heading would give the first problem the wrong question. Drop the answer's h4 'Svar:' from the stored answer text. Keep the trackingId (book/chapter/section#answerId) as the item's address; PED-13's renderer uses it. Refresh the stored question and answer on the next reveal of that problem, because quiz.ts:342 keeps the first snapshot and the broken ones in students' browsers would otherwise never heal. Collected equation and table text is still flattened, and figures give no text, until PED-13.

**Acceptance test.** Unit in practiceReveal.test.ts, running the real action (not a copy of the walk) on the §4.4 structure (p + div.equation + aside.check-knowledge-answer): the stored question contains 'prósentunýtni' and the stored answer does not start with 'Svar:'. A fixture with two CYL problems in one example gives each its own question. Revealing a problem whose saved snapshot has an empty question replaces it. All three fail today.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: walk back to the example's last p.para-title — use the nearest preceding one or a previous check-knowledge answer; 3 examples hold two CYL problems (5-2, 12-3, 13-4).
- NOT: re-running scripts/profTextAll.mjs shows the fix — it embeds its own copy of the old walk; test the real action.
- NOT: the 47 and 19 were measured on namsbokasafn.is — they are on the review content copy; it is the code that is live on main.

**Source findings:** PED3-02

### PED-13. Practice review stores problems as flattened text, so powers of ten read as plain digits ('1022' for 10²²) and fractions collapse.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** main, v1.1 · **Also in:** Code, UI

_Severity note:_ The verifier kept high: in the one feature meant to consolidate a procedure, the key reads as a different number (57 of 292 items on the review content copy). Charges and subscripts stay readable.

**Problem.** Practice problems are stored as textContent snapshots (main practiceReveal.ts:191) and the quiz surfaces (/prof's AdaptiveQuiz, /nam's PracticePhase and ReviewPhase) render them as escaped text, which drops `<sup>`/`<sub>` and flattens MathJax's hidden assistive MathML. On the review content copy, 57 of 292 stored items show a power of ten as plain digits (all 57 checked against the source), e.g. 'Svar: 4,586 × 1022 Au-atóm' for 4,586 × 10²² and '2,957 × 10−2 L', and equilibrium expressions lose their fraction ('Qc=[SO3]2[SO2]2[O2]'); 126 items carry MathJax in the question or answer. Charges and subscripts are flattened but their signs survive ('2Na+(aq)+2Cl−(aq)+2H2O(l)⟶…'), so ionic equations stay readable; the meaning-changing cases are powers of ten and fractions. Live on main and inherited by v1.1. The original fragment already carries the formatting: HTML sup/sub plus pre-rendered MathJax SVG, with a data-latex span on every mjx-container on the three pages checked.

**Evidence.**

- main:src/lib/actions/practiceReveal.ts:191 (answer stored as textContent, cut at 2,000 characters)
- main:src/lib/components/AdaptiveQuiz.svelte:265, :281; main:src/lib/components/study/PracticePhase.svelte:72, :89; main:src/lib/components/study/ReviewPhase.svelte:167, :181 (v1.1 :183, :197): content and answer rendered as text
- profTextAll measurement, review content copy: 126 of 292 items carry MathJax (91 in the question, 75 in the answer); 57 show a flattened power of ten, each matched to a real `10<sup>` or msup in the source
- Real-action run: §3.1 gold-atoms answer (source `10<sup>22</sup>`) stored as 'Svar: 4,586 × 1022 Au-atóm'; §13.2 stored as 'Svar: (a) Qc=[SO3]2[SO2]2[O2]; …'
- main:src/lib/components/ContentRenderer.svelte:72-73 ({@html} on first-party content; no sanitiser dependency); data-latex spans equal mjx-containers on 3-1 (23), 13-2 (76) and 4-1 (65)

**Fix.** Step 1 (S, before release): at reveal time store a trimmed HTML snapshot instead of textContent — drop the mjx-container SVG, keep `<sup>`/`<sub>` and un-hide the assistive `<math>`, which browsers render natively — limited to a small allow-list of tags, which also keeps localStorage small; or at least serialise sup, msup and mfrac to '10^22' and 'a/b'. This fixes the 57 numbers offline with no fetch. Step 2 (M): one shared renderer that uses the stored address (book/chapter/section#answerId) to fetch the section's static HTML (same origin), takes the question siblings found by PED-12's walk plus the answer aside by id, and inserts them with their pre-rendered MathJax SVG (no MathJax runtime needed), falling back to the snapshot when the fetch fails. Sanitising adds no trust here: ContentRenderer already injects the same first-party HTML with {@html}, and the repo has no sanitiser dependency. Renames are covered, because the rename migration rewrites the stored address. Whether the fetched page is served offline for a downloaded book was not verified; test it. Build the renderer once, so end-of-chapter exercises and problem cards can reuse it.

**Acceptance test.** E2E: reveal the gold-atoms CYL in §3.1, open /prof and press 'Sýna svar': the answer box contains a `sup`, `math` or `svg` element for the exponent 22 (or, if only the serialise option is built, the text '10^22'). Assert on elements or innerHTML, never on textContent, which reads '1022' even after a correct fix. Unit: the snapshot or extractor for the §13.2 fixture keeps the Qc fraction (an mfrac element, or 'a/b' in the serialised form), and for the §4.4 fixture it includes the div.equation.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: ionic equations lose their charges and subscripts — they are flattened to inline text, but the signs survive ('2Na+(aq)+2Cl−(aq)…'), so they stay readable.
- NOT: an acceptance check that textContent no longer contains '× 10' followed by two digits — it cannot pass even after a correct fix, because the textContent of `10<sup>22</sup>` is still '1022'.
- NOT: sanitising the fetched fragment adds trust — ContentRenderer already injects the same first-party HTML unsanitised, and there is no sanitiser dependency.
- NOT: the fetched section is known to be in the offline cache for a downloaded book — not verified.

**Source findings:** PED3-03

### PED-14. 'Hreinsa gögn' on /greining promises to erase all study data but resets only the analytics store, and students get little information on what stays on the device.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** UI, Code

_Severity note:_ The verifier kept high: a confirm dialog tells a student on a shared device that everything is erased when most of it stays, and the reader branches' recall store adds free text with no delete.

**Problem.** On /greining, 'Hreinsa gögn' confirms 'Ertu viss um að þú viljir eyða öllum námsupplýsingum? Þetta er ekki hægt að afturkalla.' but then calls analyticsStore.clearAllData(), which resets only the analytics store (sessions, reading times, daily stats, activity log, streaks, the tab's goals and hourly data). Reading progress, bookmarks, highlights and notes, flashcards and predictions, learning objectives, quiz progress and, on v1.1 and v1.2, the student's free-text recall entries all stay, so on a shared school device the next user inherits them after the student was told they were gone; clearAllStorage(), which removes every `namsbokasafn*` key, exists but has no caller on main, v1.1 or v1.2. Bookmarks and learning objectives have their own delete-all; nothing deletes recall entries, flashcards, quiz or reading progress. There is no general data or privacy note: Settings says only 'Stillingar eru vistaðar sjálfkrafa í vafranum þínum' (settings), nothing mentions GoatCounter's page-view count, and /feedback says it opens the mail client only after submission. Pre-existing on main; a school vetting the reader against Persónuvernd's checklist (R5-23) will ask exactly these questions.

**Evidence.**

- main:src/routes/[bookSlug]/greining/+page.svelte:162-165 (confirm text, then analyticsStore.clearAllData()), :220 ('Hreinsa gögn'); the same on v1.1 and v1.2
- main:src/lib/stores/analytics.ts:728 (clearAllData resets the analytics state only; v1.1 :721); main:src/lib/utils/storageMigration.ts:97-102 (clearAllStorage, no caller on any of the three branches)
- v1.1:src/lib/stores/recall.ts:95-110 (removeEntry and reset exist, with no UI); main:src/routes/[bookSlug]/bokamerki/+page.svelte:109 and markmid/+page.svelte:119 (the only other delete-alls)
- main:src/app.html:13-26 (GoatCounter); main:src/lib/components/SettingsModal.svelte:416 (settings-only storage note)
- R5-22 (EDPB Guidelines 2/2023, para 44: on-device storage is not 'gaining access' while nothing leaves the device), R5-23 (Persónuvernd 2022 school checklist; abstract; not legal advice)

**Fix.** (1) Rename the /greining button 'Hreinsa tölfræði' and make the confirm say what it clears, naming both senses of 'markmið' (the tab's 'Markmið' are analytics goals, which students will read as Námsmarkmið): e.g. 'Þetta eyðir lestrartölfræði, lotum og markmiðum í Námsgreiningu á þessu tæki. Námsmarkmið, minniskort og framvinda haldast.' (add 'upprifjanir' where the recall store exists). (2) Add 'Eyða öllum gögnum á þessu tæki' in Settings: list what is stored, confirm, call clearAllStorage(), then reload at once, because each store writes its in-memory state back on its next update (e.g. recall.ts:57-62). clearAllStorage() also removes namsbokasafn:offline, the record of downloaded books, while their files stay in Cache Storage (`offline-book:<slug>`), so either delete those caches in the same action or keep that key, and say which in the confirm. (3) Add a short page 'Gögnin þín og persónuvernd', linked from the footer and /for-teachers: what you do here is stored only in this browser on this device and is not sent to us; page views are counted without cookies (GoatCounter), so page-view data does leave the device ('síðuflettingar eru taldar án vafrakaka'); a report opens your own e-mail; on a shared device, use a private window or erase the data at the end of the lesson. Mark it as not legal advice. (4) Ship per-entry delete with any view of recall text (P2.3). All strings need a native-speaker check.

**Acceptance test.** Playwright: seed namsbokasafn:reader, :flashcards, :recall (reader branches), :annotations, :objectives, :quiz and :analytics; run 'Eyða öllum gögnum á þessu tæki'; after the reload no `namsbokasafn*` key holds seeded data (a fresh default namsbokasafn:settings is allowed), /greining, /minniskort and /bokamerki show their empty states, and the download state agrees with Cache Storage (no book is listed as downloaded without its `offline-book:` cache, and no cache is left behind unlisted). The /greining confirm text no longer contains 'öllum námsupplýsingum'. The privacy page is reachable from the footer.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: nothing tells students the feedback form opens their e-mail — /feedback says so after submission ('Tölvupóstforrit ætti að hafa opnast með endurgjöfinni þinni'); nothing says so before.
- NOT: nothing tells students where their data is stored — SettingsModal says 'Stillingar eru vistaðar sjálfkrafa í vafranum þínum', which covers settings only; there is no general data or privacy note.
- NOT: recall entries persist only on v1.1 — v1.2 has recall.ts too.
- NOT: nothing can be deleted — bookmarks (/bokamerki) and learning objectives (/markmid) have their own delete-all; the gap is the global erase, plus no delete for recall entries, flashcards, quiz and reading progress.
- NOT: 'markmiðum' in the proposed confirm is unambiguous — it means /greining's analytics goals, which the button clears, while students will read it as Námsmarkmið (/markmid), which it does not.
- NOT: the privacy note can say nothing leaves the device — GoatCounter sends page-view data off the device.

**Source findings:** PED4-03

### PED-15. Students cannot report a machine-translation error from the page they are reading; /feedback is a mailto form with no prefill.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** after release · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** main, v1.1 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ Medium. The verifier raised the member to high only because, in paged mode (v1.1's current default; the launch default is open call 3), the MT notice is off-screen on arrival, and that part is UI-09. For the reporting link alone the verifier gives medium.

**Problem.** A student who spots a machine-translation error cannot report it from the page: the 'Vélþýtt efni' banner (PreviewBanner.svelte:16-22) is text only, and /feedback is a mailto form whose chapter and section fields are typed by hand (placeholders 't.d. 1 eða 1.2' and 't.d. 1-1', an old slug form) with no URL-parameter prefill. Neither /feedback nor /for-teachers is reachable from the reader, the catalogue or the navigation: each is linked only from the other. The OpenStax original is linked at book level from every section page (BookAttribution) and from /leyfi, but no page links to the matching English section. Every section of the review content copy is unreviewed MT (no `reviewed` key on any of the 216), though R4-24 suggests readers notice disfluent or inconsistent wording more readily than fluent but wrong text. Pre-existing on main and inherited by v1.1; the banner being off-screen on paged arrival is UI-09.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/PreviewBanner.svelte:16-22 (text only, no link)
- v1.1:src/routes/feedback/+page.svelte:57-59 (mailto), :157-162 (typed chapter and section fields, placeholders 't.d. 1 eða 1.2' and 't.d. 1-1'); no searchParams use
- main: /feedback is linked only from src/routes/for-teachers/+page.svelte:164, :206, :244, and /for-teachers only from src/routes/feedback/+page.svelte:206
- v1.1:src/lib/components/BookAttribution.svelte:42-46 (book-level link to the OpenStax original), rendered on every section page; review content copy toc.json: no `reviewed` key on any of 216 sections (absent means MT)
- R4-24 (Martindale & Carpuat 2018, adult pilot, abstract: users react to disfluency, much less to adequacy); v1.1:src/lib/components/TextHighlighter.svelte:449 (selection popup on mouseup only)

**Fix.** (1) Put a 'Tilkynna villu' link in the MT banner, and in the compact MT indicator UI-09 adds near the pager, opening /feedback?bok=…&kafli=…&hluti=… with the fields prefilled. /feedback is prerendered, so read page.url.searchParams in the browser (onMount or an effect), never in a load function or during prerender; Svelte escapes the values. (2) Optionally add 'Tilkynna villu í þessum texta' to the selection popup, passing the selection as `texti`, capped in length (URL and mailto body limits); the popup opens on mouseup only and may never appear on phones, so the banner link is the primary path. (3) Link /feedback (or /for-teachers) from the reader's navigation or footer. (4) Keep the 'opens your e-mail' caveat, say it before submission, and repeat it in the privacy note (PED-14). (5) Optional: a per-section English link needs efni to emit the OpenStax page slug in page-data; the book-level link already exists. All strings need a native-speaker check.

**Acceptance test.** E2E at 390×844 on a section page, in either reading mode: the MT notice contains a 'Tilkynna villu' link; following it opens /feedback with the book, chapter and section fields equal to the section's own route params (read from the URL, not hardcoded); /feedback is reachable from the reader's navigation or footer. Unit: a `texti` value longer than the cap is truncated before it reaches the mailto body.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: no page links to the English original — BookAttribution ('Byggt á Chemistry 2e …') links the OpenStax book from every section page, and /leyfi links it; what is missing is a link to the matching English section.
- NOT: toc.json marks the 216 sections reviewed=false — there is no `reviewed` key at all (absent means MT, content.ts:38).
- NOT: /feedback is reachable through /for-teachers — /for-teachers is itself linked only from /feedback, so neither is reachable from the reader.
- NOT: students are the readers best placed to catch errors — that is opinion; R4-24 suggests fluent but wrong MT passes unnoticed, so students can flag disfluency and inconsistent terms more readily than adequacy errors.
- NOT: an error in §1.5 as a verified example — it was not checked.
- NOT: lang='en' on the whole .term-en span — wrap only the English word, not the '(e. …)' wrapper; and showTermEnglish already defaults to on (settings.ts:82).

**Source findings:** PED4-12

### PED-16. Open call 2 (recall per section or per sub-section): v1.1 prompts once per section while the plan asks for per sub-section, and the pager's units are not a clean sub-section structure.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P0.2 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** UI, Code

_Severity note:_ Medium, as three of the four verifiers gave it (PED3-11's authored-question variant was lowered to low, as new scope). Nothing is broken: the build and its own plan disagree, and the call has to be made before v1.1 reaches students.

**Problem.** On v1.1 the recall prompt fires once per section, on first completion (section `+page.svelte:171-180`): `PagedReaderControls` declares `onsubsectioncomplete` and calls it once per paged unit (`:37`, `:40`, `:214-218`), but the section page passes only `container` and `oncomplete` (`:442`), so the hook has no consumer. The plan asks for recall per sub-section (plan line 189; acceptance criterion 6, line 275), so plan and build disagree and criterion 6 cannot pass until one of them changes. The pager's units are not a clean sub-section structure: `buildUnits` (`paginate.ts:155-175`) makes one unit of the content before the first `<section>` (with its `<header>`) and one of every top-level `<section>`, footnotes included, so on the ② preview the 114 numbered sections hold 428 units (median 4 per section, median 437 words per unit, 75 under 150 words, 3 title-only: 14.3, 14.4 and 16.3), §3.1's units run 1 / 9 / 17 / 1 pages (unit 1 is the title and one paragraph, unit 4 the footnote list) and §1.3 is a single unit. Sections run a median 1,782 words (p90 3,781; 9 of 114 at 4,000 or more), and 172 of the 428 units already contain an authored Check-your-learning question with its answer. Unit boundaries exist only in paged mode (`+page.svelte:439-443`), so a per-unit trigger would never fire for scrolled readers. v1.1 only (neither the recall prompt nor the pager is on main); that today's prompt also fires on intros and rollups is PED-04's type gate, not this call.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:37, :40, :214-218 (hook declared and called once per unit, 0 consumers); v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:442 (only oncomplete passed), :171-180 (recall on first completion)
- v1.1:docs/plans/2026-04-22-screen-vs-paper-reader-plan.md:189 ('fires on sub-section completion') and :275 (acceptance criterion 6: 'free-recall prompt fires per sub-section')
- Unit census with a mirror of buildUnits over the ② preview: 428 units in 114 numbered sections, median 4 per section and 437 words per unit, 75 under 150 words, 3 title-only; 172 of 428 contain aside.check-knowledge-answer; under '≥ 400 words and not the last unit' 157 units qualify, 125 after a cap of 2 per section
- UI capture, paged §3.1: units of 1 / 9 / 17 / 1 pages; page 1 is header + p, page 28 is h2 'Neðanmálsgreinar' + ol.footnotes-list; §1.3 is one unit
- R2-03 (Wissman et al. 2011: interim recall helps later sections; abstract, lab); R5-05 (Nückles et al. 2010: fixed, repeated prompts lost their effect, fading kept it; university); R5-06 (Sonnenberg & Bannert 2016: about half of prompts used as intended)

**Fix.** This is open call 2, so the options go to [USER]. (A) Per section, as built: amend plan line 189 and acceptance criterion 6 to match and leave the hook unwired (effort S). (B) Per sub-section, as a short interim prompt on top of the section-end recall: wire `onsubsectioncomplete` to a one-line question such as 'Áður en þú heldur áfram: hvað var aðalatriðið í „{heading}“?' with 'Halda áfram' (saves when not empty) and a one-tap 'Sleppa', no autofocus. Guards: a length threshold to tune (R2 suggests about 300 words, PED2-11 400); at most 2 per section; never after the last unit, so 'Næsta' still finishes the section (ruling 3); never after the intro run or a footnotes unit, keyed on `section.footnotes` / `ol.footnotes-list` (a 'starts with an h2' rule lets 'Neðanmálsgreinar' through); fall back to the section title where the intro run has no heading (unit 1 of §1.2, 858 words, is one); fading (e.g. none after 3 saved interim recalls in a chapter, or after 3 skips in a row) as a hypothesis to test; `RecallEntry` gains a `unitIndex`. Tell [USER] the scale: 157 units qualify under '≥ 400 words, not the last unit', 125 after the cap, before fading. The section summary that PED-19 uses as a standard has no sub-section grain, and scrolled readers get no interim prompt unless a scroll trigger is added (effort M). (C) Per sub-section, as an authored check question instead of a second free-recall box: after a sub-section whose Check-your-learning item has not been attempted, show 'Stutt athugun' with that item (attempt-first, PED-22) and 'Sleppa'; efni authors new items only for the sub-sections that have none; pilot on 2–3 chapters (effort M, plus efni authoring). The case for questions over free recall rests on between-study moderators (Yang et al. 2021), not a head-to-head test. B or C can sit behind a setting with section-end recall as one value (R2's design implication), but a scheduler, fading, a setting and counters is a lot for one developer; a lighter path is to pilot a fixed rule on a branch with one class and read PED-17's counters first. Reviewers' recommendation, for [USER] to accept or reject: launch v1.1 with A and amend the plan, then decide on B or C from a class pilot. Whatever [USER] chooses, write it into the plan.

**Acceptance test.** Fails today: plan line 189 and criterion 6 say 'per sub-section' while the build fires once per section. Passes when the plan matches [USER]'s choice and: if [USER] chooses A, an e2e pins that paging through all four units of §3.1 shows no `[aria-label='Upprifjun']` region before 'Næsta' on the last page; if [USER] chooses B, scheduler unit tests show no prompt for a unit one word under the chosen threshold T and one at T, at most 2 per section, none after the last unit, none after the intro run or a `section.footnotes` unit, and the section title used where the unit has no heading, plus an e2e on §3.1 with interim prompts after units 2 and 3 only, each dismissed with one tap; if [USER] chooses C, an e2e on a paged section shows 'Stutt athugun' once after a sub-section with an unattempted Check-your-learning item, 'Sleppa' hides it, answering stores an attempt, and sub-sections without an item show nothing.

**Decision for [USER].** Open call 2: recall per section (as built) or per sub-section; if per sub-section, whether as a short free-recall prompt (B) or an authored check question (C), as a fixed rule or a student setting, and whether to pilot it with one class first. Whichever is chosen, plan line 189 and acceptance criterion 6 must be updated to match.

**efni side.** Only if [USER] chooses option C, and only for sub-sections that have no Check-your-learning item (about half): efni would author short Icelandic check questions with answers and emit them as page-data keyed to the sub-section id. Options A and B, and a C pilot that uses the existing items, need nothing from efni.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: 425 units, median 439 words per unit, 80 units under 150 words — that script skips `<header>`; the runtime's buildUnits gives 428, 437 and 75 (75–83 depending on whether the title and CSS-hidden objectives count).
- NOT: sub-sections have no authored question — 172 of 428 units (141 of 293 content sub-sections) contain a Check-your-learning question with its answer; only the Samantekt summary and the end-of-chapter exercises are section-level.
- NOT: Foroughi et al. 2015 shows that unit boundaries are a safe place for related prompts — it interrupted reading with unrelated maths tasks between paragraphs; it shows that unrelated interruptions hurt comprehension.
- NOT: the event fires only after content sub-sections — it fires for every unit, including the intro run (title-only in 3 sections) and the footnotes section.
- NOT: an 'opens with an h2' rule keeps the footnotes unit out — its h2 is 'Neðanmálsgreinar'.
- NOT: multiple-choice or numeric items beat free recall head to head — that rests on between-study moderators in Yang et al. 2021 (free recall g = 0.238, multiple choice 0.567; feedback 0.537 vs 0.374).
- NOT: the authored-question variant is medium severity, or effort S with nothing from efni — it is low-severity new scope, and authoring the missing items is efni work.
- NOT: 'decide from the counters' works as written — localStorage counters are per device and reach [USER] only through student exports (PED-17).

**Source findings:** PED2-11, PED4-11, UI1-18, PED3-11

### PED-17. No event records that a prompt was shown, skipped or abandoned, or that a reader switched reading mode, so the open calls cannot be revisited from usage data.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ Medium, as all three verifiers gave it (CODE3-04 and PED4-06 were lowered from high): nothing is broken and no learner is harmed; without counters the open calls can only be revisited from impressions.

**Problem.** No code records that a recall prompt was shown, skipped ('Sleppa' only calls `onclose`, `RecallPrompt.svelte:36-38`) or abandoned, v1.2's pre-question records nothing (`PreQuestionPrompt.svelte` only calls `onclose`), `setReadingMode` (`settings.ts:166`) logs no switch, and page turns are not logged. Two things are already stored and need no new event: each saved recall is a `RecallEntry` with book, chapter and section keys and `createdAt` (`recall.ts:18-25`, `:77-93`), and each flashcard review stores `predictedKnown` with `wasCorrect` (`flashcard.ts:295-301`); prediction compliance is 100% by construction, because the card flips only through `predictAndFlip`. `analyticsStore.activityLog` is the wrong home: `ActivityType` is a closed union with no recall or pre-question type (`analytics.ts:62`), the log keeps 500 entries (`:221`) and the /greining export carries only the last 100 (`:713`) and no recall or flashcard data, while `logActivity` has three callers (the Pomodoro timer twice, the objective toggle), so `dailyStats.flashcardsReviewed` and `quizzesTaken` never increase. The flashcard history is one global list of the last 500 reviews across all books, each carrying only `cardId` (`flashcard.ts:41-49`, `:63`). With no backend, any of this reaches [USER] only through exports that students choose to send; for reading mode, a stored value that differs from the shipped default already shows a switch, so what an uninstrumented first release loses is explicit-versus-default for profiles on the default and the timing and count of switches (recording the choice itself is CODE3-03's settings-store fix). The missing events concern v1.1 and v1.2; the analytics gaps (export contents, the daily counts that never increase, and 'Hreinsa gögn', whose confirm promises to erase 'öllum námsupplýsingum' but which clears analytics only) are pre-existing on main.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/stores/analytics.ts:62 (closed ActivityType), :221 (activityLog slice(-500)), :696-719 (exportAnalytics: no recall or flashcard data), :713 (last 100 entries); same on main
- v1.1:src/lib/components/RecallPrompt.svelte:36-38 ('Sleppa' writes nothing); v1.2:src/lib/components/PreQuestionPrompt.svelte:52-57 (only onclose); v1.1:src/lib/stores/settings.ts:166 (setReadingMode logs nothing)
- v1.1:src/lib/stores/recall.ts:18-25, :77-93 (saved recalls stored with keys and createdAt); v1.1:src/lib/stores/flashcard.ts:41-49, :63, :295-301 (predictedKnown and wasCorrect per review; one global history of 500, cardId only)
- grep for 'logActivity(' in non-test src on main, v1.1 and v1.2: 3 callers each (PomodoroTimer.svelte:62, :81 and the objective toggle); main:src/routes/[bookSlug]/greining/+page.svelte:163-164 ('Hreinsa gögn' calls analyticsStore.clearAllData() only)
- R5-06 (Sonnenberg & Bannert 2016: about half of prompts used as intended); R5 and R2 design implications ('measure compliance before deciding the open calls')

**Fix.** Keep small aggregate counters, never free text, and never through `logActivity` (its 500-entry log and 100-entry export would drop them). Two homes were proposed: a `counters` field on `AnalyticsState` (R2's design implication), which reuses `exportAnalytics` and `clearAllData` with no new store, or a pure `recordEvent()` plus a store under `namsbokasafn:study-log` (CODE3-04); the first is lighter. Key counters by book and event, not by section, so no rename migration is needed (a later per-section breakdown needs a `renameSections()` and a line in `migrateRenamedSections()`, #258). Events, as a closed union: recall shown, skipped and abandoned (the prompt unmounts with neither save nor skip, e.g. when `afterNavigate` closes it at `+page.svelte:111`), recall length in buckets, also keyed by book, with a trigger dimension (section end or interim) if both recall triggers ship (PED-16); pre-question shown and dismissed once P1.2 is ported; reading-mode switches to paged and to scrolled from the settings modal; optionally pager next, back and oversized-page counts. Do not re-log what is stored: count saved recalls from the recall store, and export aggregates of the existing prediction-and-outcome pairs in `reviewHistory`, scored with the miss rule PED-20 settles rather than raw `wasCorrect`; add an optional `deckId` to `FlashcardReviewEntry` in `rateCard` (additive, no migration) so the pairs can be split by book. Export through one student-initiated 'Flytja út' on /greining, counts only, showing what leaves the device; make 'Hreinsa gögn' clear the new keys and do what its confirm promises. Optionally show the student a self-referenced line such as 'Þú hefur vistað 12 upprifjanir og sleppt 5' (native-speaker check). Ship it with v1.1 so the first classroom weeks produce counts.

**Acceptance test.** Unit and component tests (component tests need CODE3-05's two config lines): mounting RecallPrompt records recall-shown exactly once; 'Sleppa' records recall-skipped; unmounting with neither save nor skip records recall-abandoned; a stored unknown counter key is dropped on load; 10,000 recorded page-turn events keep the serialised counters under 2 KB; the /greining export contains the counters and no field holding the typed recall text; after 'Hreinsa gögn' no counter remains. All fail today (no counters exist).

**Decision for [USER].** Whether to ask classes for these exports and which fields to include (the readers are minors; a data sheet for schools belongs to the teacher-integration work), and whether any count may leave the device other than through a student's own export, for example as events to the GoatCounter counter the site already loads: a privacy decision about minors.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: saved recalls and flashcard predictions go unrecorded — both are stored already (the recall store; predictedKnown and wasCorrect in reviewHistory).
- NOT: a 'prediction' event is needed — the card flips only through predictAndFlip, so prediction compliance is 100% by construction.
- NOT: the first release is the only chance to see who switches the default — a stored readingMode that differs from the default already shows a switch; what is lost is explicit-versus-default on the default, and the timing and count of switches.
- NOT: every open call depends on these counts — the page-height budget (call 1) does not turn on compliance data.
- NOT: add recall, prequestion, prediction and readingMode to ActivityType and log through logActivity — the log keeps 500 entries and the export the last 100; use aggregate counters.
- NOT: the 500 cap belongs to analyticsStore as a whole — it belongs to activityLog; a counters field on AnalyticsState is not affected.

**Source findings:** CODE3-04, PED2-12, PED4-06

### PED-18. The recall prompt asks for 'það helsta sem þú manst úr kaflanum' without naming the section, and on phones it shows a Ctrl+Enter hint and a save button that wraps.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P0.2 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ Medium, as all three verifiers gave it: the prompt works, but its wording asks for the weakest form of recall and two phone defects show on every prompt.

**Problem.** v1.1's prompt is headed 'Kafli lesinn — rifjaðu upp' and asks, in the imperative, 'Skrifaðu niður, án þess að fletta til baka, það helsta sem þú manst úr kaflanum.', followed by the accurate rationale 'Upprifjun úr minni styrkir námið meira en endurlestur.' (`RecallPrompt.svelte:62`, `:65-66`); it never names the section and asks for an unbounded free recall. What the evidence supports is a change in the form of the retrieval, not question-versus-command wording: free recall has the smallest classroom quiz effect and short answer one of the largest (Yang et al. 2021: g = 0.238 vs 0.638, format being a named moderator), short-answer tests beat multiple choice and rereading for 16-year-olds (Stenlund et al. 2016), while in Bisra et al. 2018 the interrogative-versus-imperative contrast (.559 vs .395, pooled across all levels) was not significant post hoc. 86 of the 114 numbered sections contain at least one worked example (289 in all; 28 have none), where what is worth recalling is a procedure, which 'það helsta' does not ask for. On phones the hint 'Ctrl+Enter til að vista' renders unconditionally (`:77`; the handler at `:40-45` also takes Meta+Enter) in `--text-tertiary` (2.90:1 in light mode), and 'Vista upprifjun' wraps to two lines at 390 px (114.5×56); in the phone capture the timer pill covers the hint and the tools button overlaps the save button. The placeholder 'Það sem ég man...' uses three dots rather than an ellipsis. v1.1 only (v1.2's copy is the same apart from icons, and superseded); the prompt firing on rollups and Formáli is PED-04's type gate.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/RecallPrompt.svelte:62 (heading), :65-66 (instruction and rationale), :72 (placeholder), :77 (hint, always rendered, --text-tertiary), :90 ('Vista upprifjun')
- UI capture at 390×844: 'Vista upprifjun' is 114.5×56 (two lines) and the hint is visible; the timer pill covers the hint and the tools button overlaps the save button
- Worked-example census (② preview): 86 of 114 numbered sections contain an aside.example, 289 in all, 28 with none, 19 with exactly one
- R2-01 (Yang et al. 2021: free recall g = 0.238 vs short answer 0.638); R2-06 (Stenlund et al. 2016, age 16, abstract); R3-04 (Bisra et al. 2018: format contrast n.s. post hoc, high school g = .426); R2-07 (Pyburn et al. 2014, general chemistry)

**Fix.** Release subset (S): (1) show the shortcut hint only on fine pointers (`@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`, or Tailwind 4's pointer variants), label it '⌘+Enter til að vista' on macOS if the platform check is cheap, and move it to `--text-secondary` (5.45:1); (2) shorten the button to 'Vista' so it fits on one line, and check at 390×844 that neither the timer pill nor the tools button covers the hint or the buttons; (3) use a real ellipsis: 'Það sem ég man…'; (4) name the section and bound the request, e.g. heading 'Rifjaðu upp kafla {1.2}' and body 'Hver eru 2–3 meginatriði kaflans? Svaraðu með eigin orðum, án þess að fletta til baka.', keeping the rationale sentence; the point is the bounded '2–3 meginatriði', not the question mark. Land PED-04's type gate first, or copy that names the section reads wrong at the end of the Lykilhugtök and Samantekt rollups. The first-encounter explainer is PED-25. Native-speaker check of every string. After release (M): where a section has an answered exercise or Check-your-learning item, pose that question instead and use its key answer as the compare step (shared with PED-19 and PED2-06; under PED2-06's verified strict filter, 60 of 114 sections have an answered conceptual exercise), with the bounded recall as the fallback. In worked-example sections prefer a Check-your-learning-style 'Leystu …' item to a 'Lýstu skrefunum' stem, which is itself imperative and is the self-explanation-on-examples form Bisra rates lowest (.362), and do not anchor on the first example (§3.1 has 8). Compare each variant with today's wording on the same sections, using PED-17's save and skip counts; a three-arm A/B on voluntary exports from a few classes would be underpowered. Interim prompts, if [USER] chooses them in open call 2 (PED-16), name the sub-section instead.

**Acceptance test.** Component tests: with `matchMedia('(pointer: coarse)')` matching, the Ctrl+Enter hint is absent; on a fine pointer it is present and uses `--text-secondary`; on §1.2 the heading contains the section number taken from the section's TOC entry; the placeholder ends with '…' (U+2026). E2E at 390×844: the save button is one line, at most 44 px tall, and its box does not intersect the timer pill or the tools button. All fail today. If the exercise-question variant ships: on a section with an answered exercise the prompt poses that exercise, and PED-17's counters split saved and skipped by variant.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the instruction opens with its subordinate clause — it opens with the imperative 'Skrifaðu niður'; 'án þess að fletta til baka' sits between the verb and its object.
- NOT: prompts work better as questions than as commands (Bisra et al. 2018: .559 vs .395) — post-hoc tests found no significant difference among formats, and content-specific prompts (.510) did not beat general ones (.678).
- NOT: the Bisra figures are high-school estimates — they are pooled across all levels; high school is g = .426, and education level was not a significant moderator (p = .384).
- NOT: summarising without training is the weakest generative form — R3-06 says only that summarising needs training.
- NOT: 86 of 114 sections are built around worked examples — 86 contain at least one; 19 have exactly one and 28 none.
- NOT: Pyburn et al. 2014 supports open questions — its benefit came entirely from multiple-choice questions, and elaborative interrogation had a negative effect.
- NOT: the prompt was captured on Formáli, key-equations and exercises pages — only the key-terms and summary rollups were captured; the rest is inferred from code.
- NOT: about 55 of 114 sections have an answered conceptual exercise — PED2-06's verifier recounted 92 exercises in 60 of 114 sections.
- NOT: worked-example headings ('Lausn') are a standard to compare a recall against.
- NOT: an A/B of the procedure stem against the explain stem — the two apply to different sections; compare each with today's wording on the same sections.
- NOT: the exercise-question variant is effort S — it must fetch the exercises rollup and answer key and pick by data-section (M).

**Source findings:** UI1-17, PED2-13, PED3-09

### PED-19. Recall is write-only: the acknowledgement disappears after 1.2 s and nothing lets the student compare what they wrote with the section's own summary.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P0.2 · **Stage:** after release · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** UI, Code

_Severity note:_ Medium, as both verifiers gave it (both reviewers said high): a compare step goes beyond the P0.2 spec (plan line 48 asks only to replace the celebration), and recall without feedback still has a classroom effect (Yang et al. 2021: g = 0.374 without feedback, 0.537 with corrective feedback), so the gap is a smaller benefit, not harm.

**Problem.** After 'Vista upprifjun', v1.1 shows 'Vistað — góð upprifjun festir efnið í minni.' and closes the prompt after 1,200 ms (`RecallPrompt.svelte:33`, a timer that is never cleared); nothing ever shows the entry again, because the store's read API (`getForSection`, `getForBook`, `removeEntry`, `reset`; `recall.ts:95-116`) has no UI caller, and there is no edit, no delete and no second chance once a section's prompt is gone. The book already ships a standard to compare against: each of the 114 numbered sections has its own block in the chapter's Samantekt rollup (`section.summary-section` with `id="summary-<data-module-id>"`, e.g. `#summary-m68667` for §1.2 in `1-summary.html`), a median 5 sentences (p10 3, p90 9, max 17) and about 10,700 words in all, and all 21 `N-summary.html` files are in the offline manifest. The section's objectives (on 113 of 114 numbered modules) are not that standard: they are goals, and the Námsmarkmið panel already lets the student tick them. The pager's `completedUnits` set knows which units the student skipped before 'Næsta' finished the section, but it is private to `PagedReaderControls` and not reactive, so naming skipped units needs new wiring. The prompt also appears at the wrong moment when the header 'Merkja sem lesið' is pressed on arrival (it stays mounted under page 1 until 'Sleppa', a save or navigation), an unsent draft is lost when the student navigates away, and a dismissed prompt never returns. v1.1 only (v1.2's June copy is the same and superseded).

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/RecallPrompt.svelte:28-34 (save, then setTimeout(onclose, 1200) at :33), :57 (saved line); v1.1:src/lib/stores/recall.ts:95-116 (read API, no UI caller)
- Summary-standard census (② preview): 114 of 114 numbered sections have a section.summary-section block keyed by module id; sentences median 5, p10 3, p90 9, max 17; about 10,700 words; only 3 of 114 blocks contain 'þ.e.' or a decimal that a naive [.!?] split would cut
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:55 (completedUnits, private), :214-229; v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:111 (prompt reset on navigation), :354-362 (header 'Merkja sem lesið')
- R2-01 (Yang et al. 2021: corrective feedback g = 0.537 vs 0.374); R2-18 (Lipko et al. 2009: a standard cut credit given to wrong definitions from 73% to 44%; middle school, abstract)

**Fix.** (1) Replace the auto-dismiss with a compare step after saving: heading 'Berðu saman við samantektina', lead 'Merktu við það sem þú nefndir:', one checkbox per sentence of the section's Samantekt block (keeping inline markup such as `<sup>`), then 'Þú nefndir {k} af {n} atriðum.' and a 'Lokið' button that also works with nothing ticked (native-speaker check). (2) Fetch `chapters/NN/N-summary.html` when the step opens and read `#summary-<moduleId>`, which keeps `toc.json` lean (building it in would add about 10,700 words); split sentences with an abbreviation-safe splitter shared with the P1.3 fix; label the list 'Vélþýtt' while the summary is unreviewed, since its terms can differ from the section's (PED2-17). (3) Store `k`, `n` and the ticked indices as optional fields on `RecallEntry` (the validator only checks that `entries` is an array); they ride on the recall `renameSections()` that the main merge must add. (4) When units were skipped before 'Næsta' finished the section (ruling 3, unchanged), add 'Þú last ekki: „{heading}“' links that open each skipped unit; this needs `completedUnits` exposed through `onsubsectioncomplete` or the completion callback. (5) Give the prompt a second chance: a small 'Rifja upp aftur' button in the footer of a read section, with the earlier entry ('Upprifjun þín frá {dagsetning}') shown only after the new attempt so that it does not turn into rereading; read `$recallStore` inside any `$derived`, never `getForSection()`, which reads through `get()` and would compute once. The automatic delayed re-recall is PED-28. (6) Keep an unsent draft per section, in the recall store so the same rename hook covers it, with storage calls in try/catch. Do not use the objectives as the standard, and add no second tick store. If [USER] chooses per-sub-section prompts in open call 2 (PED-16), the compare step stays at the section end, because the summary has no sub-section grain. Later, P2.3 'Upprifjanir mínar' can list entries with per-entry delete and a Markdown download like AnnotationSidebar's.

**Acceptance test.** E2E on §1.2: after typing a recall and pressing 'Vista', a list appears whose items equal the sentences of `#summary-m68667` in `1-summary.html` (read from the file at test time); it is still there 2 s later and closes only on 'Lokið', which works with nothing ticked; the saved `namsbokasafn:recall` entry carries `n` and the ticked indices. A unit the student never paged through is named in a 'Þú last ekki' link after 'Næsta' on the last page, and units paged through are not. Reloading a read section shows 'Rifja upp aftur', which opens the prompt and shows the earlier entry only after the new one is saved; text typed into an open prompt survives navigating to §1.3 and back. A unit test of the splitter over the 114 summary blocks finds no sentence ending in 'þ.e.' and no split decimal. All fail today.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: severity high — medium (see the severity note).
- NOT: free recall without feedback is 'the weakest classroom quiz format' — Yang et al. 2021 has two separate moderators: quiz format (free recall g = 0.238, the smallest) and feedback (0.537 vs 0.374).
- NOT: the current prompt leads students to credit their own wrong answers — Lipko et al. 2009 presupposes self-scoring, which v1.1 lacks; it argues for showing a standard once a tick step exists. Its population is verified only as 'middle school', not as grade 8.
- NOT: a summary sentence is an idea unit, or the blocks match the size of the idea-unit lists in that research — the abstract gives no idea-unit counts.
- NOT: completedUnits can drive the skipped-unit links as it is — it is private and not reactive, and the page does not pass onsubsectioncomplete.
- NOT: objectives on 113 of 115 numbered sections — 113 of 114 (19.1 lacks them; 115 counted Formáli).
- NOT: the section's objectives as the compare checklist — they are goals, not a standard, and are already tickable.
- NOT: pressing the header 'Merkja sem lesið' on arrival uses the prompt up at once — it stays mounted until 'Sleppa', a save or navigation; the problem is the moment it appears and that it never returns.
- NOT: about 10,000 words added to toc.json — about 10,700.

**Source findings:** PED2-04, PED4-02

### PED-20. Predict-first never shows the prediction back, and confident errors are neither flagged nor repeated in the session.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P0.3 · **Stage:** after release · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ Medium, as both verifiers gave it (both findings confirmed): predictions are collected and stored, but the session makes no use of them.

**Problem.** On v1.1 the card flips only through 'Man það' / 'Man það ekki' (`minniskort/+page.svelte:32-35`, `:206-220`), and after the flip the prediction disappears: nothing on the answer side, the rating row or the completion screen ('Vel gert!', 'Þú hefur lokið öllum kortum fyrir í dag.', `:149`, `:152`) shows it back, although `predictedKnown` is written to `reviewHistory` (`flashcard.ts:300`) and nothing on v1.1 reads it. A card rated 'Aftur' does not come back in the session: `rateCard` just advances the index (`flashcard.ts:324`), the queue holds each card once (`:119-132`, `:229-243`) and 'again' is next due a day later, and /nam's `ReviewPhase` builds its items as `$derived` from props (`:25-28`) and never re-queues either. High-confidence errors are the ones most likely to be corrected after feedback (Butterfield & Metcalfe 2001; Metcalfe 2017; adult lab, abstracts), and practising to a criterion of correct recalls within a session and then relearning in spaced sessions gave durable retention (Rawson & Dunlosky 2011: 3 experiments, 533 students; they recommend three correct recalls). Two traps for any fix: `wasCorrect` is `quality >= 3` (`flashcard.ts:299`) while 'Erfitt' is quality 2, a lapse in SM-2 (`srs.ts:18`, `:29`), so a correct but effortful 'Erfitt' would count as a confident error (PED2-02); and rating a repeated card through `processReview` would move the SM-2 ease and interval a second time the same day. The two-button tap does not by itself ensure an attempt: making judgements barely changes memory (Double et al. 2018: g = 0.054), and for definitions overt retrieval beats covert (Tauber et al. 2018: d = 0.43, seen only in a secondary source). v1.1 only; P0.3 is not on main.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/minniskort/+page.svelte:32-35 (predictAndFlip), :195-204 (answer side shows no prediction), :206-220 (predict), :149, :152 (completion copy); v1.1:src/lib/components/study/ReviewPhase.svelte:25-28 (items derived from props)
- v1.1:src/lib/stores/flashcard.ts:299 (wasCorrect = quality >= 3), :300 (predictedKnown stored), :324 (currentCardIndex + 1, no re-queue); v1.1:src/lib/utils/srs.ts:18, :29 ('Erfitt' = quality 2, a lapse)
- UI capture: no echo of the prediction after the flip (phone, rating-after-predict)
- R2-14 (Butterfield & Metcalfe 2001; Metcalfe 2017: hypercorrection, abstract); Rawson & Dunlosky 2011 (abstract: criterion of three correct recalls, then three spaced relearning sessions); R2-13 (Double et al. 2018: g = 0.054); R2-04 (Tauber et al. 2018)

**Fix.** (1) Echo the prediction on the answer side, on /minniskort and in /nam's ReviewPhase: 'Þú sagðir: Man það' / 'Þú sagðir: Man það ekki' (`predictedKnown` is already component state; S). (2) After the rating, when the student predicted 'Man það' and missed, show 'Þú hélst að þú myndir þetta — skoðaðu svarið vel. Kortið kemur aftur í lok æfingarinnar.' and append the card once to the end of the session queue, deduplicated because `studyQueue` persists in localStorage; the message can come only after the rating, since only then is the miss known. Define the miss explicitly: with today's labels only 'Aftur', not 'Erfitt'; after PED2-02's correctness step ('Rétt' / 'Að hluta' / 'Rangt'), 'Rangt' or 'Að hluta'. The first rating sets the schedule; the repeat is logged with `repeat: true` and never calls `processReview`, which leaves `srs.ts` untouched (CLAUDE.md asks for care there), and a ported P1.1 ignores repeats. ReviewPhase needs its own local re-queue. (3) Optionally re-queue every missed card until one correct in-session recall, with a cap of a few repeats; a single extra trial is not a criterion. (4) On the completion screen keep 'Vel gert!' and add one informational line, 'Þú spáðir rétt um {x} af {y} kortum', computed with the same miss rule (not raw `wasCorrect`) and paired with an action when x is low, such as a button to practise the cards the student thought they knew (native-speaker check). (5) Optionally make the hint ask for an attempt, e.g. 'Segðu svarið í huganum eða upphátt — manstu það?', with an optional 'Skrifa svar' field shown as 'Þitt svar' beside the back.

**Acceptance test.** Store unit tests: a review with `predictedKnown: true` rated 'again' appends the card id to the session queue exactly once (a second such rating adds no second copy), and rating the repeat leaves that card's `studyRecords` entry (ease, interval, due date) as the first rating set it; with today's labels, a `predictedKnown: true` review rated 'Erfitt' appends nothing. E2E on /minniskort: after 'Man það' the answer side contains 'Þú sagðir: Man það'; after 'Man það' and 'Aftur' the message appears and the card returns as the session's last card; after a 3-card session the completion line's x and y equal counts recomputed from that session's `reviewHistory` with the miss rule above. The echo test also passes on /nam. All fail today.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: show the confident-error message on the answer side — the miss is known only after the rating.
- NOT: compute 'x af y' (or a test of it) from wasCorrect — it is quality >= 3, so 'Man það' followed by an effortful 'Erfitt' would count as a mis-prediction, and the test would lock that in.
- NOT: re-queueing every 'Rangt' card once is a criterion of one correct recall — it is one extra trial; a criterion re-queues until correct, with a cap.
- NOT: rate the repeated card through processReview — that moves the SM-2 ease and interval twice in one day.
- NOT: store tests on /minniskort cover the fix — /nam's ReviewPhase derives its items from props and needs its own re-queue.
- NOT: calibration feedback at the moment of judgement is Kaliisa et al.'s result (R6-08) — it is R6's design implication; the review itself found no achievement gain from dashboards.
- NOT: a low 'x af y' is the positive informational feedback Deci et al. 1999 supports — pair it with an action.

**Source findings:** PED4-14, PED2-15

### PED-21. On chapter introductions efni emits the chapter outline (and in chapter 1 a PDF test module) as page-data 'objectives', which the live Námsmarkmið panel already shows as objectives and which a ported pre-question would pose as is.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** needs efni first · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ Medium, as both verifiers gave it. It is live today, but it mislabels one panel on 20 intro pages; it becomes a posed question only if P1.2 is ported with chapter introductions in its scope.

**Problem.** On 20 of the 21 chapter introductions (3-0 has none), efni emits the chapter's outline, i.e. its section titles (1-0-introduction: 'Efnafræði í samhengi', 'Hamur og flokkun efnis', …), as page-data `objectives`, and chapter 1's list ends with 'Eining til að prófa virkni ýmissa atriða til að sjá hvernig þau myndast í PDF-skjali', the title of a PDF test module that is not a published section. This is live: the tickable Námsmarkmið panel ('Eftir að hafa lesið þennan kafla ættirðu að geta:', with an 'x/y metin' count and tick buttons; v1.1 `+page.svelte:373-412`, main `:363-402`) is gated only on a non-empty list, and the live chapter-1 introduction (HTTP 200) shows 7 'Merkja markmið sem kláruð' buttons, item 7 being the test-module title; the same lists are on efni main (98a5fe2) and in the deployed content. A ported P1.2 accepts any non-empty list (unread and objectives present), so its hash pick would pose a bare title after 'Hugleiddu í smástund hvað þú veist nú þegar um eftirfarandi — það bætir námið þótt þú vitir lítið enn:', for example 'Stærðfræðileg meðferð á niðurstöðum mælinga' on 1-0 and 'Efnaformúlur' on 2-0. The root is efni's renderer: `cnxml-render.js:962` sets `objectives` from the abstract for every module although `isIntro` is computed a few lines later (`:966-968`), and `:1061-1074` renders the same list as a 'Námsmarkmið' block in the intro HTML. Separately, two module objectives in 9.5 are imperatives ('Tilgreindu…', 'Notaðu…'), unlike the other 306 of 308, which are supine phrases. Pre-existing on main and live; the fix reaches readers only through a chemistry sync, which is held.

**Evidence.**

- Intro-objectives scan of three trees (② preview, efni main 98a5fe2, the deployed content): 20 of 21 chapter introductions carry page-data objectives; ch01's list is six section titles plus the test-module line ('prófa virkni': 2 hits in the ch01 intro)
- Live check: GET /efnafraedi-2e/kafli/01/1-0-introduction/ answers 200 with 'Námsmarkmið', 'ættirðu að geta' and 7 'Merkja markmið sem kláruð' buttons, item 7 the test-module title
- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:373-412 (panel, stem at :388); main: same file :363-402 (gate: a non-empty list only, :363; stem :378); v1.2:src/lib/components/PreQuestionPrompt.svelte:21-29 (hash pick), :46-50
- efni main 98a5fe2 tools/cnxml-render.js:962 (objectives from the abstract), :966-968 (isIntro), :1061-1074 (rendered 'Námsmarkmið' block), read-only via git show; objectives scan: 306 of 308 module objectives are supine, the exceptions in 9-5-hreyfiorkukenningin.html

**Fix.** The fix is efni's: emit an introduction's chapter outline under its own page-data key (e.g. `outline`) instead of `objectives`, and render it as an outline rather than a 'Námsmarkmið' block in the intro HTML (both `cnxml-render.js:962` and `:1061-1074`); drop non-content modules such as the PDF test module from the chapter-1 outline; normalise the two 9.5 objectives to the supine form; and add an efni-side test that no chapter-introduction page carries page-data objectives. Ask efni to land it before or with the ② re-render that the sync hold waits on. vefur adds no workaround: do not hide the panel on intro pages to compensate; once the content changes, the panel and a ported P1.2 find no objectives on intros and need no code. A warning in `validate-content.js` would show only in local builds, because CI runs `build:no-validate`. Whether P1.2 runs on chapter introductions at all is part of open call 4 (PED-06), not a filter this issue settles: if [USER] chooses 'every unread section', only efni's fix keeps titles out of the card; if [USER] chooses QA batch G's 'first section per chapter', the first section of every chapter is its introduction, so the rule has to say whether it means the intro or the first module page; a 'numbered sections only' rule would leave intros out by design.

**Acceptance test.** In efni: no chapter-introduction page under `05-publication/mt-preview` carries page-data `objectives`, and `grep -c 'prófa virkni'` over its chapters returns 0 (today 20 intros carry them and the ch01 intro has 2 hits). In vefur after the next permitted sync: no `toc.json` entry of type 'introduction' has `metadata.objectives`, and `/efnafraedi-2e/kafli/01/1-0-introduction/` shows no Námsmarkmið panel while §1.1 still does.

**efni side.** Ask efni to (1) emit chapter-introduction outlines under a separate page-data key and stop rendering them as 'Námsmarkmið' (tools/cnxml-render.js:962, :966-968, :1061-1074 on efni main 98a5fe2); (2) remove the PDF test module from the chapter-1 outline; (3) rewrite the two 9.5 objectives ('Tilgreindu…', 'Notaðu…') in the supine form; (4) add a test that no intro page carries page-data objectives. Land it with the ② re-render: it reaches readers only through the held chemistry sync.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: a v1.1 matter or only a P1.2 risk — the mislabelled panel is live on namsbokasafn.is and on main today.
- NOT: the intro panel is known only from code — the live chapter-1 introduction was fetched (HTTP 200) and shows the 7 tick buttons.
- NOT: the v1.1 panel spans :373-416 — it spans :373-412.
- NOT: the intro items are exact copies of the TOC titles — outlines and TOC titles come from different render passes and match only partly (ch01: 4 of 7), though they are still outline titles.
- NOT: the PDF test module is a published TOC section.
- NOT: the lists are shown to feed P1.1's per-section confidence — that rests on the codemap's chain and was not traced independently.
