### PED-22. Check-your-learning answers can be revealed and self-graded 'Rétt hjá mér' without any attempt.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** new scope · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** main, v1.1 · **Also in:** UI, Code

_Severity note:_ Medium, as both verifiers gave it (PED3-04 was lowered from high): new scope on a pre-existing feature, not a release blocker.

**Problem.** In worked examples `practiceReveal` hides the 'Kannaðu þekkingu þína' answer behind 'Sýna svar' and offers 'Rétt hjá mér' / 'Þarf að æfa meira' only after the reveal (`practiceReveal.ts:145-149`, `:158-179`); /nam's practice items reveal and then ask 'Rétt' / 'Rangt' (`ReviewPhase.svelte:45-48`, `:186-216`), and /prof asks 'Hvernig gekk?' (`AdaptiveQuiz.svelte:287`). Nothing records whether an attempt came before the reveal (`quiz.ts:379-400` keeps only aggregate `attempts` and `successfulAttempts`), so 'Rétt hjá mér' after a peek looks like a solved problem, and once v1.2's P1.1 is merged with main that self-grade feeds 'Árangur í dæmum'. This is pre-existing on main: v1.1 leaves `practiceReveal.ts` and `quiz.ts` untouched, so the roadmap's note to give the inline rating the predict-first pattern (roadmap :111-112, the same on main) was not carried out, and an attempt-first loop would replace that note. Of the 292 Check-your-learning answers in the ② preview (MathML text kept), 260 contain a digit, 214 are 60 characters or fewer and 75 carry math, some of them formula-only (12 to 18 depending on the method); about 86 (29%) are a single number with an optional unit or an all-numeric multi-part answer and could be checked automatically, with two hazards: '.' as a thousands separator ('(d) 28.684' in Dæmi 1.3, a significant-figures item) and the atomic mass unit written both 'amu' (96) and 'ame' (16). The research supports a committed, recorded attempt rather than more of today's feedback: showing the key after an attempt is already knowledge of correct response (0.32) and an automatic right/wrong alone is 0.05, against 0.49 for elaborated feedback (Van der Kleij et al. 2015, abstract), and the closest chemistry evidence, web homework with immediate feedback for 200 first-semester students, found no exam difference (Cole & Todd 2003).

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/actions/practiceReveal.ts:145-149 (toggle), :158-179 (self-assessment shown only after the reveal); main: same buttons; git diff main...v1.1 for practiceReveal.ts and quiz.ts is empty
- v1.1:src/lib/components/study/ReviewPhase.svelte:45-48, :186-216; main:src/lib/components/AdaptiveQuiz.svelte:287 ('Hvernig gekk?'); v1.1:src/lib/stores/quiz.ts:379-400 (aggregate attempts only)
- Check-your-learning census (② preview, MathML text kept): 292 answers, 260 with a digit, 214 at 60 characters or fewer, 75 with math; about 86 numerically checkable; 'amu' 96 and 'ame' 16 after a digit
- R3-18 (Van der Kleij et al. 2015: elaborated 0.49, correct response 0.32, right/wrong 0.05; abstract); R3-19 (Roschelle et al. 2016: g = 0.18, 0.29 for low achievers; a multi-component programme); Cole & Todd 2003 (full text: N = 200, no significant difference); v1.1:src/lib/utils/textAnchor.ts:33 (INJECTED_SELECTOR is '.term-en' only)

**Fix.** Phase 1, vefur only (M), in `practiceReveal` and in /nam's practice items: before 'Sýna svar', add 'Svarið þitt' with a one-line input (placeholder 'Skrifaðu svarið þitt') and two link-style options, 'Ég leysti þetta á blaði' (counts as a committed attempt; most answers with math are worked on paper) and 'Veit ekki'; any of the three enables 'Sýna svar', and nothing blocks reading. After the reveal show 'Þitt svar: {x}' beside the key and ask 'Berðu saman: Rétt / Að hluta / Rangt'. Record new aggregate counters (committed attempts, committed successes) with a mapping for 'Að hluta', keep 'Veit ekki' reveals out of success rates, and when P1.1 is ported compute 'Árangur í dæmum' from committed attempts only; the quiz store is already covered by main's rename migration. The echoed answer and the new labels are nodes injected into the content: add them to `textAnchor`'s `INJECTED_SELECTOR` (today `.term-en` only), which both `contentText()` and `createRangeAtPosition()` use, and to `bionicReading`'s `SKIP_SELECTORS`, or highlight offsets desync (CLAUDE.md's injected-node rule). Phase 1b (M, optional), automatic checking of single-number keys: parse the key in Icelandic notation; accept a decimal comma or point from the student; apply the '.'-plus-three-digits thousands rule only when the key's '.' is a thousands separator and the student's input has no comma; use a ±1% relative tolerance with a separate note, 'Rétt gildi — en gættu að fjölda markverðra stafa'; on a wrong answer offer one retry and 'Berðu saman við lausnina í Dæmi {n} hér fyrir ofan', which scrolls to that example's 'Lausn'; store `checked: true`. Phase 2, efni: machine-readable answer attributes (see the efni note). Measure in class: the committed-attempt rate and first-try accuracy on the matching end-of-chapter exercise. Native-speaker check of all strings.

**Acceptance test.** E2E on a §3.1 worked example: 'Sýna svar' is disabled until the student types an answer, taps 'Ég leysti þetta á blaði' or taps 'Veit ekki'; after the reveal the typed text appears beside the key; the quiz-store entry counts a committed attempt with its self-grade, while a 'Veit ekki' reveal adds no committed attempt and does not count towards the success rate; a highlight made before the echo appeared restores to the same text afterwards. If Phase 1b ships, unit tests: `parseKey('28.684') === 28684`; `parseStudent('151,16')` and `parseStudent('151.16')` both equal 151.16; '151,16' is correct and '15,1' wrong against the key '151,16 amu'; '28,684' is wrong against the key '28.684'. All fail today.

**Decision for [USER].** Sign off that an attempt-first loop replaces the roadmap's predict-first note for inline practice (roadmap :111-112, the same on main) and amend the roadmap; decide whether automatic checking (Phase 1b) and efni's machine-readable answers (Phase 2) are wanted.

**efni side.** Phase 2 only; Phase 1 needs nothing from efni. Ask efni whether it can emit data-answer-value, data-answer-unit and data-answer-sigfigs on numeric aside.check-knowledge-answer and answer-entry elements, derived from the English OpenStax source where possible; whether the pipeline can do that has not been verified.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the self-grade already feeds P1.1's 'Árangur í dæmum' — only once v1.2 is merged with main; v1.2 alone has no inline writer.
- NOT: 239 answers contain a digit and 233 are 60 characters or fewer — those counts deleted math first; with the MathML text kept, 260 and 214.
- NOT: 90 answers (31%) can be checked from the text — about 86 (29%): 3 'single-number' keys are IUPAC names and 1 'multi-part' key is orbital labels.
- NOT: exactly 18 answers have no text outside math — 12 strictly, 15–16 ignoring part labels and punctuation; 18 came from deleting math before measuring.
- NOT: Van der Kleij et al. 2015 supports the attempt field — showing the book's answer is knowledge of correct response (0.32), as today; the study supports adding explanations.
- NOT: Richland et al. 2009 shows that the attempt is the active ingredient in practice items — it is a pretest-before-reading study.
- NOT: Roschelle et al. 2016 isolates attempt-and-feedback — it tested a programme of feedback, hints, mastery skill builders and teacher reports and training.
- NOT: the self-grade lacks a standard (Lipko et al. 2009) — the key is shown when the student grades; the case is for recording the attempt.
- NOT: Cole & Todd 2003 is known only from a search summary — it was verified in full text (N = 200 first-semester general chemistry, no significant difference).
- NOT: apply the '.'-plus-three-digits rule to either side — '28,684' typed against the key 28.684 would then pass.

**Source findings:** PED2-14, PED3-04

### PED-23. One-tap verbatim cloze of machine-translated sentences is the least supported card type; term-to-definition and procedure cards with a student-written answer are better supported.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P1.3 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.2 · **Also in:** UI, Code

_Severity note:_ Medium, as both verifiers gave it (PED2-09 was lowered from high): the one-tap button is optional and works, and the evidence shows little benefit for conceptual learning, not harm.

**Problem.** v1.2's P1.3 'Eyðukort' blanks the selected words verbatim inside the sentence around them (`cloze.ts:13` `SENTENCE_END /[.!?]/`, `:25-33`, `:47`), taken from the raw `textContent` of the nearest block (`TextHighlighter.svelte:429-455`), so the card drills unreviewed machine-translated phrasing. The sentence finder cuts at the '(e. …)' gloss and at cross-references: of 1,226 digit.digit sequences in 5,842 numbered-section paragraphs (math removed), 1,170 are references such as 'Mynd 1.2' or 'Tafla 3.1' and about 52 are thousands separators, while decimals use a comma; abbreviation cuts are real but rare (þ.e. 34, t.d. 14, o.s.frv. 8, u.þ.b. 4), and a sentence-initial glossed term returns null and falls back to the card modal (`:451-455`). Card faces are plain text (v1.1 `minniskort/+page.svelte:201`, `ReviewPhase.svelte:112`), so a selected formula cannot be carried, and the key-equation tables hold equations without names (18 pages, 107 one-cell rows), so an equations deck would need efni-authored labels. The evidence ranks verbatim cloze low: fill-in-the-blank helped only the repeated items (Hinze & Wiley 2011), term quizzing did not consistently help conceptual questions (Pan et al. 2019), higher-order and mixed quizzes, not fact quizzes, improved higher-order performance (Agarwal 2019), and self-made cards beat premade ones (Pan et al. 2023, secondary); these are abstracts, mostly of university students. A curated source ships with the book: `glossary.json` has 755 terms, each with an Icelandic definition (691 with English), and at least 467 of the 850 in-text `<dfn>` terms match an entry exactly by English (a lower bound), though the definitions are machine-translated too and some books have no glossary. v1.2 only, to be ported; no part of P1.3 is on v1.1 or main.

**Evidence.**

- v1.2:src/lib/utils/cloze.ts:13 (SENTENCE_END), :25-33 (first occurrence, sentence expansion), :47 (verbatim blank), :49 (null under 10 characters of context); v1.2:src/lib/components/TextHighlighter.svelte:429-455 (raw textContent; null falls back to the modal)
- digit.digit scan of numbered-section paragraphs (② preview, math removed): 1,226 sequences in 5,842 paragraphs, 1,170 cross-references, about 52 thousands separators, no decimals; MathML numbers: 92 with a comma, 3 with a period (thousands)
- Glossary coverage (② preview): 755 terms, 0 without a definition, 691 with English; 850 dfn.term in numbered sections, 467 exact English matches; only 28 match on exact Icelandic text because of the inline '(e. …)' gloss
- R2-22 (Hinze & Wiley 2011; Pan et al. 2019), R2-23 (Agarwal 2019), R2-24 (Lin et al. 2018; Pan et al. 2023, secondary); v1.1:src/lib/components/FlashcardModal.svelte:42-72 (existing modal: front prefilled, back typed)

**Fix.** Port fixes, whatever the design, before any P1.3 release (with PED2-01): build card text from the Selection's range with `span.term-en` and `math.assistive-mathml` excluded (the precedent for dropping assistive MathML is `htmlToPlainText`, main `html.ts:47-49`; `textAnchor` filters only `.term-en`), and never end a sentence at 'e.', 't.d.', 'þ.e.', 'o.s.frv.', 'u.þ.b.', inside a number or inside a cross-reference such as 'Mynd 1.2'. The card design is [USER]'s call, because it changes plan P1.3 ('Highlight → cloze card in one tap', plan :58). Options: (A) port the one-tap verbatim cloze as specified, with the fixes; (B) one tap only for a `<dfn class="term">` or glossary term, creating a term-to-definition card and its reverse, and a two-field template for other selections ('Spurning' prefilled with 'Hvað er …?' or 'Hvernig reiknar maður …?', 'Svar' written by the student); (C) drop the separate 'Eyðukort' button and extend the existing 'Minniskort' modal (front prefilled, back typed, saving blocked while it is empty): for a term, prefill only the front ('Hvað er styrkur?'), let the student write the back, then show the glossary definition, labelled 'vélþýdd', as the standard to compare against (or default to definition → term), with cloze at most one more template, for glossary terms only. For B or C, resolve terms through `glossaryTerms.ts`'s existing lookup rather than a new exact-English match (lowercase `data-en`; `englishMap` is not a clean key space), and fall back to the template for books without `glossary.json`. Reviewers' recommendation, for [USER] to accept or reject: B or C rather than A on the evidence, C being the smaller change (one button fewer, no parallel one-tap path); a back prefilled from the glossary would make a premade card. Later, with efni labels for the key equations and HTML card faces (PED3-03's renderer), a 'Lykiljöfnur' deck becomes possible.

**Acceptance test.** Port fixes, unconditionally (unit): selecting 'prósentusamsetningu' in its §3.2 paragraph gives a front with no '(e.' fragment, a selection in a sentence containing 'Mynd 1.2' gives a front not cut there, and no front contains assistive-MathML token text. If [USER] chooses B or C: selecting 'styrkur' inside its `<dfn>` in §3.3 produces a card or modal whose front is 'Hvað er styrkur?' and whose definition (the back in B, the compare standard in C) equals the 'styrkur' entry in `glossary.json`, read from the file; a non-term selection opens the template with an empty answer and saving stays disabled until it is filled in; on a book without `glossary.json` the term branch falls back to the template. Report the share of generated cards whose back is at most 2 words, before and after.

**Decision for [USER].** P1.3's card design: port the one-tap verbatim cloze as specified (A), one-tap term cards plus a template for other selections (B), or fold it into the existing 'Minniskort' modal (C). B and C change plan P1.3 (plan :58).

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: sentences are cut at decimals — this content writes decimals with a comma; the digit.digit cuts are cross-references and thousands separators.
- NOT: 1,235 digit.digit sequences in 5,898 paragraphs — 1,226 in 5,842, with math removed.
- NOT: only higher-order quizzes improved higher-order performance — higher-order and mixed quizzes did (Agarwal 2019).
- NOT: term quizzing did not help conceptual questions — it 'did not consistently help' them (Pan et al. 2019).
- NOT: a sentence-initial glossed term loses the card — the builder returns null and the plain card modal opens.
- NOT: 467 of 850 is the glossary's full reach — it is a lower bound by exact English; the reader's four-tier matcher would resolve more (not measured).
- NOT: severity high — the button is optional and works.
- NOT: textAnchor drops assistive MathML — it filters only .term-en; follow htmlToPlainText (main html.ts:47-49).
- NOT: a back prefilled from the glossary keeps a generation step — it makes a premade card; prefill the front and show the definition after the student writes.
- NOT: match terms with a new exact-English lookup — resolve them through glossaryTerms.ts.
- NOT: no owner decision is needed — dropping or reshaping the one-tap 'Eyðukort' changes plan P1.3.

**Source findings:** PED3-10, PED2-09

### PED-24. Every intervention uses the same fixed, unfaded prompt with no student control, the form that lost its effect over a term in the studies.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** after release · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ Medium, as the verifier gave it: a risk that builds over a term of use, not a defect; one-tap skipping already keeps the prompts from being forced.

**Problem.** Taken together the interventions use the 'permanent prompt' form that lost its effect and lowered interest and effort over a term in Nückles et al. 2010 (university students' learning journals; fading kept the benefit): every completed page ends with the same recall box, every flashcard needs a prediction, and a ported P1.2 adds the same card to every unread page with objectives. Ported unchanged, P0.2 and P1.2 give 350 prompt sites per book on the ② preview (217 recall pages including Formáli, and 133 pre-question pages: 113 modules and 20 intros), a median of 16 per chapter (range 10–29) before any flashcards; these are sites, not exposures, since the pre-question re-fires on every visit to an unread section, and PED-04's type gate would remove 103 of the recall sites. Nothing adapts or fades, and neither branch has a setting for recall, the pre-question or predict-first (`settings.ts` has no such key). One-tap skipping ('Sleppa'; both predict buttons reveal the answer) keeps them from being forced, and that must stay: prompts that required a response limited low performers' transfer in Pape & Thomas 2025 (6th graders). The evidence for remedies is mixed: choice supports motivation and effort (Patall et al. 2008) and W3C COGA asks for user control over interruptions, but fading made no difference in Belland et al. 2017, and because the pre-question benefit is specific to the asked item (St. Hilaire et al. 2024: g = 0.54 vs 0.04), limiting its frequency removes the benefit from the sections not asked. On v1.1 (recall, predict-first) and v1.2 (the pre-question, to be ported).

**Evidence.**

- Prompt-site count from the ② preview toc.json: 216 recall + 1 front matter + 133 objective-bearing pages (113 modules + 20 intros) = 350; per chapter median 16, min 10, max 29
- v1.1 and v1.2:src/lib/stores/settings.ts (no recall, pre-question or predict key); v1.2:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:103-107 (pre-question gate evaluated on each navigation); v1.1:src/lib/components/RecallPrompt.svelte:80-84 ('Sleppa')
- R5-05 (Nückles et al. 2010: interest η² = .15 and effort η² = .11, Exp 1; full text); R5-02 (Belland et al. 2017: fading made no difference); R5-07 (Pape & Thomas 2025); R5-19 (Patall et al. 2008); R4-19 (W3C COGA: Limit Interruptions)
- R2-09 (St. Hilaire et al. 2024: specific g = 0.54, general 0.04); R2-08 (Pan & Carpenter 2023: in a MOOC, pretests raised dropout)

**Fix.** Keep all three interventions on for students by default, keep one-tap skipping, and never let a prompt gate reading, navigation or mark-as-read. Then: (1) Give students control: a settings group (e.g. 'Námsstuðningur') with two or three choices per intervention, each explained in one line, and a 'Stilla' link inside each prompt that opens it. Which choices exist for recall and for the pre-question, and which one is preselected, follow open calls 2 and 4 (PED-16, PED-06), so [USER] sets them: for example, if [USER] chooses per-section recall, the recall choices could be the section-end prompt and 'Aðeins þegar ég bið um það'; if [USER] limits the pre-question, the shipped default follows that limit. Predict-first is not an open call, so an on/off choice with 'on' preselected is enough. Word every option with a native speaker: the app uses 'kafli' for both chapter and section, and 'hluti' is ambiguous. (2) Show at most one card at the start of a page (the pre-question or the continue-reading prompt, never both) and one at the end; this lands with the P1.2 port. (3) Treat fading as a hypothesis to trial, not a fix: if [USER] wants a trial, one candidate rule is to offer one end-of-chapter recall instead once a student has saved three recalls in that chapter; judge it, and the choices students make, from PED-17's counters.

**Acceptance test.** Unit: the settings validator accepts each new key's allowed values, rejects anything else, and fills a profile that lacks the keys with the values in `defaultSettings` (the test reads them from `defaultSettings`, never from literals). E2E: with the recall control on its 'Aðeins þegar ég bið um það' value, finishing §1.3 shows no prompt but a 'Rifja upp' button that opens it; with predict-first off, one tap flips a card; each prompt carries a 'Stilla' link that opens the settings group; on a page with both a saved reading position and a pre-question, at most one start-of-page card is in the DOM. All fail today (no such keys exist).

**Decision for [USER].** Which frequency choices the recall and pre-question controls offer, and which is preselected, follow open calls 2 and 4; also whether students may switch predict-first off, and whether to trial a fading rule, and which.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: preselect 'Fyrsti nýi hluti dagsins (mælt með)' for the pre-question — that decides open call 4, and with an item-specific effect (g = 0.54 vs 0.04) the sections not asked would lose the benefit.
- NOT: preselect 'Í lok hvers hluta (mælt með)' for recall — that arguably decides open call 2 ('hluti' is ambiguous between section and sub-section).
- NOT: a test that the validator 'defaults to the recommended values' — expectations derive from defaultSettings.
- NOT: fading is known to keep the benefit — Nückles et al. 2010 is one study of university journal writers, and Belland et al. 2017 found fading made no difference.
- NOT: 350 is the number of prompts a student sees — it counts sites; the pre-question re-fires on every visit to an unread section.

**Source findings:** PED4-05

### PED-25. Students meet three effortful interventions with almost no explanation of why, and some rationale lines claim more than the evidence.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** after release · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1, v1.2, main · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ The verifier's medium (confirmed). Nothing is broken, but students who find a strategy effortful rate it worse and choose it less (R5-11), so unexplained interventions are likely to be skipped or switched off; two P1 lines also make claims the research does not support.

**Problem.** On v1.1 the three effortful interventions carry almost no 'why': the recall box has two lines ('Upprifjun úr minni styrkir námið meira en endurlestur.' at RecallPrompt.svelte:65-66 and the saved note at :57), the predict-first card has none ('Reyndu fyrst að rifja upp svarið — manstu það?', minniskort/+page.svelte:210), and the only word on paging is that scrolling suits screen readers (SettingsModal.svelte:323). No branch has an onboarding or 'seen it' flag, and main's /for-teachers (:39-44) says nothing about the learning design, so the gap is pre-existing on main. Learners rate effortful strategies as less effective even after seeing their own test results (Kornell & Bjork 2008, adult lab participants) and are less likely to choose them (Kirk-Johnson 2019, adults); a one-line rationale gives knowledge only, the weakest form of uptake support (R5-13). Two P1 lines on v1.2 also claim more than the evidence: P1.2's 'það bætir námið þótt þú vitir lítið enn' (PreQuestionPrompt.svelte:46-47; no source shows that thinking about an objective improves learning) and P1.1's 'það er algengt við skjálestur' (CalibrationTab.svelte:161; over-confidence is common in any medium and the screen gap is small). The font and bionic labels are PED-27.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/RecallPrompt.svelte:57, :65-66; v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/minniskort/+page.svelte:210; v1.1:src/lib/components/SettingsModal.svelte:323
- git grep for onboarding or 'seen it' flags (onboarding|firstVisit|hasSeen|seenIntro|Ekki sýna aftur) over src on main, v1.1 and v1.2: 0 hits each (control 'Sleppa': 2/3/3 files); main:src/routes/for-teachers/+page.svelte:39-44 has no learning-design section
- v1.2:src/lib/components/PreQuestionPrompt.svelte:46-47 (string split across the two lines); v1.2:src/lib/components/analytics/CalibrationTab.svelte:161; R2 planCitationAudit rows on the P1.2 prompt (covert pre-question, no source) and on CalibrationTab:159-163 (drop the screen attribution)
- R5-10 (Kornell & Bjork 2008), R5-11 (Kirk-Johnson 2019), R5-12 (Dirkx 2019: secondary students use suboptimal strategies), R5-13 (Biwer 2020; KBCP: knowledge alone is not enough)

**Fix.** Add a dismissible explainer of 2–3 sentences the first time each intervention appears, one flag per feature (e.g. seenIntro.recall / .predict / .paged / .prequestion in localStorage, wrapped in try/catch; a per-viewer convenience), with an 'Af hverju?' link to one short static 'Námsráð' page written for 16-year-olds (retrieval, spacing, predicting, why effort feels hard). Draft copy, for a native-speaker check. Recall: 'Af hverju þetta? Að rifja upp án þess að líta í textann festir efnið betur en að lesa aftur. Það er eðlilegt að það taki á, og það þýðir ekki að þú lærir minna.' Predict-first: until predictions are shown back (finding PED4-14's prediction echo), explain only why to try recalling before turning the card; add 'Þannig sérðu hvað þú kannt í raun' once they are. Paging: say what paging does and how to switch to 'Samfellt skrun', with no learning-gain claim (R1 rates the evidence hypothesis-level); it shows on the first paged section, whichever launch default [USER] picks (open call 3). Pre-question: write its explainer during the P1.2 port; showing an explainer once does not decide the frequency (open call 4). Knowledge alone is not enough (KBCP, R5-13): belief comes from showing predictions back (PED4-14), and commitment and planning from a short /for-teachers section on the learning design with a suggested class routine. Copy rule for all of it: a rationale line states only what the cited evidence supports. During the P1 port, drop 'það bætir námið þótt þú vitir lítið enn' unless the rebuilt P1.2 asks for an answer attempt, and replace P1.1's screen attribution with what the student can do (R2 audit).

**Acceptance test.** E2E on a fresh profile: the first recall prompt shows the explainer and the second does not; the first predict-first card shows its explainer and the next deck session does not; with readingMode set to 'paged' in the test, the first paged section shows the paging explainer and the second does not; each 'Af hverju?' link opens the Námsráð page; /for-teachers has a learning-design section. None of these exists today. After the P1 port, `git grep -n -E 'bætir námið þótt|algengt við skjálestur' -- src` returns nothing (both hit on v1.2 today).

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: 'Ef þetta er erfitt, þá er það að virka' as explainer copy — difficulty caused by confusing machine-translated text is not 'working'; say that effort is normal and does not mean less learning.
- NOT: 'Þannig sérðu hvað þú kannt í raun' on v1.1 as it stands — it is untrue until predictions are shown back (PED4-14).
- NOT: a string check banning 'Ég hugleiddi' — P1.2's dismiss button is not a rationale claim and belongs to the P1.2 rebuild.
- NOT: a line-based grep for 'þótt þú vitir lítið enn' — the string is split across PreQuestionPrompt.svelte:46-47; search for 'bætir námið þótt'.
- NOT: Kornell & Bjork 2008 as evidence about strategy choice — it measured ratings after a test, with adult lab participants; the choice evidence is Kirk-Johnson 2019 (adults).
- NOT: the literal grep 'Rannsókn|styrkir|minni' as returning only rationale lines — it returns 32 lines, mostly 'minniskort', and needs a manual filter.
- NOT: 'Sumir lesendur kjósa þetta letur' as plainly false — it is literally true for some readers and is the weakest of the four overclaiming examples (see PED-27).

**Source findings:** PED4-13

### PED-26. The plan and the code comments misattribute or overstate the research behind P0.1 and P0.4, and those claims are steering the open calls.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ The verifier's medium. Documentation only, but these claims are the written basis for the paged default and the page budget (open calls 3 and 1), so they should be right before [USER] decides those calls.

**Problem.** The April reader plan (docs/plans/2026-04-22-screen-vs-paper-reader-plan.md, byte-identical on main and v1.1; v1.2 has an older copy) misstates the research basis of P0.1 and P0.4, which is pre-existing on main. Plan:50 credits 'g 0.35–0.48 → 0.03–0.12' to Salmerón 2024, but the numbers are Clinton-Lisell & Litzinger 2026 (doi 10.1007/s10639-025-13843-8), a contrast between subsets of studies that was never tested directly; Salmerón found no significant paging/scrolling difference (a between-study moderator, paging k = 19 vs scrolling k = 5, p = .867, phone studies excluded) and names no 'worst case' (plan:14) or 'needle' (plan:90). Plan:347's 'every documented driver … has been directly designed against' is overstated, since the documented moderators are time pressure and genre while scrolling and device were not significant (Delgado 2018); plan:47 cites a 50–75 cpl band that is convention, not comprehension evidence; plan:165 calls tall blocks 'rare', yet all 8 of §3.1's worked examples are taller than the 427 px page budget at 1280×800; and plan:59 names Atkinson Hyperlegible Next with citations (Rello 2013; Wery 2017) that test neither Atkinson nor polarity, while v1.2 ships the original Atkinson Hyperlegible 1.006. Acceptance criterion 1 (plan:270: no required scrolling within any page at 1024×768) is inferred to fail from 800 px-high measurements but was never measured at 1024×768, and the e2e suite runs at 1280×720 and 375×667. The same overstatement sits in v1.1's settings.ts:73-74 ('the highest-evidence intervention') and in docs/plans/2026-06-25-perplexity-report-analysis.md:9 and :40, and these claims are the written basis for open calls 1 and 3. The stale mechanism comments (paginate.ts:80-82, :93-94) and the QA doc's old mechanics are CODE-25.

**Evidence.**

- main:docs/plans/2026-04-22-screen-vs-paper-reader-plan.md:14, :47, :50, :59, :90, :165, :270, :347 (byte-identical on v1.1)
- R1 planCitationAudit[2] (plan:50 'wrong': the numbers are Clinton-Lisell & Litzinger 2026; Salmerón paging k = 19, g = −0.109 vs scrolling k = 5, g = −0.149, p = .867), [3] ('worst case' and 'needle': wrong), [4], [5], [9]; R1 E01 (Delgado 2018), E04 (Salmerón 2024), E14 (Joshi 2025)
- R4 planCitationAudit[0] and [1] (Rello and Wery test neither Atkinson nor polarity; v1.2 ships Atkinson Hyperlegible 1.006, not Next)
- §3.1 worked examples at 1280×800: 8 of 8 taller than the 427 px budget (869–1,636 px); book scan: 252 of 289 examples at least as long as the shortest measured one (an estimate); both on efni's ② preview content
- v1.1:src/lib/stores/settings.ts:73-74; main:docs/plans/2026-06-25-perplexity-report-analysis.md:9, :40

**Fix.** Add a dated 'Evidence corrections (2026-10)' erratum to main's copy of the plan (v1.1's is identical; leave v1.2's older copy alone) and a one-line pointer in each affected row, rather than rewriting the research-basis cells; that keeps the record of why P0 was built (the append-only habit). The erratum says: P0.4's basis is a hypothesis: Clinton-Lisell & Litzinger 2026 (a subset contrast never tested directly, confounded with device and text length, possibly limited to college students reading science in English); Salmerón 2024 found no paging/scrolling difference and excluded phones; Joshi 2025 found none on phones (adults, narrative texts). P0.1's basis is convention and comfort; WCAG 1.4.8 (AAA) asks only for a mechanism giving 80 characters per line or fewer. Drop 'worst case', 'needle' and 'every documented driver', and name time pressure and genre as the documented moderators. Tall blocks are not rare: give the §3.1 measurement and label the book-wide figure (252 of 289 examples) an estimate. P1.4: name the family that ships (Atkinson Hyperlegible 1.006) and cite Azzarello et al. 2026 for fonts and Piepenbrock 2013/2014 and Dobres 2017 for polarity (a legibility advantage for dark-on-light; Legge 1985 found reading rate unaffected). Give the perplexity analysis (:9, :40) the same pointer. In v1.1's settings.ts:73-74, replace 'the highest-evidence intervention' with wording that decides nothing, e.g. 'Paged is the default pending the owner's launch call; the evidence for paging is hypothesis-level (review R1)'; this is one edit, shared with CODE-25 (the line-width comment is PED-33). Do not restate acceptance criterion 1 yet: it must name the oversized-block policy [USER] chooses; once chosen, add a 1024×768 case to the pagination e2e that checks it.

**Acceptance test.** On main, the plan has an 'Evidence corrections (2026-10)' section that attributes 'g 0.35–0.48 → 0.03–0.12' to doi 10.1007/s10639-025-13843-8 and names Atkinson Hyperlegible 1.006, and each quoted row (today :14, :47, :50, :59, :90, :165, :347) carries a pointer to it; the perplexity analysis carries the pointer at :9 and :40; `git grep -n 'highest-evidence' -- src` on v1.1 or the merged tree returns nothing (today: settings.ts:74). None of this holds today. Criterion 1 is tested only after [USER] has chosen the oversized-block policy: then a pagination e2e case at 1024×768 asserts the restated criterion.

**Decision for [USER].** Only acceptance criterion 1 waits on [USER]: it must name the oversized-block policy [USER] chooses, from the QA doc's keep / split / raise-the-budget question (raising the budget is open call 1). The erratum and the comment edit need no decision.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: 'Salmerón found paging = scrolling' — it found no significant difference in a between-study moderator test (paging k = 19, scrolling k = 5, p = .867), with phone studies excluded.
- NOT: 'every worked example overflows' as a book-wide measurement — only §3.1's 8 examples were measured (all overflow, 869–1,636 px against 427 px at 1280×800); 252 of 289 book-wide is an estimate.
- NOT: paginate.ts:10-11 ('scrolls there') as wrong — it is ambiguous, since the window does scroll; the wrong comments are :80-82 and :93-94 (CODE-25).
- NOT: acceptance criterion 1 measured failing at 1024×768 — the failure is inferred from 800 px-high measurements.
- NOT: rewriting the plan's research-basis cells in place — add a dated erratum with pointers in the affected rows.
- NOT: restating criterion 1 with PED1-02's preferred oversized-block policy filled in — it waits on [USER]'s choice.
- NOT: editing v1.2's older copy of the plan — edit main's (identical on v1.1).

### PED-27. The font and reading-aid labels (P1.4's new ones and main's bionic setting) still claim a benefit or preference that the studies do not show.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P1.4 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ The verifier's medium for PED1-15, whose theme half is now UI-22. The label half keeps it: the bionic label, live on main, promises easier reading where every recent test found no benefit or a cost, and Settings is where a struggling reader looks for help.

**Problem.** Settings describes the reading aids with claims the studies do not support. Main's bionic setting, 'Hraðlestur' with 'Feitletrað upphaf hvers orðs til að auðvelda lestur' (main SettingsModal.svelte:365-368; v1.1 :394-397; v1.2 :366-369), is contradicted by every test found since 2024, which show no benefit or a cost (abstract-level studies of adults and L2 readers; none with adolescents). P1.4 (v1.2, 1f40724) replaces OpenDyslexic's 'Letur hannað fyrir lesblinda' (still on main :39 and v1.1 :41) with 'Sumir lesendur kjósa þetta letur', which is true only for some individuals: at group level OpenDyslexic was preferred less than standard fonts (Rello & Baeza-Yates 2013; Wery & Diliberto 2017; Kuster et al. 2018 for Dyslexie), preference does not predict reading speed (Wallace et al. 2022), and a 2026 meta-analysis of dyslexic children found no effect (Azzarello et al., g = −0.04, N = 688, abstract-level). Its new Atkinson label 'Hannað fyrir hámarks læsileika' states design intent; no reading study of either Atkinson family was found across four indexes (R4-09). The bionic and OpenDyslexic labels are pre-existing on main; the theme half of P1.4 is UI-22.

**Evidence.**

- main:src/lib/components/SettingsModal.svelte:39, :365-368; v1.2:src/lib/components/SettingsModal.svelte:39, :43 (commit 1f40724)
- R4 planCitationAudit[4] ('Sumir lesendur kjósa': partly), [5] ('hámarks læsileika': unverifiable), [6] (bionic label: wrong), [3] (cite the meta-analysis alongside Wery & Diliberto)
- R4-01 (Azzarello et al. 2026, g = −0.04 [−0.15, 0.07], N = 688, abstract), R4-02 and R4-03 (fulltext), R4-04, R4-08 (Wallace 2022: preference does not predict speed), R4-09 (no Atkinson reading study found)
- R4-10, R4-11 (bionic: Parker 2026, Spear 2025, Beelders 2025, Zhang 2026; abstract-level, adults and L2 readers)

**Fix.** Relabel each option with what it is and what the evidence says (native-speaker check; R4 audits [4]–[6]). Bionic: rename 'Hraðlestur' to 'Feitletra upphaf orða' with 'Útlitsstilling. Ekki hefur verið sýnt að hún bæti lestur', default still off. OpenDyslexic: 'Hannað fyrir lesblinda. Rannsóknir sýna ekki að það bæti lestur, en veldu það ef það hentar þér' (long for a radio-card description; shorten if needed but keep both the design intent and the missing benefit). Atkinson, when P1.4 is ported: 'Skýr munur á líkum stöfum (I, l, 1 og O, 0)'. In 1f40724's SettingsModal comment, cite Azzarello et al. 2026 and Kuster et al. 2018 alongside Wery & Diliberto 2017. Order: the bionic label sits 25 lines from 1f40724's nearest hunk, so it can be relabelled on main at any time; make the OpenDyslexic and Atkinson changes in a fix commit right after picking 1f40724 onto v1.1, so the clean cherry-pick order is not disturbed. The theme choice is UI-22.

**Acceptance test.** `git grep -n -E 'auðvelda lestur|hámarks læsileika|kjósa þetta letur|Hraðlestur' -- src` returns nothing on the ported tree (today main and v1.1 hit 'Hraðlestur' and 'auðvelda lestur', and v1.2 hits all four); a copy review finds 'bæti lestur' only in negated form; defaultSettings.bionicReading is still false.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: '15 studies' for Azzarello et al. 2026 — R4-01 gives g, the CI and N = 688 but no study count; it is abstract-level and covers dyslexic children, not general 16–20-year-old readers.
- NOT: 'no reading study of either Atkinson family exists' — none was found across four indexes (an absence claim).
- NOT: the bionic studies as evidence about adolescents — they are abstract-level and cover adults and L2 readers.
- NOT: 'dark text on light reads better' — positive polarity has a legibility advantage (proofreading and acuity, larger at small sizes and in the dark), and reading rate is unaffected for normal vision (Legge 1985); this belongs with UI-22's theme work.

**Source findings:** PED1-15

### PED-28. Each recall is asked once, straight after reading, and never returns; there is no delayed re-recall.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P0.2 · **Stage:** new scope · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ The verifier's medium. The immediate recall still gives a retrieval benefit; what is missing is the delayed attempt that the spacing and monitoring evidence favours (R2-21, R2-17).

**Problem.** On v1.1 the recall prompt fires at most once per section per browser profile, and only when the first completion happens on the section page (header or bottom 'Merkja…' button, the scroll-end detector, or 'Næsta' past the last page; [sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:171-180). A section marked 'Lesið' in the /nam guided session (ReadingPhase.svelte:24) never gets a prompt, a prompt left unanswered is closed by the next navigation (:111) and never returns, and saved entries are never read back (recall.ts:95-104's getForSection is used only in tests). The benefit of retrieval shows after a delay (Roediger & Karpicke 2006: restudy better at 5 minutes, testing better at 2 days and 1 week), spaced retrieval beats massed (Latimier et al. 2021: g = 0.74 after trim-and-fill, 1.02 uncorrected), and delayed, not immediate, summaries improve monitoring (Yang et al. 2023: +0.210 vs +0.030, n.s.). So an immediate end-of-section recall can help retention but should not be expected to improve students' judgments of what they know. The recall prompt is new in v1.1; main has none.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:111, :171-180; v1.1:src/lib/components/study/ReadingPhase.svelte:20-32
- v1.1:src/lib/stores/recall.ts:95-104 (getForSection, used only in recall.test.ts)
- R2-02 (Roediger & Karpicke 2006, abstract), R2-17 (Yang et al. 2023, fulltext), R2-21 (Latimier et al. 2021); R2 designImplications, 'P0.2, open call' row, arm (b): a delayed section-level recall
- R5-24 (reminders and nudges: keep surfacing in the app); main:docs/plans/2026-04-22-screen-vs-paper-reader-plan.md:66-67 (P2.2 spaced-review surfacing, P2.3 recall-review tab)

**Fix.** This is new prompt scope, and R2 frames a delayed section-level recall as arm (b) of open call 2 (per section vs per sub-section), so it is [USER]'s to approve; adding it adds a trigger and does not answer that call. If [USER] approves it: on the book home, at most once a day, offer one delayed re-recall of the most recently completed section: 'Upprifjun frá síðast: hvað manstu enn úr kafla {1.2}?' with 'Sleppa' and 'Vista' (native-speaker check). Do not put it on arrival at a section, which already carries the continue-reading prompt, the arrival scroll past the objectives and, once P1.2 is ported, the pre-question whose frequency is open call 4. Show the earlier entry ('Það sem þú skrifaðir síðast') only after the new attempt is saved, so the prompt does not turn into rereading; when there is no earlier entry (the student pressed 'Sleppa' or completed the section in /nam), leave that block out. If the compare step (PED2-04) has landed, show it after saving too; this does not depend on it. Read entries through $recallStore inside the derived, not by wrapping getForSection in $derived (it reads via get({subscribe}) and would compute once). It needs the recall store's renameSections() and its line in main's migrateRenamedSections() (one of the two semantic breaks the main merge must fix), or the earlier entry is lost after a section rename. In the app only, no notifications (R5-24). Build the prompt so finding PED4-02's student-initiated 'Rifja upp aftur' button can reuse it; together they are a small first form of the planned P2.2/P2.3.

**Acceptance test.** If [USER] approves it, e2e with a recall entry for §1.2 dated yesterday: opening the book home shows the delayed prompt once; after 'Vista', 'Það sem þú skrifaðir síðast' shows the earlier entry; a second visit the same day shows nothing; with no earlier entry the prompt shows without that block; after §1.2 is renamed through migrateRenamedSections() the earlier entry is still found. Today no delayed prompt exists, so the first step fails.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] decides whether to add a delayed re-recall at all (new prompt scope). R2 frames it as arm (b) of open call 2; approving it adds a trigger and leaves the per-section vs per-sub-section call open.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: 'fires once per section per device, on first completion' — it fires at most once per browser profile, and only when the first completion happens on the section page; completing a section in /nam never prompts, and an unanswered prompt closed by navigation never returns.
- NOT: Latimier's g = 0.74 as the uncorrected estimate — it is the trim-and-fill figure; 1.02 uncorrected.
- NOT: offering the re-recall on arrival at the next section — that arrival already carries other prompts, one of them tied to open call 4; use the book home.
- NOT: wrapping getForSection in $derived — it reads via get({subscribe}) and would compute once.
- NOT: a settled fix that needs the compare step — it is new scope for [USER] to approve, and it stands without PED2-04.

**Source findings:** PED2-16

### PED-29. /prof tells students that the next question adapts after each answer, but the set is chosen once when the page mounts.

**Severity:** low · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** after release · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** main, v1.1 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ The verifier lowered it from medium: misleading copy on a secondary page, live but with no functional or learning harm.

**Problem.** On main (pre-existing; also on v1.1, where prof/+page.svelte shifts by one line) the /prof help list says 'Eftir að þú svarar er næsta spurning aðlöguð' (main prof/+page.svelte:59), which is false: up to five problems are chosen once in onMount (AdaptiveQuiz.svelte:44-51), and handleAnswer (:65-90) only records the attempt and advances. The subtitle 'Aðlagað að þínum framförum' (:42) is not false, because getAdaptiveProblems (quiz.ts:522-568) orders by mastery at mount (novice first, then oldest attempt) and handleRestart re-ranks between runs. The adaptive claim recurs in the title and og:title (:20-21), the h1 (:37), the help heading 'Hvernig virkar aðlögunarpróf?' (:54), the component header (AdaptiveQuiz.svelte:225), the empty state (:142) and the catalogue card 'Aðlöguð verkefni til að prófa þekkingu' (src/routes/+page.svelte:361), and the meta description 'til að meta skilning' (:22) contradicts the help line 'ekki að meta þig'. Adaptive item selection itself showed no average benefit over static practice in Dutch secondary schools (R3-20), so the label also oversells. The footer reads 'Nuverandi einkunn' (AdaptiveQuiz.svelte:312), missing the accent.

**Evidence.**

- main:src/routes/[bookSlug]/prof/+page.svelte:20-22, :37, :42, :54-60
- main:src/lib/components/AdaptiveQuiz.svelte:44-51 (problems chosen once in onMount), :65-90 (handleAnswer only records and advances), :142, :225, :312
- main:src/lib/stores/quiz.ts:522-568 (mastery order at mount); main:src/routes/+page.svelte:360-361 (catalogue card)
- `git grep -n -i -E 'aðlög|nuverandi' -- src` on main: 9 hits; 'Aðlagað' at prof/+page.svelte:42
- R3-20 (van Klaveren et al. 2017, abstract: no average effect of adaptive vs static practice)

**Fix.** Make the copy say what the page does (native-speaker check). Retitle the page and component, e.g. 'Æfingadæmi', and use the same label in the nav (Sidebar.svelte:454, MobileBottomNav.svelte:92) and the catalogue h3 (src/routes/+page.svelte:360), or pick one label that fits both, so the h1 and the nav match. Subtitle: 'Dæmi sem þú hefur opnað í köflunum — þau sem þú hefur ekki enn náð tökum á koma fyrst.' Help list: 'Hér birtast dæmi sem þú hefur opnað í köflunum.', 'Dæmi sem þú hefur ekki enn reynt, eða hefur átt erfitt með, koma fyrst.', 'Markmiðið er að hjálpa þér að læra, ekki að meta þig.' Rewrite the help heading (:54), the empty state (AdaptiveQuiz.svelte:142), the component header (:225), the title, og:title and descriptions (:20-23) and the catalogue card (:361) to match, and fix 'Núverandi einkunn'. Revisit the copy if the selection logic changes (finding PED3-05).

**Acceptance test.** `git grep -n -i -E 'aðlög|nuverandi' -- src` returns nothing (9 hits on main today); the /prof subtitle no longer contains 'Aðlagað'; Playwright: the /prof h1 and the nav entry that links to /prof show the same label.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the subtitle 'Aðlagað að þínum framförum' as false — the set is ordered by mastery at mount and re-ranked between runs; the flatly false line is the help item at :59.
- NOT: medium severity — wording only; live but harmless.
- NOT: the case-sensitive acceptance grep for 'aðlöguð|Aðlögunarpróf|Nuverandi' — it matches only 6 lines and would pass while :54, AdaptiveQuiz.svelte:142, the catalogue card and the subtitle remain.
- NOT: retitling the h1 alone — the nav and the catalogue still say 'Próf', so rename them too or pick one matching label.

**Source findings:** PED3-13

### PED-30. Streaks are on by default and weighted into the 0–100 'Samræmi' score, an engagement mechanic that guidance for minors advises switching off by default.

**Severity:** low · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** after release · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ The verifier's low (confirmed): a motivational risk resting on weak, indirect evidence; no study tests streaks themselves.

**Problem.** On main, v1.1 and v1.2 (pre-existing; neither branch adds it) the flashcard deck list shows '{n} daga námsruna!' (v1.1 minniskort/+page.svelte:103; main :100), /greining shows 'Núverandi röð' and 'Lengsta röð' tiles, and its 0–100 'Samræmi' score gives up to 40 points to a 7-day streak (ReadingPatternsTab.svelte:75-83), all with no setting; the 'Halda röð daga' goal (GoalsTab.svelte:36-40) is opt-in, but the tiles and the deck line show without it. Streaks are one of the engagement features that the European Commission's 2025 guidance for minors recommends disabling by default (R5-25; a benchmark here, not an obligation), and a semester-long study found that compulsory gamified mechanics lowered intrinsic motivation (Hanus & Fox 2015, secondary source; badges and leaderboards, not streaks). v1.1 rightly removed 'Vel gert!' from section completion, which leaves streaks as the riskiest motivational remnant. The deck line counts flashcardStore.studyStreak while /greining uses analytics currentStreak, two separate counters.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/minniskort/+page.svelte:96-106 ('daga námsruna!' at :103; main :100)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/analytics/ReadingPatternsTab.svelte:75-83 (streak up to 40 of 100), :121-123 ('Samræmi'), :129-157 (streak tiles); v1.1:src/lib/components/analytics/GoalsTab.svelte:36-40
- v1.1:src/lib/stores/analytics.ts:126 (default goals []); v1.1:src/lib/stores/flashcard.ts:52 (FlashcardDailyStats), :73, :421 (studyStreak, a separate counter)
- R5-25 (European Commission 2025: streaks off by default; a benchmark), R5-17 (Hanus & Fox 2015, secondary), R5-18 (Deci et al. 1999, abstract)

**Fix.** If [USER] agrees, make streaks opt-in rather than remove them. Show the streak tiles, the streak share of Samræmi and the 'námsruna' line only when the student has turned on the 'Halda röð daga' goal; that means reading the analytics goal in both places, since the deck line uses flashcardStore.studyStreak and /greining uses analytics currentStreak. With the goal off, compute Samræmi without the streak term and rescale the remaining terms to 0–100 (the streak and days-active terms already overlap). Replace the deck-list line with informational, self-referenced text computed from FlashcardDailyStats (flashcard.ts:52), e.g. 'Þú hefur æft {n} af síðustu 14 dögum' (native-speaker check; if the streak line stays for opted-in students, also fix today's '1 daga námsruna!' for n = 1). Never show a broken-streak message or any notification. Keep the 'Vel gert' acknowledgements at session ends.

**Acceptance test.** Fresh profile with 3 seeded study days and no goals: /minniskort and /greining show no 'námsruna', 'Núverandi röð' or 'Lengsta röð' text, and the deck list shows the 14-day line (today the streak line and tiles show, so this fails); after adding the 'Halda röð daga' goal, the streak tiles appear. Unit: with the goal off, Samræmi is computed without the streak term and its maximum is still 100.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] decides whether streak displays become opt-in (tied to the 'Halda röð daga' goal). It changes live behaviour on main on indirect evidence; it is not one of the four open calls, and no ruling is touched.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: evidence that streaks themselves harm learning — no study tests streaks; the support is a policy benchmark (R5-25) and secondary gamification findings about badges and leaderboards (R5-17).
- NOT: gating one streak counter — the deck line (flashcardStore.studyStreak) and /greining (analytics currentStreak) use separate counters, so both displays must be gated, and Samræmi must be rescaled when its streak term is dropped.

**Source findings:** PED4-18

### PED-31. Highlights lead nowhere: the reader should keep highlighting a convenience, not present it as a way to study, and give highlights one route into retrieval.

**Severity:** low · **Feature:** P1.3 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ The verifier's low. No UI string oversells highlighting today; this is a small missed opportunity plus two adjacent polish fixes.

**Problem.** On main, v1.1 and v1.2 (pre-existing) highlights are stored and exported but never reused: annotationStore's only consumers are TextHighlighter and AnnotationSidebar (plus main's rename migration), and P1.3 on v1.2 builds a cloze card from a fresh selection, not from an existing highlight. No UI string presents highlighting as a way to study (the 'yfirstrik' grep returns AnnotationSidebar.svelte:238, :314, SelectionPopup.svelte:86 and annotation.ts:260's export header '# Athugasemdir og yfirstrikun'). That is right: learner-generated highlighting helped college students but not school students (Ponce et al. 2022: 0.39 vs 0.24, abstract-level) and Dunlosky et al. 2013 rate it low utility, while instructor-provided highlighting helped school students (0.48). Two adjacent defects: the aria-label at SelectionPopup.svelte:72 is misspelt 'Valmoguleikar yfirstrokunar', and restored highlight marks get cursor-pointer and a data-annotation-id that nothing reads (main and v1.1 TextHighlighter.svelte:170, :188; v1.2 :180, :198).

**Evidence.**

- R2-25 (Ponce et al. 2022, abstract; Dunlosky et al. 2013); R2 planCitationAudit row on P1.3 ('partly': no experiment of highlight-to-retrieval conversion found)
- codemap_v1.2 features[P1.3].gapsVsSpec: the cloze 'neither creates a highlight nor offers cloze from an existing highlight'
- main:src/lib/components/AnnotationSidebar.svelte:243-297 (per-highlight actions); main:src/lib/components/FlashcardModal.svelte:12-18, :64 (front = selectedText)
- main:src/lib/components/SelectionPopup.svelte:72 (aria-label); main:src/lib/components/TextHighlighter.svelte:170, :188 (cursor-pointer, no handler reads data-annotation-id)

**Fix.** No new investment in highlighting, and no copy that presents it as studying. Where highlights are listed (AnnotationSidebar), add one retrieval route: 'Búa til minniskort' on each highlight, opening the same prefilled FlashcardModal as the selection popup's 'Minniskort' (front = the highlight's selectedText, back written by the student). Keep it a small convenience: R2 calls highlight-to-retrieval conversion plausible but untested. Do it with the P1.3 port, so the card flow is the one P1.3 settles on (finding PED2-09). The adjacent fixes can land on main at any time: correct the aria-label to 'Valmöguleikar yfirstrikunar' and the same word in NoteModal.svelte:141 ('Litur yfirstrokunar'), with a native-speaker check, and either give restored marks a handler (e.g. open that highlight's entry in AnnotationSidebar) or drop cursor-pointer. If summary sentences or glossary terms are later used as reviewed material, remember they are machine-translated and contain term errors (PED2-17).

**Acceptance test.** In the annotation sidebar a highlight offers 'Búa til minniskort', which opens FlashcardModal with that highlight's text as the front, the same modal and prefill as the selection popup's 'Minniskort' (no such action exists today); `git grep -n -E 'Valmoguleikar|yfirstrokunar' -- src` returns nothing (2 hits today); a restored mark either responds to a click or has no cursor-pointer; a copy review finds no UI string recommending highlighting as a study method.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: SelectionPopup.svelte:72 as a 'yfirstrik' grep hit — :72 is the misspelt aria-label, which the pattern does not match; the hit is :86, and annotation.ts:260 also matches.
- NOT: Ponce et al. 2022's figures as independently checked — they are abstract-level, via R2-25 only.
- NOT: highlight-to-card conversion as evidence-backed — it is plausible but untested (R2-25), so keep it a small convenience.
- NOT: summary sentences as a clean study standard — they are machine-translated and contain term errors (PED2-17).

**Source findings:** PED2-18

### PED-32. OpenStax feature boxes make the tallest paged pages; collapsing them in paged mode is worth testing as an experiment, not shipping as a default.

**Severity:** low · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** after release · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ The verifier's low (confirmed): an experiment proposal. The overflow it targets is real, but most overflowing pages are worked examples (PED-02), not feature boxes.

**Problem.** On v1.1's pager the tallest overflowing pages are feature boxes, not core content: a 'sciences-interconnect' note fills a 3,633 px page on the phone (§3.1, 'Hluti 3 af 4 · Síða 17 af 17'), and 'everyday-life' notes fill 2,099 and 1,272 px pages in §1.2. Feature boxes make the tallest page at every viewport measured (§1.2, §3.1 and §1.3 at desktop, tablet and phone), but they are only 12 of the 36 overflowing pages; 20 are aside.example worked examples (PED-02). Of the 115 figures that sit inside boxes, 83 are in feature boxes (48 everyday-life, 26 sciences-interconnect, 9 chemist-portrait). Seductive details slightly reduce learning (Cheng 2026: g = −0.16, through extraneous load), but whether OpenStax feature boxes count as seductive details (irrelevant by definition) is an untested assumption, and they may raise interest for 16–20-year-olds. Paging is new in v1.1; the numbers were measured on efni's ② preview content.

**Evidence.**

- §3.1 phone page: aside.note.note-chemistry.sciences-interconnect, 3,633 px ('Hluti 3 af 4 · Síða 17 af 17'); §1.2 phone: everyday-life pages of 2,099 and 1,272 px
- v1.1 paged measurements (measurements.json paged[]), all 36 overflowing pages over 9 surfaces (3 sections × 3 viewports): 20 aside.example, 12 feature boxes
- figure count by container on the v1.1 content: 115 figures inside boxes, 83 in the three feature-box classes (48 / 26 / 9)
- R3-09 (Cheng 2026, abstract: g = −0.16, mediated by extraneous load; population and interest not stated); R3 designImplications (coherence experiment)
- v1.1:src/lib/utils/paginate.ts:12-27 (ATOMIC_SELECTOR includes .note and .example); v1.1:src/lib/utils/textAnchor.ts:33 (INJECTED_SELECTOR)

**Fix.** Run it as an experiment and change no default before a result. If [USER] runs it: in paged mode only, render .everyday-life, .sciences-interconnect and .chemist-portrait collapsed behind 'Lesa nánar' (native-speaker check), expanded on tap and always expanded in scrolled mode and in print, against today's rendering. Collapse with the hidden attribute or CSS on the existing nodes, never by moving or re-rendering them, and make expanding a box trigger a re-split. Add the injected toggle to textAnchor's INJECTED_SELECTOR (textAnchor.ts:33; its walker reads hidden text) and to bionicReading's SKIP_SELECTORS, or highlight offsets differ between paged and scrolled mode (the .term-en lesson). Style it in src/app.css, not static/styles/content.css (efni's class contract), and keep the boxes expanded under app.css's own @media print block (Ctrl+P on a reader page), not only in print.css. With no backend there is no way to count page turns or completions per arm, so run the A/B as a teacher-assigned toggle with an in-class core-content quiz on §1.2 and §3.1; the spring term (January–May 2027) is the next window. It reaches only readers in paged mode, whatever launch default [USER] picks (open call 3), fixes a minority of overflow, and does not replace the worked-example decision (PED-02) or the page-budget call (open call 1).

**Acceptance test.** With the experiment toggle on in paged mode, each of the three box classes renders collapsed under 120 px and expanding one triggers a re-split; with the toggle off, in scrolled mode and under @media print the boxes render expanded; a highlight made inside a box restores to the same text in paged and scrolled mode with the toggle present. None of this exists today. The class A/B (a core-content quiz per arm) runs before any default changes.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] decides whether to run the experiment, with which class and when (the next pilot window is the spring term, January–May 2027); the default rendering stays as it is until there is a result.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: collapsing as a fix for most overflow — 20 of the 36 overflowing pages are worked examples and 12 are feature boxes.
- NOT: OpenStax feature boxes as established seductive details — that classification is an untested assumption.
- NOT: ui-capture's 3,013 px as a conflicting height for the §3.1 box — it is the pager-below-the-fold distance on the same 3,633 px page (a 3,573 px element).
- NOT: page turns and completion per arm as collectable — there is no backend; use a teacher-assigned toggle and an in-class quiz.
- NOT: S effort — closer to M once the anchoring, bionic and print constraints are included.

**Source findings:** PED3-15

### PED-33. P0.1's 38rem measure is set in root rem, so it ignores the reader's font size; it also reaches only new profiles and changes nothing on phones.

**Severity:** low · **Feature:** P0.1 · **Stage:** after release · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** UI, Code

_Severity note:_ The verifier lowered it from medium: only 'small' (14 px, 81 cpl) leaves the 50–75 band, the band is about comfort rather than learning, and readers can already change the width.

**Problem.** On v1.1, P0.1 sets --line-width-narrow: 38rem (app.css:23) while the reader's font size is set on a wrapper (app.css:305-319; +layout.svelte:83), so the measure does not scale with the text: §1.2 paragraphs measure a median of 81 cpl at 14 px, 72 at 16, 63 at 18 and 56 at 20 (68.2 in the running app at 16 px). The 50–75 band is typographic convention and comfort (Bringhurst, Baymard; the NN/g source the plan cites was not found), not comprehension evidence: long lines read faster, and one adult study favoured 55 over 100 cpl for comprehension, so the settings.ts:69-71 comment's 'the readability literature recommends' overstates it. Because the settings store saves every key on first load, existing profiles keep main's 'medium' (52rem, about 101 cpl) and only new profiles get 'narrow', and no test pins the new default. On phones the screen sets the line (36–37 cpl in a 318 px column) whatever the setting, which is fine and may help struggling readers (Schneps 2013), so type and padding should not be shrunk there to lengthen lines. P0.1 is new in v1.1; main's default is 'medium'.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/app.css:23, :305-319, :356-358, :434-440; v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/+layout.svelte:83; v1.1:src/lib/stores/settings.ts:69-72, :118-122
- cpl harness over 12 §1.2 paragraphs of efni's ② preview (Literata, 1280×800; R1 planCitationAudit[6], re-run by the verifier): 38rem gives a median of 81/72/63/56 cpl at 14/16/18/20 px; 38em gives 69/72/71/72
- ui-capture_v1.1 layout[10]: 68.2 cpl at 560 px on the desktop, 65–71 on the tablet, 36–37 in the 318 px phone column
- R1 planCitationAudit[5] (the 50–75 band is convention; NN/g source not found); R1 E17 (Dyson 2004), E19 (Shaikh & Chaparro 2005), E20 (Schneps et al. 2013)
- health_v1.1 mutations[39]: no test fails if the lineWidth default changes

**Fix.** Express the widths in em so they scale with the reader's size: --line-width-narrow: 38em, which resolves where max-width uses it against .reading-content's own font size (608 px at 16 px, unchanged), and likewise medium and wide; the Settings preview (SettingsModal.svelte:426) then resolves em against its own font size, which is acceptable. Rewrite the settings.ts:69-71 comment as 'convention and comfort (50–75 cpl), not a measured learning gain'. Add a settings.test.ts case pinning lineWidth 'narrow' (P0.1's default, not an open call; the same test UI-31 asks for). Leave stored 'medium' values alone, since a stored value cannot tell a choice from an auto-save, and say in the plan that P0.1 reaches new profiles only. Leave phones as they are. The Settings help line under 'Línubreidd' is UI-31.

**Acceptance test.** A Chromium measurement of §1.2 paragraphs at 1280×800 under 'narrow' gives a median of 60–75 cpl at each of 14, 16, 18 and 20 px (today 81/72/63/56); the narrow column stays 608 px at 16 px; at 390×844 the body font stays at least 16 px at default settings; a settings.test.ts case fails if the lineWidth default changes (none does today).

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: medium severity — only 'small' (14 px, 81 cpl) leaves the 50–75 band; 18 and 20 px give 63 and 56, inside it.
- NOT: a defect — it is an improvement; by the finding's own evidence the band is about comfort, not learning.
- NOT: a one-time migration of stored 'medium' values — the store cannot tell a deliberate choice from an auto-save, so leave them and document that P0.1 reaches new profiles only.

**Source findings:** PED1-13

### PED-34. The deferred P3 AI tutor needs a provider, hosting and data-handling decision that neither plan doc records, and should wait for a working non-AI feedback loop.

**Severity:** low · **Feature:** new-scope · **Stage:** new scope · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** main · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ The verifier lowered it from medium: no P3 code exists and the plan defers P3, so this is a planning risk, not a defect on any branch.

**Problem.** P3.1, a 'Socratic-only AI tutor' of 8–12 sessions, is deferred in main's plan (:75, :345), and neither the plan nor the 2026-06-10 roadmap mentions a backend, API keys, a provider or data handling. The repo did anticipate a self-hosted proxy: `nginx-config-example.conf` has carried a commented 'Optional: AI Tutor API' server block (`tutor.namsbokasafn.is` proxied to `localhost:8000`, :390-428) since 04a0f0a (2025-11-30), and the `connect-src` of all 10 CSP headers in that file already allows that host (e.g. :89); the file is a recommendation, not a mirror of production, and the live state was not checked. So what is missing is a written decision on provider, keys, retention and data from readers aged 16–20 (some of them minors), which would also amend CLAUDE.md's 'no backend, intentional' principle; in-browser models and bring-your-own-key avoid a proxy but are impractical here (multi-GB downloads on phones, weak Icelandic, keys in students' hands). The evidence argues for waiting: in high-school maths, unrestricted GPT-4 practice lowered unassisted exam scores (−0.054, about 17% of the control mean) and a guardrailed tutor removed the harm without a gain (−0.004, n.s.) (R3-22), while gains came with app-enforced steps (R3-23, undergraduates) or a human approving every message (R3-25). R3's search found no measurement of LLM accuracy on Icelandic chemistry explanations, which is a gap in the evidence, not a finding. Nothing for P3 exists on v1.1 or v1.2.

**Evidence.**

- main:docs/plans/2026-04-22-screen-vs-paper-reader-plan.md:75 (P3.1: 'Build only after P0.2/P0.3 ship; constraints in research') and :345 (deferred); main:docs/decisions/ holds only `.gitkeep`
- main:nginx-config-example.conf:390-428 (commented 'Optional: AI Tutor API' block, since 04a0f0a) and :89 (`connect-src` allows `https://tutor.namsbokasafn.is`; 17 lines in the file name that host)
- R3-22 (Bastani 2025, about 1,000 grade 9–11 students: GPT Base exam −0.054, GPT Tutor −0.004 n.s., students in both AI arms overestimated their learning); R3-23 (Kestin 2025, undergraduates); R3-25 (LearnLM/Eedi 2025, tutor-approved drafts)
- R6-13 (Korea's AI digital textbooks made optional after one semester; the reasons given were teacher resistance, screen time, literacy and budget)

**Fix.** Keep P3 deferred until the non-AI feedback loop exists: the attempt-first 'Kannaðu þekkingu þína' loop (finding PED3-04) and interleaved practice from the answered exercises (PED-37). Before any P3 code, [USER] writes a decision record in docs/decisions/ covering provider, keys and hosting, retention, the handling of data from students aged 16–20, the amendment to CLAUDE.md's 'no backend' principle, and what happens to the dormant tutor block and the `connect-src` allowance in `nginx-config-example.conf` (the verifier suggests dropping both until a decision is made). If P3 is ever built: ground every hint in the OpenStax worked solution or answer key; give hints, never full solutions; enforce the steps in the app, not in the prompt; add an unassisted check afterwards and a teacher-review mode; and run a pre-launch accuracy audit in which the teacher rates at least 50 generated hints on Icelandic chemistry problems (a suggested gate: launch only if at least 95% are correct and none reveals a full solution). The decision record is S; P3 itself is L (8–12 sessions in the plan).

**Acceptance test.** docs/decisions/ holds a P3 decision record (today it holds only `.gitkeep`), and `grep -c tutor.namsbokasafn.is nginx-config-example.conf` returns 0 (17 today) unless that record keeps the stub. If P3 is ever built: an accuracy-audit report of at least 50 teacher-rated hints that meets the gate the record sets, and, before release, a classroom comparison with an unassisted post-test.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] decides whether P3 is pursued at all and, before any code, records the provider, keys and hosting, retention and the handling of data from students aged 16–20 (some minors), and whether the dormant tutor proxy block and CSP allowance stay in the example nginx config. This is not one of the four open calls.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the repo is silent on hosting — nginx-config-example.conf has carried a commented tutor proxy block and a `connect-src` allowance for it since 2025-11-30; the missing piece is the privacy and data-handling decision.
- NOT: any LLM call needs a secret API key — in-browser models and bring-your-own-key need none (both impractical here).
- NOT: the readers are minors — they are 16–20, so some but not all are.
- NOT: 'no one has measured LLM accuracy on Icelandic chemistry explanations' as an established fact — it is R3's gap note (its search found nothing).
- NOT: Korea's AI textbooks were downgraded because of factual errors — the reasons given for the law were teacher resistance, screen time, literacy and budget, and they were AI digital textbooks, not LLM tutors.
- NOT: severity medium — lowered to low (no code exists; P3 is deferred).

**Source findings:** PED3-14

### PED-35. Evidence for the launch default (open call 3) can be gathered in class without a backend, through a counterbalanced two-section crossover with a paper quiz and a confidence prediction.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** new-scope · **Stage:** new scope · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1

_Severity note:_ The verifier kept medium. It is new scope (a classroom protocol plus an optional URL override), not a defect; it matters because nothing in the app can produce evidence for open call 3.

**Problem.** On v1.1 the pager persists nothing and no branch logs page turns or mode switches (codemap_v1.1 P0.4); prompt use is half-recorded, since the recall store keeps every saved recall (recall.ts:15-29) and flashcard reviewHistory records predictedKnown, but nothing records a prompt shown or skipped, so no usage rate can be computed. Preference and self-prediction diverge from performance (Singer & Alexander 2017, 90 undergraduates, abstract: students preferred the screen and predicted better comprehension there, yet print did better on key points and details, with no main-idea difference), so neither a preference poll nor the app can settle the paged/scrolled default. A counterbalanced two-section crossover in [USER]'s own classes, with the quiz and a confidence prediction on paper or in the school's own platform, needs no new data flow, whereas an export to the teacher would bring in Persónuvernd's school checklist (R5). Its limit: with one class, three main-idea items per condition and no margin fixed in advance, 'not worse' is almost always met, so R1's proposed rule ('keep paged as the default only if it is not worse on main-idea score or calibration and fewer than half of students switch it off') would nearly always keep the branch's current default; one class can expose large harms and show acceptance, not prove equivalence. Norway's screen committee (weaker readers are most affected by screens) is hedged and rests mostly on higher-education studies (R6-15). The next natural window is the spring term (January–May 2027), and from 1 November most of [USER]'s time goes to the chemistry review.

**Evidence.**

- codemap_v1.1 features[P0.4].dataModel: 'The pager persists NOTHING'; no mode-switch or page-turn logging on any branch
- v1.1:src/lib/stores/recall.ts:15-29 (saved recalls kept with section slug and time; prompts shown or skipped are not recorded)
- R6-16 (Singer & Alexander 2017: 90 undergraduates; preference and predictions favour the screen; print better on key points and details; main idea no different; abstract)
- R1 designImplications[0] (counterbalanced crossover, its proposed keep rule, and a local, exportable study log)
- R5 synthesis (a teacher-facing export changes the privacy position: Persónuvernd's school checklist); R6-15 (Skjermbrukutvalget 2023)

**Fix.** If [USER] chooses to run it (the spring term 2027 is the next window): pick two adjacent chemistry sections of similar length (toc readingTime); half the class reads A paged then B scrolled, the other half the reverse; after each, students predict their score (0–5), then answer five teacher-written questions (three main-idea, two detail) on paper or in the school's platform, and say which mode they would keep. Fix the decision rule and a practical non-inferiority margin in advance (for example, paged at most 1 of 3 main-idea items worse on average); R1's keep rule is one candidate, which [USER] may adopt or replace. Report main-idea and detail scores, |prediction − score| and the keep/switch choice (say whether it is a stated choice or observed switching), with the weakest third of readers by prior grade shown separately, and pool over classes or terms before drawing a conclusion. Tell students what is being compared, and get consent if results will be published. Optional app support, local only and with no export: a query parameter (e.g. `?lestur=sidur` / `?lestur=skrun`, native-speaker check) that sets the reading mode for that visit without saving it, read from `location.search` in the browser (for example in onMount), because `url.searchParams` throws during prerendering and the section route is prerendered (SvelteKit's documented rule, not run here); keep the override out of the settings store, which saves every change. The crossover informs open call 3 without deciding it and does not test the page budget (open call 1); if v1.1 ships before the spring term, call 3 is made on today's evidence and the crossover can revisit it.

**Acceptance test.** A one-page protocol (the two sections, the questions, the decision rule and the margin) is committed to docs/plans before the class runs; none exists today. If the parameter is built: an e2e test seeds `readingMode: 'paged'`, opens a section with `?lestur=skrun` and finds no pager and no `[hidden]` block; seeds `readingMode: 'scrolled'`, opens a section with `?lestur=sidur` and finds the pager; then reloads each without the parameter and finds the seeded mode, with the stored setting unchanged.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] decides whether and when to run the crossover, with which classes and sections, the decision rule and margin (fixed before the class), whether results will be published (then consent), and whether to build the URL override. The result feeds open call 3 (launch default) without deciding it; it does not test open call 1 (page budget).

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: neither branch records whether prompts are used — v1.1 keeps every saved recall and reviewHistory records predictedKnown; only prompts shown or skipped go unrecorded.
- NOT: a classroom crossover can settle the default — one class with three main-idea items and no preset margin almost always meets 'not worse'.
- NOT: dropping the export follows R1 — R1 proposes a local, exportable study log; dropping it is a defensible departure.
- NOT: a main-idea score alone as the outcome — Singer & Alexander found no main-idea difference; print's advantage was on key points and details, so detail items are reported too.
- NOT: Norway's screen committee as a firm conclusion — it is hedged ('kan ha større utbytte av å lese på papir') and rests mostly on higher-education studies.
- NOT: that the protocol measures reach (R6-19, R6-20) — it measures none.

**Source findings:** PED1-18

### PED-36. Predict-first reaches only students who make their own cards; ready-made key-term starter decks from glossary.json would bring it to every student.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** new-scope · **Stage:** new scope · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1, v1.2, main · **Also in:** Code, UI

_Severity note:_ The verifier kept medium: new scope, not a defect; it widens the reach of P0.3 and of P1.1's flashcard half.

**Problem.** Predict-first (P0.3) and SM-2 flashcard review need cards, and on main, v1.1 and v1.2 a card can only be made from the selection popup (v1.1: FlashcardModal opened from TextHighlighter.svelte:392; v1.2 also the cloze at :466/:477), which opens only on `onmouseup` (:449 on main and v1.1, :520 on v1.2); the /minniskort empty state says so (:79-82). Whether a phone's long-press opens the popup was not tested on a device (a synthetic touch did not open it). Students who never make cards therefore meet neither predict-first nor SM-2 review, and P1.1 Kvörðun's flashcard half stays empty for them; /nam's review phase does bring back practice problems attempted in the reader without any card (getProblemsForReview, main quiz.ts:571, v1.1 :564). The material already exists: on the ② preview copy, glossary.json holds 755 terms, all with an Icelandic definition and a chapter, 691 with `english` (64 without), and the key-terms rollups' `<dt>` counts match the glossary's per-chapter counts. glossary.json carries no per-term review status, and its terms are under efni's terminology review (the reason the chemistry sync is held).

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/minniskort/+page.svelte:79-82 (empty state: 'Til að búa til minniskort, veldu texta í kafla og smelltu á „Minniskort“…')
- v1.1:src/lib/components/TextHighlighter.svelte:449 and v1.2 :520 (`onmouseup` only); the only `addDeck` callers: FlashcardModal.svelte:55 (main, v1.1) and, on v1.2, TextHighlighter.svelte:466 (cloze)
- glossary.json on the ② preview copy: 755 terms, 691 with `english`; key-terms `<dt>` counts match the glossary per chapter (54/61/20/8/67 for chapters 1/2/3/13/21)
- R2-24 (Lin et al. 2018: flashcards did not beat free study on authentic material; Pan 2023, secondary: self-made beat premade cards); R2-22 (cloze helps only the exact items: reserve automatic cloze for glossary terms)
