**Fix.** If [USER] wants it: a ready-made 'Lykilhugtök {n}. kafla' starter deck per chapter from glossary.json (front: the Icelandic term, with an '(e. …)' hint only when `english` exists; back: the definition), created on demand from the end of the key-terms rollup ('Æfa lykilhugtök kaflans') and from /minniskort, and studied with P0.3 and SM-2 unchanged. Key each card by term and render front and back from the current glossary.json at study time, storing only the SM-2 records, so efni's corrections reach existing cards instead of being frozen in localStorage; chapter keys need no rename migration. Offer it as a starter the student can edit or rephrase, not a replacement for making cards (R2-24: self-made beat premade). Mark decks 'Vélþýtt' through the chapter's key-terms rollup `reviewed` flag in toc.json until efni ships a per-term status, and leave out terms that fail a term-consistency check only once efni ships that result in the content (finding PED4-04); vefur builds no term filter of its own. Putting the deck at the end of the rollup depends on the page-type gate for the recall prompt (finding PED4-01); rollups still scroll (ruling 2). Native-speaker check for all strings.

**Acceptance test.** Unit: `buildChapterDeck(glossary, 1)` returns one card per glossary term whose chapter is 1 (the count derived from the fixture, not hardcoded), each with a non-empty back, and no '(e. …)' hint on a term without `english`; after the fixture's definition for a term changes, the existing card shows the new back while its SM-2 record is unchanged. E2E on a fresh phone profile (390×844): from the chapter-1 key-terms page one tap creates the deck, and /minniskort starts a session showing 'Man það' and 'Man það ekki'. Today no such deck can be created.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] decides whether to offer ready-made decks at all (the evidence favours self-made cards, so this trades generation for reach), and whether to wait for efni's per-term review status and consistency result before showing them.

**efni side.** Ask efni for two optional fields in glossary.json: a per-term review status (to replace the toc.json rollup proxy for 'Vélþýtt') and the result of the term-consistency check proposed in finding PED4-04, so that terms failing it can be left out of decks. The deck can be built on the proxy before either arrives.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: 755 glossary terms with English equivalents — 755 terms, of which 691 carry `english` (64 do not), on the ② preview copy.
- NOT: phone-first students never meet spaced review — /nam's review phase brings back practice problems attempted in the reader without any card; only predict-first and SM-2 flashcard review need self-made cards.
- NOT: phones cannot open the selection popup, as a measured fact — it opens on `onmouseup` only and a synthetic touch did not open it, but a real long-press was not tested on a device.
- NOT: copying the definitions into localStorage when the deck is created — that freezes MT text; render from the current glossary.json at study time.
- NOT: premade decks as a replacement for making cards — R2-24's evidence (Pan 2023, secondary) favours self-made cards.

**Source findings:** PED4-15

### PED-37. The 873 answered end-of-chapter exercises (counted on the ② preview) enter no practice feature; interleaved section practice could be built from them.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** new-scope · **Stage:** new scope · **Effort:** L · **Branches:** main, v1.1 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ The verifier lowered it from high: every factual claim reproduced, but this is a new L feature, not a fix needed before release.

**Problem.** On main and v1.1, /prof and /nam draw only from practiceProblemProgress, which only practiceReveal fills when a student reveals a 'Kannaðu þekkingu þína' answer in the reader: markPracticeProblemViewed (main quiz.ts:332-363) is the only creator and practiceReveal.ts (main :185, v1.1 :192) its only caller, and the type allows source 'bank' (quiz.ts:37) but nothing writes it. So /prof is empty for a new student ('Engin æfingadæmi tiltæk') and otherwise re-serves problems whose answers the student has already seen, /nam's review re-surfaces the identical item, and getAdaptiveProblems orders by mastery and then recency, with no deliberate mixing. On the ② preview copy the chapter exercise pages hold 1,736 exercises, 873 of them with answer-key answers, all paired by `data-exercise-id` (0 orphans), and all 114 exercise groups carry `data-section` (answered exercises per group: min 1, median 6, max 30). Only 128 of the 873 answers (99 single numbers, 29 multi-part) look auto-checkable by the reviewers' heuristic, so mastery would mostly be self-graded. Evidence: interleaving beat blocking for grade-7 mathematics practice in a classroom trial (d = 0.83, abstract, R3-16) and helped mathematical tasks in a meta-analysis (g = 0.34) but not expository texts (R3-17), and spaced retrieval beat massed retrieval (g = 0.74 after trim-and-fill, mostly undergraduate lab studies, R2-21).

**Evidence.**

- main:src/lib/stores/quiz.ts:37 (source 'inline' | 'bank'; nothing writes 'bank'), :332-363 (markPracticeProblemViewed, the only creator), :522-568 (getAdaptiveProblems: mastery, then recency), :571-607 (getProblemsForReview)
- main:src/lib/actions/practiceReveal.ts:185 (v1.1 :192), the only caller; main:src/lib/components/AdaptiveQuiz.svelte:121-142 (empty state 'Engin æfingadæmi tiltæk')
- EOC count on the ② preview copy: 1,736 exercises, 873 with `data-has-answer`, all paired with a `div.answer-entry`; 114 exercise groups, each with `data-section`; answer classes text 321, has-math 273, multi-mixed 151, single-num 99, multi-part 29
- R3-16 (Rohrer 2020: 54 grade-7 maths classes, 61% vs 38%, d = 0.83, abstract); R3-17 (Brunmair 2019: maths g = 0.34, expository texts n.s.); R2-21 (Latimier 2021: spaced g = 0.74 after trim-and-fill); R3-19, R3-20 (mastery criteria; adaptive no better than static)

**Fix.** If [USER] schedules it (an L feature, after release), build it in three steps on v1.1/main. (1) A build-time index: a script next to process-content.js writing `static/content/<book>/practice-index.json` (exerciseId, chapter, section from `data-section`, hasAnswer, answer id, and the answer class from the parser in finding PED3-04), listed in offline-manifest.json so a downloaded book has it. (2) /prof offers 'Æfing úr N. kafla' sets (native-speaker check) that mix that chapter's sections, plus earlier chapters once the student has some, never two consecutive items from the same section; items use the attempt-first loop (PED3-04) and a notation-preserving renderer (finding PED3-03), and a section counts as mastered after 3 correct in a row, which will mostly be self-graded. (3) A spaced re-check after 1–2 weeks (this is P2.2 for problems) with a DIFFERENT answered exercise from the same section, never the item whose answer is already known. Keep the reading order unshuffled, and use one scheduler: extend quiz.ts with source 'bank', or reuse the SM-2 store, not both. Any new section-keyed state (per-section mastery, re-check due dates) needs a `renameSections()` and a line in `migrateRenamedSections()`, or a section rename orphans it.

**Acceptance test.** Unit: the index builder returns as many answered exercises as the synced content has `data-has-answer` exercises (873 on the ② preview; derive the count, never hardcode it), each with a section; the set builder never puts two items from the same section in a row; mastery flips after 3 consecutive correct items; a rename-migration test moves per-section mastery to the new slug. E2E on a fresh profile: `/prof?kafli=03` offers at least 5 items without any answer revealed first (today it shows 'Engin æfingadæmi tiltæk'). Optional, in class: a delayed, unannounced quiz comparing blocked and interleaved sets.

**Decision for [USER].** Whether and when to build an L feature is [USER]'s call (the verifier's note); from 1 November most of [USER]'s time goes to the chemistry review in efni.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: severity high, or a fix needed before release — it is a new L feature, medium.
- NOT: the effect sizes as chemistry or upper-secondary evidence — d = 0.83 is grade-7 mathematics (abstract), Brunmair's mathematics estimate is g = 0.34, and g = 0.74 comes mostly from undergraduate lab studies.
- NOT: an acceptance test that hardcodes 873 — the count is from the ② preview copy, and CI syncs efni main; derive it from `data-has-answer`.

**Source findings:** PED3-05

### PED-38. Coupled letter- and word-spacing and a larger text size are the typographic levers with evidence; a new font is not.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** new-scope · **Stage:** new scope · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ The verifier kept medium: new scope, with abstract-level evidence that applies mainly to readers with dyslexia.

**Problem.** On main, v1.1 and v1.2 the reader offers text sizes of 14/16/18/20 px and no letter- or word-spacing control: app.css defines no spacing token, letter-spacing appears only as fixed values, there is no word-spacing, and settings.ts has no spacing key; P1.4 on v1.2 adds a font, not spacing. The evidence, all read at abstract level, is that dyslexia fonts' advantage comes from their spacing, not their letterforms (R4-05, including Duranovic 2018), that extra-large letter spacing helped 13-year-olds with dyslexia more than their peers (R4-06), and that more letter spacing slows reading unless word spacing grows with it (R4-07). It therefore supports a coupled spacing setting for dyslexic readers, and as a matter of personal fit for everyone else. Larger text improved comprehension at 18 and 26 'points' on a text-heavy desktop site (R4-14: adults, unit not verified), which only weakly motivates a step above today's 20 px. WCAG 1.4.12 gives a natural widest step (0.12em letters, 0.16em words; R4-18), and EPUB RS 3.3's non-normative §14 suggests letting users change line and word spacing (R4-20). v1.1's pager already re-splits on any settings change (PagedReaderControls.svelte:470-479).

**Evidence.**

- main:src/app.css:11-15 and v1.1:src/app.css:11-15 (font-size tokens 14/16/18/20 px; no spacing token, no word-spacing); no spacing key in settings.ts on main, v1.1 or v1.2
- v1.1:src/lib/components/SettingsModal.svelte:31-48 (sizes Lítið/Miðlungs/Stórt/Mjög stórt; Línubil Venjulegt/Rýmra/Rúmt)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:470-479 (every settings emission schedules a re-split)
- R4-05 (Marinus 2016: 7% faster in Dyslexie, not significant once Arial's spacing matched; Duranovic 2018: 'only spacing plays a role'), R4-06 (Stagg & Kiss 2021: 32 adolescents with dyslexia, mean age 13), R4-07 (Galliussi 2020: letter spacing alone slows reading), R4-14 (Rello 2016), R4-18 (WCAG 1.4.12 values)

**Fix.** Add a 'Stafa- og orðabil' setting (native-speaker check) with steps Venjulegt / Rýmra / Rúmt, mirroring the Línubil labels, each raising word spacing at least as much as letter spacing, the widest at letter-spacing 0.12em and word-spacing 0.16em, applied to `.reading-content` through classes next to the line-height ones (CSS letter-spacing also widens the space character, so word gaps grow by both values). Give the new key a default and a validator in settings.ts (validateStoreData fills absent keys). Add a 22 px size step ('Stærst', native-speaker check), and a 24 px one only if paged mode still fits on a 390 px phone. Present both as personal fit, with no benefit claim in the UI. This needs no new font: the evidence is for spacing and size, not letterforms.

**Acceptance test.** With the widest spacing and the largest size, at 390×844 and 1280×800, and with readingMode set explicitly in the test profile: a multi-page paged section re-splits after the change (its page boundaries are recomputed), scrollWidth equals innerWidth and Næsta is not covered; computed letter-spacing and word-spacing on a `.reading-content p` equal the step values; the same holds in scrolled mode and under a WCAG 1.4.12 user stylesheet. settings.test.ts covers the new key's default (read from defaultSettings) and its validator. Today neither the setting nor the 22 px step exists.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the ~40% of Iceland's 15-year-olds below baseline (PISA 2022, R4-21) as 'the readers these supports are for' — R4-21 is population context, and the spacing studies are of readers with dyslexia.
- NOT: spacing as a benefit for all readers — typical peers gained less (Stagg & Kiss) and letter spacing alone slowed reading (Galliussi).
- NOT: Rello et al. 2016 as strong support for a step above 20 px — adults reading Wikipedia in Arial on desktop, unit not verified; if its 'points' were CSS px, 18 is already below today's maximum.
- NOT: EPUB RS 3.3 as requiring a spacing control — its spacing text is in the non-normative §14.
- NOT: 'the page count changes' as the re-split check — it can fail on a short section; test a multi-page section.

**Source findings:** PED1-17

### PED-39. /for-teachers says nothing about the learning design or data handling, no export carries recall or flashcard data for a teacher, and any data sheet must list the live page-view beacon.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** new-scope · **Stage:** new scope · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** main, v1.1 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ The verifier kept medium: new scope. The data-handling part weighs most, because the deployed reader sends page-view events that a 'nothing is sent' sheet would deny.

**Problem.** Main's /for-teachers (contents :39-44) covers access, content status, known issues, quality, feedback and support, but says nothing on using the recall prompt, pre-questions or flashcards in class and nothing a school's data protection officer needs, and there is no privacy page anywhere in src/routes. Its status text is stale beyond the two lines first quoted: 'Kaflar 1-4 tiltækir' (:63), 'Kafli 1: Vantar inngangskafla og æfingar' (:159) and 'Kaflar 5-21: Ekki þýddir enn' (:160), while the deployed TOC has 21 chapters, and chapter 1 is badged 'Yfirfarið' (:104) though only 2 of its 11 pages carry `reviewed: true`. Neither export carries recall or flashcard data (v1.1 analytics.ts:696-719; the annotation export covers annotations only), and v1.1's recall entries are write-only: no UI reads, exports or deletes them, and /greining's erase clears analyticsStore only. 'Nothing is sent' would be false for the deployed reader: with `VITE_API_URL` set at build time (the gitignored .env.production), every [bookSlug] route change POSTs `{eventType, book, chapter, section}` by sendBeacon to the editorial server's `/api/analytics/event` (main `[bookSlug]/+layout.svelte:23-30` calls `trackPageView`, `api.ts:26-58`), which the live chunk, the live CSP and a browser probe of the live §1.1 page confirm; efni main's handler records those fields plus user-agent, referrer, any sessionId cookie and the time, and whether production stores rows was not tested. All of this is pre-existing on main except the recall store, which v1.1 adds.

**Evidence.**

- main:src/routes/for-teachers/+page.svelte:39-44 (contents), :63, :104, :159-160 (stale status text), :248-252 (4-hour response promise)
- v1.1:src/lib/stores/analytics.ts:696-719 (export omits recall and flashcards); v1.1:src/lib/components/AnnotationSidebar.svelte:79-90 (Markdown download precedent); recallStore's only UI consumer is RecallPrompt.svelte:30 (addEntry)
- main:src/routes/[bookSlug]/+layout.svelte:23-30 → main:src/lib/utils/api.ts:26-58 (sendBeacon event when `VITE_API_URL` is set, main:src/lib/config.ts:15); confirmed by the live chunk, the live CSP `connect-src` and a headless-browser probe of the live §1.1 page
- R6-05 (Russell 2023: 59 US university courses; reading almost three times higher where instructors used reading strategies; abstract, correlational); R5-13 (KBCP framework plus an N = 47 university pilot); R5-22, R5-23 (ePrivacy scope of local storage; Persónuvernd's school checklist)

**Fix.** Add a 'Kennsla með Námsbókasafni' section (native-speaker check for all strings): (a) what each intervention does and why, in 2–3 lines each; (b) three routines that need no backend: assign a section and ask students to finish its recall; open the next lesson by re-asking that section's question on the board; let pairs compare their recall with the section's objectives; (c) a one-page data sheet for schools, written only after [USER] decides what happens to the page-view beacon (if [USER] chooses to drop it, unset `VITE_API_URL` or remove `trackPageView`; if opt-in, a setting that is off by default; if kept, the sheet names it). The sheet lists everything that leaves the device (GoatCounter's count and, if kept, the editorial-server event with what it carries and the fact that the server logs user-agent and referrer), what stays in localStorage, and how to erase it (clear site data in the browser, since no in-app control erases recall, flashcards or highlights); write it from a request log of a build made with the production env, because a CI or worktree build has no `VITE_API_URL` and would wrongly show nothing. (d) A student-initiated 'Sækja upprifjanir mínar' Markdown download, previewed first and modelled on AnnotationSidebar.svelte:79-90, so a student can choose to hand work in through the school's LMS; it is also the first way a student could see their own recall entries (P2.3's recall-review tab is the planned in-app view). Replace the stale status text, preferably with a block derived from toc.json, as the landing page derives its chapter counts. Changing what the server logs would be a request to efni, whose `server/` (AGPL) owns the handler.

**Acceptance test.** /for-teachers contains the teaching section and the data sheet, and its status text matches the toc.json the build ships (chapter count; no 'Yfirfarið' badge on a chapter unless all its pages carry `reviewed: true`). A Playwright request log of a production-env build on a section page shows exactly the outgoing requests the sheet names. The download produces `upprifjanir-{bók}-{dagsetning}.md` containing exactly the seeded entries after a preview, and the request log shows no network request during the export.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] decides first whether to keep the editorial-server page-view beacon (drop it, make it opt-in, or keep it and list it), then whether to publish the routines and the data sheet and to build the recall download. This is not one of the four open calls.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: a data sheet saying that nothing is sent — the deployed reader POSTs page-view events to the editorial server when built with the production env, besides GoatCounter's count.
- NOT: only 'Kaflar 1-4 tiltækir' and 'Kaflar 5-21: Ekki þýddir enn' are stale — 'Kafli 1: Vantar inngangskafla og æfingar' is too, and chapter 1's 'Yfirfarið' badge overstates review (2 of 11 pages).
- NOT: 'assigned reading mostly happens when teachers build it in (about three times more)' as evidence for Icelandic upper-secondary — R6-05 is an abstract-level correlation across 59 US university courses.
- NOT: class routines as an established requirement for strategy uptake — R5-13 is the KBCP framework (no data) plus an N = 47 university pilot.

**Source findings:** PED4-16

### PED-40. The reader has no read-aloud (the React version had one), and paged mode may cut off the read-aloud tools students already use.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** new-scope · **Stage:** new scope · **Effort:** L · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ The verifier kept medium: new scope with low reviewer confidence; the paged-mode cut-off of OS and browser read-aloud is inferred from the code, not measured on a device.

**Problem.** Main, v1.1 and v1.2 have no read-aloud (0 hits for speechSynthesis or SpeechSynthesisUtterance in src on all three), but the React reader on archive/react-v1 had one: a Web Speech service with is-IS voice detection (src/services/piperTts.ts, src/hooks/useTextToSpeech.ts), built 2025-12-29/30 and dropped at the SvelteKit migration (435b62a). Its history is evidence: a bundled Piper voice cost about 56 MB each (0edb7bb), the Tiro.is API went 404 after its funding ended (8422ad0), and typical Windows machines offered no is-IS voice (9600ecf, 1821374); vestiges remain on all three branches (`VITE_TTS_PROXY_URL` in .env.production.example and a print `.tts-controls` selector in app.css). For students with reading disabilities, text-to-speech and read-aloud tools improve comprehension (weighted effect .35, R4-16, abstract), while for typical self-paced readers listening adds nothing (R4-17), so any control should be opt-in. Separately, v1.1's paged mode sets `hidden` on off-page blocks (PagedReaderControls.svelte:148-168), so OS and browser read-aloud tools may read only the current page (not verified on a device). On v1.1 as it stands that is every reader's default (readingMode 'paged', settings.ts:77) and the only switch to scrolled sits in Settings (SettingsModal.svelte:311); whether paged stays the launch default is open call 3.

**Evidence.**

- git grep of src on main, v1.1 and v1.2: 0 hits for speechSynthesis or SpeechSynthesisUtterance; archive/react-v1: src/services/piperTts.ts and src/hooks/useTextToSpeech.ts (is-IS detection), commits 920daa1 to bc5064e, dropped at 435b62a
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:148-168 (`hidden` on off-page blocks); v1.1:src/lib/stores/settings.ts:77 (readingMode 'paged'); v1.1:src/lib/components/SettingsModal.svelte:311 (the only mode switch)
- R4-16 (Wood 2018: .35 [.14, .56] for students with reading disabilities, abstract); R4-17 (Clinton-Lisell 2022/2023: no reliable gain when reading is self-paced); R4-21 (PISA 2022 Iceland: about 40% below baseline)
- Web search 2026-10-16, results only (pages not opened): Hljóðbókasafn Íslands news of a pilot with MMS; an Alþingi answer snippet (the page returned HTTP 403)

**Fix.** [USER]'s call, in three parts. (1) Now, with no code: ask Hljóðbókasafn Íslands whether a CC BY Icelandic chemistry text fits its student programme (its pilot with MMS comes from search results only). (2) Before investing, measure `speechSynthesis.getVoices()` on the students' actual devices; if enough offer an `is-*` voice, add an opt-in 'Lesa upphátt' control (native-speaker check) that appears only when such a voice exists, starting from the archive's piperTts.ts and useTextToSpeech.ts rather than from scratch. It reads the current page sentence by sentence and highlights through the CSS Custom Highlight API or Ranges, never by injecting nodes into the content (CLAUDE.md: textAnchor offsets, bionicReading and the glossary's aria-labels depend on that), skips or deliberately reads the assistive MathML, and treats `.term-en` as lang='en'. No backend and no bundled neural voice (about 56 MB per voice, and everything in static/ is precached). (3) The in-view 'Lesa samfellt' switch proposed in finding PED4-10 would let OS read-aloud read a whole section; it is useful under either launch default and does not decide open call 3.

**Acceptance test.** With `getVoices()` mocked to return no 'is' voice, no read-aloud control renders. With a mocked 'is-IS' voice, pressing it calls `speechSynthesis.speak` with the first sentence of the current page, that sentence is highlighted, and the content's DOM node count is unchanged. If the in-view switch ships: in a test profile seeded with readingMode 'paged', after 'Lesa samfellt' the rendered text length equals scrolled mode's.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] decides whether to contact Hljóðbókasafn Íslands, whether a device survey of is-IS voices justifies building 'Lesa upphátt', and whether to ship the in-view switch from finding PED4-10, which informs but does not decide open call 3.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: no branch has ever had read-aloud — archive/react-v1 had a Web Speech read-aloud with is-IS voice detection, dropped at the SvelteKit migration (435b62a).
- NOT: paged mode cuts off OS read-aloud, as a measured fact — it is inferred from the `hidden` attribute, not tested on a device.
- NOT: the Alþingi and Hljóðbókasafn claims as verified — search snippets only; althingi.is returned 403.

**Source findings:** PED4-17

## Code

### CODE-01. After main is merged into v1.1, main's #249 flashcard e2e expects a card `<button>` that flips on tap and is predicted to fail against predict-first's `<div>`, and no e2e test exercises predict-first.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P0.3 · **Stage:** with the code that brings it to main · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1, main, v1.2

_Severity note:_ Both verifiers rated it high: the merge cannot go green without this change, and P0.3, one of the interventions the design principle names, has no browser test and no unit test of a false prediction.

**Problem.** main's #249 rewrite of e2e/flashcards.spec.ts finds the card through currentCard() as getByRole('button', { name: /^Spurning/ }) and flips it by clicking (:51-59, clicks at :83 and :95), while v1.1's predict-first card is a `<div class='flashcard-card'>` that reveals the answer only through 'Man það' / 'Man það ekki'. Reading the trial-merge tree ced46892 (main's spec beside v1.1's card), the three tests that call currentCard() (:73, :79, :91) fail because no button's name there starts with 'Spurning'; this is a prediction from reading, since e2e has not been run on any merged tree. On v1.1 today all 6 flashcard e2e tests skip in CI (run 37191708681), so no e2e test exercises P0.3, and flashcard.test.ts records only true and undefined predictions, so the 'predictedKnown true whenever defined' mutant survives. The card also has no accessible name or live region, and the focused predict button is removed when the answer is revealed. The break exists only on the merged tree (main's spec passes on main); on v1.2 the same surviving mutant would also silently zero CalibrationTab's underconfident cell (CalibrationTab.svelte:35).

**Evidence.**

- main:e2e/flashcards.spec.ts:51-59 (currentCard() = getByRole('button', { name: /^Spurning/ })), :83 and :95 (flip by click); the tests at :73, :79 and :91 call it
- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/minniskort/+page.svelte:195-204 (div.flashcard-card), :206-220 ('Man það' / 'Man það ekki'); v1.1:src/lib/components/study/ReviewPhase.svelte:117-130 (the same predict flow on /nam)
- trial-merges trials[0].notes and semantic-drift items[5]: 3 of 5 tests fail in currentCard() on tree ced46892 (by reading; e2e not run)
- health_v1.1 e2eSkipped[0]-[5] (all 6 flashcard tests skipped in CI run 37191708681) and mutations[29] ('predictedKnown true whenever defined' survived)
- v1.1:src/lib/stores/flashcard.test.ts:37-58 (only true and undefined predictions recorded); v1.1:src/lib/stores/flashcard.ts:299 (wasCorrect = quality >= 3)

**Fix.** In the merge change itself, rewrite main's spec around predict-first. Do not 'repair' it by restoring tap-to-flip: predict-first is the intervention. (1) Give the card an accessible name in both places it renders (the minniskort page and ReviewPhase), e.g. `<section class='flashcard-card' aria-label={isFlipped ? 'Svar' : 'Spurning'} aria-live='polite'>`. A named `<section>` is role=region, so the same attribute is the test hook and the screen-reader hook. (2) currentCard() becomes page.getByRole('region', { name: 'Spurning' }). (3) Replace the flip test with 'the answer stays hidden until a prediction; Man það ekki shows the answer and the four ratings'. Playwright matches names as substrings, so 'Man það' also matches 'Man það ekki': use exact: true. (4) Rating test: press 'Man það ekki', rate 'Gott', reload, and assert that the last reviewHistory entry has predictedKnown false and wasCorrect true (the underconfident case P1.1 counts) and that the card is still in studyRecords. (5) When the predict buttons are removed ({#if !isFlipped}), move focus to the revealed answer or the first rating button rather than relying on aria-live alone (see finding CODE1-01 on focus). (6) Add a unit test in which rateCard(id, 'good', false) stores predictedKnown false; it kills the surviving mutant. (7) Add one /nam review-phase e2e seeded with one due card (plausible by reading SessionPlanner.svelte:42-57, not verified). When P1.1 is ported, add a CalibrationTab assertion over a seeded false prediction.

**Acceptance test.** On the merged tree, e2e/flashcards.spec.ts against a seeded deck reports every test passed and none skipped in CI's list reporter (predicted today: 3 fail in currentCard()). A mutant that renders the ratings without the predict step fails 'the answer stays hidden until a prediction'. Re-running the 'predictedKnown true whenever defined' mutant (health_v1.1 mutations[29]) makes flashcard.test.ts fail.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: '3 of 5 tests fail after the merge' as a measured result: it is a prediction from reading the trial-merge tree; e2e has not been run on a merged tree.
- NOT: per-test CI log lines show the 6 flashcard skips on v1.1: the attribution comes from mapping run 37191708681's dot sequence onto 'playwright test --list' order, corroborated by the merge-base run.
- NOT: locating the card by its visible 'Spurning' label text (getByText): use a role and accessible name, or a data-testid.
- NOT: plain name matching for 'Man það': it also matches 'Man það ekki' unless exact matching is used.

**Source findings:** CODE1-07, CODE3-02

### CODE-02. The recall store is keyed by section slug but has no renameSections() and is missing from main's migrateRenamedSections(), so entries stay under a renamed section's old slug.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P0.2 · **Stage:** with the code that brings it to main · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1, main

_Severity note:_ CODE1-06's verifier kept high on the rename-hook half alone: it breaks an explicit CLAUDE.md rule, it is silent, and no test lists the section-keyed stores. CODE3-01's verifier rated it medium, because stranded entries are deferred rather than lost (the migration re-runs on every TOC load) and nothing reads them before P2.3. High stands: this register's scale makes high 'fix before release', the fix belongs in the merge change, and the deferral holds only while the redirect row stays in SECTION_REDIRECTS.

**Problem.** v1.1's recall store (src/lib/stores/recall.ts) keys every entry by bookSlug/chapterSlug/sectionSlug (:18-25) and has no renameSections(), and main's #258 migrateRenamedSections() (sectionRenameMigration.ts:30-36) migrates five stores and not recall, so on the merged tree an active rename leaves a reader's recall entries under the retired slug. The defect exists only on a merged tree: recall.ts is not on main, sectionRenameMigration.ts is not on v1.1 or v1.2, and v1.2's recall.ts is byte-identical to v1.1's. The loss is deferred, not final: the migration runs on every TOC load with no done flag (main contentLoader.ts:58), so a hook added later still moves the entries while the redirect row remains, and nothing reads entries before P2.3; but no test enumerates the section-keyed stores, and main's three rename e2e tests skip in CI. The store is also thin: the validator checks only that entries is an array, so one null element makes getForSection (and removeEntry) throw; the get({ subscribe }) getters and recallEntryCount have no consumer; and 8 of 10 recall mutants survived (load validation, the validator, the cap, trim, the chapter match, cross-tab sync). On a quota failure the global StorageWarning banner ('Gagnageymsla er full (…) — breytingar vistast ekki.', role='alert') appears while RecallPrompt still says 'Vistað — …', which is contradictory messaging rather than a missing signal.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/stores/recall.ts:18-25 (keys), :35-37 (isArray-only validator), :57-62 (the subscribe callback writes and discards safeSetItem's result), :74-111 (methods: no renameSections)
- main:src/lib/stores/sectionRenameMigration.ts:30-36 (reader, annotations, analytics, objectives, quiz); main:src/lib/stores/annotation.ts:109-113 (the renameSections form to mirror); main:src/lib/utils/contentLoader.ts:58 (runs on every TOC load)
- trial-merges trials[0].notes and semantic-drift items[0] (invisible to git: recall has no renameSections())
- section-keyed-store guard (a source scan): on main '5 section-keyed store modules; offenders: none' (exit 0); on merged tree ced46892 'offenders: src/lib/stores/recall.ts' (exit 1)
- health_v1.1 mutations[18]-[27] (recall: 2 of 10 mutants killed)

**Fix.** In the merge change: (1) In recall.ts, import { renameInAnnotations, type SectionRename } from '$lib/utils/sectionRenames'. It already fits RecallEntry: it moves only the matching book, chapter and slug, keeps id, text and createdAt, and returns the same array when nothing matched. Mirror main's annotation.ts:109-113: renameSections: (renames: SectionRename[]) => { const s = get({ subscribe }); const entries = renameInAnnotations(s.entries, renames); if (entries !== s.entries) set({ ...s, entries }); }. Do not use update((s) => next === s.entries ? s : { entries: next }): Svelte's safe_not_equal treats every object as changed, so that form rewrites the key on every TOC load that has an active rename. (2) Add ['recall', recallStore] to the list in sectionRenameMigration.ts. (3) Add a guard unit test (e.g. src/lib/stores/sectionRenameMigration.test.ts) that reads every non-test `src/lib/stores/*.ts`. For each module that mentions sectionSlug, createSectionKey or sectionKey, it asserts that the module defines renameSections and that sectionRenameMigration.ts imports it, with an explicit allowlist for false positives. It is a source scan, and it stops a future study or pre-question log from shipping unmigrated. Cheap in the same pass: validate each entry on load (keep objects whose id, bookSlug, chapterSlug, sectionSlug, text and createdAt are strings); delete the unused get()-based getters and recallEntryCount until P2.3 has a consumer; and have RecallPrompt read $storageWarning.quotaExceeded right after addEntry (subscribers run synchronously) and show 'Ekki tókst að vista' (new copy; native-speaker check) instead of 'Vistað' when the write failed. A read-back, export or delete UI for entries is P2.3 scope, not part of this fix.

**Acceptance test.** Unit tests on the merged tree: (1) Seed an entry under a synthetic old slug and call recallStore.renameSections([{ bookSlug, fromChapter, fromSlug, toChapter, toSlug }]) with a rename built in the test (not a chemistry slug, which changes at the next sync). Read through get(recallStore), the entry carries the new chapterSlug and sectionSlug and none remains under the old slug, and a second call performs no localStorage write (this tells the get/compare/set form from the update form). (2) The guard test passes with the fix and fails when the recall row is removed from migrateRenamedSections (control: it fails on tree ced46892 today). (3) Seeding { entries: [null, 5, `<valid entry>`] } loads 1 entry and does not throw.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: main is a site of the defect: recall.ts is not on main; main holds only the migration half, so the defect exists only on a merged tree.
- NOT: entries are lost on a rename: they are deferred, because the migration re-runs on every TOC load and moves them once the hook lands, while the redirect row remains.
- NOT: update((s) => next === s.entries ? s : { entries: next }) avoids a write: it rewrites the key on every TOC load that has an active rename.
- NOT: a failed save is silent: the global StorageWarning banner appears; the defect is the contradictory inline 'Vistað'.
- NOT: addEntry ignores safeSetItem's result (or should return it): the write happens in the store's subscribe callback, which discards the result; addEntry never calls safeSetItem.
- NOT: nothing can delete entries: the store has removeEntry and reset; what is missing is any UI that reads, exports or deletes them.
- NOT: the 500-entry cap drops entries in practice: it is unreachable today (the prompt fires only on a section's first completion, and the one published book has 216 TOC entries plus one front-matter page).
- NOT: the get({ subscribe }) getters are the CLAUDE.md $derived defect: they use the same idiom as main's renameSections and are a latent hazard only if a consumer wraps them in $derived.
- NOT: a read-back or delete UI for entries is needed before release: it is P2.3 scope.

**Source findings:** CODE1-06, CODE3-01

### CODE-03. The v1.1 + main merge needs a correct acceptance floor (about 863 unit tests, a skip set equal to main's 31, all pagination tests running), not 730.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** branch-status · **Stage:** with the code that brings it to main · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1, main

_Severity note:_ The single finding's verifier rated it high: with the stated 730 floor, a merge that silently lost all 133 of main's added unit tests would still pass.

**Problem.** The integration stage's stated floor, 'at least 730 unit tests pass', is far too low: main at 3dbf50b runs 837 unit tests in 51 files, v1.1 adds 26 over the merge base fcf2ba7 (704 → 730), and the two sides' changed test files do not overlap, so the merged tree should run about 863. For e2e, the expected post-merge skip set is exactly main's 31 (glossary-gating 2, index-gating 2, section-redirects 24, section-rename-migration 3), with all 19 pagination tests running. v1.1's 39 skips at dde768e are 31 − 3 + 11, because #249 replaced v1.1's 11 skipping flashcard and glossary tests (6 + 5) with 9 rewritten ones that pass on main (5 + 4); main's list reporter (7921d78) names every skip, so the two sets can be diffed. The merge has 1 textual conflict, e2e/helpers/content-fixtures.ts, where both sides appended independent helpers (keep both, and restore rollupSection's closing brace), and src/lib/stores/index.ts, the section page and the minniskort page auto-merge. The two semantic breaks (CODE-01, CODE-02) are the known ones, not a complete list: nobody has run unit tests, svelte-check or main's 5 new and 4 modified e2e specs on the merged tree under v1.1's paged default.

**Evidence.**

- CI job logs: merge base fcf2ba7 704 unit tests in 44 files; v1.1 dde768e 730 in 46; main 3dbf50b 837 in 51 (job 111559150517)
- v1.1 CI run 37191708681: 111 e2e, 72 passed, 39 skipped, svelte-check 0 errors and 0 warnings; main run 37244347862: 110 e2e, 79 passed, 31 skipped (list reporter)
- trial-merges trials[0] (T1): 1 conflict, e2e/helpers/content-fixtures.ts; svelte-check was not run on any trial tree
- main:playwright.config.ts:11 (list reporter in CI since 7921d78; v1.1 never touched the file)

**Fix.** Constraints for the merge change (facts, not a strategy): (1) Land CODE-01 and CODE-02 in the merge change itself. (2) Resolve content-fixtures.ts by keeping both sides' helpers. (3) On the merged tree, run npm run lint, npm run check (svelte-check), npm test, npm run build:no-validate and the full e2e suite with v1.1's settings; locally, run Playwright with TMPDIR on disk (TMPDIR=~/.cache/pw-tmp npx playwright test), as CLAUDE.md requires. (4) Diff CI's named skip list against main's 31 and explain every extra before merging. (5) Resolve no P0 conflict in favour of v1.2 (CODE-04). (6) Re-run manual QA batches D–E on the merged build, not on dde768e. For the later P1 port, run svelte-check and the unit suite after each pick, and judge P1 only by CI on the ported tree (CODE-04).

**Acceptance test.** On the merged tree: svelte-check reports 0 errors, and no warning that main at 3dbf50b does not already report (v1.1 had 0 and 0 at dde768e); the unit suite runs about 863 tests (no fewer than main's 837 plus v1.1's 26 additions), with every test file from both parents present and passing; e2e reports 0 failed and a named skip set identical to main's 31, with none of the 19 pagination tests skipped; CODE-02's guard test passes. A merge that drops any of main's test files fails the count.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: 'at least 730 unit tests pass' as the acceptance floor: the merged tree should run about 863.
- NOT: #249 'makes 11 tests run': it replaced v1.1's 11 skipping flashcard and glossary tests with 9 rewritten ones that pass on main (5 + 4).
- NOT: P1.2 onto main conflicts only in P0 context lines: each of its 3 hunks also carries P1.2's own import, showPreQuestion state and gate assignment, which must be re-added.
- NOT: the two semantic breaks are a complete list: they are the two known ones.

**Source findings:** CODE3-06

### CODE-04. v1.2's June copy of P0 is superseded and broken against today's markup, so P1 is replayed onto v1.1 after it merges main (never v1.2 into main, never main into v1.2) and re-measured on the ported tree.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** branch-status · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.2, v1.1, main · **Also in:** Pedagogy, UI

_Severity note:_ CODE2-09 and PED1-20 were rated high: a direct merge of v1.2 into main, or of main into v1.2, would bring back a June pager that is broken against today's markup. UI3-19 and UI2-19 were rated low because they only qualify what v1.2's captures show. High is kept for the merged issue because a wrong integration path would ship a broken P0.

**Problem.** feature/reader-v1.2 (e3b29c7, 18 commits ahead of main and 337 behind) carries a June copy of P0 that v1.1 supersedes, and its pager is broken against today's markup: buildUnits never looks through `<main>` (a module became one unit; page 2 was 6,144 px in a 720 px window), the budget is innerHeight − 260, rollups are paged, completion needs every unit, and ←/→ never turn pages because the June keyboard handler calls preventDefault before the pager's defaultPrevented check (a direct main merge would make → leave the section). The June RecallPrompt and Lestrarstilling block are superseded, not broken (RecallPrompt differs from v1.1's only by inline SVG versus Icon), and v1.2 lacks main's Icon registry and #246's phone fixes. The P1 work is small and portable: onto v1.1, 1f40724 (P1.4), 02c5ce9 (P1.3) and 9b74bb2 (P1.2) cherry-pick cleanly in that order and c493bd2 (P1.1) conflicts only in AnalyticsTabs ('scale' is not an IconName; the registry reserves grid-2x2 for calibration); the same holds onto a simulated v1.1+main, and merging v1.2 INTO v1.1 gives the same tree as the four picks. v1.2's head has no real CI result (run 30154161644 never started: billing; the tree-identical June run 27268902714 was green), and re-running it today fails the old unsplit blocking npm audit (16 vulnerabilities, 8 high) regardless of the code. Numbers captured on v1.2's P1 surfaces do not transfer: they are relative to the June pager or come from a build with no objectives (v1.2's process-content.js:130 writes objectives: [], so P1.2 never renders there), and the cloze card text changes on v1.1 and main because a ' (e. …)' gloss span is injected into 140 of 852 dfn.term in this content copy, which blockTextAround's raw textContent picks up; the colour tokens are identical, so contrast does transfer.

**Evidence.**

- v1.2:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:73 (max(320, innerHeight − 260)), :100-123 (buildUnits over direct `<section>` children only); ui-capture_v1.2 layout[1] (2 pages: `<header>`, then all of `<main>`)
- semantic-drift items[4] (June keyboard handler: ←/→ never turn pages) and items[20] (June P0 copy broken against today's markup)
- trial-merges trials[3]-[10] (T4a-T4h: per-commit picks onto v1.1 and onto main) and trials[1] (T2: a direct v1.2→main merge)
- health_v1.2 ci: run 30154161644 has 0 steps (billing); run 27268902714 on f8a245f was green; v1.2:.github/workflows/ci.yml:124 (blocking full-tree audit, exit 1 today with 8 high)
- v1.2:scripts/process-content.js:130 (objectives: []), fixed on main in 9267a7d (#151); v1.1:src/lib/actions/glossaryTerms.ts:570-572 (the gloss span: 140 of 852 dfn.term, ② preview content)

**Fix.** The rule: never merge v1.2 into main directly and never merge main into v1.2, and take no P0 file from v1.2 (PagedReaderControls.svelte, paginate.ts, keyboardShortcuts.ts, RecallPrompt.svelte, the section page's paged wiring). After v1.1 has merged main (CODE-03), replay P1 onto v1.1 in the order P1.4 (1f40724) → P1.3 (02c5ce9) → P1.2 (9b74bb2) → P1.1 (c493bd2), each followed by its fix commits from this review. Resolve P1.1's AnalyticsTabs conflict with the registry's 'grid-2x2' glyph; P1.1 needs P0.3's predictedKnown, so it can land only on a tree with P0.3. Cherry-picking is preferred for per-feature review with fix commits interleaved; merging v1.2 into v1.1 gives the same tree. P1.4 has no P0 dependency and also picks cleanly onto main alone, so its place in the order is a choice. Land the design-system fixes (tokens, focus ring, white-on-amber) once on main or v1.1, and write the ported P1 against them rather than patching v1.2's June strings; token changes on main show on the live site at the next deploy, so they need a visual check first. Run svelte-check and the unit suite after each pick, and judge P1 only by CI on the ported tree, not by re-running e3b29c7. Re-measure every pager-relative number and the cloze strings on the port, and treat v1.2's screenshots as design evidence only; the v1.2 capture scripts assume the June pager (they click 'Næsta síða' and expect page 2 to be all of `<main>`) and need adapting. Open call 4 (pre-question frequency) must be decided before P1.2 is deployed, because on main's pipeline P1.2 renders as soon as it is ported and deployed (the live chemistry pages already carry page-data objectives); P1.2's gating code then follows [USER]'s answer.

**Acceptance test.** On the port branch: git diff against v1.1-after-merge for src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte, src/lib/utils/paginate.ts and src/lib/actions/keyboardShortcuts.ts is empty, or holds only a reviewed P1.2 placement hook, and RecallPrompt.svelte shows only design-system edits. svelte-check and the unit suite pass after each pick. CI's lint-and-test and e2e jobs are green on the ported tree with all 19 pagination tests running. Each of the four P1 features has at least one e2e test that fails when the feature is removed (a desktop e2e for P1.3 does not exercise the phone path, finding CODE2-06). A capture of the port reproduces the component-level rows (contrast, targets) and replaces every pager-relative number. grep for text-white in PreQuestionPrompt.svelte, CalibrationTab.svelte and the cloze toast returns nothing.

**Decision for [USER].** Retiring feature/reader-v1.2 after the port is a branch-hygiene choice for [USER]. Open call 4 (pre-question frequency) is not decided here, but it has to be decided before P1.2 is deployed.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: merging feature/reader-v1.2 into v1.1 would bring back the June P0: it gives the same tree as the four picks (they differ only in AnalyticsTabs' conflict-marker labels); the hazard is a direct v1.2→main merge or a main→v1.2 merge.
- NOT: 'never merge the branch' with only P1.4 replayed: P1.1–P1.3 must be replayed too, or they are stranded.
- NOT: P1.4 must wait for v1.1 to merge main: 1f40724 also cherry-picks cleanly onto main alone (T4e); the order is a choice.
- NOT: P1.2's gating 'must change first': pre-question frequency must be decided first; it is open call 4.
- NOT: the June RecallPrompt and Lestrarstilling block are broken against today's markup: only the June pager is; those two are superseded.
- NOT: all 3 extra white-on-amber lines on v1.2 come from P1: practiceProblems.ts:184 is June code that main has since replaced with practiceReveal.ts (#150); P1's two are the pre-question button and the cloze toast.
- NOT: the cloze card text in v1.2's captures transfers to the port: v1.1's and main's gloss spans change it.
- NOT: design-system token changes on main are 'no behaviour change': they change what the live site shows at the next deploy and need a visual check first.
- NOT: the v1.2 capture scripts can be re-run unchanged against the port: they assume the June pager.

**Source findings:** CODE2-09, PED1-20, UI3-19, UI2-19

### CODE-05. The settings store saves every default on first load and keeps no version or record of explicit choices, so a new default reaches a profile only if that key was never saved, and a later change of default cannot tell a choice from an auto-save.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1, v1.2, main · **Also in:** Pedagogy, UI

_Severity note:_ CODE3-03's verifier rated it high because the window closes at the first release that writes readingMode and the store bears on open call 3; CODE1-15's verifier rated it medium. High fits the merged issue: after that first deploy, no later change of default can be applied selectively.

**Problem.** The settings store (src/lib/stores/settings.ts) writes its full state to localStorage at module init through its own subscribe (v1.1 :113-122), the root layout imports the stores on every page, and validateStoreData fills only keys that are absent (storeValidation.ts:44-58); there is no schema version and no record of which values a reader chose. The pattern is already on main; it matters now because v1.1 changes defaults: the first v1.1 deploy writes defaultSettings.readingMode into every profile that loads the site (no profile stores readingMode today), while P0.1's lineWidth 'narrow' reaches only first-time visitors, because returning profiles that never changed their width store main's 'medium', a split that follows from storage history rather than from a decision. After that first load, a later change of the launch default (open call 3) would reach nobody, and nothing, including the paged-versus-scrolled comparison the research suggests (R1 designImplications[0]), can tell a reader who chose 'Síðuskipt' from one who never opened Stillingar. P1.4's port has the same freeze: v1.2's 'follow prefers-color-scheme only when nothing is stored' is saved on the first load and then frozen (measured: after the OS switches to light, the next load still says 'dark'). Prerendering also uses the defaults, so a returning 'medium' profile's text column reflows at hydration on every hard load (608 px → min(available, 832 px), e.g. 736 px at 768 px wide; phones and pagination are unaffected). No unit test pins the new defaults or validators: the mutants for settings.ts:72, :77 and :91 all survived.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/stores/settings.ts:72 (lineWidth 'narrow'), :77 (readingMode 'paged'), :85-97 (validators), :113-122 (the store's own subscribe saves the full state at module init)
- v1.1:src/lib/utils/storeValidation.ts:44-58 (an absent key takes the default; a valid stored value is kept); main:src/routes/+layout.svelte:7 (the stores are imported on every page)
- settings-frozen scratch tests on the real modules: a main-shaped profile with no readingMode gets readingMode written and keeps lineWidth 'medium' on first import with no setter called (4/4 passed); v1.2 theme: OS dark on a fresh profile is written on first import and still reads 'dark' after the OS switches to light (3/3)
- health_v1.1 mutations[37] (:77 'paged'→'scrolled'), [38] (:91 validator) and [39] (:72 'narrow'→'medium'): all survived
- R1-surface designImplications[0] (treat the launch default as an experiment) and its item 'remember each reader's mode choice per device and never silently switch it'

**Fix.** Before the first deploy of a build that writes readingMode (that first load closes the window), build a mechanism that can apply whichever answer [USER] gives to open call 3, without choosing one. (1) Record explicit choices, by either of two mechanisms. Option A: add settingsVersion: number and chosen: Partial&lt;Record`<keyof SettingsState, true>`> to SettingsState. Every setter and toggle marks its key, including toggleTheme (the 't' shortcut), toggleBionicReading, toggleGlossaryHighlighting and toggleShowTermEnglish, and the mark survives the cross-tab onStorageChange path; sidebarOpen is UI state and can stay unmarked. A pure migrateSettings(stored, fromVersion) in src/lib/utils/settingsMigration.ts applies a list of default changes [{ version, key, from, to }] only where !chosen[key] && value === from. Option B: persist only explicitly set keys (sparse, as shortcutPreferences already is) and overlay them on the code defaults, so a later default change reaches every reader who never chose, with no migration list. (2) Add one unit-tested migration in loadSettings for profiles that predate the release. A stored object without readingMode identifies a pre-v1.1 profile exactly once, because the store saves the full state on that same load. If [USER] chooses that existing readers keep the scrolled reading they have today, the migration writes 'scrolled' for those profiles; if [USER] chooses that existing readers get the launch default, absent keys already take it. If [USER] chooses to move existing 'medium' line widths to 'narrow', do it once there, knowing it also overrides readers who picked 'medium' on purpose, since existing full-state profiles cannot be told apart. (3) Pin P0.1's lineWidth default and the validators in settings.test.ts, and pin readingMode's default to the ruled value once [USER] has ruled. (4) Optional, for the reflow: a small inline script in src/app.html that sets data-line-width on `<html>` from localStorage before hydration, with the CSS keyed on that attribute. app.html already ships one inline script (the GoatCounter loader), but confirm the live server's CSP, since nginx-config-example.conf is a recommendation, not production. The same script could also remove the light-theme flash for dark-theme readers. (5) When porting P1.4, build 'follow the OS until the reader picks a theme' on the chosen record or on sparse storage.

**Acceptance test.** settings.test.ts on the release branch: (1) A fresh profile loads defaultSettings.readingMode and lineWidth 'narrow' (P0.1's default, pinned literally). (2) A stored main-shaped profile (lineWidth 'medium', no readingMode) loads with the values the migration maps it to under [USER]'s ruling, and loading it a second time changes nothing. (3) With a test-only migration entry from one valid reading mode to the other, a profile whose readingMode is not recorded as chosen moves, and one recorded as chosen (or, under Option B, explicitly stored) stays. (4) setReadingMode(x) and toggleTheme() record their keys as chosen (Option A) or persist only those keys (Option B), and the record survives a cross-tab storage event. (5) A stored readingMode 'foo' loads as defaultSettings.readingMode. (6) settings.test.ts kills health_v1.1 mutations [38] (validator) and [39] (lineWidth default); mutation [37] (the readingMode default) is killed once the ruled value is pinned. Tests (3) and (4) fail today because no choice is recorded.

**Decision for [USER].** Open call 3 (launch default: paged vs scrolled, for new and for existing profiles) stays with [USER]; this issue only builds the mechanism, so that either answer can ship and a later change can still reach readers who never chose. Separately, [USER] decides whether existing profiles that stored lineWidth 'medium' move to 'narrow' once. The mechanism has to be in the first release that writes readingMode, whatever the ruling.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the state is saved on a component's first subscribe: the store's own subscribe saves it at module init.
- NOT: every returning profile stores 'medium': only returning profiles that never changed their width do.
- NOT: the hydration reflow affects pagination: the pager measures after hydration, and phones are narrower than both widths.
- NOT: a version field is strictly required for this release: a stored object without readingMode identifies a pre-v1.1 profile exactly once; a version or chosen record is what makes later default changes selective.

**Source findings:** CODE1-15, CODE3-03

### CODE-06. On a page taller than the screen, Space and PageDown turn the page instead of scrolling, so the unseen rest of an oversized page is skipped.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** UI, Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ Both verifiers rated it high: in v1.1's default mode the browser's own read-on keys silently skip content on 10 to 12 of §3.1's 28 pages (② preview content), and nothing tells the reader.

**Problem.** On v1.1, with focus on `<body>` or any non-control, Space, PageDown and → call preventDefault and turn the page (PagedReaderControls.svelte:295-301); the only early returns (:288-293) are typing, an open dialog, Ctrl/Meta/Alt held, and Space on a focused button, link, summary or [role=button]. A block taller than the page gets a page of its own and the window scrolls (paginate.ts:80-91; overflow-y visible, max-height none), so Space or PageDown there jumps to the top of the next page while the rest of the block is still below the fold, and nothing says content was skipped. On the ② preview content, §3.1 has 10 of 28 pages whose pager sits below the fold at 1280×800 (up to 2005 px) and 12 of 28 on the phone (up to 3013 px); nine of the ten desktop cases hold an oversized aside (the tallest desktop worked example is a 1576 px aside.example with its pager 1107 px below the fold, and the 2005 px case is a 2473 px aside.note), so the unseen remainder is about 340–2005 px. The skip is inferred from code (the keyboard measurement ran on pages that fit), but the inference is deterministic. The window scroll itself is documented behaviour (paginate.ts:10-11; only the comment at :80-82 claiming an internal scroll is stale), and the plan defines Space/PageDown as 'next' (plan:170, QA doc :182) while its answer for oversized blocks, an internal scroll (plan:222), was never built, so scroll-first keys are a spec change. The defect is v1.1-only (new in P0.4).

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:288-293 (early returns), :295-301 (preventDefault, then next()/prev())
- v1.1:src/lib/utils/paginate.ts:80-91 (an oversized block gets its own page); the comment at :80-82 says it 'scrolls internally', which it does not
- ui-capture_v1.1 layout[5]: §3.1 desktop 10 of 28 pages with the pager below the fold (up to 2005 px), phone 12 of 28 (up to 3013 px)
- v1.1 measurements paged[13] pages[20]-[26] (desktop: six aside.example pages with the pager 411-1107 px below the fold, then 2005 px) and keyboard.desktop.keysBodyFocus (Space and PageDown turn the page with scrollY unchanged, on pages that fit)
- docs/plans/2026-04-22-screen-vs-paper-reader-plan.md:170 (Space/PageDown = next) and :222 (internal scroll for oversized figures, never built); docs/manual-qa-2026-06.md:182

**Fix.** Put this to [USER] as a spec change; it is not one of the three rulings, and it does not decide open call 1 (the page-height budget). If [USER] approves: in handleKeyDown, make Space and PageDown scroll first. When the end of the current page's content (its last visible block) is below the viewport by more than a small tolerance, return without preventDefault so the browser scrolls (or scroll by innerHeight − header − 48 px), and turn the page only once that end is in view. Shift+Space and PageUp do the same upward against the content top (SCROLL_OFFSET). Use a tolerance of a few px: measured pages overflow by as little as 1–5 px, and such a page should not cost an extra keypress. Test the content end, not the nav's bottom: if UI1-05's sticky bar lands, the nav is always in view. Keep ←/→ and swipes as explicit page turns. Put the decision in a pure, unit-tested helper (part of the pager unit tests finding CODE1-17 asks for), and reword the stale comment at paginate.ts:80-82. Whether to split long worked examples at step boundaries (R1 designImplications[5]) or raise the budget stays part of open call 1.

**Acceptance test.** e2e at 1280×800: find, at run time, the first page whose last visible block ends below innerHeight (do not hard-code §3.1 or a 'Hluti · Síða' label; chemistry slugs and titles change at the next sync). Press Space: the position label is unchanged and scrollY has increased. Repeat until the page's end is in view; the next Space changes the label. Shift+Space after scrolling down scrolls up before it turns back. Unit test of the helper, which takes page geometry as input and asserts no budget value: an overflowing page plus PageDown gives 'scroll'; a page that fits, or overflows by no more than the tolerance, gives 'next'; ArrowRight gives 'next' in both cases. Today the first Space on that page changes the label.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] approves or rejects a change to the plan's keyboard spec (plan:170 and QA doc :182 define Space/PageDown as 'next'). This does not touch open call 1: whether to split long examples or raise the page budget stays open.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: 2005 px is a block height: it is how far the pager sits below the fold, and that page holds a 2473 px aside.note.sciences-interconnect, not a worked example.
- NOT: about 1500 px is left unseen after the first screen: the unseen remainder on desktop §3.1 pages is about 340–2005 px.
- NOT: Space and PageDown always turn the page: the handler returns early while typing, in a dialog, with Ctrl/Meta/Alt, and for Space on a focused button, link, summary or [role=button]; with focus on a glossary term, Space scrolled the window.
- NOT: the window scroll on an oversized page is itself the defect: it is documented behaviour (paginate.ts:10-11, pagination.spec.ts:65-73) and an owner judgment call listed in v1.1's QA doc; only the comment at paginate.ts:80-82 is stale.
- NOT: paginate.ts:10-11 needs correcting: only :80-82 does.
- NOT: the pager is fully below the fold on all 12 phone pages: it is on 11; on the twelfth it overruns by 1 px and sits under the timer pill and the tools button.
- NOT: the skip was measured on an oversized page: the keyboard measurement ran on pages that fit; the skip is a deterministic inference from code.
- NOT: the nav's bottom as the scroll-first condition: with a sticky pager bar the nav is always in view.

**Source findings:** CODE1-02, UI1-04

### CODE-07. Paged mode keeps no record of the reader's page: a return visit opens page 1, 'Halda áfram' restores a stale scroll position, and a mode switch drops the reader at the top.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** UI, Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ All three verifiers rated it high (PED1-06's reviewer had medium): scrolled mode has a working resume on main, and in v1.1's current default (paged) a reader who returns through a link to a 34-page section always starts on page 1.

**Problem.** On v1.1 (new in P0.4; main has no pager) the pager persists nothing (PagedReaderControls.svelte has no storage writes); its only memory is the #sub-U-p-P hash, which the sidebar, section-nav and search links drop (Sidebar.svelte:200, NavigationButtons.svelte:102 and :119, SearchModal.svelte:138), so a return through a link opens page 1 (§1.2 has 34 flat pages at 1280×800 on the ② preview content). Browser Back does restore the page, because updateHash wrote the hash into that history entry; the loss is on link returns, fresh visits and mode switches. Scrolled mode's continue-reading code still runs in paged mode: beforeNavigate saves window scrollY and a percentage (section +page.svelte:138-145), the next visit inserts a role='alert' prompt, 'Haltu áfram að lesa' / 'Þú varst komin(n) N% í gegnum þennan kafla', at the top of the article when N > 10 (:113-126, :233-268), and 'Halda áfram' scrolls to that stale offset (:155-161) and restores no page. Because every page sits at the same scrollY, N reflects the height of the page shown on leaving, not progress: the code's formula gives 71/74/61% for pages 1–3 of §1.2 at 1280×800 (from measured scrollY 426 and document heights 1404/1377/1504 px; the prompt itself was not run), and the prompt may scroll out of view on arrival and hides itself after 8 s. Switching to 'Samfellt skrun' tears the pager down with restoreAll() and no position handoff (:501-510): from 'Hluti 2 af 3 · Síða 4 af 13' the figure that was in view ended up at y=4738 in a 13,317 px document. The 2px progress bar and the sidebar ring also read window scroll (about 70% on page 1), so in paged mode they are misleading rather than missing.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:113-126 (prompt when the saved percentage > 10), :138-145 (saves scrollY and percentage), :155-161 ('Halda áfram' = window.scrollTo)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:176-190 (position only in the hash, via replaceState), :501-510 (teardown: restoreAll() with no anchor); codemap_v1.1 localStorageKeys[5] ('the pager persists nothing')
- ui-capture_v1.1 layout[14]: after a mode switch from 'Hluti 2 af 3 · Síða 4 af 13', the previously visible figure is at y=4738
- v1.1 meas.section-paged §1.2 desktop pages[0]-[2]: scrollY 426, document heights 1404/1377/1504 px, innerHeight 800, giving 71/74/61% by the code's formula
- main:src/lib/utils/sectionRenames.ts:108-148 (renameInReader moves progress, scrollPositions, bookmarks and the current section; moveKey carries the whole ScrollPosition record)

**Fix.** (1) In paged mode, skip saveScrollPosition and the percentage prompt. (2) Have the pager save the current page's anchor per section inside the existing ScrollPosition record in the reader store, not in a sibling field: main's renameInReader moves whole scrollPositions entries but nothing beside them, so a sibling field would be stranded on a rename. Store a content anchor, never a page index, because splits depend on viewport and font size (§1.2 is 34 flat pages at 1280×800 and 390×844 but 20 at 768×1024). Use the first block's own id, or its nearest enclosing or descendant id, when one exists, and otherwise {unit, itemIndex} (the anchor recompute() already uses, PagedReaderControls.svelte:339-340), since sub-section h2s and the module header often carry no id. Resolve it on return with pageForElement() or pageIndexForItem(). (3) On arrival without a hash, either restore that page or offer it at the content top. The offer copy should reuse positionLabel(), e.g. 'Halda áfram í hluta 2 af 3, síðu 2?' with 'Halda áfram' and 'Byrja á byrjun' (native-speaker check). Either way, the restored page still scrolls to the content top, so ruling 1 holds. (4) In the pager's teardown, when the wrapper is still connected (a mode switch, not a navigation), capture the current page's first block before restoreAll(), and afterwards call scrollIntoView({ block: 'start' }) on it, which honours scroll-padding-top; on the way back to paged, open the page that holds the first block in view. (5) Hide the 2px bar in paged mode or drive it by page (plan:172 says the position label replaces it). The Settings-close jump that also moves a paged reader is a separate bug already on main (findings CODE1-16 and UI1-12), fixed at its cause with focus({ preventScroll: true }) in SettingsModal.close() and SearchModal.close(); do not wire the modal to the pager. UI1-12 also describes this mode-switch loss, so fix it once. PED1-06's find-in-page part is covered by CODE-08.

**Acceptance test.** Playwright at 1280×800 on a paged section with at least two units (found at run time): go to a page past the first in unit 2, follow a sidebar link to another section and come back through the sidebar. The label shows the same unit and the page that holds the same anchor block, either at once or after the offer's 'Halda áfram', whichever [USER] chose, and no element's text contains 'komin(n)' followed by a percentage. From such a page, switch to 'Samfellt skrun': that page's first block stays within 100 px of its previous viewport top. Switch back: the label names the same page and the pager is in view. Unit test: renameInReader carries the stored anchor across a rename. Today the return opens page 1 and the mode switch puts the block thousands of px away.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] chooses whether a return restores the saved page directly or offers it ('Halda áfram' / 'Byrja á byrjun'). Neither option conflicts with ruling 1, because a restored page still scrolls to the content top.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: 'well over 10%' as a measured saved value: the percentages (71/74/61% on §1.2 pages 1–3 at 1280×800) come from measured geometry through the code's formula; the prompt was not run.
- NOT: sighted readers never see the prompt: it renders on afterNavigate and stays until init() finishes (fonts plus up to 1500 ms of image waiting) and the arrival scroll moves it off, and it hides itself after 8 s; a reader may never see it (not verified at runtime).
- NOT: Browser Back loses the page: the hash is in that history entry, so Back restores it; the loss is on link returns, fresh visits and mode switches.
- NOT: a pagedPosition field beside scrollPositions is carried by main's renameInReader: it moves only progress, scrollPositions, bookmarks and the current section; store the anchor inside the ScrollPosition record.
- NOT: the '#sub-U-p-P' hash or a bare page or block index as the saved key: splits depend on viewport and font size, and indices shift when content is re-rendered.
- NOT: the first block's id as the only key: sub-section h2s and the module header often carry no id in the chemistry HTML.
- NOT: a flat-count offer such as 'Halda áfram á síðu 13 af 34?': the label counts pages per sub-section; reuse positionLabel().
- NOT: the bottom label is the only place cue: at 1024 px and wider the sidebar highlights the current section; the window-scroll cues are misleading, not missing.
- NOT: the Settings-close jump is specific to paged mode: it is a focus-restore bug already on main that also hits scrolled mode (CODE1-16, UI1-12).
- NOT: Mangen, Olivier & Velay 2019 shows that paging loses the sense of place: it compared a Kindle with a print book (both paged; 50 adults; a narrative), and its overall location effect was not significant (F(1,48)=1.91); the defect stands on the code without it.

**Source findings:** CODE1-05, UI1-06, PED1-06

### CODE-08. Paged mode hides off-page blocks with display:none in every medium, so 'Prenta kafla' or Ctrl+P prints only the current page and the browser's find-in-page cannot reach the other pages.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1, main · **Also in:** UI, Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ CODE1-03 and UI1-15 were rated high and PED1-12 medium. High fits: this is a regression in v1.1's default mode on the only print path the live site has, since no PDFs are served yet.

**Problem.** On v1.1, paging hides off-page blocks and `<section>` wrappers with the hidden attribute (PagedReaderControls.svelte:141-168); Tailwind preflight forces [hidden] to display:none !important inside @layer base in every medium, app.css's unlayered @media print block (:1749 on) has no override, and nothing in src listens for beforeprint or afterprint. So the toolbar's 'Prenta kafla' (window.print(), section +page.svelte:47-49, :303-310) and Ctrl+P print only the current page, one of §1.2's 34 pages at 1280×800 (② preview content), in v1.1's default mode; the `/print/*` PDF routes do not use the section page and are unaffected, but no PDFs are served on the live site yet. The same display:none keeps the browser's find-in-page from matching text on other pages, and search and subject-index results open a section's first page because they carry no anchor, which is already so on main (SearchModal.svelte:137-140, atridiordasskra/+page.svelte:177-183). A CSS-only un-hide in app.css's print block does not work: on synthetic pages in Chromium 153, preflight's layered !important beat both an unlayered and a later-layer !important override, while an earlier-layer or inline !important, or a screen-only data-attribute rule, did print; the app itself was not printed. Also already on main and outside P0.4: app.css's print block hides every aside (v1.1 :1784-1792), so even a fixed print lacks the section's worked examples and notes (the book has 289 aside.example blocks on the ② preview), and the button says 'Prenta kafla' (chapter) although it prints one section.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:141-168 (toggleAttribute('hidden') on off-page blocks and section wrappers)
- v1.1:node_modules/tailwindcss/preflight.css:396-398 ([hidden]:where(:not([hidden='until-found'])) { display: none !important }, in @layer base); v1.1:src/app.css:1749 on (unlayered print block, no [hidden] rule); grep for beforeprint|afterprint in src: 0 hits (control: window.print at +page.svelte:48)
- print-cascade measurements (two synthetic-page harnesses, Chromium 153, print media): plain [hidden], an unlayered !important and a later-layer !important stay display:none; an earlier-layer (@layer theme) or inline !important prints; a @media screen-only [data-page-off] rule prints
- until-found measurement (synthetic page): an aside.note with padding and border still rendered 52 px under hidden='until-found' (content-visibility: hidden)
- live site: /downloads/efnafraedi-2e/manifest.json answers 200 text/html (the SPA shell), so no PDFs are served; R6-15 (Norway's screen-use committee, fulltext: pupils should read longer informational texts on paper)

**Fix.** Print (S): pick one of two mechanisms; both were verified to print. (a) Events: in PagedReaderControls' init(), add window listeners beforeprint → restoreAll() and afterprint → applyVisibility(), removed through the existing cleanups. The layout observer watches childList only, so the attribute toggles do not trigger a re-split, and the DOM returns to its exact state. (b) Screen-only hiding: have applyVisibility set a pager-owned attribute (data-page-off) instead of hidden, and add @media screen { [data-paged-section] [data-page-off] { display: none !important } } to src/app.css (section pages do not load static/styles/print.css). Print then needs no script, and the e2e selectors that count [hidden] (pagination.spec.ts:139, :430) must change. Either way, add no blanket print override of [hidden]: it would also reveal practiceReveal's hidden self-assessment row (practiceReveal.ts:161, :188). Find-in-page (M; evaluate before committing), one of two options. (i) hidden='until-found' with a beforematch handler that opens the matching page, feature-detected with ('onbeforematch' in document.body) and falling back to plain hidden. Off-page note, example and figure frames must be zeroed (padding, border, margin) in paged mode and the budget re-measured, because the UA's content-visibility: hidden keeps each box. Author rules such as content.css:652's table{display:block} override the UA's display:none, which is all that remains once preflight exempts until-found. Support in iPadOS and iOS Safari is unverified, and (i) needs the hidden attribute, so it pairs with print option (a). (ii) A 'Leita í kaflanum' box (new copy; native-speaker check) that searches the section's text and opens the matching page; this is the safer path on phones. Give search and subject-index results element anchors: resolveHash already handles #id deep links (PagedReaderControls.svelte:252-263), and anchors help scrolled mode too. In the same pass or as a follow-up, fix the print hiding of asides that is already on main, or point readers to the /print PDFs once they are published, and consider whether the label 'Prenta kafla' should say that it prints the section.

**Acceptance test.** Playwright at 1280×800 on a paged section opened on page 1: (1) Under print (dispatch window 'beforeprint' for option (a), or emulateMedia({ media: 'print' }) for option (b)), every block that renders when the same section is shown in scrolled mode under print media has getClientRects().length > 0. Compare with scrolled mode, because app.css's print block hides aside, nav, button and [role=button]. Today only page 1's blocks render. (2) After 'afterprint', or back on screen media, the label and the visible blocks are as before. (3) practiceReveal's hidden self-assessment row is still hidden under print. (4) For the find fix: searching for a phrase that occurs only on the section's last page shows that page and updates the label. Check whether Chromium's page.pdf() fires beforeprint before using it to test option (a); dispatching the events is the reliable test. A manual Ctrl+P preview shows the whole section.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: a CSS-only un-hide in app.css's @media print block: preflight's layered !important beats an unlayered or later-layer rule (measured); only an earlier-layer or inline !important, or a screen-only rule, prints.
- NOT: hidden='until-found' as a drop-in swap: it keeps each element's own box (an aside.note rendered 52 px), so off-page frames show as empty boxes and distort page heights unless they are zeroed.
- NOT: 'browsers without until-found keep today's behaviour': preflight exempts until-found, leaving only the UA's display:none, which author rules such as content.css:652 override; feature-detect and fall back to plain hidden.
- NOT: acceptance by 'every block of article.cnx-module renders under print': app.css's print block hides aside, nav, button and [role=button], so that check fails even after the fix.
- NOT: page.pdf() as the test for the event approach without first checking that it fires beforeprint.

**Source findings:** CODE1-03, UI1-15, PED1-12

### CODE-09. On phones a sideways swipe that scrolls a wide table or equation also turns the page, and by the code pinches and text-selection drags can too.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** UI, Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ Both verifiers raised it from the reviewers' medium to high: in v1.1's default mode (paged; the launch default itself is open call 3), the natural gesture for reading a wide table or equation on a phone misfires on most pages that have one, and the fix is small.

**Problem.** On v1.1 (new in P0.4), handleTouchEnd turns the page whenever |dx| > 60 px and |dy| < 40 px anywhere in the content wrapper (PagedReaderControls.svelte:304-318, passive listeners at :463-468), with no check for a horizontally scrollable ancestor (the keyboard path has one, keyBelongsElsewhere in keyboardShortcuts.ts:125-141), a second finger, or an active text selection. In headless Chromium 153 touch emulation at 390×844, on standalone pages carrying the handler verbatim and the app's table CSS, a 120 px leftward drag that starts inside an overflowing table both scrolled it (scrollLeft 0→193 in one harness, 147 in another) and turned the page; it still turned with 25 px of vertical drift, a vertical swipe and a 40 px drag did not, and a horizontal two-finger spread zoomed the page (visualViewport.scale 5) and fired prev(), because the second finger's touchstart overwrites touchStartX.
