The scope is most pages with tables or equations: at the 326 px phone column (② preview content), 84 of 97 tables overflow (on 48 of the 50 table pages; tables are white-space:nowrap at 640 px and below) and at least 777 of 1,528 display equations overflow (on 94 of 136 pageable pages). The cost is a misfired turn and disorientation, not lost data: a hidden table keeps its scroll offset, so 'Fyrri' brings it back where the reader left it. Not verified on a device or in iOS Safari, and the selection-drag case is unverified and weaker, since native selection handles may not dispatch touch events to the page.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:304-318 (turns on |dx| > 60 and |dy| < 40, no guards), :463-468 (passive touch listeners on the content wrapper)
- v1.1:src/lib/actions/keyboardShortcuts.ts:125-141 (keyBelongsElsewhere checks overflow-x, for keys only)
- v1.1:static/styles/content.css:647-655 (table display:block; overflow-x:auto), :586-592 (div.equation overflow-x:auto); v1.1:src/app.css:1637-1642 (tables white-space:nowrap at max-width 640px)
- swipe harnesses (Chromium 153, 390×844, CDP Input.dispatchTouchEvent): table scrollLeft 0→193 and a page turn with dx −120; a two-finger spread gives visualViewport.scale 5 and prev()
- overflow census at the 326 px column (② preview content): 84 of 97 tables and at least 777 of 1,528 div.equation overflow

**Fix.** In handleTouchStart, record the event target and whether more than one touch is down, with a flag set at touchstart and reset on a fresh single-finger touchstart, so it covers both touchends of a two-finger gesture (event.touches.length > 0 at touchend misses the last finger to lift, so it is only a belt-and-braces check). In handleTouchEnd, ignore the gesture if a second finger was down, if window.getSelection() is not collapsed, or if any element from the target up to the content wrapper has computed overflow-x auto or scroll and scrollWidth > clientWidth. Share only that overflow test with keyBelongsElsewhere, as a small scrollsSideways(el) helper: the touch walk starts at event.target and stops at the wrapper, while the keyboard walk starts at document.activeElement. Optional refinement: let the swipe turn the page when the box is already scrolled to its end in the swipe direction; never turning on a scrollable child is simpler and more predictable. Add a swipe hint (PED1-05) only after this guard lands, or the hint invites the misfire.

**Acceptance test.** Playwright at 390×844 with hasTouch, driving CDP Input.dispatchTouchEvent, on a paged section whose current page holds a table with scrollWidth > clientWidth (found at run time): a 120 px leftward drag that starts inside the table increases its scrollLeft and leaves the position label unchanged; the same drag over a paragraph turns the page; a two-finger gesture leaves the label unchanged. Today the table drag turns the page.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: tables overflow at every width: above 640 px a table overflows only when its intrinsic width exceeds the column.
- NOT: a text-selection drag is shown to turn the page: that case is unverified and weaker, since native selection handles may not dispatch touch events to the page.
- NOT: the student loses the table to the next page: the hidden table keeps its scroll offset, and 'Fyrri' brings it back where it was.
- NOT: ui-capture_v1.1 keyboardAndAria[14] as the swipe capture: it is [15]; [14] is about flashcards.
- NOT: event.touches.length > 0 at touchend as the whole multi-touch guard: it misses the last finger to lift.

**Source findings:** CODE1-09, PED1-11

### CODE-10. In paged mode a second click on the same in-content link does nothing, because the pager rewrites the URL hash behind SvelteKit's back.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ The verifier confirmed the reviewer's high. It is derived from v1.1's source and SvelteKit's client code and was not run in a browser; how many same-page links sit on a different page from their target was not measured.

**Problem.** On v1.1 the pager's updateHash writes page hashes with a raw history.replaceState, so SvelteKit's own record of the URL (current.url, also $page.url) keeps the last hash SvelteKit itself saw. SvelteKit's link handler short-circuits a click whose hash equals that stale value: it calls preventDefault, scrollIntoView() and focus() on the target and no hashchange fires, and in paged mode the target sits on a hidden page, so nothing happens. Sequence: click 'Mynd 1.5' (the pager shows the figure's page), press 'Fyrri' (updateHash rewrites the hash without SvelteKit knowing), click 'Mynd 1.5' again: nothing; the same short-circuit hits a different link to the same target after any page turn. The ② preview content has 977 same-page anchor links in 132 of 251 files, 740 of them to figures, and the e2e test clicks a link only once. Paging exists only on v1.1, so this is not on main.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:176-190 (updateHash: history.replaceState(history.state, '', hash)), :320-327 (only hashchange is handled)
- SvelteKit 2.70.3 client.js in v1.1's node_modules: :2763-2786 (same-hash short-circuit against current.url), :2551-2582 (the kit's shallow replaceState stores PAGE_URL_KEY and leaves page.url unchanged), :2709 (the click handler returns on defaultPrevented)
- Same-page anchor probe (② preview): 977 links in 132 of 251 files; targets: figure 740, table 96, aside 44
- ui-capture v1.1 consoleIssues[1]: SvelteKit's 'Avoid using history.replaceState' dev warning, 48 times, only on paged section pages
- v1.1:e2e/pagination.spec.ts:224-269 (clicks a link once)

**Fix.** Let the pager handle in-content fragment links itself, so SvelteKit's stale current.url no longer matters. In init, add a click listener on the content root (it runs before SvelteKit's documentElement listener, which returns on defaultPrevented) and remove it in teardown. For an a[href^='#'] whose target resolves through elementForHash and pageForElement: call preventDefault, set target, call showPage(idx, { keepHash: true }), write the element hash with history.pushState so Back returns to the link's page, and scroll the element into view after showPage, which by default scrolls to the content top. Settle two mechanics with a runtime test: a raw pushState that copies history.state also copies SvelteKit's history index, so on Back the kit's popstate handler returns early and the pager's own hashchange handler must restore the page; and for a link on the section's first page updateHash writes the bare path and resolveHash('') returns null, so map an empty hash to page 0 or Back stays on the figure's page. Keep updateHash's raw replaceState for page turns, with a comment saying why: the kit's replaceState never updates current.url, so switching to it would only silence the dev warning. Do this in the same change as CODE-16, which rewrites the same hash code.

**Acceptance test.** e2e, extending pagination.spec.ts:224, with the fixture found at run time (a paged section with an in-content link whose target figure is on another page; test.skip if none): click the link and the figure is visible; click 'Fyrri' until the link is visible again; click the same link, and within 2 s the figure is visible and location.hash is '#`<figure id>`'; then page.goBack() shows the link's page again, also for a link on the section's first page when the fixture has one. The second click fails today.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: going to look at a figure and coming back is 'the common case': how many of the 977 links sit on a different page from their target was not measured.
- NOT: pushing the element hash is enough for Back to return to the link's page: for a link on the section's first page the bare path resolves to null, so Back stays on the figure's page unless an empty hash maps to page 0.

**Source findings:** CODE1-04

### CODE-11. One-tap cloze builds its front from raw textContent, so most key-term selections give a defective card: the English gloss left in as a cue, powers of ten flattened, sentences cut short.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P1.3 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.2, v1.1, main · **Also in:** Pedagogy, UI

_Severity note:_ All four verifiers gave high. PED2-01's critical was lowered because P1.3 is unreleased on a stale branch, a null front falls back to the plain flashcard modal, and no data is lost; wrong card text still matters, because cards render as plain text and spaced repetition then repeats it.

**Problem.** On v1.2, the only branch with cloze code (v1.1 and main are the port targets), buildClozeFront is pure and has 6 passing tests, but TextHighlighter.svelte:429-441 feeds it the raw textContent of the nearest P/LI/TD/TH/BLOCKQUOTE/FIGCAPTION/DIV/SECTION (no DT or DD) and :448-451 pass only the selected text, so cloze.ts:25 and :47 blank the first occurrence in the block. SENTENCE_END /[.!?]/ ends a sentence at the 'e.' of the inline '(e. …)' gloss and at '298.000', 'Mynd 1.2' and 'f.Kr.'; raw textContent flattens `<sup>`/`<sub>` (10`<sup>`5`</sup>` becomes '105', so a stored card reads '… sem 2,98 × 105 kg.') and runs structured assistive-MathML token text together, while the 351 lone '×' signs get their only text from that MathML. Measured with the real function on the ② preview content, when the Icelandic term alone is selected (the likelier selection after a port, where the gloss becomes a separate italic span), at most 132 of 848 dfn.term fronts (15.6%) are clean, 534 (63.0%) end in a dangling '(e.', most of which also lose the rest of the sentence, and 181 (21.3%) return null and open the plain modal; selecting the whole bold dfn, 75.5–82% are clean depending on the check and 92 still end in '(e.'. In 15 of 850 selections an earlier occurrence of the term is blanked instead of the selected one. Fixing the sentence split alone leaves the full '(e. exothermic process)' on the front as a cue to the blanked term, and on v1.1 and main the injected .term-en span (shown by default, showTermEnglish true) adds the same cue.

**Evidence.**

- v1.2:src/lib/components/TextHighlighter.svelte:429-441 (blockTextAround returns node.textContent), :448-455 (selection text only; a null front opens the plain flashcard modal)
- v1.2:src/lib/utils/cloze.ts:13 (SENTENCE_END /[.!?]/), :25 (indexOf: first occurrence), :47 (replace: first occurrence)
- dfn probe with the real buildClozeFront over 114 numbered sections (② preview): 848 dfn.term; Icelandic-only selection at most 132 clean, 534 end in '(e.', 181 null; whole-dfn selection 92 still end in '(e.'
- v1.2 browser capture (flashcards-after-cloze): stored fronts 'Breyting sem ______ er kölluð útvermið ferli (e.' and '000 kílógrömm, sem ______ sem 2,98 × 105 kg.'
- sup/sub and math counts (② preview): 894 prose '10`<sup>`n`</sup>`' powers of ten; 172 of 251 pages carry prose `<sup>`, 185 carry `<sub>`; 351 of 1,238 inline-math spans are a lone '×'

**Fix.** Do not port blockTextAround as it is; move the text step into src/lib/utils/cloze.ts. (1) A DOM helper, e.g. clozeContext(range, block) returning { text, start }, that builds the block text with its own walker or from a clone: drop the injected .term-en span (export textAnchor's INJECTED_SELECTOR constant and reuse it) and drop each dfn's inline '(e. …)' gloss with stripEnglishSuffix, moved out of glossaryTerms.ts into a shared util that printGlossary.ts can also import; both strip paths are needed while efni migrates the gloss to data-en. Keep single-token assistive-MathML text such as the lone '×'; for structured math (mfrac, msup, msub, msubsup, munder, mover, msqrt, mtable) render span.math-inline from its data-latex or return null so the modal opens; map `<sup>`/`<sub>` digits and signs in the format [USER] chooses, or return null; treat DT, DD, H1-H6 and CAPTION as blocks; derive the offset from the Range. Never widen textAnchor's shared filter: that shifts contentText offsets and desyncs every saved highlight in a paragraph with math (CLAUDE.md). (2) buildClozeFront(text, start, length) blanks exactly [start, start + length) and rejects selections that start or end inside a word. (3) Segment with Intl.Segmenter('is', { granularity: 'sentence' }), keeping the regex as fallback, and merge after an abbreviation (u.þ.b., t.d., þ.e., m.a., sbr., nr., bls.) only when the next segment starts with a digit, a lowercase letter or an element symbol. (4) A term-first defining sentence 'X (e. Y) er Z' gives '______ er Z' rather than null. (5) Find the deck by a CLOZE_DECK_ID constant instead of its name 'Eyðukort', use `<Icon name='rectangle-ellipsis'>` instead of the inline SVG, and build test fixtures from real chemistry markup. Which selections should become cards at all is a separate pedagogy question; these changes make either answer testable.

**Acceptance test.** cloze.test.ts under jsdom, fixtures copied from real paragraphs, run with both selection models (Icelandic term alone, whole dfn): in 5-1 p#fs-idm61604880, selecting 'útvermið ferli' gives 'Breyting sem losar varma er kölluð ______.' with no English on the front, and selecting 'losar varma' gives the whole sentence with no dangling '(e.' (keeping '(e. exothermic process)' complete or dropping it both pass); in 1-4 p#fs-idm128012432 the front keeps '298.000', the '×' and the exponent ('2,98 × 10⁵ kg', or whitelisted `<sup>` markup) or is null, never '105' or '2,98 10'; in 1-1 p#fs-idp128238864, 'grískir heimspekingar' gives a front starting 'Strax á sjöttu öld f.Kr. ræddu ******'; selecting the second 'vatn' in 'Ís er vatn í föstu formi. Gufa er vatn í gasham.' blanks the second sentence; '… sýnt á Mynd 1.2 og …' stays one sentence; an injected .term-en span leaves the front unchanged. A corpus re-run over the content copy with both selection models: 0 fronts ending in '(e.', 0 blanks inside another word, 0 flattened powers of ten, at most 5% of key-term selections null or seriously defective, and sentence-initial terms give fronts starting with '******'. Today's code fails the corpus check (534 fronts end in '(e.' under the Icelandic-only model).

**Decision for [USER].** Two teaching and format choices, neither one of the four open calls: (1) when the blank is the key term, is its English dropped from the card or moved to the back; (2) are exponents stored as Unicode super- and subscripts (the value is right, but Literata and Atkinson lack most of those characters, so they draw in a fallback face) or as whitelisted `<sup>`/`<sub>` markup (a change to the stored card format). Whether verbatim one-tap cloze ships at all is a separate pedagogy call.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: a fixed 15% (or 21%) clean rate: it depends on what the student selects; selecting the whole bold dfn gives 75.5–82% clean fronts.
- NOT: 490 dangling '(e.' fronts and 160 nulls as the true count: those reproduce under a gloss regex that leaves nested glosses such as '(e. kilogram (kg))' in the needle; proper stripping gives 534 and 181 of 848.
- NOT: a null front means no card: it opens the plain flashcard modal (TextHighlighter.svelte:452-455).
- NOT: every gloss-cut front is unanswerable: 337 of the 490 keep at least six words before the blank; the defect there is the dangling '(e.' and the lost end of the sentence.
- NOT: 152 of 191 defining sentences fail: 196 terms are sentence-initial (153 null), and only 85 of them are followed directly by er/eru/nefnist/kallast/kallað.
- NOT: assistive MathML is the leak to strip: removing it deletes the only text of the 351 lone '×' signs ('2,98 10⁵ kg'); the defects are the 826 structured spans and the `<sup>`/`<sub>` flattening.
- NOT: rejecting .term-en fixes the measured fronts: in the ② preview 496 of 568 paragraphs with dfn[data-en] carry the gloss as inline dfn text, which a span filter does not touch.
- NOT: put 'o.s.frv.' on an unconditional abbreviation merge list: it usually ends a sentence, and the segmenter already keeps it when a lowercase word follows.
- NOT: the 24.9% serious-defect rate for phrase selections as exact: it is 18.9% under a stricter reading ('Mynd' matched 'myndi', and the MathML flag fired on a lone ×).
- NOT: critical severity (PED2-01's reviewer): the verifier gave high.

**Source findings:** CODE2-01, PED2-01, UI2-03, CODE3-09

### CODE-12. Atkinson Hyperlegible would ship without its OFL copyright line in static/fonts/OFL.txt or a row in LICENSES.md, and nothing checks the rule.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P1.4 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.2, v1.1, main · **Also in:** UI, Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ All four verifiers gave high and say the licence lines alone carry it; the precache, Greek-coverage, settings and chrome points the members also raise are low and sit in CODE-30. Legal exposure is low (each file embeds its copyright in name ID 0 and an OFL notice in name ID 13, and main serves /fonts/OFL.txt); the binding gap is the repo's own rule, in a public repo.

**Problem.** Commit 1f40724 (P1.4 on v1.2) adds four Atkinson Hyperlegible woff2 files (the original family, version 1.006; 97,040 B) and touches neither static/fonts/OFL.txt nor LICENSES.md, which arrived on main later (6e66f0c, 2026-07-25) and are identical on v1.1 and main with 0 Atkinson mentions. The pick onto v1.1 is clean and no test, script or workflow reads the licence files, so the port would break LICENSES.md:24-38 ('do not add fonts here without adding their copyright line'; 'Adding a font' steps 1-2) and CLAUDE.md with every check green; that missing guard is pre-existing on main. Upstream (googlefonts/atkinson-hyperlegible) is OFL-1.1 with the line 'Copyright 2020 Braille Institute of America, Inc.' and no Reserved Font Name, so no RFN clause is needed. Besides the new line and row, OFL.txt:17-18 ('The other three fonts here carry no Reserved Font Name') must say four, README.md:234 names the fonts, and CLAUDE.md's licensing-table cell 'OFL-1.1 ×4' becomes ×5; LICENSE §3 says only 'SIL OFL-1.1' and stays accurate.

**Evidence.**

- v1.2: git show --stat 1f40724 (8 files: app.css, SettingsModal, settings.ts, [bookSlug]/+layout.svelte and four woff2; no OFL.txt or LICENSES.md)
- main:static/fonts/LICENSES.md:24-38 ('Obligations', 'Adding a font' steps 1-2); main:static/fonts/OFL.txt:5-8 (four copyright lines), :17-18 ('other three fonts')
- woff2 name-table parse of AtkinsonHyperlegible-Regular.woff2: name 0 'Copyright 2020 Braille Institute of America, Inc.', name 5 'Version 1.006', name 13 a one-line OFL notice plus URL (control: Literata-latin returns its known copyright)
- git grep for OFL.txt, LICENSES.md and static/fonts over main's tests, scripts, e2e, .github and src: 0 hits (control: CLAUDE.md 6)
- R4-09: both Atkinson families are OFL-1.1 with no Reserved Font Name; the Next family's copyright line differs from the original's

**Fix.** In the same commit as the font files, follow LICENSES.md's 'Adding a font' procedure: append the SHIPPED family's name 0 line verbatim to static/fonts/OFL.txt ('Copyright 2020 Braille Institute of America, Inc.' for the original 1.006 on v1.2; if [USER]'s family choice in CODE-30 brings in Next instead, R4-09 gives its line as 'Copyright 2020-2024 The Atkinson Hyperlegible Next Project Authors (https://github.com/googlefonts/atkinson-hyperlegible-next)', so re-read the shipped files' name table and the upstream OFL.txt either way); add a LICENSES.md row (family, the shipped files' glob such as `static/fonts/AtkinsonHyperlegible-*.woff2`, OFL-1.1, no Reserved Font Name, upstream URL); change 'other three' to 'other four' in OFL.txt:17-18; update README.md:234 and CLAUDE.md's 'OFL-1.1 ×4'; leave LICENSE as it is. Add a small guard test so the next font cannot slip through (optional for a one-developer project, but cheap): it lists `static/fonts/*.woff` and `*.woff2`, derives each family from the filename prefix, and asserts a LICENSES.md row whose 'Where' glob matches plus an OFL.txt copyright line for each OFL row, through a family-to-copyright map, because Atkinson's line names the Braille Institute, not the family.

**Acceptance test.** The guard test (e.g. scripts/font-licences.test.js) passes on main's four families, fails on v1.1 with 1f40724 cherry-picked, and passes once the OFL.txt line and the LICENSES.md row are added; as a control it fails again when the Literata line is deleted from OFL.txt. OFL.txt contains the shipped files' name 0 string verbatim, and fc-scan --format '%{family}' on them matches the LICENSES.md row.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: name ID 13 carries the OFL licence text: it is a one-sentence notice plus a URL; name ID 0 carries the copyright.
- NOT: the port would redistribute the fonts 'without their notice': the binaries embed the copyright and an OFL notice and main serves /fonts/OFL.txt, so the gap is the repo's own rule rather than a clear OFL breach.
- NOT: whether Atkinson has a Reserved Font Name is unknown: upstream's OFL.txt has none.
- NOT: LICENSE needs an edit: its §3 says only 'SIL OFL-1.1'.
- NOT: OFL.txt:10-11 ('Most files here are subsets … split by unicode-range') becomes wrong: 8 of 15 files would still be unicode-range subsets.
- NOT: a guard test that reads only LICENSES.md is enough: it cannot catch a missing OFL.txt copyright line.
- NOT: both fonts lack lowercase Greek: Atkinson has π and μ (it lacks α β γ δ ε θ λ ν ρ σ ω), and the Literata subsets have µ (U+00B5); a CODE-30 point.
- NOT: the 82 and 119 pages with missing characters are lowercase Greek: those counts cover every missing character (29 and 38 distinct, including ↩, ⋅ and ⟶); αβγδλνρσω occur on 44 of 251 pages; a CODE-30 point.
- NOT: the studies contradict 'Sumir lesendur kjósa þetta letur': they undercut a group-preference reading, not an existential 'some'; a CODE-30 point.
- NOT: the settings merge needs manual work: 1f40724's settings.ts hunks apply to v1.1 and main without conflict and keep showTermEnglish and readingMode; a CODE-30 point.
- NOT: a stored 'hyperlegible' is silently ignored on a build without it: the store logs a warning and re-persists 'serif', overwriting the choice; a CODE-30 point.
- NOT: the layout's reading-font class turns the controls into Atkinson: they move from Tailwind's ui-serif stack to the body's Literata, because app.css's .font-serif collides with Tailwind's utility; a CODE-30 point.
- NOT: keep Atkinson out of the precache while OpenDyslexic, also opt-in and precached today, stays in: treat both the same, or document the +95 KiB; a CODE-30 point.

**Source findings:** CODE2-07, CODE3-10, UI2-10, PED1-16

### CODE-13. Ported as written, the pre-question renders above v1.1's arrival-scroll target and ends up off-screen in paged mode on most arrivals, on exactly the unread sections it targets.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P1.2 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.2, v1.1 · **Also in:** UI, Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ All three verifiers gave high; UI2-01's critical was lowered because the card is hidden on 6 of 9 measured arrivals, not all, and the position is computed from code and measured heights rather than observed (the port does not exist yet).

**Problem.** v1.2 renders PreQuestionPrompt between the objectives panel and {#key sectionKey} (+page.svelte:422-429), outside the content wrapper that v1.1's pager scrolls to: after fonts, a tick and up to 1.5 s of image waiting, every paged arrival scrolls to put the wrapper's top 96 px below the viewport top (ruling 1), and the cherry-pick of 9b74bb2 onto v1.1 is textually clean, so nothing flags it. Computed from v1.1's measured arrivals: on 6 of 9 section × width arrivals the wrapper lands at 96 px, so the card would end at y≈72 under the 56 px sticky header with its button (≈15–51 px) hidden, and on the other 3 the scroll clamps at the document end (172–217 px), leaving the button visible but the card's heading under the header. A reader who scrolls up to dismiss it removes 198 px (desktop and tablet) or 250 px (phone) of layout and the pager never re-scrolls, so page 1's title can land under the header. Moving the card into the wrapper is not enough: gapAbove is measured once per split and subtracted from every page, applyVisibility never hides a card that sits outside the content units, and the MutationObserver watches only the content root, so nothing re-splits on dismissal. The card also manages no focus (on v1.2 its button is the 57th/58th Tab stop from a fresh load and focus drops to `<body>` after dismissal) and is laid out at the 896 px page-column width instead of the 608 px reading column. P1.2 is not on main.

**Evidence.**

- v1.2:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:422-432 (card before {#key sectionKey} and the content wrapper)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:170-174 (scrollToContentTop: container top − 96), :85-90 and :121-125 (gapAbove in every page's budget), :427-431 (arrival showPage), :483-484 (MutationObserver on the content root, childList only)
- v1.1 arrival measurements: wrapper at 96 px on 6 of 9 section × width arrivals, clamped at 172, 184 and 217 px on the other 3; sticky header 56 px
- v1.2 pre-question capture: card 174 px (desktop, tablet) and 226 px (phone) tall and 896 px wide against a 608 px reading column; focus after dismissal `<body>`; button is Tab stop 58 (desktop) and 57 (phone)
- trial-merges T4c: 9b74bb2 cherry-picks cleanly onto v1.1

**Fix.** Keep ruling 1 and put the card where the arrival scroll lands; each of three options keeps the ruling, because the card sits after the objectives. (a) Smallest change: keep the card in flow above the wrapper but outside the pager's container, so gapAbove and the page budget are untouched; while it is pending, aim the arrival scroll at the card, and when 'Ég hugleiddi þetta — byrja að lesa' is pressed call scrollToContentTop() again so page 1 lands at 96 px, which also removes the jump. (b) In paged mode, show the card in place of page 1 and start the pager only after dismissal (the complete option). (c) An overlay over page 1 inside the reading column, with visibility:hidden on the content behind it (visibility keeps layout for measuring; give the slot a min-height equal to the card's where page 1 is short, as §1.3's page 1 is the title alone). Do not simply move the card into contentWrapper: it would shrink pages 2 onwards by its height and stay above every page unless it is limited to page 1 or dismissed on the first page turn, with a re-split on unmount (export a refresh from the pager). Scrolled mode has no arrival scroll, so its placement can stay; in both modes put the card in the 608 px reading column. Move focus to the card's region (tabindex=-1) when it appears and to the section h1 or the content wrapper after dismissal, and give the card a non-heading title inside its existing role=region with aria-label 'Forspurning' (the objectives panel's 'Námsmarkmið' h3 already precedes the content h1, so fix the heading order above the content in one change). Use `<Icon name='circle-help'>` (main icons.ts:121) instead of the inline SVG. This does not touch open call 4: whatever frequency [USER] chooses decides whether the card shows; this issue decides only where.

**Acceptance test.** Playwright on the port branch at 390×844, 768×1024 and 1280×800, on an unread section with objectives picked at run time for which the pre-question gate returns true under the configured frequency policy (test.skip if none). With readingMode set explicitly to 'paged': once loading settles, the [aria-label='Forspurning'] region lies fully between the header's bottom and innerHeight and its button is visible without scrolling; after clicking 'Ég hugleiddi þetta — byrja að lesa', document.activeElement is inside the content wrapper, the 'Næsta síða' button is inside the viewport, and the page count equals the count for the same section with the card suppressed. With readingMode set explicitly to 'scrolled': the card is no wider than the reading column, and focus lands inside the content wrapper after dismissal. The paged checks fail if the v1.2 placement is ported unchanged.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] picks the paged-mode presentation: (a) scroll to the card and re-scroll to the content on dismissal, (b) the card in place of page 1 with the pager started after dismissal, or (c) an overlay over page 1. Ruling 1 holds under all three. Open call 4 (how often the card shows) is separate and not decided here.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: off-screen on every paged arrival: on 3 of 9 measured arrivals the scroll clamps at the document end (172–217 px) and the button stays visible.
- NOT: observed in a browser: the position and the jump on dismissal are computed from code and measured heights; browser scroll anchoring was not checked.
- NOT: rendering the card as the first child of contentWrapper and re-splitting on unmount is enough: while it is shown it shrinks pages 2 onwards, and applyVisibility never hides it.
- NOT: an h2 fixes the heading order: it would still precede the section h1, and the objectives' 'Námsmarkmið' h3 already does.
- NOT: inert is needed on top of visibility:hidden in the overlay option.
- NOT: never pose an item on an introduction page: that filter decides part of open call 4 (it removes 'first section per chapter' if that means the x.0 intro page).
- NOT: any frequency limit needs persisted per-section state: read progress or one global date covers 'first section per chapter' or 'per day'; only show-once or show-N-times rules need it.
- NOT: the code contradicts its 'first visits only' comment: the comment itself glosses it as 'already-read sections skip straight to the text'; the card returns on each visit until the section is marked read.
- NOT: 'circle-help' is at icons.ts:119 (it is :121; :119 is 'timer').

Porting P1.1 also needs 'grid-2x2' instead of 'scale' (not an IconName; the registry reserves grid-2x2 for calibration), c493bd2's single-source TabId (v1.1 and main declare TabId twice) and a tree with P0.3's predictedKnown (0 occurrences on main). As written, P1.1's flashcard half also shows all books under one book's /greining (review entries carry only cardId; latent while only chemistry is published), and after the merge its per-section half compares objective confidence with inline self-grades.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:vitest.config.ts:7-8 (`src/**/*.test.ts` under jsdom), :23-26 (coverage include `src/lib/**/*.ts`); no @testing-library/svelte in package.json
- main:src/lib/components/icons.ts:117 ('grid-2x2'; no 'scale'); main:docs/design/icon-handback-2026-06.md:73-76 (grid-2x2 reserved for calibration)

For P1.1, use { id: 'kvordun', label: 'Kvörðun', icon: 'grid-2x2' }, re-apply c493bd2's single-source TabId, and land it only on a tree with P0.3. Add one Playwright test per feature with fixtures derived from `static/content/*/toc.json` at run time (CLAUDE.md: e2e gating fixtures are derived, never hardcoded), since CI syncs efni main fresh. The two P1.1 data-scope choices in the decision note need [USER]; the module serves any of the answers, and also the research's alternative design (an item list instead of a headline percentage).

### CODE-15. Paged arrival downloads every figure in the section, and waits up to 1.5 s for them before the first page, because content images carry no width or height.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** needs efni first · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ Both verifiers kept medium: it costs bandwidth and up to 1.5 s before page 1, not correctness, and a reader who finishes the section downloads the same images lazily in scrolled mode. It weighs most for phone readers on mobile data.

**Problem.** Before the first split, v1.1's loadImages sets loading='eager' on every `<img>` in the section and waits up to IMAGE_WAIT_MS (1,500 ms) for decode(); page 1 then appears while the remaining downloads continue, and scrolled mode stays lazy. It does so because 0 of the 1,148 content `<img>` in the ② preview content carry width or height, so an unloaded image measures about 0 px and a lazy image on a hidden page never loads. Over the pages paged mode serves (114 numbered sections, 21 introductions and the preface), an arrival downloads a median of about 1.9–2.0 MB of figures, p90 about 7.8–7.9 MB and up to 25.1 MB (§10.6, 24 images), and 117 of the 118 figures over 1 MiB sit on those pages; the extra cost against scrolled mode is the figures on pages never reached plus the wait. After the main merge, #260's 1 MiB size gate keeps those large figures out of the browsing cache, and one 24-figure section fills a quarter of its 100 entries. The root cause is the content markup, which vefur reported to efni on 2026-10-04; the eager load is vefur's interim, and main, which lazy-loads, is not affected.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:70 (IMAGE_WAIT_MS 1500), :372-379 (loadImages: eager, decode, race), :410-415
- img scan (② preview): 1,148 `<img>`, 0 with width=, 0 with height=, 1,148 with loading='lazy'; 1,147 unique files, 118 over 1 MiB
- image bytes per paged page (② preview): median 1.91–1.97 MB, p90 7.80–7.88 MB, max 25.1 MB (§10.6, 24 images)
- main:docs/handoffs/2026-10-04-assistive-mathml-and-image-dimensions-for-efni.md §2 ('Content images carry no width or height')
- main:src/lib/sw/runtimeCaching.ts (#260: figures StaleWhileRevalidate, 100 entries, 1 MiB gate); v1.1 does not contain 99e13b4

**Fix.** Fix at the source: efni emits intrinsic width and height on every content `<img>` (see the efni note). In vefur, make the pager dimension-aware now, so the code is ready before such content arrives and changes nothing on today's content: an image that carries width and height keeps loading='lazy' and skips the decode() wait (with the existing max-width:100%; height:auto its box is already right), only images without them keep today's eager-plus-1.5 s path, and once boxes are known only the current page ±1 loads eagerly. Optional interim that cuts bytes on today's content, inside the pager's own measuring rather than in the content: split and eager-load one sub-section at a time and prefetch the next, as the plan's 'lazy on visit' (plan :202) describes; the label already counts pages per sub-section, and a deep link into a later sub-section splits that one first. Waiting for naturalWidth > 0 instead of decode() only shortens the wait; loading='eager' still fetches every figure. The per-section cost is evidence for open call 3 (launch default), not a ruling on it.

**Acceptance test.** efni: over the synced chapters, the number of `<img>` tags carrying width and height equals the `<img>` count (1,148 in the ② preview). vefur, unit: the pager's image-load helper leaves an `<img>` that has width and height lazy and sets eager only on images without them. vefur, e2e (with dimensions added to the served HTML through page.route until efni's markup is synced): on a paged arrival at 390×844 of the most image-heavy section, picked at run time, the request log shows image requests only for the current and next page, and the pager label does not change after all images load (pagination.spec.ts 'the page count does not change while a page is read'). Today every figure in the section is requested.

**efni side.** Already asked in the 2026-10-04 handoff (§2): emit intrinsic width and height on every content `<img>` in the renderer (the handoff names tools/cnxml-render.js and tools/lib/cnxml-elements.js), taking an SVG figure's size from its width, height or viewBox, and report the count check with the re-render. Students benefit only once content with dimensions is synced, and the chemistry sync is held, so vefur's dimension-aware code should land first and do nothing until then.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: a median 1.47 MB / p90 6.74 MB per section: those cover all 161 HTML files with images, including rollups, answer keys and appendices that never page; over paged pages it is about 1.9–2.0 MB / 7.8–7.9 MB.
- NOT: every figure is decoded before the first page: the wait is capped at 1,500 ms, then page 1 shows while downloads continue.
- NOT: on v1.1 the large figures never enter the browsing cache: that 1 MiB gate is #260 on main, so it applies only after the merge.
- NOT: waiting for naturalWidth > 0 instead of decode() cuts the download: it cuts only the wait.

**Source findings:** CODE1-13, PED1-10

### CODE-16. Page hashes past a unit's first page encode a viewport-dependent index, so a shared or saved link opens a different page on another device, and an unknown page falls back to the section's first page.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ The verifier confirmed the reviewer's medium: shared and reloaded links open the wrong page, but nothing is lost, and #sub-U and the bare section URL resolve the same on every device.

**Problem.** v1.1 addresses pages as #sub-U-p-P, and for P ≥ 1 that index depends on the viewport and the font size: §1.2's unit 2 is 12 pages on a phone and 7 on a tablet, and 'Stórt' turns 'Síða 3 af 11' into 'af 12' (#sub-U for a unit's first page resolves everywhere, and unit 0 page 0 writes no hash). When page (U, P) does not exist, resolveHash returns null and init opens fromHash ?? 0, the first page of the whole section rather than of unit U. 'Deila kafla' copies window.location.href, hash included, so a link shared from a phone opens a different page on another device, or the section's first page when that page number does not exist there; a reload after a font-size change behaves the same. Paging exists only on v1.1, so this is not on main.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:176-190 (updateHash), :252-263 (resolveHash returns null with no fallback), :425-431 (showPage(fromHash ?? 0))
- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:52-59 (handleShare shares window.location.href)
- v1.1 page counts: §1.2 phone 12/12/10 vs tablet 6/7/7 pages per unit; desktop 'Stórt': 'Síða 3 af 11' → 'Síða 3 af 12'
- block-id scan (② preview): 4,174 of 4,742 pageable blocks (88%) in 136 paged pages carry a CNXML id

**Fix.** First the cheap, clearly right part: clamp P to unit U's page count and fall back to unit U's first page, never the section's page 0. Then make the address stable: write the page's first id-bearing block as the hash (88% of pageable blocks carry a CNXML id, and resolveHash already resolves element ids through elementForHash and pageForElement while recompute keeps them on screen), which survives viewport, font-size and re-render changes; or #sub-U-b-K with K the block's index in the unit, resolved by pageIndexForItem, which survives viewport and font-size changes but not an efni re-render. Keep accepting #sub-U-p-P for old links, move hash parse, format and resolve into paginate.ts or a sibling pager.ts as pure functions (see CODE-18), and update e2e/pagination.spec.ts:58, which asserts today's format. Consider also having 'Deila kafla' share the page-free section URL, since its label promises the section; that complements the hash fix, because a reload or a URL copied from the address bar still carries the hash. Do this in the same change as CODE-10, which touches the same hash code.

**Acceptance test.** Unit tests on the pure resolver with unit 2 at 7 pages: '#sub-2-p-10' resolves into unit 2 (clamped to its last page), and the stable form resolves to the page that holds its block under any split. e2e: opening `<section>`#sub-1-p-99 shows a label that starts 'Hluti 2 af'; a hash written at 390×844 for a page past a unit's first page, reopened at 1280×800, shows that page's first block. The clamp test and both e2e checks fail today.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: every page hash depends on the viewport: #sub-U (a unit's first page) resolves on every device and unit 0 page 0 writes no hash; only -p-P with P ≥ 1 does.

**Source findings:** CODE1-08

### CODE-17. The ←/→ step-aside and read detection key on the readingMode setting rather than on the pager's actual state, so → is dead after the last page completes, and after a pager failure keys and read marking misbehave.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** after release · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1

_Severity note:_ The verifier kept medium. Only the dead → after completion happens in normal use, and it is a keyboard-efficiency gap, not a WCAG 2.1.1 failure (Tab still reaches the section links); the failure cases need an exception, and the mode-switch case needs a switch inside the ~0.1–1.5 s init window.

**Problem.** On v1.1 the section page sets data-paged-section from the setting (paged || undefined, +page.svelte:421) and enables readDetection with !isRead && !paged (:451), and neither follows what the pager is doing. In normal use, after 'Næsta' completes the section on the last page, → does nothing and the global section shortcut still steps aside, so the documented → 'Næsti kafli' breaks exactly where a reader would use it. After an init failure the section scrolls, but ←/→ change neither page nor section and reaching the end never marks it read. After a recompute failure the window keydown, hashchange and touch listeners stay attached and the handlers (:287-327) check neither ready nor failed, so the next →, Space or swipe re-hides blocks with no controls on screen, or throws a TypeError in applyVisibility if computePages threw (a forced-throw run reproduced both; see CODE-18); and a switch to 'Samfellt skrun' during init's wait leaves a destroyed instance hiding blocks and attaching listeners its teardown never removes. The failure and mode-switch cases need an exception or a narrow timing window, and paging exists only on v1.1, so none of this is on main.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:421 (data-paged-section={paged || undefined}), :451 (readDetection enabled: !isRead && !paged)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:287-327 (handlers without ready/failed checks), :366-370 (fail() keeps listeners, units and flatPages), :432-485 (listeners attached after await tick() with no guard)
- v1.1:src/lib/actions/keyboardShortcuts.ts:156 (steps aside whenever [data-paged-section] exists), :275-347 (the global handler ignores defaultPrevented)
- forced-throw jsdom mount: after a recompute() throw, ArrowRight leaves 4 [hidden] blocks, no nav and a rewritten '#sub-0-p-2'; a throw inside computePages makes ArrowRight throw from applyVisibility

**Fix.** Use one AbortController per $effect run: pass { signal } to every addEventListener, hook the settings unsubscribe and the observer disconnect to its abort, check signal.aborted after every await, and abort in both the teardown and fail(); fail() also clears units and flatPages, and next()/prev() return unless ready && !failed. That fixes the recompute-failure and mode-switch cases, and CODE-10's new click listener should use the same signal. Have the pager report its state (for example a bindable 'pending' | 'ready' | 'failed' | 'complete'), drop data-paged-section only on 'failed', and enable readDetection when !paged || state === 'failed'. For the dead →, keep data-paged-section and ruling 3: once the pager is on its last page and completion has fired, have it forward → to the next-section link itself (or let the global shortcut step aside only for keys the pager can still use, via canNext/canPrev), and return the state to 'ready' when the reader pages back. Do not remove data-paged-section on 'complete': the global listener ignores defaultPrevented, so ← would then leave the section instead of going back a page.

**Acceptance test.** e2e in paged mode: (1) forced failure (route the section HTML to an empty article): no [data-paged-section], scrolling to the end marks the section read, and → changes section; (2) on the last page 'Næsta' completes the section, then, with focus moved out of the recall prompt, → goes to the next section, while ← after completion still shows the previous page; (3) with images delayed 3 s, switch to 'Samfellt skrun' 300 ms after arrival: after 5 s, .reading-content [hidden] counts 0. Unit (jsdom mount): after a forced recompute() throw, ArrowRight leaves no [hidden] element. Checks (1), (3), the → part of (2) and the unit check fail today.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: after any failure ←/→ neither turn pages nor change section: that holds for init failures; after a recompute failure the listeners stay attached and a key re-hides blocks.
- NOT: :402 and :415 are the only guards: recompute (:337) and calibrate (:384) check ready/failed too; it is the event handlers (:287-327) that lack the check.
- NOT: after a recompute failure the next key cleanly hides the page again: if computePages threw, it hides earlier units' wrappers and throws a TypeError in applyVisibility.
- NOT: removing data-paged-section on 'complete' safely frees → for the next section: it frees both keys, and ← would leave the section.
- NOT: the dead → after completion is a WCAG 2.1.1 failure: Tab still reaches the section links, and the recall prompt takes focus on the first completion.
- NOT: props.js:360-363 is the code that runs: the compiler inlines $$props.container (the conclusion is the same).

**Source findings:** CODE1-11

### CODE-18. PagedReaderControls keeps the pager's pure decisions (page budget, hash codec, key and swipe rules, step logic) inside about 600 untested component lines.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** after release · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1

_Severity note:_ Both verifiers kept medium: it has no user-visible defect of its own, but open call 1 (the page budget) and ruling 3 ('Næsta' finishes) live in this untested code, and two e2e tests are weaker than they look.

**Problem.** v1.1's PagedReaderControls.svelte (602 lines, 0% unit coverage, like RecallPrompt; keyboardShortcuts 21.5%) holds pure logic that no unit test reaches: the budget constants and floor (SCROLL_OFFSET 96, repeated as a literal at :172; BOTTOM_CLEARANCE 76; gapBelow 150; floor 240), the hash codec split between updateHash and resolveHash, the position label written twice, the key, swipe and touch-resize rules, the next/prev/complete state machine that carries ruling 3, and an isTyping that detects contenteditable differently from keyboardShortcuts.ts. The 19 pagination e2e tests (about 4.2 min of CI) cover part of it, and the unit suite killed 15 of 40 mutants. The page-count e2e (pagination.spec.ts:200-222) would fail on a correct app for any fixture of 40 or fewer pages, because 'Næsta' on the last page completes the section and the label never changes; it passes only because its derived fixture (20-1-vetniskolefni, 42 images) is deduced to have at least 41 pages at 1280×720, which a larger page budget could change. 'Every page fits' compares with innerHeight rather than the budget and exempts single-block pages, and in paginate.ts the atomic flag changes no split for positive heights (0 of 200,000 random inputs; 4,332 differ once 0-px blocks are allowed) while the <= vs < budget boundary is untested. Of the open calls only the page budget is an edit here (availableHeight :85-90); paging exists only on v1.1, not on main.

**Evidence.**

- health v1.1: PagedReaderControls 0%, RecallPrompt 0%, keyboardShortcuts 21.5% lines; 15 of 40 mutants killed; 19 pagination e2e tests take about 4.2 min of CI
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:63-90 (budget constants, floor 240), :172 (literal 96), :176-190 and :252-263 (hash codec), :192-196 and :527 (label twice), :214-250 (state machine), :102-109 vs v1.1:src/lib/actions/keyboardShortcuts.ts:108-116 (two isTyping rules)
- v1.1:e2e/pagination.spec.ts:200-222 (page-count loop: 40 iterations, breaks only if 'Næsta' is disabled), :74-111 (compares with innerHeight; single-block pages exempt)
- paginate differential: atomic branch disabled, 0 of 200,000 positive-height inputs differ (4,332 with 0-px blocks); all 10 paginate unit-test inputs give identical output under the no-atomic and <= → < mutants

**Fix.** Move the DOM-free decisions into src/lib/utils/paginate.ts or a sibling pager.ts as pure functions with Vitest tests, in the branch's own idiom (paginate.ts's header: pure logic kept testable; no component-test library). Start with the pieces that carry open call 1 and ruling 3: PAGE_BUDGET constants with pageBudget(innerHeight, gapAbove, gapBelow), the hash codec (encodePageHash/decodePageHash, landing with CODE-16's clamp and stable form if that is done first; the component turns (0,0) → '' into writing pathname+search), pageTurnForKey (today's key mapping, unchanged) and stepNext/stepPrev returning their events; swipeDirection and shouldResplit are optional. Use one exported isTyping with the isContentEditable rule and replace the literal 96 with the constant; in the component, fail() runs the cleanups and clears units and flatPages, and next()/prev() return unless ready && !failed (with CODE-17). In e2e, break the page-count loop when the label reads 'Hluti M af M · Síða N af N', and compare page heights with the budget exposed on a data attribute. A key rule for oversized pages would be new behaviour and is a separate decision. The budget's value stays [USER]'s call; if open call 1 changes it before release, do the PAGE_BUDGET extraction and the page-count e2e fix first, in the same change, so the new value is one constant edit with a test.

- NOT: recall per sub-section (open call 2) is an edit in this component: the pager already emits onsubsectioncomplete; the missing step is the section page passing it, plus RecallPrompt and recall-store changes.

### CODE-19. The pagination e2e depends on the launch default and has loops and guards that can pass vacuously or time out on a correct app.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1

_Severity note:_ Medium, as the verifier kept it: a test-suite risk, not a user-facing defect. It turns acute when [USER]'s launch-default call (open call 3) is applied: under a 'scrolled' default three tests pass vacuously and most of the others go red.

**Problem.** On v1.1, e2e/pagination.spec.ts never seeds readingMode 'paged' (its only readingMode write is 'scrolled', at :125), so all 19 tests ride on the launch default at settings.ts:77, which is open call 3: under a 'scrolled' default the three rollup tests (:418-441) would pass vacuously and most of the others would go red. The settings-seed snippet is copied four times across three specs. Three checks are unsound: the 'Section fits a single page' skip (:54) can never fire, because Næsta is disabled only after completion (PagedReaderControls.svelte:534); 'every page of a section fits the viewport' compares visible height with window.innerHeight (:99, :102) instead of the page budget, so a budget overrun stays green; and 'the page count does not change while a page is read' would time out on reaching the last page, where clicking Næsta completes the section and the label cannot change, so it passes only while its fixture runs past the 40-page cap (an estimate, not measured). openFirstSection's isVisible({ timeout: 10000 }) checks return at once (Playwright 1.63 ignores the option), so a slow journey becomes a skip instead of a failure, and Playwright's retries: 2 in CI hides flakes. The spec is v1.1's own; main is not affected.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:e2e/pagination.spec.ts:125 (the only readingMode write, 'scrolled'), :54 (single-page skip that cannot fire), :99 and :102 (fits test compares with innerHeight), :212-220 (page-count loop with a 40 cap)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:534 (Næsta disabled only on the last page after completion has fired)
- Four settings-seed copies: v1.1:e2e/section-shortcuts.spec.ts:13-23, e2e/phone-layout.spec.ts:22-30, e2e/pagination.spec.ts:122-127 and :363-369
- v1.1:playwright.config.ts:7 (retries: 2 in CI); Playwright 1.63 types: Locator.isVisible's timeout option is ignored and the call returns immediately
- health_v1.1 mutation 37 (readingMode default flipped to 'scrolled') survives the unit suite; that the e2e would catch it was not verified by a run

**Fix.** (1) Add e2e/helpers/settings.ts with seedSettings(target: Page | BrowserContext, patch) and use it at all four seed sites. (2) In pagination.spec's beforeEach, seed { readingMode: 'paged' } through that helper, and have the continuous-scroll test seed 'scrolled' through the same helper: an addInitScript seed re-runs on reload and would otherwise overwrite that test's page.evaluate write (:122-128). (3) Add exactly one test, 'the default reading mode is …', and write its expected value only when [USER] makes the launch call (open call 3); until then no test pins either answer. Playwright cannot import the store, so this one test carries a literal by design. (4) Replace openFirstSection with page.goto(`sectionsContaining('<p', N)[0]`) in most tests, but keep one client-side click-through test that uses waiting expects (toBeVisible, toHaveURL) instead of isVisible plus skip: it is the only test of the component-reuse path the CLAUDE.md reactivity pitfalls warn about. (5) Expose the computed budget on the nav (for example data-page-budget) and assert on every page without an allowed overrun that visible height ≤ budget and nav bottom ≤ innerHeight. (6) End page loops when the label reads the last unit's last page ('Hluti M af M · Síða N af N') instead of on isDisabled(); pick a fixture of moderate length and keep a cap above its page count that fails the test when reached, since an uncapped loop at 1.2 s per page adds about a minute on an 80+ page section. (7) Remove the :54 guard, or pick a fixture with at least two pages and assert that it has them. Land steps 1-3 no later than the commit that applies [USER]'s launch-default choice.

**Acceptance test.** With the default at settings.ts:77 temporarily flipped to the other value, pagination.spec stays green apart from the single default test (today the rollup tests pass vacuously and most others fail). A mutant that adds 200 px to availableHeight() fails the rewritten fits test (today's test passes it, because content stays below innerHeight). No pagination test calls test.skip on a navigation helper's result, the click-through test fails instead of skipping when the journey breaks, and the page-count test ends on the last page's label without timing out.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the navigation race that #249 removed from main is unmitigated here. openFirstSection already waits on toHaveURL after each click (:24, :30); what remains is that its isVisible({ timeout }) checks do not wait and a false return becomes a skip.
- NOT: re-implementing the block flattening in page.evaluate is a fragility. It is a deliberate independent oracle (:71-73); the defect is only the comparison with innerHeight.
- NOT: the page-count fixture was measured at over 40 pages. That is an estimate (78 pageable blocks in the local preview copy), and the CI fixture may differ.
- NOT: the seeds sit at section-shortcuts.spec.ts:13-21 and phone-layout.spec.ts:22-31. They are at :13-23 and :22-30.

**Source findings:** CODE3-14

### CODE-20. 25 of 40 mutants survive on v1.1, and each survivor maps to a specific missing test in paginate, the recall store, flashcards, settings or the keyboard guard.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1, main

_Severity note:_ Medium, as reviewer and verifier agree: no user-visible defect today, but regressions in the paging rules, in the load path of students' own recall text and in the settings validators would ship green.

**Problem.** On v1.1, 25 of 40 mutants survived the unit suite (15 killed), and each survivor maps to a missing test. paginate.ts has 100% line coverage, yet its exact-fit boundary (:95), its first-item rule (exposed only by a heading taller than the page), the contents of ATOMIC_SELECTOR and KEEP_WITH_NEXT_SELECTOR (no unit test uses them) and the atomic branch are unpinned; the atomic flag changes no split for heights of 1 px or more only because the two selectors are disjoint. recall.ts has no test for load, validation, the 500 cap, trimming, the chapter match or cross-tab sync; flashcard.ts is never asked to record a prediction of false; settings.test.ts covers the theme, font and sidebar defaults but not readingMode (:77) or lineWidth (:72), and never loads an invalid stored value; and the keyboard step-aside guard is exercised only by e2e, the isTyping guard not at all. vitest's retry: 2 (vitest.config.ts:22) can hide flakes, although the suite passed 730/730 with retry 0 in two independent runs. The mutated code is v1.1's own except the isTyping guard (main keyboardShortcuts.ts:304) and retry: 2, which are already on main.

**Evidence.**

- health_v1.1 mutations: 15 of 40 killed. Survivors: 4 (exact fit, paginate.ts:95), 5-6 (atomic branch: 0 of 200,000 random inputs differ), 13-15 (selectors), 17 (first-item rule), 19-21 and 23-27 (recall), 29 and 31 (flashcard), 32-36 (keyboard), 37-39 (settings)
- health_v1.1 coverage: paginate.ts at 100% of lines while 7 of its mutants survive
- v1.1:src/lib/stores/settings.test.ts:32-47 (theme, fontSize, fontFamily, sidebar defaults only), :172-196 (load test feeds only valid values); settings.ts:64 (defaultSettings is module-private), :72, :77
- v1.1 and main: vitest.config.ts:22 (retry: 2); the full v1.1 suite passed 730/730 with retry 0 in two independent runs
- main:src/lib/actions/keyboardShortcuts.ts:304 (the isTyping guard predates v1.1 and is untested at every level)

**Fix.** Order: the paginate, recall and settings tests first (they guard code the budget and launch-default calls will tune, and the load path of students' own text); the keyboard tests and retry: 0 can follow. paginate.test.ts: 'blocks that sum exactly to the budget share one page' (kills 4); a heading taller than the page followed by a block shares one page (17); and, if the atomic flag stays, a block whose height equals the budget (6). Export classifyBlock(el) from paginate.ts and test it on a DOM fixture with figure, table, .example, h2, h3, h4 and p (13-15). Then either delete the inert atomic flag (nothing is lost: its only effect today is a page holding a 0 px block) and rename the three tests named after it, or give it a meaning with a test that fails without it. The plan gives it one (oversized figures and tables scroll internally on their own page, plan :165, :222-223); which fits depends on [USER]'s QA-E verdict on oversized blocks, which this entry leaves open. recall.test.ts: loads valid entries stored before import; replaces a non-array entries value with []; keeps only the newest 500 (add a 501st); stores trimmed text; matches chapter as well as section (the same slug in chapters 01 and 02); applies another tab's write (dispatch new StorageEvent('storage', { key, newValue })). flashcard.test.ts: records a prediction of false (29); for 31, assert that the key is absent when no prediction is given, or accept the mutant as equivalent under JSON. settings.test.ts: export defaultSettings; check that a stored 'scrolled' and a stored 'paged' both survive load (38); check that an unknown stored readingMode loads as defaultSettings.readingMode, compared with the exported value and never with a literal, because the literal is open call 3; and check that a fresh profile gets lineWidth 'narrow' (39; P0.1's shipped default, not an open call). Mutant 37 (flipping the launch default) is killed only by the single default test CODE-19 adds once [USER] decides. keyboardShortcuts.test.ts: mount the action on a node in jsdom and cover four cases (32-36): with [data-paged-section] present, ArrowRight neither clicks [data-nav=next] nor prevents the default; without it, ArrowRight clicks and prevents the default; nextSection rebound to 'w' still navigates on a paged page; a key typed in a textarea does nothing. Finally set vitest retry to 0 as its own commit, after repeated local runs and one CI run, watching for the vi.resetModules timing flake the config comment cites (CODE-21 leaves retry to this entry).

**Acceptance test.** Re-running the 40 health-agent mutations (same targets) on the changed tree kills every one of today's 25 survivors except mutant 37 (flipping the launch default, killed only once CODE-19's default test encodes [USER]'s call), mutant 31 if it is accepted as equivalent, and any mutant on code that was deliberately deleted (the atomic flag, if deleted). With retry 0, npm test passes in repeated local runs and in CI.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: settings.ts has no default or validator tests. settings.test.ts:32-47 tests the theme, fontSize, fontFamily and sidebar defaults; what is missing is the readingMode and lineWidth defaults and any invalid stored value.
- NOT: the atomic flag changes no page break for any input with heights of 1 px or more. That holds only because ATOMIC_SELECTOR and KEEP_WITH_NEXT_SELECTOR are disjoint; an item carrying both flags changes the split.
- NOT: the originally listed tests kill every survivor. Mutant 17 needs a heading taller than the page followed by a block, mutant 38 needs 'a stored scrolled is kept', mutant 6 needs a height equal to the budget if the flag stays, and mutant 31 is equivalent under JSON.
- NOT: a test that an unknown readingMode falls back to 'paged'. That literal asserts the launch default (open call 3); compare with the exported default instead.

### CODE-21. No test mounts a Svelte component and coverage excludes every .svelte file, though two config lines and a small setup stub (no new dependency) would enable them; the first such test exposes RecallPrompt's uncleared timer.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1, v1.2, main

_Severity note:_ Medium: the verifier lowered it from high because it is test infrastructure, not a release blocker. It is staged before release because it is small, fixes a real P0.2 timer bug, and the acceptance tests of CODE-23 and CODE-24 rely on it.

**Problem.** No test on any branch mounts a Svelte component (the two test files under src/lib/components cover .ts helpers), and vitest.config.ts:26 limits coverage to '`src/lib/**/*.ts`' on v1.1, v1.2 and main, so npm run test:coverage never lists a .svelte file and the 0% rows for RecallPrompt, PagedReaderControls, CalibrationTab and PreQuestionPrompt are invisible in the project's own report. Measured in a scratch copy: Svelte 5's own mount() works in the existing vitest and jsdom setup once the test config resolves the 'browser' export condition and Element.prototype.animate is stubbed (RecallPrompt's transition:fly needs it), with no new dependency; the full v1.1 suite still passed 730/730 under that configuration, twice, and the same mount works on v1.2's toolchain. Widening coverage.include lists most .svelte files; five fail to parse and are excluded (PWAUpdater, SearchModal, MobileBottomNav, Header, Sidebar). The first proposed test exposes a small real bug: RecallPrompt.svelte:33 schedules onclose 1200 ms after saving and never clears the timer, so onclose fires even after the prompt has unmounted (the 'not after unmount' case failed: called 1 time).

**Evidence.**

- v1.1, v1.2 and main: vitest.config.ts:26 (coverage include '`src/lib/**/*.ts`'); no test file mounts a .svelte component (bookCover.test.ts and icons.test.ts test .ts helpers)
- Scratch mount test: 'lifecycle_function_unavailable' with today's config, then 'element.animate is not a function' after adding resolve.conditions ['browser'], then a pass with an animate stub; the full v1.1 suite (46 files) passed 730/730 under that config with retry 0, twice
- v1.1:src/lib/components/RecallPrompt.svelte:29 (blank guard), :33 (setTimeout(() => onclose?.(), 1200), never cleared), :87 (disabled={!text.trim()})
- Coverage with '`src/lib/**/*.{ts,svelte}`': .svelte rows appear (RecallPrompt, PagedReaderControls); PWAUpdater, SearchModal, MobileBottomNav, Header and Sidebar fail to parse and are excluded

**Fix.** In vitest.config.ts (test-only; the app build is unaffected) add resolve: { conditions: ['browser'] } and set coverage.include to ['`src/lib/**/*.{ts,svelte}`']; leave retry alone here (CODE-20 lands retry: 0 as a separate commit). In src/test/setup.ts add a small Element.prototype.animate stub that returns { cancel(){}, finish(){}, onfinish: null } and fires onfinish on a timer. First test file: src/lib/components/RecallPrompt.test.ts, with five cases: blank text keeps 'Vista upprifjun' disabled; Ctrl+Enter on blank text shows no 'Vistað' confirmation and calls no onclose (the only case that kills removing the :29 guard, since addEntry also trims and returns on blank); Ctrl+Enter with text saves exactly once; 'Sleppa' calls onclose without saving; onclose fires 1200 ms after saving (fake timers) and not after unmount. In the same change, clear the timer on unmount (an $effect cleanup or onDestroy). If the auto-dismiss itself is later removed (the compare-step proposal in R2's design implications), the timer and its test case go with it. Keep rules in pure modules (CODE-22, the pager model in CODE3-11's entry, and the P1 extraction in CODE-14) and use component tests for wiring only.

**Acceptance test.** npm test passes with the new config. RecallPrompt.test.ts fails if `if (!text.trim()) return;` (RecallPrompt.svelte:29) or `disabled={!text.trim()}` (:87) is removed, and its unmount case fails on today's component and passes once the timer is cleared. npm run test:coverage lists RecallPrompt.svelte and PagedReaderControls.svelte (today it lists no .svelte file).

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the gather agents called the logic 'untestable as written' because @testing-library is absent. That quote (health_v1.2) attributes it to logic sitting inline in $derived.
- NOT: widening coverage.include surfaces every .svelte file. Five fail to parse and are excluded.
- NOT: the four originally proposed cases kill removing RecallPrompt.svelte:29's blank guard. Only a blank Ctrl+Enter case does, because addEntry also trims and returns on blank text.
- NOT: npm test passes with the new config as is. The 'not after unmount' case fails until the 1200 ms timer is cleared.
- NOT: severity high. It is test infrastructure, not a release blocker.

### CODE-22. The reader-policy rules (which page types are paged, when recall fires, when the pre-question shows) are scattered inline across files, with a second copy in studySession, so each open call is an untested component edit.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ Medium, as reviewer and verifier agree: not a defect by itself, but each of [USER]'s open calls on recall and pre-questions is today a scattered component edit with no unit test.

**Problem.** The rules [USER]'s open calls turn on sit inline in different files, none with a unit test. On v1.1 the section page's ROLLUP_TYPES (+page.svelte:35-41) is an untyped `Set<string>` that decides which page types are paged (ruling 2: rollups always scroll); studySession.ts:120-128 keeps its own typed SKIP_SECTION_TYPES (the five rollup types plus 'introduction'); the recall gate in markAsRead (:171-180) checks only first completion, so the prompt fires on Lykilhugtök and Samantekt (captured) and, by reading the code, on Formáli; and on v1.2 the pre-question gate is a boolean inside afterNavigate (+page.svelte:103-107). 'answer-key' is a declared SectionType, but generate-toc.js hoists answer-key pages into toc.answerKey, so that set entry is dead in practice; removing 'glossary', 'summary' or 'exercises' from the set fails the e2e at pagination.spec.ts:418-441, removing 'equations' or 'answer-key' fails no test, and no unit test guards any of the five. Per-sub-section recall is not one prop away: onsubsectioncomplete is declared and called in PagedReaderControls but never passed, RecallEntry has no sub-section field, the prompt's copy is section-level, and scrolled mode has no per-sub-section completion signal.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:35-41 (ROLLUP_TYPES and the paged $derived), :171-180 (recall gate: first completion only), :442 (onsubsectioncomplete not passed)
- v1.1:src/lib/utils/studySession.ts:120-128 (SKIP_SECTION_TYPES: the five rollup types plus 'introduction'); v1.1:scripts/generate-toc.js:656-669 (answer-key pages hoisted into toc.answerKey)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:37, :214-218 (onsubsectioncomplete declared and called); src/lib/stores/recall.ts:18-25 (RecallEntry has no sub-section field)
- v1.2:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:103-107 (pre-question gate inline in afterNavigate)
- ui-capture v1.1 brokenOrSuspicious[7]: the recall prompt fires under Lykilhugtök and Samantekt; v1.1:e2e/pagination.spec.ts:418-441 guards only 'summary', 'exercises' and key terms ('glossary')

**Fix.** Create src/lib/utils/readerPolicy.ts as the only home of these rules, typed against SectionType so a typo fails svelte-check: `ROLLUP_SECTION_TYPES: ReadonlySet<SectionType>`; isRollup(type); shouldPage(mode, type), which encodes ruling 2; recallTrigger({ wasRead, type, granularity }) returning 'section' | 'subsection' | null; and shouldShowPreQuestion({ read, type, objectives, policy, … }), with its type filter and frequency passed in as policy inputs, never baked in. studySession uses isRollup plus 'introduction'. The recall page-type gate (no prompt on rollups or front matter) is a defect fix owned by the PED2-03 and UI1-17 entries, not an open call; it should live here as recallTrigger's type rule. Hold [USER]'s choices as named constants (RECALL_GRANULARITY, PREQUESTION_POLICY) and preset neither. If [USER] chooses per-sub-section recall, the section page passes onsubsectioncomplete when the granularity is 'subsection', RecallEntry gains a sub-section key (or one entry per unit), the prompt needs sub-section copy, and scrolled mode needs its own answer, since it has no per-sub-section completion signal; if [USER] chooses per-section recall, delete onsubsectioncomplete and completedUnits (see CODE-25). If [USER] chooses a limited pre-question frequency and the policy counts showings, the new persisted per-section counter must get renameSections() and a line in migrateRenamedSections() (or be keyed by data-module-id, which survives renames); showing it on every unread section needs no counter. Recall-prompt wording variants are [USER]'s wording choice, not an A/B test: with no backend there is nothing to measure.

**Acceptance test.** readerPolicy.test.ts: isRollup is true for each of the five rollup types and false for 'introduction'; shouldPage('paged', t) is false for every rollup type (ruling 2 names summary, key terms, exercises and answer key; the 'equations' case pins current behaviour, not the ruling). Removing any type from ROLLUP_SECTION_TYPES fails a unit test (today none fails at unit level, and removing 'equations' or 'answer-key' fails no test at all). recallTrigger and shouldShowPreQuestion are tested for every value their policy inputs can take, so the suite tests the mechanism and asserts no answer to open calls 2 or 4. grep finds one definition of the rollup set, imported by both the section page and studySession.ts.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] chooses the values the module holds: recall per section or per sub-section (open call 2), and the pre-question frequency, every unread section or limited (open call 4). The module presets neither; it makes each choice a one-line change with a test. The recall page-type gate is not one of these calls (PED2-03, UI1-17), and ruling 2 (rollups always scroll) is settled.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: 'answer-key' never occurs as a section type. It is a declared SectionType (content.ts:111); generate-toc.js moves answer-key pages into toc.answerKey, so the set entry is dead in practice.
- NOT: per-sub-section recall is one prop away. RecallEntry has no sub-section field, the prompt's copy is section-level, and scrolled mode has no per-sub-section completion signal.
- NOT: removing a type from the rollup set fails no test today. Removing 'glossary', 'summary' or 'exercises' fails pagination.spec.ts:418-441; only 'equations' and 'answer-key' are unguarded, and all five are unguarded at unit level.
- NOT: the prompt was observed firing on Formáli. That is reasoned from the code; only Lykilhugtök and Samantekt were captured.
- NOT: a wording A/B test for the recall prompt. With no backend it cannot be measured; the wording is [USER]'s choice.

### CODE-23. The predict-then-rate UI exists twice (/minniskort and ReviewPhase), and calibration will need one shared rule for what counts as correct.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P0.3 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1, v1.2, main · **Also in:** Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ Medium, as reviewer and verifier agree: a maintainability issue, plus a correctness risk for P1.1, whose calibration must not inherit the frozen quality >= 3 rule.

**Problem.** The flip-then-rate flow is written twice, in /minniskort (+page.svelte) and study/ReviewPhase.svelte, whose header already says 'adapted from minniskort page' on main; v1.1 deepened the duplication by adding the identical predict step (predictAndFlip, 'Man það' / 'Man það ekki') to both copies, with parallel CSS. The flashcard store freezes the outcome at write time as wasCorrect: quality >= 3 (flashcard.ts:295-301), so 'Erfitt' (quality 2, which srs.ts treats as a lapse) counts as wrong, and v1.2's CalibrationTab.svelte:32-37 classifies predictions from r.wasCorrect inline into knewIt, overconfident, underconfident and knewGap. Each review entry also stores the raw rating, so a later definition of 'correct' can be recomputed, but only over the last 500 reviews (MAX_REVIEW_HISTORY, flashcard.ts:63, :327), and dailyFlashcardStats.correctCount stays frozen at quality >= 3. The research changes on the table (a correctness-first rating, a re-queue for confident errors, an optional typed answer) would each have to be made in every copy. The roadmap asked P0.3 to upgrade the inline practice self-assessment instead of building a parallel flow; neither v1.1 nor main did (practiceReveal.ts has no prediction step), and v1.2 still has practiceProblems.ts, which has none either.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/minniskort/+page.svelte:32 and src/lib/components/study/ReviewPhase.svelte:40 (two predictAndFlip copies); main:src/lib/components/study/ReviewPhase.svelte:2 ('adapted from minniskort page')
- git diff main...v1.1 on the two files: a 52-line change to ReviewPhase and a 49-line change to minniskort, each adding the same predict step
- v1.1:src/lib/stores/flashcard.ts:295-301 (raw rating stored; wasCorrect: quality >= 3), :63 and :327 (history capped at 500); src/lib/utils/srs.ts:27-32 (hard maps to quality 2)
- v1.2:src/lib/components/analytics/CalibrationTab.svelte:32-37 (classifies from r.wasCorrect inline); v1.1:src/lib/actions/practiceReveal.ts:178 (binary 'Rétt hjá mér' / 'Þarf að æfa meira', no predict step)
- R2 design implications for P0.3: a correctness-first rating ('Rétt' / 'Að hluta' / 'Rangt') and a re-queue for confident errors, both of which touch every copy
