### CODE-24. No UI path reads, deletes or exports a student's recall text and the store has no selectors, so it is not ready for P2.3, while P2.2 has a clean extension point.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P0.2 · **Stage:** after release · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ Medium, as reviewer and verifier agree: latent until P2.3 reads entries, since nothing reads them today.

**Problem.** On v1.1 the only production caller of recallStore is RecallPrompt's addEntry (RecallPrompt.svelte:30): no UI path reads, deletes or exports a student's recall text, although the store has removeEntry(id) and reset() (recall.ts:106-110), which only recall.test.ts calls. Its read API, getForSection and getForBook (:95-104), reads through get({ subscribe }), so a P2.3 list that wraps either in $derived would compute once (the CLAUDE.md reactivity pitfall); this is latent, since only the tests call them, and recallEntryCount (:116) is unused. recallStore is not exported from stores/index.ts, unlike every other store. For P2.2 the extension point is clean: studySession.buildSessionPlan is pure and tested (27 tests), so delayed section recalls can be computed from createdAt with no schema change. Three related gaps belong to other entries: the rename hook (renameSections() and a line in migrateRenamedSections(), a merge item: CODE3-01, CODE1-06), per-entry validation on load (CODE1-06), and the 'Hreinsa gögn' confirm, which promises to erase all study data but clears analytics only, a mismatch already on main that v1.1 widens with a store it leaves behind (PED4-03).

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/stores/recall.ts:95-104 (get({ subscribe }) getters, called only from recall.test.ts), :106-110 (removeEntry and reset, no UI caller), :116 (recallEntryCount, unused)
- v1.1: the only production caller of recallStore is src/lib/components/RecallPrompt.svelte:30 (addEntry); src/lib/stores/index.ts exports no recall store
- v1.1:src/lib/stores/annotation.ts:238 (exportAnnotations, a Markdown export to model on)
- v1.1:src/lib/utils/studySession.ts:64-70 (SessionPlanInput), :131-134 ('Pure function — no store access'); studySession.test.ts has 27 passing tests
- CLAUDE.md 'Reactivity pitfalls': never wrap a store method that reads via get({ subscribe }) in $derived

**Fix.** Before P2.3 reads entries: (1) Add pure selectors in src/lib/utils/recall.ts, entriesForSection(state, book, chapter, section) and entriesForBook(state, book), used as $derived(entriesForBook($recallStore, bookSlug)); delete the get()-based getters, which is simpler than keeping them until P2.3 has a consumer (CODE1-06's verifier notes the same). (2) Export recallStore from stores/index.ts. (3) In the read view, add a per-entry 'Eyða' button on the existing removeEntry, and exportRecall(bookSlug) as Markdown modelled on exportAnnotations (annotation.ts:238); both suit a localStorage-only site where students may share school computers. (4) For P2.2, add dueRecalls: DueRecall[] to SessionPlanInput, computed by a pure findDueRecalls(entries, toc, now). If CODE1-06's per-entry validation lands, put the predicate in a shared helper (storeValidation.ts) so recall does not become stricter than every other store by accident. The 500 cap needs no work now; if [USER] chooses per-sub-section recall (open call 2), revisit it, since entries multiply. For 'Hreinsa gögn', follow PED4-03's entry (call each store's reset(), recall included, or narrow the confirm to analytics) and never rely on clearAllStorage alone: a store write before a reload can put the in-memory state back (reasoned, not verified).

**Acceptance test.** A component test (CODE-21's config): a list that renders entriesForBook($recallStore, b) shows a new entry after addEntry without a remount. Clicking 'Eyða' on one of two entries leaves exactly the other, and exportRecall(b) on a book with two entries returns Markdown containing both texts and none from another book. recallStore is importable from $lib/stores. buildSessionPlan with one due recall (from findDueRecalls) enables the review phase. After the 'Hreinsa gögn' change, the recall store holds zero entries (assert on the store, not on the localStorage key).

**Decision for [USER].** Timing only: whether a minimal read-back with a per-entry 'Eyða' (and an export) ships before v1.1 reaches students, or waits for P2.3's recall-review tab. CODE1-06's verifier calls it P2.3 scope and [USER]'s call. The stage above assumes it waits; if [USER] wants it sooner, it moves to release. The selectors and the barrel export are needed either way.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: array-only validation is a recall-specific lapse. It is the codebase convention (annotation.ts, analytics.ts, reader.ts).
- NOT: the get({ subscribe }) getters are a live bug. Their only callers are tests; they are a latent hazard for P2.3.
- NOT: an acceptance check that no 'namsbokasafn:recall' key remains after reset(). The persist subscriber writes {"entries":[]} straight back; assert zero entries instead.
- NOT: a 'study log' store to clear. No store has that name; name the stores.

### CODE-26. Kvörðun's per-section list is sorted most under-confident first, the opposite of its own comment.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P1.1 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.2 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ Medium: the verifier lowered it from high, because nothing is hidden. Every row keeps its verdict badge and both percentages; only the priority order is inverted.

**Problem.** In v1.2's CalibrationTab.svelte:102 the per-section list is sorted by descending (confidencePct − successPct) and then reversed with .reverse(), so it ends up ascending: the most under-confident section comes first and the most over-confident last, the opposite of the comment at :101 ('Most overconfident first — that's where review pays off'). Evaluating line 102 verbatim gives the order −60 | 0 | +25 | +60, and the seeded browser captures at desktop, tablet and phone sizes, light and dark, list '1.2 … Vanmat' first and '1.1 … Ofmat' (100% vs 25%) last. Nothing is hidden: every row still shows its verdict badge (Ofmat, Vanmat, Í jafnvægi) and both percentages; only the priority order is inverted. CalibrationTab.svelte exists only on v1.2, so v1.1 is the port target and main is unaffected.

**Evidence.**

- v1.2:src/lib/components/analytics/CalibrationTab.svelte:101 (comment: most overconfident first), :102 (descending sort, then .reverse())
- Evaluating line 102 verbatim: rendered order −60 | 0 | +25 | +60 (most under-confident first)
- v1.2 seeded Kvörðun captures (desktop, tablet, phone; light and dark): '1.2 … Vanmat' first, '1.1 … Ofmat … 100% · … 25%' last
- health_v1.2 finding 0: the tab's logic sits inline in $derived and has no unit test

**Fix.** Delete .reverse(): sort with `(a, b) => delta(b) − delta(a)` only, inside an extracted pure sortMostOverconfidentFirst(rows) in src/lib/utils/calibration.ts with a unit test. Array.prototype.sort is stable (ES2019), so ties already keep the Map's insertion order; breaking ties by section number is optional. Ship it in the P1.1 port together with the rework of the same file (CODE2-03's headline and 'Erfitt' fixes) and the move of its logic into utils/calibration.ts (CODE-14; CODE-23 puts classifyPrediction in the same module).

**Acceptance test.** Unit: sortMostOverconfidentFirst on rows with deltas −60, 0, +25, +60 returns +60, +25, 0, −60 (line 102 as written returns the reverse). e2e seeded with one over-confident section (100% predicted known vs 25% correct) and one under-confident section: the over-confident row is first in #tabpanel-kvordun (today it is last).

- NOT: severity high. Nothing is hidden; every row keeps its verdict badge and both percentages.
- NOT: v1.1 is an affected branch. CalibrationTab.svelte exists only on v1.2; v1.1 is the port target.

### CODE-27. The pager's re-split triggers miss some real layout changes and fire on unrelated ones.

**Severity:** low · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** after release · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1

_Severity note:_ Low: the verifier lowered it from medium. No re-split is missed at the default narrow measure, the content has no `<details>`, and pages with oversized blocks already put the controls below the fold by design.

**Problem.** On v1.1, PagedReaderControls re-splits from four sources: a capture-phase load listener (:436-438), a window resize listener that ignores small height changes on touch screens (:443-455), a settings subscription that fires on every settings key, theme, shortcuts and sidebar included (:471-479), and a MutationObserver on the article root's direct children (:481-485). With efni's markup those children are only header and main, so the observer never sees a block-level change, and bionic reading now unwraps in place, so the observer never fires on a bionic toggle and its comment is stale. Nothing fires when a block grows within a page: 'Sýna svar' expands the atomic .example with no trigger, so that page can extend past the fold until the next re-split; the impact is small, because pages with oversized blocks already put the controls below the fold by design (§3.1 at phone size: 12 of 28 pages, on efni's ② preview content). A container-only width change is missed only for the 'medium' or 'wide' measure at windows of about 1024-1183 or 1024-1247 px, for example on toggling focus mode, which returning readers who kept 'medium' can hit (CODE1-15); at the default narrow measure the column is 608 px either way.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:436-438 (load listener), :443-455 (touch-filtered window resize), :471-479 (settings subscription on every key), :481-485 (MutationObserver, root childList only)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:336-359 (recompute anchors on the current page's first block unless a target is set)
- Article root children in the ② preview content: header+main (164 pages) or header+main+section (24); 0 `<details>` in the content and none injected by actions or components
- semantic-drift item 24 ('Sýna svar' causes no re-split); ui-capture v1.1 layout[5] (§3.1 at phone size: the controls fall below the fold on 12 of 28 pages)
- docs/plans/2026-04-22-screen-vs-paper-reader-plan.md:203 (a ResizeObserver on the reading container, debounced 150 ms)

**Fix.** Keep the window resize listener: availableHeight() depends on innerHeight, and a window that only gets shorter fires no element observer. Add a debounced ResizeObserver (about 150 ms, as plan :203 specifies) on the content root that re-splits when its inline size changes; it covers the medium/wide focus-mode case and sidebar changes. Narrow the settings subscription to the keys that change block heights (fontSize, fontFamily, lineHeight, lineWidth, bionicReading, showTermEnglish) instead of deleting it, since smaller type must still re-split; watching the root's block size instead would need the blocks that showPage and restoreAll hide and show filtered out, or the observer feeds back into recompute. Delete the dead MutationObserver and its stale comment. For growth within a page ('Sýna svar'), do not re-split on the spot: recompute keeps the page's first block (:336-359), so an immediate re-split could push the expanded example onto the next page, out of view. Recommended: mark the pages stale, let the window scroll meanwhile, as oversized pages already do, and re-split at the next page turn, continuing from the first block the reader had not reached; the alternative is an immediate re-split that passes the grown block as target. The fixed 96 px SCROLL_OFFSET, which focus mode's missing header makes wrong, is a scroll-offset matter that also feeds the page budget (open call 1), so it belongs with the budget work, not here.

**Acceptance test.** e2e, with a test hook that counts splits (for example a data attribute on the nav): toggling the theme or a shortcut binding in paged mode runs no re-split (today one runs); at a 1100 px window with lineWidth 'medium', toggling focus mode runs one (today none, since only the column width changes); after 'Sýna svar' on a page holding an Example, the label is unchanged and the example's top stays in the viewport, and after Næsta and then Fyrri each page fits (nav bottom ≤ innerHeight) or holds one oversized block with its headings (today the grown page stays oversized). Regression guards that pass today and must still pass: a desktop window made only shorter re-splits, and a smaller font size re-splits. grep finds no MutationObserver in PagedReaderControls.svelte.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: a `<details>` opening is a missed trigger. The content has no `<details>`, and no action or component injects one.
- NOT: focus mode misses a re-split at the default measure. The narrow column is 608 px with or without the sidebar margin at every lg+ viewport; only 'medium' or 'wide' at about 1024-1183 / 1024-1247 px change width.
- NOT: focus mode's header removal (56 px to 0) is a missed re-split. It is a scroll-offset matter (SCROLL_OFFSET 96).
- NOT: replace all four triggers with two ResizeObservers. Dropping the window resize listener stops a window that only gets shorter from re-splitting, watching only growth stops smaller type from re-splitting, re-splitting on growth can push the expanded example onto the next page, and hidden or shown blocks would feed back into recompute.
- NOT: a focus-mode acceptance test on where the first block's top lands. At the narrow default nothing fires, and recompute uses scroll: false.

**Source findings:** CODE1-12

### CODE-28. Block heights add up margins that CSS collapses, so pages under-fill.

**Severity:** low · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1

_Severity note:_ Verifier: low. By calculation, pages stop short of the budget by about one to three lines and nothing breaks; the reason to land it before release is that it changes how any page budget looks on screen (open call 1), not its severity.

**Problem.** On v1.1, PagedReaderControls' measure() (:111-115) charges each block its border-box height plus both of its margins, and paginate() adds those up against the page budget, but CSS collapses adjacent vertical margins (`article.cnx-module p` is 10px 0, `div.equation` and `table` 20px 0), so every boundary between two margined blocks on a page is overcounted by min(marginBottom, marginTop). Pages whose first visible block has its own top margin are also overcharged by about 15 px, because gapAbove (:121-125) holds the h1's margin collapsing through `<header>` while that block's height holds its own top margin; page 1 is unaffected, since `<header>` has 0 margins. By calculation (no runtime measurement of page fill exists) the shortfall grows with the number of blocks, to about 65 px for a page of six paragraphs. In the other direction the model misses the chrome of top-level `section.footnotes` units (content.css:781-785: 40px margin-top, 20px padding, 1px border; 20 of 135 chemistry modules in the ② preview copy) and inner margins that collapse through wrappers such as `<header>`; no rule styles a plain nested `<section>`. P0.4 exists only on v1.1, so this is not on main.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:111-115 (measure: rect.height + marginTop + marginBottom), :121-125 (gapAbove, taken from the first block)
- v1.1:static/styles/content.css:180-183 (p 10px 0), :586-588 (div.equation 20px 0), :647-651 (table 20px 0), :781-785 (section.footnotes margin, padding, border)
- Screen measurements: gapAbove 63 / 47 / 39 px = .reading-content padding 48 / 32 / 24 px + the h1's 15 px collapsed margin; app.css's `.reading-content p { margin-bottom: 1.5rem }` loses to `article.cnx-module p` on specificity, so 10px 0 is what applies
- Structure survey of the 135 chemistry modules (② preview): each article is `<header>` + `<main>`, with `section.footnotes` in 20; 64 nested sections, none with a class

**Fix.** Put a collapse-aware height rule in the pure module (paginate.ts), not the component, and test it there. Verification offers two variants: keep measure() and subtract min(previous marginBottom, marginTop) at each boundary (simplest); or measure offset deltas with every block visible (height_i = top_{i+1} − top_i, and bottom − top + marginBottom for a unit's last block), which also absorbs padding between blocks but charges a page's last block the gap to the next page and still misses a unit wrapper's own top chrome. With either, stop the ~15 px double count (gapAbove should not carry the h1's collapsed margin onto a page whose first block brings its own) and add `section.footnotes`' margin, padding and border to its unit's first page. Fuller pages interact with open call 1 (the page-height budget) but do not settle it: whatever budget [USER] chooses, today's pages stop short of it, so land this before the budget is judged on rendered pages.

**Acceptance test.** Unit, approach-neutral: adding a fourth identical block (100 px, 10 px top and bottom margins) to a run of three raises the helper's total by 110 px, the height plus one collapsed gap; today it rises by 120 px. e2e at 1280×800 and 390×844: on a page after the first that holds k ≥ 3 consecutive paragraphs, chosen from content rather than hardcoded, the pager's modelled height for that page (its top gap plus the heights it fed paginate, read through a test hook or recomputed with the same helper) is within 10 px, one paragraph margin, of the rendered height from the container top to the bottom margin edge of the page's last visible block; by calculation it is about 10 × (k − 1) + 15 px over today (35 px for three paragraphs).

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the first block's top margin is double-counted on every page; only on pages whose first visible block has its own top margin, and page 1 is unaffected (its `<header>` has 0 margins; the 15 px in gapAbove is the h1's margin collapsing through it).
- NOT: pages stop 10–20 px short; that was never measured, and 10–20 px is the overcount per boundary, so the shortfall grows with the number of blocks (about 65 px for six paragraphs, by calculation).
- NOT: measure() misses the padding and borders of nested `<section>` wrappers in chemistry; no rule styles a plain section, and what goes uncounted is the top-level `section.footnotes` chrome and margins collapsing through wrappers such as `<header>`.
- NOT: offset deltas give heights exactly as rendered; a page's last block takes the gap to the next block, and the unit wrapper's top chrome is still missed.
- NOT: the e2e reference '(last bottom + marginBottom) − (first top − marginTop)'; it contradicts the delta definition, which never includes the first block's top margin.

**Source findings:** CODE1-18

### CODE-29. P1's components hand-roll inline SVG, and the Kvörðun tab's 'scale' icon is not an IconName, so the port must use the registry's icons.

**Severity:** low · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.2, v1.1 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ Verifier: low (the reviewer said high). The type error appears only in a port resolution that keeps 'scale', and CI's npm run check would block it rather than let it ship; the inline SVGs are aria-hidden, so the cost is design-system consistency.

**Problem.** v1.2's P1 code draws by hand icons that v1.1 takes from the Icon registry: PreQuestionPrompt.svelte:40-42 (P1.2, 9b74bb2) and the new cloze button in SelectionPopup.svelte:149-156 (P1.3, 02c5ce9) are inline SVG, while v1.1's SelectionPopup has 0 `<svg>`, so the clean cherry-picks bring one inline SVG into each file. P1.1 (c493bd2) gives the Kvörðun tab `icon: 'scale'`, but v1.1's AnalyticsTabs, byte-identical to main's, types its tabs `{ id: TabId; label: string; icon: IconName }[]` (:13-18) and renders `<Icon name={tab.icon}>` (:40), and IconName (icons.ts:172) has no 'scale', so a port resolution that keeps it fails svelte-check; v1.2 itself type-checks only because its array is typed `icon: string`. The AnalyticsTabs conflict appears in every trial merge that includes P1.1, and only the tabs array and the icon branch conflict. The registry already holds the three glyphs P1 needs, on v1.1 and main: 'grid-2x2' (:117, which the icon hand-back §3a reserves for calibration), 'circle-help' (:121) and 'rectangle-ellipsis' (:123). Both inline SVGs carry aria-hidden="true", so this is design-system consistency, not accessibility, and none of it is on main.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/analytics/AnalyticsTabs.svelte:13-18 (IconName-typed tabs), :40 (`<Icon name={tab.icon}>`); v1.1:src/lib/components/icons.ts:117, :121, :123 (the three glyphs), :172 (IconName)
- v1.2:src/lib/components/PreQuestionPrompt.svelte:40-42 and v1.2:src/lib/components/SelectionPopup.svelte:149-156 (inline SVG, aria-hidden); `grep -c '<svg'` on SelectionPopup gives 6 on v1.2 and 0 on v1.1
- main:docs/design/icon-handback-2026-06.md §3a (grid-2x2 already serves the calibration matrix)
- Trial merges: AnalyticsTabs 2-hunk conflict in T2, T3, T4b, T4f, T5 and S, the trials that include c493bd2

**Fix.** Treat it as a port-time checklist. Give the Kvörðun tab `icon: 'grid-2x2'` and delete the inline-SVG branch in AnalyticsTabs; replace PreQuestionPrompt's heading SVG with `<Icon name="circle-help" size="md" />` and the cloze button's with `<Icon name="rectangle-ellipsis" size="sm" />`; icons.ts and icons.test.ts need no change. For TabId, cherry-picking c493bd2 brings its `export type { TabId } from './AnalyticsTabs.svelte'` re-export by itself (re-apply it only if P1.1 is rebuilt by hand), but that re-export reverses 9fa3501 (2026-02-05), which replaced it on purpose because 'TS can't see named type exports through the Svelte shim'; it type-checks on v1.2 and is unverified on main's toolchain, so run npm run check and lint on the port, and prefer moving TabId into a plain analytics/types.ts that both files import (KEEP-06).

**Acceptance test.** On the port branch: npm run check reports 0 errors (a resolution that keeps 'scale' fails it); src/lib/components/icons.test.ts passes unchanged; `grep -c '<svg'` returns 0 for PreQuestionPrompt.svelte and for SelectionPopup.svelte (the straight picks of 9b74bb2 and 02c5ce9 add one each); and `git grep -nE 'type TabId *='` finds exactly one definition (a bare `git grep -n 'type TabId'` also matches the inline import in greining/+page.svelte).

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: severity high; the verifier rates it low.
- NOT: c493bd2's single-source TabId fix is lost on the cherry-pick path; analytics/index.ts is unchanged between c493bd2^ and v1.1, so the re-export applies by itself.
- NOT: v1.2 fails svelte-check; its tabs array is typed `icon: string`, so the error exists only in a port resolution that keeps 'scale'.
- NOT: the inline SVGs are an accessibility defect; both are aria-hidden.
- NOT: the AnalyticsTabs conflict appears in every trial merge; it appears in every trial that includes P1.1.
- NOT: `git grep -n 'type TabId'` counts definitions; it also matches the inline import in greining/+page.svelte.

**Source findings:** CODE2-08

### CODE-30. P1.4 ships the original Atkinson Hyperlegible 1.006, not the Next family the plan names, and its four files would be precached for every visitor although the font is opt-in.

**Severity:** low · **Feature:** P1.4 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.2 · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ Verifier: low. About 95 KiB more on each visitor's service-worker install is a data cost, not a correctness one, and nothing ships until P1.4 is ported.

**Problem.** v1.2's P1.4 adds four static Atkinson faces (23,196 + 23,776 + 24,908 + 25,160 = 97,040 B, +22.2% on main's 437,844 B of fonts) whose name tables read 'Version 1.006', the original family, while the plan's P1.4 row says 'add Atkinson Hyperlegible Next'. The files exist only on v1.2, but the precache glob they match (`client/**/*.{js,css,html,ico,png,svg,woff,woff2}`) is the same on every branch and PWAUpdater registers the service worker for every visitor with immediate: true, so v1.2 or any port of it would have every visitor download all four although the font is opt-in; this follows from the glob, since no built sw.js was inspected, and main's runtimeCaching.ts has no font route. The Atkinson @font-face rules carry no unicode-range, but that is not what costs: Workbox precaches every file the glob matches, whatever its unicode-range. All 20 Icelandic letters are in all four faces; Atkinson has Δ, Ω, μ and π, which Literata's subsets lack, but no lowercase Greek or arrows, and characters it lacks occur on 82 chemistry pages against 119 for Literata (R4-09, on the ② preview content). The OFL.txt and LICENSES.md lines the port must add are tracked in CODE-12.

**Evidence.**

- `v1.2:static/fonts/AtkinsonHyperlegible-*.woff2`: 97,040 B in four files; name[5] 'Version 1.006' (fc-query fontversion 65929)
- v1.1:vite.config.ts:21 (main :22) globPatterns; v1.2:src/lib/components/PWAUpdater.svelte:22-35 (useRegisterSW, immediate: true), mounted in src/routes/+layout.svelte:44; main:src/lib/sw/runtimeCaching.ts (content and image routes only)
- docs/plans/2026-04-22-screen-vs-paper-reader-plan.md:59 (P1.4: 'add Atkinson Hyperlegible Next')
- R4-09: no reading or legibility study of either family; Next's upright variable woff2 is 48,176 B (italic not measured); characters missing from Atkinson occur 474 times on 82 pages, from Literata's subsets 976 times on 119 (② preview content)

**Fix.** Keep the opt-in font out of the precache rather than subsetting it: add globIgnores for `AtkinsonHyperlegible-*` (and, optionally, OpenDyslexic's ~60 KB, which main already precaches) plus a CacheFirst runtime route for /fonts/ in src/lib/sw/runtimeCaching.ts, written to that file's source-text rules (arrow functions only, no identifier from outside the function body) and pinned by its unit test and an offline e2e; font-display: swap keeps a not-yet-cached font harmless. Do not split the faces into latin and latin-ext: that precaches 8 entries at nearly the same bytes and drops the 33 other code points, Δ μ π ≤ ≥ ≈ among them, that chemistry text uses. Choose the family before readers can pick the font, because a later switch changes the text for everyone who did: if [USER] keeps the plan's Next, replace the four files before the port; if [USER] keeps 1.006, amend the plan's P1.4 row. Next is not smaller (its upright variable woff2 alone is 48,176 B; the italic was not measured), so this is a design choice, not a size one.

**Acceptance test.** After npm run build on the port branch, build/sw.js's precache manifest lists no AtkinsonHyperlegible entry (the glob would add all four today) while the house-font entries are still listed (control); runtimeCaching.ts's unit test, which rebuilds each function from its source text, covers the /fonts/ route; an offline e2e renders body text in Atkinson after one online visit with the font selected; and the shipped faces' name tables match the family [USER] chose.

**Decision for [USER].** Which Atkinson family ships with P1.4: the original Atkinson Hyperlegible 1.006 that v1.2 carries, or Atkinson Hyperlegible Next, which the plan names. Neither has a reading or legibility study (R4-09) and Next is not smaller, so it is a design choice; make it before the port ships, since switching later changes the font for every reader who picked it.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the cost falls only on PWA (home-screen) installs; PWAUpdater registers the service worker for every visitor from the root layout, with immediate: true.
- NOT: v1.1 or main carry the Atkinson files; only v1.2 does, and the other branches share only the precache glob.
- NOT: Greek letters fall back exactly as with Literata; Atkinson has Δ, Ω, μ and π, which Literata's subsets lack, while lowercase Greek and arrows fall back in both.
- NOT: subsetting to latin + latin-ext with unicode-range reduces the precache; Workbox precaches every matched file, so it would be 8 entries at nearly the same bytes, minus Δ μ π ≤ ≥ ≈.
- NOT: an acceptance test that passes on 'latin/latin-ext subsets totalling less than 97,040 B'; it can pass without fixing anything.

**Source findings:** CODE2-18

## UI

### UI-01. None of the new interventions manages focus or announces its result: focus falls to `<body>` after a prediction, a rating, a key or swipe page turn, a recall save or skip, or a pre-question dismissal, and 'Vistað' is never announced.

**Severity:** critical · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1, v1.2, main · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ Critical rests on 'Vistað — góð upprifjun festir efnið í minni.' failing WCAG 4.1.3 Status Messages (AA) on P0.2's core save path: no role, no live region, focus on `<body>` (UI1-02, UI3-03, both critical). The focus losses alone are high: a regression from main on P0.3 and P0.4, but browsers keep the sequential-focus starting point, so Tab order survives and a 2.4.3 failure is not established (CODE1-01, lowered from critical; UI1-19, raised to high). The pager part alone is medium (UI1-20), and the pre-question part is high only once P1.2 is ported (UI2-11).

**Problem.** No new component on v1.1 or v1.2 moves focus when it removes or hides the focused control, and none announces its result. On v1.1's recall prompt, Vista, Ctrl+Enter and Sleppa remove the form at once (`{#if saved}`) and focus lands on `<body>`; 'Vistað — góð upprifjun festir efnið í minni.' is in no live region and never takes focus, and the textarea's only name is its placeholder. On /minniskort and in /nam's ReviewPhase the card is now a `<div>` and the 'Man það' / 'Man það ekki' row unmounts on click, so focus drops twice per card where main's card `<button>` dropped it once, nothing announces the revealed answer, and the card keeps `cursor: pointer` and a hover scale although it is no longer clickable and never echoes the prediction (the answer replacing the question is inherited from main). In paged mode ←/→ or a swipe with focus on a glossary term hides the term and drops focus to `<body>` (showPage moves no focus), while Fyrri/Næsta keep focus as plan :209 intends; Space on a focused term scrolls the window 179 px (desktop) / 344 px (phone), because the term (role=button) handles no keys, a gap pre-existing on main, and the swipe has no hint and no guard for a horizontally scrollable table or equation. For the P1 port, measured with objectives injected because a v1.2 build never shows the card, dismissing the pre-question drops focus to `<body>` and Escape does nothing, and the cloze toast has role=status but is inserted together with its text (failing ARIA22 test step 1; whether it is announced was not verified). The pager's persistent live region (PagedReaderControls.svelte:543-547) is the one correct pattern; the next Tab still continues from where the removed control was (Chromium, Firefox, WebKit), and screen-reader output was not captured.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/RecallPrompt.svelte:54-58 ('Vistað' block: no role=status, no aria-live), :68-75 (placeholder-only name), :28-38 (no focus move after Vista or Sleppa)
- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/minniskort/+page.svelte:195-220 (card is a `<div>`; predict row inside `{#if !isFlipped}`) and v1.1:src/lib/components/study/ReviewPhase.svelte:106-130 (same pattern); main's card was `<button onclick={flip}>`
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:206-212 (showPage: no focus code), :291 (Space guard), :310-318 (swipe: no scrollable-ancestor check), :543-547 (persistent live region outside `{#if}`)
- ui-capture_v1.1 keyboardAndAria[5] (ArrowRight on a focused term: focus to `<body>`; Space scrolls 426→605 / 626→970), [10] (focus on `<body>` after Vista and Sleppa), [14]; ui-capture_v1.2 keyboardAndAria[1] (pre-question), [2] (predict-first), [4] (toast)
- Verifier repros: focus-start-point (Chromium 153, Firefox 155, WebKit 26.6: the next Tab continues after the removed node); repeat-and-print (Chromium 153: with focus moved to 'Aftur' after a prediction, a held or double Enter rated 'Aftur')

**Fix.** Work in session-sized steps; steps 1–5 land before v1.1 reaches students, step 6 with the P1 port. (1) One announcer: `src/lib/stores/announcer.ts` exporting `announce(text)`, backed by one persistent `<div class='sr-only' role='status' aria-live='polite' aria-atomic='true'>` in `src/routes/+layout.svelte` that clears and resets its text on each call (an assertive twin only for errors); fold the pager's region into it so two polite regions do not compete. 'Vistað' and the cloze toast call it; no role=status is ever created inside an `{#if}` together with its text. (2) RecallPrompt: name the textarea with aria-labelledby on the h3 (aria-describedby on the instruction); announce the save; after a save or Sleppa move focus to the next step, preferring the 'Næsti kafli' link or the pager over the content wrapper, which would send a screen reader back to the start of the content; Escape acts as Sleppa only while the textarea is empty, so a stray key never discards typed recall (R4-19). This does not have to wait for the end-of-section redesign in finding UI1-11. (3) Predict-first (/minniskort and ReviewPhase): after 'Man það' / 'Man það ekki' focus the revealed card or answer (tabindex=-1, accessible name starting with the existing 'Svar' label and the answer text), with the ratings one deliberate Tab away; after a rating focus the next card's question the same way and announce the position. Do not focus 'Aftur' or the next card's 'Man það': a held or double Enter then rates 'Aftur' (SM-2 quality 0) or records a prediction before recall. Guard ReviewPhase.advance(), which fires oncomplete and unmounts the phase on the last item, and gate the /minniskort reset `$effect` so it does not take focus on first mount, calling the focus through `untrack` (the CLAUDE.md `$effect` pitfall). In the same change drop `cursor: pointer` and the scale(1.01) hover from .flashcard-card and .rp-card, show the question above the answer after the reveal in --text-secondary, add one line 'Þú spáðir: Man það' / 'Þú spáðir: Man það ekki' (on v1.1 it stands in for P1.1's comparison), consider 'Hvernig gekk?' for 'Hversu auðvelt var þetta?', and keep the two equal-weight predict buttons (R2-12); the hint's contrast is UI-02. The merge has to rewrite main's #249 flashcard e2e for predict-first anyway; that rewrite stays independent of this fix, and the focus assertions go into it once step 3 lands, or right after it. (4) Pager: when a key or swipe turns the page and focus is on `<body>` or inside a now-hidden block, focus the new page's content root (tabindex=-1, preventScroll); leave focus on Fyrri/Næsta when they were used (plan :209). Make the glossary term handle Space and Enter itself (glossaryTerms.ts registers no keydown); do not exclude it from the Space guard, which would turn the page and recreate the focus loss. Ignore swipes that start inside an element with computed overflow-x auto/scroll and scrollWidth > clientWidth, walking up from the `touchstart` target recorded in handleTouchStart (mirror keyBelongsElsewhere), and mention swiping in the reading-mode help text. (5) Last page: never leave a disabled control focused; once completion has fired, swap Næsta for the next-section link (completion still fires on the last-page Næsta, so ruling 3 holds). (6) At the P1 port: after 'Ég hugleiddi þetta — byrja að lesa' move focus to the section h1 (tabindex=-1), because the pager's region speaks only when its text changes; let Escape dismiss the card, coexisting with the layout's `closeModal: 'Escape'` binding; the cloze toast uses announce(). Persisting the dismissal is PED-06 and must not settle open call 4. Roles: the recall prompt and the pre-question stay inline `<section aria-labelledby>` regions, not dialogs; the selection popup becomes a toolbar (finding UI3-09); Settings and FlashcardModal stay modal dialogs; a shared InterventionCard.svelte gives the design track one place to unify their look. Every new string ('Upprifjun vistuð', 'Kort 2 af 6' and the like) is a proposal for a native-speaker check.

**Acceptance test.** Keyboard-only Playwright at 1280×800 and 390×844. RecallPrompt: after Ctrl+Enter and after 'Vista upprifjun' the shared announcer's text is non-empty within 500 ms and document.activeElement is not `<body>`; the same focus check holds after Sleppa and after Escape on an empty textarea, and Escape on a textarea holding text keeps the text and the prompt; with the placeholder removed, the textarea's accessible name equals the h3's text. /minniskort and /nam with a seeded deck: after 'Man það' the active element is the revealed card or answer and its accessible name starts with 'Svar'; after 'Gott' it is the next card's question; holding Enter on 'Man það' records no rating. Paged section, with readingMode set explicitly in the profile: focus a glossary `dfn` on page 1 and press ArrowRight, and activeElement is neither `<body>` nor inside a `[hidden]` element; Space on a focused term leaves scrollY unchanged; a CDP touch swipe that starts on a horizontally scrollable table scrolls the table and leaves the page label unchanged. Static check: in RecallPrompt, PreQuestionPrompt, TextHighlighter, the /minniskort page and ReviewPhase no element with role=status is created inside an `{#if}` together with its text (the pre-existing OfflineIndicator and PWAUpdater regions are out of scope). At the port: after dismissing the pre-question the active element is the section h1, and Escape dismisses it.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the next Tab starts again at the top of the page (the 60-Tab cost) — in Chromium 153, Firefox 155 and WebKit 26.6 the next Tab continued from where the removed control was.
- NOT: a WCAG 2.4.3 Focus Order failure as established — Tab order is preserved; this is a focus-loss and announcement defect (CODE1-01 lowered from critical to high).
- NOT: screen-reader users are left at the top of the document after every step — screen-reader behaviour was not captured.
- NOT: plan :209 asks for focus on the new page after every turn — it also says Prev/Next keep focus for repeated activation; the gap is key and swipe turns only.
- NOT: focusing 'Aftur' after a prediction, or the next card's 'Man það' after a rating — measured: a held or double Enter then rates 'Aftur' (SM-2 quality 0), or a prediction is recorded before recall.
- NOT: focusing a role=group of ratings after the reveal — it does not tell a screen-reader user the answer; focus the revealed card.
- NOT: focus drops at the 1200 ms unmount — it drops at save time, when `{#if saved}` removes the form; sighted keyboard users do see 'Vistað' for 1.2 s.
- NOT: the placeholder-only textarea name as an A/AA failure — axe raised no label violation and the name persists after typing; it is a best-practice gap.
- NOT: Escape as a requirement for the recall prompt — it is not required for a non-modal region, and an unconditional Escape would discard typed recall.
- NOT: 'Næsta is disabled while it holds focus' on first completion — RecallPrompt's `$effect` moves focus to the textarea first; only re-completing an already-read section could leave a disabled Næsta focused (not verified).
- NOT: the cloze toast lacks role=status — it has one; the defect is that its container is inserted together with its text.
- NOT: SelectionPopup ignores Escape — it handles Escape (SelectionPopup.svelte:43) but never takes focus.
- NOT: comparing the prediction with the outcome is the point of P0.3 — P0.3 is the pre-prediction; the comparison is P1.1 Kvörðun on v1.2.
- NOT: the answer replacing the question, 'Hversu auðvelt var þetta?' and the tertiary hint colour as v1.1 changes — all three are inherited from main.
- NOT: the pre-question focus, reload and Escape numbers as a description of the v1.2 build — they were measured with objectives injected; a v1.2 build never shows the card (process-content.js:130 writes objectives: []).
- NOT: 57/58 Tabs to the pre-question button as its own cost — about 40 of them are stops in the closed phone sidebar; counted from `<main>` it is the 12th stop.
- NOT: with UI2-01's placement the pre-question card is the first stop after the skip link — on v1.1 the toolbar and the objectives buttons come before it.
- NOT: a 44 px button height as a WCAG requirement — 226×36 passes 2.5.8; 44 px is 2.5.5 (AAA) or platform guidance.
- NOT: 'nothing is announced' after the pre-question as captured screen-reader output — it is inferred from the DOM.
- NOT: UI1-19 as medium — raised to high.

**Source findings:** CODE1-01, UI3-03, UI1-02, UI1-19, UI1-20, UI2-11

### UI-02. White text on the amber accent fails WCAG AA in both themes, and amber text, tertiary grey and the light focus ring fall short too: site-wide on main, with new instances on v1.1's controls and on every P1 surface.

**Severity:** critical · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ Critical: WCAG 1.4.3 (AA) failures on v1.1's primary and selected controls on core paths ('Vista upprifjun', 'Síðuskipt', the default-selected 'Þröngt': 2.92 light / 2.08 dark), rated critical by the verifiers of UI1-01, UI3-01 and UI2-02. The Kvörðun part alone is medium (CODE2-13: a secondary path with marginal ratios).

**Problem.** White text on --accent-color is 2.92:1 in light and 2.08:1 in dark (hover 4.02 on #a87518 and 1.74 on #f0bc5a), below the 4.5:1 that 14 px text needs. It is site-wide and pre-existing on main: .btn-accent sets color: white (app.css:401-402), the Línubil and Línubreidd selected segments use the same class (main SettingsModal.svelte:270, :289), and main has 14 such class strings in 10 files plus 22 same-block CSS rules in 17 files (among them the phone tools FAB icon, the mobile 'Næsti kafli' button with an 80% white label at 2.41, and the sidebar chapter-number circles). v1.1 adds 'Vista upprifjun' (RecallPrompt.svelte:88) and the active 'Síðuskipt' (SettingsModal.svelte:315) and makes 'Þröngt' the default selection (settings.ts:72), giving 15 strings in 11 files; v1.2's P1 adds the pre-question button (PreQuestionPrompt.svelte:52-55) and the cloze toast (TextHighlighter.svelte:538-545), both 2.92/2.08, giving 18 in 14. In light mode amber used as text is 2.66–2.71 ('Vistað', 'Gott', objective numbers, the sidebar's current section, and on main already the 'Miðlungs' and selected 'Aa' labels), Kvörðun's '56%' is 2.92 on white against the 3:1 large-text minimum (CalibrationTab.svelte:125) and its gray-500 leads are 4.41 (:110-113, :173-175, also in the empty state), and the global `*:focus-visible` ring is 2.66 against the page (main and v1.1 app.css:427, v1.2 :454); all of these pass in dark. --text-tertiary is 2.90 light / 3.13 dark yet carries hints ('Ctrl+Enter til að vista' 2.95/2.67, the predict hint 2.90/3.13), the recall placeholder (Tailwind gray-400) is 2.37 in light, and the recall textarea's #ddd6c9 border is 1.34 against its region. The design doc's rule for primary buttons (--bg-primary text on the accent) passes only in dark (8.66 against 2.66), and app.css already defines an unused --header-banner-text (#ffffff / #16161c).

**Evidence.**

- ui-capture_v1.1 contrast[20]-[23] ('Vista upprifjun' 2.92 / 2.08, hover 4.02 / 1.74), [34], [35], [41], [42] ('Síðuskipt', 'Þröngt' 2.92 / 2.08), [16], [17], [25], [46], [47] (hints and placeholder), [9], [44] (light focus ring 2.66)
- main:src/app.css:401-402 (.btn-accent, color: white) and :427-431 (`*:focus-visible`, outline var(--accent-color)); main:src/lib/components/SettingsModal.svelte:270, :289; class-string grep: main 14 lines / 10 files, v1.1 15 / 11, v1.2 18 / 14; same-block CSS scan: 22 rules in 17 files on each ref
- ui-capture_v1.2 contrast: pre-question button and cloze toast 2.92 / 2.08; Kvörðun '56%' 2.92 and gray-500 4.41 (v1.2:src/lib/components/analytics/CalibrationTab.svelte:125, :110-113, :173-175; scoped axe finds exactly those 3 nodes)
- v1.1 screenshot measurements fixups.tokenPairs (16 pairs) and the verifiers' recomputation: #1b2838 on #c78c20 5.12; #16161c on #e8a838 8.66 and on #f0bc5a 10.33; #1b2838 on #a87518 3.71 and on #dd9f2c 6.45; #e8e4de on #e8a838 1.64; white on #8a5d0e 5.75; #a87518 ring 3.67 against the page, 4.02 against white
- main:docs/design/design-coordination-2026-06.md:36 (colour tokens and the focus ring are a shared contract with the design track); main:src/app.css:66, :109 (--header-banner-text, 0 consumers)

**Fix.** Make one token change in src/app.css (:root and .dark) and apply it site-wide, not per component; it is main's defect as much as v1.1's, so it can land on main first. The light-mode fill is [USER]'s choice: if [USER] chooses ink on the unchanged amber, rename the unused --header-banner-text to --on-accent = #1b2838 light / #16161c dark (5.12 / 8.66, and 10.33 on the dark hover) and make the light fill hover lighter, not darker (--accent-fill-hover #dd9f2c, 6.45 with ink, since ink on today's #a87518 is 3.71); if [USER] prefers white text in light mode, the light fill becomes bronze #8a5d0e (white 5.75); white on the dark amber #e8a838 is 2.08, so under the bronze option dark mode still needs ink on the amber or its own darker fill. Do not use --text-primary as the ink: in dark it is #e8e4de, 1.64 on the amber. Then replace text-white, #fff and white on every accent fill with var(--on-accent) (15 class strings and 22 CSS rules on v1.1), and never put opacity on text over a fill (80% ink on amber is 3.80). Add --accent-strong #8a5d0e / #e8a838 for amber used as text or as a state ('Vistað', 'Gott', objective numbers, the sidebar's current section, amber links), and give the global `*:focus-visible` ring #a87518 in light (3.67 against the page, 4.02 against white), which also fixes main's rings and every P1 control's. Raise --text-tertiary to #616772 / #98938d, which passes 4.5:1 on all five surfaces in both themes, and add --text-disabled with today's values for disabled text only; tertiary then sits close to secondary (5.19 against 5.45), so carry the hierarchy with size and weight. Add --border-input #8f7c5c / #7a7670 for text fields and switch tracks. Main's 'Miðlungs' and selected 'Aa' labels become --text-primary with weight or an underline as the selected cue, and the selection popup's close icon becomes --text-secondary (5.98 on white). At the P1 port add no new white-on-amber: the pre-question button and the toast use --on-accent, Kvörðun's headline number uses --text-primary (the accent is for interactive elements) and its leads --text-secondary (5.45 on #f7f4ef), and the 2×2 cells keep their data-viz colours. Update the nordic-clarity design doc's primary-button rule and CLAUDE.md's accent section to name --on-accent.

**Acceptance test.** A vitest (e.g. src/lib/styles/tokens.test.ts) parses the :root and .dark blocks of src/app.css and asserts contrast ratios, not hex values, per theme: --on-accent at least 4.5 against the accent fill and against the fill hover; --accent-strong and --text-tertiary at least 4.5 against --bg-primary, --bg-secondary, --bg-tertiary, --accent-light and --accent-subtle; the focus-ring colour and --border-input at least 3 against --bg-primary, --bg-secondary and --accent-light. It fails on dde768e (the tokens are missing and tertiary is 2.90) and passes under either fill choice. A scan of `src/**/*.svelte` class strings and same-block CSS rules finds 0 places that put white text on an accent fill (on v1.1 today: 15 and 22). axe color-contrast reports 0 nodes inside [aria-label='Upprifjun'], the Settings dialog, .paged-nav, .fab-button, .nav-btn-next, .flashcard-hint and .rp-hint, in light and dark and with :hover forced, and at the port also inside [aria-label='Forspurning'], the cloze status, the selection popup and #tabpanel-kvordun, both seeded and empty.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] chooses the light-mode fill: ink #1b2838 on the unchanged amber #c78c20 (5.12:1), or white on a bronze #8a5d0e fill (5.75:1), which re-skins every primary button and departs from the amber/gold accent CLAUDE.md describes; and whether tertiary grey may sit close to secondary, with hierarchy carried by size and weight. Colour tokens are a shared contract with the design track (design-coordination-2026-06.md:36). This is not one of the four open calls.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the focus ring on an amber-filled segment has the fill's own colour (1:1) — the 3px offset with a 20% halo separates them; the failure is the light ring against the page (2.66), and in dark the ring passes (8.66).
- NOT: dark mode is worse overall — only white on amber is; in dark the placeholder (6.92), 'Vistað' (7.4) and the focus ring (8.66) pass.
- NOT: Næsta is the faintest control on screen — the disabled Fyrri is fainter, Næsta's label measures 13.07, and its 1.34 boundary is not a 1.4.11 failure because the button has a text label.
- NOT: --text-primary as the on-accent ink in both themes — dark --text-primary #e8e4de on #e8a838 is 1.64; the 7.17 figure was for the light value #1b2838.
- NOT: make the dark hover darker — with ink text the lighter hovers are better, and the light hover must turn lighter too (ink on #a87518 is 3.71).
- NOT: white on amber and the amber focus ring as v1.1 additions — both are site-wide on main; v1.1 adds two controls and makes 'Þröngt' the default selection (main's default 'Miðlungs' is drawn the same way).
- NOT: about 23 CSS rules — 22 same-block rules in 17 files on each ref; the PWA banner (PWAUpdater.svelte:147) is a class string, not a CSS rule.
- NOT: the settings 'Miðlungs' and selected 'Aa' labels and the popup's gray-400 close icon as P1 additions — they are on main (since 2026-01-22, and at v1.2's base).
- NOT: 2.54 as a P1 focus-ring value — it belongs to the pre-existing Serif font radio; new P1 rings measure 2.65–2.92 (the Atkinson radio was not measured).
- NOT: Kvörðun fails only with seeded data — the two lead paragraphs fail in the empty state too.
- NOT: CalibrationTab.svelte:123 as the headline — it is :125 (:123 is the grid wrapper).
- NOT: sibling-tab palette counts of 56/41/32 — the counting rule that gives CalibrationTab's 85 gives 88/90/46.
- NOT: a class-string scan alone as the acceptance check — it misses the 22 scoped CSS rules.

**Source findings:** UI1-01, UI3-01, UI2-02, CODE2-13

### UI-03. In Settings, Línubil, Línubreidd and Lestrarstilling show their selected state only visually (no aria-pressed or radio semantics) under unlinked labels, and the Leturgerð radios lack a group label

**Severity:** critical · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1, main, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ Critical, as UI1-03's verifier rated it: a WCAG 4.1.2 Level A failure on the route the help text gives screen-reader users out of v1.1's paged mode. UI3-04's verifier rated the wider set of groups high, because Settings is otherwise a secondary surface; the pre-existing Línubil and Línubreidd half alone fits high, and so would the whole issue once UI-05 puts a mode control in the reading view.

**Problem.** On v1.1 the Línubil, Línubreidd and new Lestrarstilling groups (SettingsModal.svelte:267-325) are plain `<button>`s whose selected option is shown only by an amber fill: no aria-pressed, role=radio or aria-checked, and each label is an unassociated `<span>` (WCAG 4.1.2 Level A, 1.3.1). Línubil and Línubreidd have the same pattern on main (SettingsModal.svelte:263-270, :282-289); Lestrarstilling is new in v1.1 (it is also in v1.2's June copy), and v1.1 changes Línubreidd's default to 'Þröngt'. The help text (:323) sends screen-reader users to this group ('Samfellt skrun hentar betur með skjálesurum'), where they hear the buttons' names but not which mode is on, and its 'síðum án skruns' is false on 10 of 28 §3.1 pages at 1280×800 (② preview content), where oversized blocks scroll by design. The Leturgerð radio cards expose their checked state natively but have no `<fieldset>` or `<legend>`, and their radios render in Chromium's default blue because nothing sets accent-color; the font-size slider (label, aria-label, aria-valuetext) is fine. In light mode the selected fill is also 2.66:1 against the modal, under 1.4.11's 3:1 for a state indicator.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/SettingsModal.svelte:267-325 (three button groups with `<span>` labels; help text at :323); main:src/lib/components/SettingsModal.svelte:263-270, :282-289 (same pattern)
- ui-capture_v1.1 keyboardAndAria[11] (18-stop Tab cycle; 'Samfellt skrun' is stop 15), [12] (state by colour only), layout[13] (phone pane of 590.8 px holding 1500 px of settings)
- UI3-04 verifier: selected fill #c78c20 vs modal #f7f4ef = 2.66:1; no accent-color declaration in src or static/styles; aria-pressed precedent at NoteModal.svelte:152
- measurements paged[13]: 10 of 28 §3.1 pages scroll the window at 1280×800, by up to 2005 px

**Fix.** Release fix (S). Change all three groups in one edit (Línubil and Línubreidd are live on main too, so that part can land on main first): give each label `<span>` an id and wrap Línubil, Línubreidd and Lestrarstilling in `role='group'` with `aria-labelledby` pointing at it; add `aria-pressed={selected}` to every segment (precedent NoteModal.svelte:152); add the registry 'check' Icon (aria-hidden) to the selected segment, so state is not carried by fill alone (the fill's own 2.66:1 and the colour of the cue are UI-02's token matter); wrap the Leturgerð radio cards in a `<fieldset>` with a `<legend>` and give the radios an accent-color (UI-07's --focus-ring once it exists). Replace the help line with accurate per-option text that drops 'án skruns', for example 'Síðuskipt: flettu í gegnum kaflann einn skjá í einu, með Næsta, örvalyklum eða með því að strjúka til hliðar.' and 'Samfellt skrun: allur kaflinn á einni síðu. Hentar betur með skjálesara.' (Næsta, the arrow keys and swipe all turn pages; native-speaker check). Follow-up (M), best done when P1.4's theme choice is ported as one more group: a SegmentedControl.svelte on native radios (`<fieldset>` with a visible `<legend>`, a visually hidden `<input type='radio'>` per option, the selected style through `:has(input:checked)` with the check icon, the focus ring through `:has(input:focus-visible)`), which gives one Tab stop per group and arrow-key selection for free; it can also replace the 4-step slider plus 'Aa' buttons with 'Lítið', 'Miðlungs', 'Stórt', 'Mjög stórt'. Moving Lestrarstilling to the top of the dialog is optional, a design preference. Nothing here touches the launch-default call.

**Acceptance test.** e2e with Settings open at 1280×800 and 390×844: getByRole('group', { name: 'Línubil' }), { name: 'Línubreidd' }, { name: 'Lestrarstilling' } and { name: 'Leturgerð' } each resolve (none does today). In Lestrarstilling the segment whose value equals the readingMode stored in localStorage 'namsbokasafn:settings' (the store writes its full state on first load, so on a fresh profile this is the shipped default, whatever it is) has aria-pressed='true' and the other 'false', and the two swap after a click; the same holds for Línubreidd against lineWidth and Línubil against lineHeight. The selected segment contains a visible check icon, a Leturgerð radio's computed accent-color is not 'auto', and the help text no longer contains 'án skruns'. If the SegmentedControl follow-up lands, the roles become radio with checked, and Tab enters each group once.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: no single-choice group in Settings exposes its state or group label. The font-size slider has a `<label for>`, an aria-label and aria-valuetext, and the Leturgerð radios expose their checked state natively; only their group label is missing.
- NOT: the 'Aa' font-size buttons show their state by colour alone. They use amber plus font-semibold, and at 11.7–18.7 px wide but 92–96 px apart they fall under 2.5.8's spacing exception.
- NOT: the pattern pre-exists only on Línubil. Línubreidd has it on main too (main SettingsModal.svelte:282-289); only Lestrarstilling is new in v1.1.
- NOT: screen-reader users cannot read the control. They hear the buttons' names, not their state.
- NOT: a test asserting that 'Síðuskipt' is selected by default. That settles the open launch-default call; derive the expected option from the stored settings.
- NOT: the group must move to the top of the dialog (its top within the first 590 px of the phone pane). That is a design preference, not part of the defect.

**Source findings:** UI1-03, UI3-04

### UI-04. On every paged page the loudest control is the 'Merkja kafla sem lesinn' button under Næsta, which finishes the section from page 1, can use up the one-time recall prompt and fails contrast

**Severity:** critical · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1, main · **Also in:** Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ Critical because of the contrast half, as UI1-08's verifier rated it: a WCAG 1.4.3 AA failure on the section page, a core path, pre-existing and live on main. The v1.1-only paged-mode half (premature completion and the control hierarchy) would be high on its own in UI1-08's verifier's view and medium in PED1-08's.

**Problem.** On v1.1 the bottom 'Merkja kafla sem lesinn' button (+page.svelte:469-480) sits inside `{#if !isRead}` and ignores paged mode, so it renders under the pager on every page while the section is unread, in view on arrival at all three widths (top 654/729/597 px); as a 249×52 filled emerald button it outweighs Næsta, an outlined 91×44 on the phone and 91×39 elsewhere. Pressing it on page 1 marks the section read and opens the recall prompt ('Kafli lesinn — rifjaðu upp'), which fires only on a section's first completion on that device (:171-180), so the one-time prompt can be spent before any reading; the later last-page Næsta then only disables itself (opacity .4), with no prompt and the toolbar already showing 'Lesið'. A prompt opened from page 1 also seems to stay open below the pager while the student pages on (from code, not verified). Separately, the button is white on #009966 at 3.65–3.69:1 (18 px, font-medium, not large text), a 1.4.3 AA failure already live on main (+page.svelte:442-445), and an emerald control departs from CLAUDE.md's amber accent rule. With the toolbar's 'Merkja sem lesið' and the last-page Næsta, paged mode shows three completion controls.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:469-480 (bottom button), :354-362 (toolbar 'Merkja sem lesið'), :171-180 (prompt on first completion only), :451 (readDetection enabled: !isRead && !paged); main: the same button at :442
- measurements arrivalChromeAndColumnGeometry[0..2]: markAsReadBottom top 654/729/597 px, 249×52, in view below the header; navNextButton 91×44 (phone), 91×39 (tablet, desktop)
- axe color-contrast on the button 3.69:1 (#ffffff on #009966); own WCAG computation 3.65:1; #ffffff on emerald-700 #007a55 = 5.36:1
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:366-370 (fail() restores scrolling), :517-540 (nav only when ready && !failed), :534 and :579-582 (Næsta disabled after completionFired, opacity .4)
- Screenshot recall-prompt__phone__light__via-mark-read-on-page-1: the prompt open at 'Hluti 1 af 1 · Síða 1 af 11'

**Fix.** Stop rendering the bottom button while the pager is working, and key that on pager readiness, not on the setting: expose ready/failed from PagedReaderControls (a callback or bindable prop, or let fail() flip a page-level flag) and render the button when `!isRead && (!paged || pagerFailed)`. In fail()'s fallback the content scrolls again, the pager nav is not rendered and readDetection stays off, so there the bottom button is the only completion control at the end of the content. Remove it from working paged mode altogether rather than keeping it on the last page: there Næsta already finishes the section (ruling 3), and a last-page button would need the pager to expose its page. Keep the toolbar 'Merkja sem lesið' as the plan's manual and accessibility path (plan:190); after the arrival scroll it sits above the viewport and is icon-only on the phone, a deliberate low-salience path, and it can move into UI-09's running head if that is built. Fix the contrast now on main, where it is live: emerald-700 (#007a55, 5.36:1) with an emerald-800 hover, or the amber primary style once UI-02's on-accent token exists, which also follows the amber accent rule. Scrolled mode keeps the button.

**Acceptance test.** Playwright on §1.2 with readingMode set to 'paged' in storage, at 390×844 and 1280×800: on every page no element with the text 'Merkja kafla sem lesinn' exists (today it is on every page); the toolbar 'Merkja sem lesið' still marks the section read and opens the 'Upprifjun' region; on the last page Næsta completes the section and the 'Upprifjun' region appears. A test of the fallback branch (the pager reporting failed, for example mounted on an empty content root, which makes init() call fail()) shows the bottom button. With readingMode 'scrolled' the bottom button is present and axe reports no color-contrast node on it (today 3.69:1).

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the later last-page Næsta does nothing visible. It disables itself (opacity .4) once completionFired is set; what is missing is completion feedback (no prompt; the toolbar already shows 'Lesið').
- NOT: hide the button with `{#if !isRead && !paged}`. That keys on the setting and removes it in the pager's fail() fallback, where it is the only completion control at the end of the content.
- NOT: keep the button on the last page only. Næsta already completes the section there (ruling 3), and it would need the pager to expose its current page.
- NOT: normal-weight text. The class is font-medium (500); at 18 px that is still not large text, so 4.5:1 applies.

**Source findings:** UI1-08, PED1-08

### UI-05. In paged mode a screen reader's continuous reading covers only the visible page and the pager, then skips the rest of the section, the h1 is gone after page 1, and the only way to scrolled mode is inside the Settings dialog

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** Pedagogy, Code

_Severity note:_ High, as both verifiers rated it. Not critical: no WCAG criterion requires an h1, and the content stays reachable through labelled buttons with each page announced. But it is a regression for every screen-reader user in v1.1's paged mode, and the only way out is a Settings group with no exposed state (UI-03).

**Problem.** v1.1 pages by setting the hidden attribute (display: none) on every off-page block (PagedReaderControls.svelte:148-168), and the module `<header>` that holds the section's only h1 is paged like any intro block (paginate.ts:155-175). From page 2 on there is no h1 (axe page-has-heading-one), heading navigation lists only the current page's headings, and a say-all reads the visible page, then the pager and the chrome below it, and skips the rest of the section; after Næsta focus stays on the button, because plan:209's rule to move focus to the new page is not implemented (these effects follow from display: none; no screen reader was run). The pager's label and live text ('Hluti 2 af 3 · Síða 4 af 13') never name the sub-section. The only switch to continuous reading is Settings → Lestrarstilling → 'Samfellt skrun', the 15th of 18 Tab stops in the dialog and below its first screen on a phone, with no exposed state (UI-03); the pager nav holds only Fyrri, the label and Næsta. This is not a WCAG 2.2 A/AA failure as such, but EPUB Reading Systems 3.3 §14 (non-normative) asks reading systems to let assistive-technology users read 'without getting trapped at the end of pages'. Whether paged stays the launch default is open call 3; these fixes are needed under either answer.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:148-168 (toggleAttribute 'hidden'), :192-202 (label and announcement text), :517-547 (nav and sr-only status region), :483-484 (childList observer)
- v1.1:src/lib/utils/paginate.ts:155-175 (the module `<header>` is an intro-unit block); measurements `paged[*].pages[*].titleVisible` true only on page 1 (194 false, 9 true across nine runs); keyboard.desktop.aria.hiddenBlocksMechanism {attr: 'hidden', display: 'none'}
- ui-capture_v1.1 keyboardAndAria[11] ('Samfellt skrun' is stop 15 of 18), [4] (focus stays on Næsta after a turn), [17]; layout[13] (the group is below the phone dialog's first screen)
- R4-20 (EPUB Reading Systems 3.3 §14, fulltext) and R4 designImplications[10]; R6-22 (Kindle and Apple Books offer the layout switch from the reading view's own settings)
- docs/plans/2026-04-22-screen-vs-paper-reader-plan.md:207-209 (screen readers cannot be detected; move focus to the new page), :250 (manual screen-reader smoke)

**Fix.** (1) Keep the h1 in the accessibility tree on every page: in applyVisibility() treat `article.cnx-module > header` as persistent and, on pages after the first, give it `sr-only` instead of the hidden attribute at least. The visible running head (section number, title, MT marker) is UI-09's; if it is built, the header collapses into it. Take the header out of the intro unit's measured items and subtract its height from every page's budget (availableHeight), or page 1's split shifts and later pages overrun; that height comes off the UI-08 budget, so count it there. (2) Name the sub-section in the live text, for example 'Hluti 2 af 3: {undirkafli}, síða 4 af 13', and keep the visible phone label short; in spoken text use a comma or a dash rather than '·', since how screen readers voice the middle dot is not verified. (3) Put the mode switch in the reading view with the existing label 'Samfellt skrun' (no new copy, and the help text already names it; 'Lesa samfellt' is the alternative if a verb reads better): an aria-pressed toggle in the section toolbar ahead of 'Merkja sem lesið' and the objective buttons, so a screen-reader user meets it before page 1, plus a button of the same name in the pager nav. Both call settings.setReadingMode; keeping the current block in view is CODE-07's mode-switch anchor. Keep it out of the content root: inside `article.cnx-module` or `.reading-content` it would be paginated as a block, trip the childList observer (:483-484) and shift textAnchor's offsets. If it carries a description, aria-describedby must point at an element holding the text, not at a string. (4) Implement plan:209: after Næsta or Fyrri move focus to the new page (its first heading or block, with tabindex='-1' and preventScroll). Optional: a one-time dismissible line, 'Efnið birtist í síðum. Notaðu Næsta og Fyrri eða strjúktu til hliðar. Viltu frekar skruna? Veldu „Samfellt skrun“.', placed by the same rule, with its dismissal flag in localStorage inside try/catch. New Icelandic copy needs a native-speaker check; the Settings entry stays.

**Acceptance test.** With readingMode set to 'paged' in storage, on every page of §1.2 at 390×844 and 1280×800: getByRole('heading', { level: 1 }) resolves exactly once with the section title and axe page-has-heading-one passes (today it fails from page 2); after a page turn the status region's text contains the current sub-section's h2 text and document.activeElement is inside the newly shown page; Tab from the skip link reaches a 'Samfellt skrun' toggle with aria-pressed='false' before the first 'Merkja markmið sem kláruð' button, and nav[aria-label='Síðuflakk'] contains a button of the same name; activating either sets the stored readingMode to 'scrolled', leaves no [hidden] element in .reading-content and turns the toggle's aria-pressed to 'true'; 'every page of a section fits the viewport' still passes. Manual, in [USER]'s pending screen-reader QA (plan:250): an NVDA or VoiceOver say-all after the switch reads the section to the end.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: a say-all ends at each page and resumes after a page turn. It reads the visible page, then the pager and the chrome below it, and skips the rest of the section.
- NOT: the Settings Tab-cycle evidence is ui-capture keyboardAndAria[10]. It is [11]; [10] covers RecallPrompt's Escape handling.
- NOT: a WCAG 2.2 A/AA failure in itself. No criterion requires an h1, the content is reachable through labelled buttons, and a live region announces each page.
- NOT: a mode toggle placed first in the content wrapper is reached within 3 Tab presses after the skip link. 'Merkja sem lesið' and the objective buttons (5 in §1.2) come first, so it would be about the 7th; put it in the toolbar ahead of them.
- NOT: aria-describedby='Sýnir allan kaflann á einni síðu'. aria-describedby takes an element id, so render that text in an element.
- NOT: the toggle or hint inside `article.cnx-module` or `.reading-content`. There it would be paginated as a block, trip the childList observer and shift textAnchor's offsets.

**Source findings:** UI3-06, PED1-05

### UI-06. Pre-existing on main: the visible desktop sidebar is aria-hidden yet focusable, and the closed phone sidebar keeps its off-screen links in the Tab order (Næsta is 60 Tab presses from the top)

**Severity:** critical · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ Critical, as the verifier kept it: a 4.1.2 Level A failure on every book page at desktop widths and a 2.4.7 AA failure on phones, both on the reading path. Neither branch introduced it; it is live on main.

**Problem.** Sidebar.svelte:113-116 sets aria-hidden='true' whenever settings.sidebarOpen is false, which is its default (settings.ts:78); the code is identical on main and v1.1, and v1.2 has the same aria-hidden at :113. At 1024 px and wider CSS always shows the sidebar, so the visible table of contents is hidden from screen readers while its links stay focusable: axe aria-hidden-focus (serious) on every book page, a 4.1.2 failure. Below 1024 px the closed sidebar is translated off-canvas but not made inert, so keyboard focus walks through 'Loka valmynd', 'Formáli' and every chapter and section link while they are off-screen, failing 2.4.7 Focus Visible (arguably 2.4.3). On the phone Næsta is 60 Tab presses from the top: 6 header stops, then the sidebar, then the last 8 content stops, so about 46 sidebar stops (inferred, not counted). On desktop the visible TOC's stops are expected and the 'Hoppa beint í efni' skip link at stop 1 bypasses them, so there the defect is the aria-hidden alone; on both it makes v1.1's new pager and recall prompt harder to reach.

**Evidence.**

- main:src/lib/components/layout/Sidebar.svelte:113-116 (aria-hidden while closed; identical on v1.1), :504-536 (always shown at min-width 1024px); v1.1:src/lib/stores/settings.ts:78 (sidebarOpen: false)
- ui-capture_v1.1 axe[1]: aria-hidden-focus (serious) on every page, desktop included
- measurements keyboard.phone.tabFromTopToNaesta: 60 presses ('Hoppa beint í efni', 'Opna/loka valmynd', 'Til baka í kafla', 'Leita', 'Stillingar', the theme toggle, then 'Loka valmynd', 'Formáli' and the TOC; the last 8 are 'Merkja sem lesið', 5 objective buttons, a term and 'Næsta síða'); desktop also 60
- Svelte 5.57.1 has MediaQuery(query, fallback) in svelte/reactivity (node_modules/svelte/src/reactivity/media-query.js:37-55)

**Fix.** Fix on main now (it is live; Sidebar.svelte is identical on v1.1, so the fix carries over with the merge). Preferred, CSS only: below 1024 px give the closed sidebar `visibility: hidden`, applied only after the 0.3 s slide-out finishes (a delayed visibility transition) and lifted at once on open; make it visible with `.sidebar--open` and at `min-width: 1024px`, and drop the aria-hidden. That takes it out of both the Tab order and the accessibility tree before hydration, with no JS. Alternative: `const desktop = new MediaQuery('(min-width: 1024px)', true)` from svelte/reactivity and `<aside inert={!desktop.current && !$sidebarOpen}>` with no aria-hidden (the fallback true keeps the prerendered desktop HTML usable before hydration, and Svelte corrects the attribute when it hydrates). Either way leave the `<aside>` unnamed (the inner `<nav aria-label='Efnisyfirlit'>` names the landmark), move focus to 'Loka valmynd' when the phone overlay opens and back to 'Opna/loka valmynd' when it closes, and keep the skip link as the desktop bypass.

**Acceptance test.** At 390×844 on §1.2 with the sidebar closed and readingMode set to 'paged' in storage: Tab from the top never focuses an element whose bounding rect lies outside the viewport or inside the closed sidebar `<aside>`, and Næsta ('Næsta síða') is reached in at most 20 presses (60 at dde768e); opening the menu focuses 'Loka valmynd' and closing it returns focus to 'Opna/loka valmynd'. At 1280×800 axe reports 0 aria-hidden-focus nodes and getByRole('navigation', { name: 'Efnisyfirlit' }) is in the accessibility tree. Run it on main as well as on v1.1.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: a 2.4.11 Focus Not Obscured failure. That criterion covers overlap by author content (sticky headers and footers, non-modal dialogs), not off-screen items; the off-screen sidebar fails 2.4.7 (arguably 2.4.3).
- NOT: about 46 sidebar stops as a measured count. It is inferred (60 minus 6 header and 8 content stops on the phone; about 45 on desktop); the middle 32 stops were not recorded.
- NOT: the 60-press figure as a desktop defect. On desktop the visible TOC's stops are expected and the skip link bypasses them; the desktop defect is aria-hidden on a visible, focusable TOC.

**Source findings:** UI3-05

### UI-07. In light mode the amber focus outline reaches only 2.54–2.92:1 against the surfaces around it, and the glossary term's own focus rule removes the outline in the reading text

**Severity:** critical · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2

_Severity note:_ Critical, as the verifier kept it: in light mode the focus indicator of every control fails 1.4.11's 3:1, on the reading path included (Næsta, in-text glossary terms, the recall textarea). Pre-existing on main; dark mode passes.

**Problem.** The global `*:focus-visible` rule (v1.1 app.css:426-431, main :427, v1.2 :454-458), outside every @layer, draws a 2 px `var(--accent-color)` outline at a 3 px offset over a 4 px halo of 20 % amber. In light mode the outline measures 2.54–2.92:1 against the page, card and accent-light surfaces and 2.22–2.41:1 against its own halo-tinted gap, under 1.4.11's 3:1 for an author-styled focus indicator; dark mode passes (6.64–8.66:1). It is not the only focus style: `.glossary-term:hover, .glossary-term:focus { …; outline: none; }` (v1.1 app.css:1240-1245, main :1229) wins on specificity (0,2,0), so an in-text glossary `<dfn>`, the Tab stop just before Næsta, shows no ring, only a 1 px dotted-to-solid amber underline, a 1.02:1 background change and the inherited halo at 1.20:1. The three search inputs (.pte-, .index- and .glossary-search-input) and the feedback form's `.form-*` fields also set outline: none, and .equation-wrapper, a.cross-reference and .pdf-btn-primary draw their own amber outline at a 2 px offset. It is pre-existing on main and every new v1.1 and v1.2 control inherits it; because the rule is unlayered it already overrides the 11 `focus:outline-none` utilities (the recall textarea measured 'solid 2px #c78c20').

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/app.css:426-431 (`*:focus-visible`, unlayered; the only @layer opens at :7), :1240-1245 (.glossary-term:focus sets outline: none); main:src/app.css:427, :1229
- measurements (v1.2 capture) f_gaps.focusRings: light 2.54–2.92:1 against adjacent surfaces, dark 7.05–8.66:1; recall textarea outline 'solid 2px #c78c20' despite focus:outline-none
- Contrast checks: #c78c20 against its halo over bg-primary, bg-secondary and accent-light 2.22/2.41/2.26 (bg-tertiary 2.06, accent-subtle 1.97); proposed #8a5d0e vs white 5.75 and vs the five light surfaces 4.55–5.75; dark #e8a838 vs the dark surfaces 6.64–8.66; ink #1b2838 vs white 14.92
- git grep focus:outline-none on v1.1: 11 sites (DownloadBookButton, FlashcardModal ×3, NoteModal, PeriodicTable, RecallPrompt, SearchModal ×2, SelectionPopup, the skip link in [bookSlug]/+layout.svelte)
- R4-18 (WCAG 2.2, W3C Recommendation, fulltext); W3C Understanding 1.4.11: an author-styled focus indicator needs 3:1 against adjacent colours

**Fix.** Add the tokens `--focus-ring` (#8a5d0e light, #e8a838 dark) and `--focus-gap` (#ffffff light, #16161c dark) and keep ONE unlayered rule, documented as the only focus style: `*:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid var(--focus-ring); outline-offset: 2px; box-shadow: 0 0 0 2px var(--focus-gap); }`. The solid gap replaces the translucent halo, so the ring's inner neighbour is a known colour on every surface: light ring vs gap 5.75:1 and vs the five surfaces 4.55–5.75:1, dark 6.64–8.66:1. In the same change delete or align the scoped overrides: remove outline: none from .glossary-term:focus, the three search inputs and the feedback form, or the glossary term's halo becomes a 2 px white shadow at 1.10:1 and in-text terms lose their last visible focus cue. Delete the 11 dead focus:outline-none utilities, or pin the rule's position with the test below. Use --focus-ring as the accent-color of native radios, checkboxes and the range input (UI-03). If [USER] prefers ink to bronze, an ink ring #1b2838 on the white gap gives 14.92:1. It can land on main now.

**Acceptance test.** Unit: for both themes, --focus-ring against --focus-gap and against each of the five surfaces is at least 3:1 (fails today: light 2.54–2.92). e2e in light mode with readingMode set to 'paged' in storage: Tab to Næsta, the selected Lestrarstilling segment, 'Vista upprifjun', the recall textarea and an in-text glossary term; for each, the computed outline-style is not 'none' (today the term's is), the outline-color equals --focus-ring, the box-shadow contains --focus-gap, and the pixel-measured ratio between the outline and the pixel just outside it is at least 3. A CSS test fails if a `*:focus-visible` rule appears inside an @layer block or if any :focus or :focus-visible rule in src/app.css or in any component `<style>` block sets outline: none.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] picks the ring colour: bronze #8a5d0e (stays in the amber family) or ink #1b2838 (14.92:1 on the white gap); both clear 3:1 on every surface. It is the same brand call as UI-02's text-on-amber tokens, so decide the two together. This is not one of the four open calls.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: every focus indicator comes from one unlayered rule. Scoped overrides exist, and .glossary-term:focus removes the outline in the reading text.
- NOT: moving the rule into @layer base would strip focus from all 11 focus:outline-none controls. Ten of them fall back to their own amber cue (all under 3:1 in light; DownloadBookButton:193 and SelectionPopup:84 keep a full ring-2 with offset); only PeriodicTable:231 keeps nothing but the faint halo.
