- NOT: the ring is 1:1 against an amber-filled selected segment. That compares it with the element's own fill, which the 3 px offset keeps it from touching; its real inner neighbour is the halo gap (2.22–2.41:1).
- NOT: the measured recall textarea is RecallPrompt.svelte:74. It was measured on v1.2, where the class is at :77; :74 is the v1.1 line.

**Source findings:** UI3-02

### UI-08. Open call 1 (page-height budget): 33–47 % of the viewport is reserved, so a desktop page holds fewer lines than a phone page, and part of the reserve is blank space left by a hard-coded 96 px offset

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** Pedagogy, Code

_Severity note:_ Medium, as all three verifiers rated it. PED1-03's verifier ties it to open call 3: high if paged ships as every profile's default, since every reader would then get pages of 9–14 lines, and medium otherwise. The cost is page turns and comfort, not comprehension.

**Problem.** v1.1 budgets each page as availableHeight() = innerHeight − 96 − gapAbove − gapBelow − 76, floor 240 (PagedReaderControls.svelte:64, :68, :85-90): 427 px at 1280×800, 514 at 390×844 and 683 at 768×1024, so 53/61/67 % of the viewport, with 33–47 % reserved. That is about 15.7 lines of budget on desktop against 18.9 on the phone; fitted pages hold 9.2–10.6 lines at 1280×800, 12.3–13.9 on the phone and 16.6–19.8 on the tablet (a desktop line carries about 67 characters, a phone line about 37–39), and §1.2 is 34 pages on desktop and phone and 20 on the tablet (② preview content). The 87 px desktop-vs-phone gap is about half the shorter viewport (44 px) and half chrome (43 px, mostly the 3rem vs 1.5rem .reading-content padding), and two parts of the reserve are code artefacts, not chrome: scrollToContentTop parks the wrapper top at a literal 96 (:172, repeating SCROLL_OFFSET) under a 56 px sticky header, leaving about 40 px blank, and gapAbove (39–63 px: the .reading-content top padding plus about 15 px of margin above the module header) is subtracted on every page because the scroll target is the wrapper top; together about 80–103 px, about a quarter of the desktop budget. The QA checklist still asks about 'viewport − 260 px' (v1.1 docs/manual-qa-2026-06.md:212), a rule v1.1 no longer has. R1 finds no measured comprehension effect of window height in the tested range (1–20-line windows; windows of 4 or more lines read as fast as 20), while small windows cost manipulation and ease ratings; all of that evidence is from adults at desktop monitors.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:64 (SCROLL_OFFSET 96), :68 (BOTTOM_CLEARANCE 76), :85-90 (availableHeight), :111-115 (measure adds both margins of every block), :172 (literal 96 in scrollToContentTop); v1.1:src/app.css:434-440 (.reading-content padding 3rem 1.5rem)
- ui-capture_v1.1 layout[1]: budgets 514/683/427 px with gapAbove 39/47/63 and gapBelow 119/122/138; layout[3]: 1.1 blocks per page on the phone and at 1280×800
- Re-run simulation of paginate() over the measured block heights: reproduces all nine page counts; trimmed estimates (not browser-verified) §1.2 desktop 34 → 23 pages (fill 0.35 → 0.59), phone 34 → 28 (0.43 → 0.58); a 75 % budget at 1280×800 gives 25 pages of about 16 lines, but only 34 → 31 on the phone
- R1 E17 (Dyson 2004, fulltext review) and E18 (Dillon, Richardson & McKnight 1990, abstract only); R1 designImplications[2] ('maximise usable lines')
- v1.1:docs/manual-qa-2026-06.md:212 ('viewport − 260 px chrome', stale)

**Fix.** Open call 1 is [USER]'s, and ruling 1 (arrival lands past the objectives) holds under every option. Do first, whatever the answer (S): rewrite the stale QA item to describe what the code does today, and again once [USER] rules; make scrollToContentTop() and availableHeight() read one offset constant, at today's value of 96, instead of a repeated literal, so the value changes in one place when [USER] rules (changing one without the other makes the budget assume a content top the scroll never reaches, and pages overflow by the difference); and add an e2e log that prints budget/innerHeight, page count and median lines per page for §1.2 at 1280×800, 768×1024, 390×844 and 375×667, so [USER] decides on numbers. The menu, each trim with its gain: (i) content top at the header's measured bottom + 8 px instead of 96, with that one offset derived from the header element (+32 px); (ii) paged-mode .reading-content vertical padding cut to 0.5rem through `[data-paged-section] .reading-content` (+80 px on desktop, +32 on the phone; more at 0); (iii) the timer pill and phone tools button moved clear of the pager, or into a sticky pager bar (UI1-05), so the 76 px BOTTOM_CLEARANCE can go (keep it until then, QA E18); (iv) collapsed margins counted once in measure() (tens of px). (i) and (ii) together bring the budget to 0.68 of the viewport on the phone and 0.67 at 1280×800, (iii) takes both to 0.77, and the tablet reaches 0.78 without (iii); (iii) is the costly part, in the MobileBottomNav $effect/untrack area of CLAUDE.md's pitfalls and the October tap-overlap fixes. Whatever is chosen, fill tops out at about 0.58–0.59 because pages break only between blocks, and on the phone block size (a paragraph is about half a screen) rather than the budget sets the page count. The budget should also measure the real content top: on §1.3 arrival it lands at 172 px (desktop) and 184 px (phone), not 96, because the hidden pages leave the document too short to scroll that far. Running heads and in-view controls added by UI-05 and UI-09 come out of this same budget. Reviewers' labelled recommendation, not a decision: choose for fewer page turns and comfort rather than tune a constant for learning ('maximise usable lines'), since no evidence ties page height to comprehension.

**Acceptance test.** Decision-independent, failing today: docs/manual-qa-2026-06.md no longer contains 'viewport − 260'; PagedReaderControls.svelte has no literal 96 outside one offset source that both scrollToContentTop() and availableHeight() read; an e2e step logs budget/innerHeight, page count and median lines per page for §1.2 at the four viewports. After the call: if [USER] chooses a target T or a set of trims, the e2e asserts the resulting budget/innerHeight at each viewport, reading the target from one exported constant rather than a literal; under every option 'page 1 shows its controls on arrival' and 'page buttons take taps' still pass and the learning objectives stay above the viewport on arrival (ruling 1). pagination.spec.ts 'every page of a section fits the viewport' is not enough on its own: it exempts single-block and heading-overrun pages.

**Decision for [USER].** Open call 1, the page-height budget: [USER] chooses how much of the viewport a page may fill, from keeping today's 53–67 % to any combination of trims (i)–(iv) or a fixed target fraction. The reviewers lean towards 'maximise usable lines', choosing for page turns and comfort (all three verifiers and R1 support that framing); it is a labelled recommendation, not a decision, and no number is proposed here.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: 39–47 % of the viewport is reserved. It is 33–47 %; the tablet reserves 341 of 1024 px.
- NOT: a desktop page holds fewer lines because of the padding alone. About half of the 87 px gap is the shorter viewport (44 px), half chrome (43 px).
- NOT: v1.1's pages are at or below the smallest windows tested. Windows of 1–20 lines were tested with no comprehension cost and 4 or more lines read as fast as 20, so 9–14 lines is inside the tested no-effect range; the valid point is that they are smaller than the 15-line window rated least easy.
- NOT: about a fifth of the desktop budget. 40 + 63 = 103 px is about a quarter of 427 px.
- NOT: a 32–42 % content share and 1.1 blocks a page on every viewport. Those are the phone and 1280×800 figures (§3.1 is 1.3); the tablet is 40–49 % and 1.5–1.9 blocks.
- NOT: change SCROLL_OFFSET on its own. The literal 96 in scrollToContentTop (:172) must change with it, or pages overflow by the difference.
- NOT: drop BOTTOM_CLEARANCE now. Keep it until a sticky pager bar takes over the floating controls it protects against (QA E18).
- NOT: trims (i) and (ii) reach a 75 % budget on the phone and at 1280×800. They reach 0.68 and 0.67; 0.77 also needs (iii).
- NOT: rewrite the QA item to a new target before [USER] rules. Describe today's formula now and the chosen rule afterwards.
- NOT: R1's window-height evidence covers upper-secondary students on phones. It is from adults at desktop monitors (Dyson 2004 a review; Dillon 1990 read at abstract level).

**Source findings:** CODE1-14, UI1-13, PED1-03

### UI-09. After the ruled arrival scroll, a paged page shows no machine-translation notice or section toolbar (and, below 1024 px, no section title), and no later page brings them back

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ High, as UI1-09's verifier rated it (PED1-09's verifier: medium): the notice is the only sign that the text is unreviewed machine translation, and in paged mode it is gone from every page once the arrival scroll runs. It is not a WCAG failure.

**Problem.** After the ruled arrival scroll (ruling 1, kept), the machine-translation notice ('Vélþýtt efni. Þessi eining hefur ekki enn verið yfirlesin og kann að innihalda þýðingarvillur.', PreviewBanner.svelte:16-22, rendered above the content at +page.svelte:272-274) sits at y = −546 (phone), −370 (tablet) and −346 (1280×800), and the section toolbar (print, share, note, bookmark, 'Merkja sem lesið') at −454/−294/−270; every page turn scrolls back to the content top, so no later page shows either. The banner is probably on screen briefly during load, because init() waits for fonts and up to 1.5 s for images before the arrival scroll (not verified). From page 2 on the h1 is hidden too (UI-05), and below 1024 px the header shows only 'Efnafræði', so a phone or tablet student mid-section sees neither the section title nor that the text is unreviewed MT; at 1280×800 the sidebar keeps the current section highlighted. In the ② preview's chemistry TOC this covers the 136 paged pages (114 numbered sections, 21 introductions, Formáli), none of them reviewed (live chemistry is unreviewed too); the 81 rollups always scroll (ruling 2) and show the banner at the top, as on main. The plan put the position label 'in the section header'; v1.1 shows it only at the bottom.

**Evidence.**

- measurements arrivalChromeAndColumnGeometry[0..2]: mtBanner top −546/−370/−346 px (inViewport false), toolbarMarkReadButton −454/−294/−270
- measurements paged[0], [6], [12]: `pages[*].titleVisible` true only on page 1 of 34/20/34
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:121-125 (gapAbove measured once, from the container top to unit 0's first block), :170-174 (every turn scrolls to the container top), :396-431 (fonts and up to 1500 ms of images before the arrival showPage)
- semantic-drift items[21]: reviewed 0 of 216 sections in the preview TOC; R4-24 (Martindale & Carpuat 2018, abstract: fluent but wrong MT draws less concern; a small adult pilot of trust)
- docs/plans/2026-04-22-screen-vs-paper-reader-plan.md:172 (position label 'in the section header')

**Fix.** Keep ruling 1 and make the MT status travel with the page, driven by section.reviewed as the banner is. This entry owns the visible running head; UI-05 only needs the h1 kept in the accessibility tree. Two designs: (A) a compact 'Vélþýtt' marker plus the section number and title in the existing position-label row, which adds no height, but on an oversized page that row sits below the fold until the pager row is made sticky (UI1-05); (B) a one-line running head at the top of the pager's container on every page, holding the same three items (the module header can collapse into it, see UI-05), always in view, whose height comes off the UI-08 budget, so count it there and show [USER] the design. Showing the full PreviewBanner on page 1 only would need the pager to budget page 1 separately, because gapAbove is measured once (:121-125) and subtracted on every page. Keep injected UI out of the content root: inside `article.cnx-module` or `.reading-content` it would be paginated, trip the childList observer and shift textAnchor's offsets, while anything in the container above the content shows on every page. The marker is a `<button aria-expanded>` (or carries aria-describedby) revealing the banner's full sentence, with a 'Tilkynna villu' link to /feedback (the route exists). Exclude it from both print surfaces as the banner is (app.css @media print and static/styles/print.css) and check its contrast against UI-02's tokens. This is a vefur-side rendering change, not a content workaround, and the 2026-10-03 'Vélþýtt' answer about figures is an analogy, not a request for a per-page notice.

**Acceptance test.** With readingMode set to 'paged' in storage, on arrival and after each page turn on §1.2 at 390×844, 768×1024 and 1280×800: an element whose accessible name contains 'Vélþýtt' intersects the viewport below the sticky header, and below 1024 px the section title is visible on every page (today neither is, after the arrival scroll). With a fixture or component test whose section is reviewed: true the marker is absent (the preview content has no reviewed section). The learning objectives are still above the viewport on arrival (ruling 1), print emulation shows no marker, and 'every page of a section fits the viewport' and 'page buttons take taps' still pass.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: a desktop student cannot tell which section this is. At 1280×800 the sidebar keeps the current section highlighted; the title loss holds below 1024 px.
- NOT: the missing h1 as a WCAG 2.2 A/AA failure. axe page-has-heading-one is a best-practice (moderate) rule.
- NOT: the notice never appears. It is probably on screen briefly during load, before the arrival scroll; after that no page turn shows it.
- NOT: every section is affected (216). The 136 paged pages are; the 81 rollups always scroll and show the banner at the top.
- NOT: [USER] asked for an MT notice on every page. The 2026-10-03 'Vélþýtt' answer is about figures; here it is an analogy only.
- NOT: Martindale & Carpuat 2018 shows that students miss MT errors. It is a small adult pilot that measured trust (verified from the abstract), and no study of students learning STEM from MT text was found.

**Source findings:** UI1-09, PED1-09

### UI-10. In paged mode the 2 px progress bar and the sidebar ring measure the short page's scroll, not the reader's place in the section, because the pager publishes no position

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ High, as UI1-07's verifier rated it (CODE3-12's verifier: medium for the missing seam): in v1.1's paged mode the visible progress cue is wrong on every page, reading about 70 % on page 1 of a 34-page section.

**Problem.** Both progress indicators compute window scroll: the layout's 2 px bar (scrollY / (scrollHeight − innerHeight), [bookSlug]/+layout.svelte:62-80, rendered on every book route) and the Sidebar's current-section rings (Sidebar.svelte:222, :280, :323), which read reader.scrollProgress, written only by the section page's scroll handler (+page.svelte:84-91) and reset to 0 at :107. In paged mode the window sits at the content top of a short document after the arrival scroll and after every turn, so the value follows the current page's height: page 1 of §1.2 at 1280×800 gives 426 / (1404 − 800) = 70.5 % (the bar is 903 of 1,280 px wide) and the last page about 30.8 %; a turn that leaves scrollY unchanged fires no scroll event, so the value can also go stale. The pager publishes no position: its state is private $state (PagedReaderControls.svelte:48-56), its props are callbacks only (:30-40) and it exports nothing, although the bounded 'Hluti … · Síða …' label already exists in its bottom nav (:525-529). The plan meant that label to replace the bar (plan:172: P2.1 'ships as part of this item'), so what is missing is the seam, not the label.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/+layout.svelte:62-80 (bar from window scroll, on every book route); v1.1:src/lib/components/layout/Sidebar.svelte:222, :280, :323 (rings read $scrollProgress)
- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:84-91 and :107 (the only writers of reader.scrollProgress)
- measurements paged[12].arrival.scrollY 426 on a 1404 px document = 70.5 %; bar widths read from screenshots: 903/1280 on page 1, 394/1280 (30.8 %) on the last page
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:30-40 (props), :48-56 (private $state), :206-212 (showPage), :525-529 (label); no exports
- docs/plans/2026-04-22-screen-vs-paper-reader-plan.md:172 ('Replaces the 2px scroll bar (P2.1 ships as part of this item)')

**Fix.** Give the pager one output: an `onposition?: (p: { unit, units, page, pages, index, total }) => void` prop called from showPage(). In paged mode the section page then calls reader.setScrollProgress(Math.round(100 `*` (index + 1) / total)) and handleScroll skips its write, which fixes all three Sidebar rings at once. Hide the 2 px bar while `[data-paged-section]` exists, the plan's 'label replaces the bar' (a CSS `:has()` rule or a flag from the section route both work), and keep the layout's own window maths on every other route. Do not make the bar read $scrollProgress: only the section page writes it, so on /ordabok, /minniskort, /greining and the other book pages it would show 0 or a stale section value. Push the value from showPage(); do not derive the label or the value in an $effect that reads the pager's $state (CLAUDE.md's effect pitfall). The same seam is what P2.1 (the label in the section header), the continue-reading fix (UI1-06, CODE1-05) and the mode-switch anchor (CODE-07) need; add an exported goTo(hash) via bind:this when they do. A public recompute() is not needed for P1.2's card: it renders outside the container, so dismissing it needs a re-scroll or goTo, not a re-split. Scrolled mode is unchanged.

**Acceptance test.** At 1280×800 on §1.2 with readingMode set to 'paged' in storage: the .scroll-progress bar is not visible, either absent or display: none (today it is 70.5 % wide on page 1); after k presses of Næsta the current section's ring has stroke-dashoffset 50.27 × (1 − round(100 × (k + 1) / total) / 100), reaching 0 (a full ring) on the last page; with readingMode 'scrolled' the bar and ring follow window scroll as they do today; on /ordabok the bar still tracks window scroll.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: make the layout bar read $scrollProgress. The bar renders on every book route and only the section page writes scrollProgress, so it would show 0 or a stale value on the glossary, flashcard and analytics pages.
- NOT: nothing on v1.1 supports P2.1. The bounded label exists in the pager's bottom nav (:525-529); the missing piece is the seam to show it in the header and retire the bar.
- NOT: plan:65 puts P2.1 inside P0.4. That is plan:172; :65 is the P2 table row.
- NOT: P1.2's pre-question card needs a public recompute(). On v1.2 it renders outside the container, so dismissing it needs a re-scroll or goTo, not a re-split.
- NOT: a test that the bar is at most 3 % wide on page 1. With the bar hidden in paged mode, assert that it is absent.

**Source findings:** UI1-07, CODE3-12

### UI-11. Closing Settings jumps the window up by 412–434 px, on main as well, because focus returns to the sticky header button without preventScroll under html's scroll-padding-top

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** main, v1.1 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ High, as UI1-12's verifier rated it: both ingredients are on main, so the jump is live and costs scrolled-mode readers their place too, and in v1.1's paged mode every Settings close, including one after a font-size change, drops the pager below the fold. CODE1-16's verifier rated the close jump alone medium.

**Problem.** SettingsModal.close() returns focus with previouslyFocused?.focus() and no preventScroll (v1.1 SettingsModal.svelte:85-88, main :80; v1.1 did not touch close()). Focus lands on the Stillingar button in the 56 px sticky header (Header.svelte:181), and `html { scroll-padding-top: 5rem }` (app.css:145-147, main :146) puts the whole header inside the 80 px padding band, so Chromium treats the on-screen button as out of view and centres it in the padded scrollport, moving the window up by (innerHeight + 80)/2 − 27.5 px from wherever it was: 412 px at 1280×800 and 434 px at 390×844. Measured in paged mode with Loka, with Escape and after a font-size change: desktop 426 → 14 (pager top 552 → 964, below the 800 px fold) and phone 626 → 192 (409 → 843); from scrollY 2000 it lands at 1588, so scrolled-mode readers on the live site lose their place too. A synthetic page with the same header and padding reproduces the numbers exactly, and without the padding or with focus({ preventScroll: true }) nothing moves (probed in Chromium only). SearchModal.svelte:146 restores focus the same way (on main too, not re-measured); the reading-mode switch loses the place for a different reason (CODE-07).

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/SettingsModal.svelte:85-88 and main:src/lib/components/SettingsModal.svelte:80 (previouslyFocused?.focus()); SearchModal.svelte:146 (same pattern)
- v1.1:src/app.css:145-147 (html scroll-padding-top: 5rem; main :146); v1.1:src/lib/components/layout/Header.svelte:181 (sticky 56 px header)
- measurements settings.closeScrollVerification: desktop 426 → 14 (navTop 552 → 964), phone 626 → 192 (409 → 843); ui-capture_v1.1 keyboardAndAria[13], brokenOrSuspicious[6]
- Focus probe on a synthetic page (56 px sticky header, 80 px scroll-padding, Chromium): focus() moves 426 → 14, 626 → 192 and 2000 → 1588; 0 px without the padding or with preventScroll; Shift+Tab into the header also 426 → 14

**Fix.** Call previouslyFocused?.focus({ preventScroll: true }) in SettingsModal.close() and in SearchModal's close(): a two-line change that can land on main now, where the jump is live, and reaches v1.1 with the merge. In paged mode it also fixes the return from scrolled to paged, because init() restores the page from the hash through showPage(). Do not add an interim scrollToContentTop() re-run after the dialog closes; it couples the modal to the pager. Separate follow-ups: Tab or Shift+Tab into the header jumps the same way (426 → 14), and its root-cause fix is to replace, not add to, the html scroll-padding-top with scroll-margin-top on content anchor targets (app.css:1268-1270 warns the two would stack), then re-test anchor jumps; NoteModal.svelte:86 and FlashcardModal.svelte:127 restore focus the same way and are affected only when their trigger sits in the top 80 px. The mode-switch place loss is CODE-07's fix.

**Acceptance test.** e2e with coordinate clicks or the keyboard (Playwright element clicks can scroll on their own): with readingMode set to 'paged' in storage, on a page after page 1 of a section (for example 'Hluti 1 af 3 · Síða 3 af 11'), and again with readingMode 'scrolled' at scrollY above 500, open Settings from the header and close it with Loka and with Escape: scrollY is unchanged ± 2 px, the Stillingar button has focus, and in paged mode the pager nav's bottom is at most innerHeight (today 426 → 14, nav top 964). Repeat with SearchModal opened from the header.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the window goes back up to the header. It moves up by a fixed (innerHeight + 80)/2 − 27.5 px (412 px at 1280×800, 434 px at 390×844); from scrollY 2000 it lands at 1588.
- NOT: the browser scrolls an off-screen button into view. The button is on screen (sticky, at 9.5–45.5 px); scroll-padding-top makes Chromium treat it as out of view.
- NOT: every close lands at scrollY 14 (desktop) or 192 (phone). Those hold only from the measured start positions, 426 and 626.
- NOT: the cause is unverified because a sticky-header focus probe kept scrollY at 426. That probe had no scroll-padding-top; with it the jump reproduces exactly.
- NOT: specific to paged mode or new in v1.1. Both ingredients are on main, and it hits scrolled mode too.
- NOT: a reading-mode switch loses the place through the same focus jump. The switch loses it because teardown's restoreAll() un-hides every block and nothing re-anchors (CODE-07).
- NOT: the previously visible figure ending at y = 4738 comes from the coordinate-click run. That is the element-click run (scrollY 0); the coordinate-click run lands at 14.
- NOT: re-run scrollToContentTop() after the dialog closes as an interim. It couples the modal to the pager; preventScroll fixes the cause.

**Source findings:** UI1-12, CODE1-16

### UI-12. There is no bottom-inset contract for fixed chrome, so the timer pill and the phone tools button partly cover Fyrri/Næsta and the recall prompt on v1.1, and the pre-question and Kvörðun badges on v1.2.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1, v1.2, main · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ UI3-07's verifier rated the missing contract high: partial cover passes WCAG 2.4.11 (AA), but it lands on Næsta, v1.1's core control, with mis-tap risk beside the phone tools button, and on the new recall prompt. UI1-05's verifier rated the pager symptom on its own medium (partial cover: Fyrri at 4-5 and Næsta at 1-3 of 5 interior points, at most 2 pages per measured section and viewport) and UI2-17's verifier rated the v1.2 P1 covers medium. High is kept for the contract as a whole; it is not critical because no control was shown entirely hidden.

**Problem.** Fixed chrome is placed component by component and nothing reserves room for it: the bottom set on main and v1.1 is the timer pill (bottom-left below lg, bottom-right at lg; PomodoroTimer.svelte:120), the 48 px tools button (bottom-right below 1024 px; MobileBottomNav.svelte:176-181), the focus-mode bar (bottom centre; FocusModeNav.svelte:11) and the PWA banners (full width on phones, anchored right from md; PWAUpdater.svelte:123, :147), and v1.2's cloze toast would make five. html has only scroll-padding-top (app.css:146), the book layout's `<main>` has only py-6, and the one reserve, the pager's BOTTOM_CLEARANCE of 76 px (PagedReaderControls.svelte:65-68), protects only pages that fit the budget; the missing contract is pre-existing on main. On v1.1's overrun pages the pill partly covers Næsta on desktop (§1.2 'Síða 5 af 11' and 'Síða 13 af 13', §3.1 'Síða 4 af 17' and 'Síða 6 af 17'), where the 896 px pager puts Næsta at x=1157-1248 beside a 560 px text column, and on the phone the pill covers Fyrri and the tools button Næsta (two §3.1 pages, one §1.3 page); phone text is covered on 4 of 34, 11 of 28 and 3 of 11 pages (§1.2, §3.1, §1.3) at 5-17 of 820-900 sample points, mostly on oversized pages, and on a tablet 27 of 348 points of the recall textarea are covered. The e2e guard cannot see any of it: allowedOverrun() (pagination.spec.ts:147-161, applied at :328) exempts 32 of 34, 24 of 28 and 10 of 11 measured pages, every obscured one among them. On v1.2 the June bottom-right pill also covers part of the pre-question card on phone arrival (a capture with a patched TOC), the first Kvörðun row's badge on the phone and the 2.1 row's 'Ofmat' badge on desktop, but main and v1.1 moved the pill bottom-left below lg, so those P1 positions must be re-measured on main's chrome at the port. Partial cover of this kind passes WCAG 2.4.11 (AA, 'not entirely hidden') and fails only 2.4.12 (AAA).

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:65-68 (BOTTOM_CLEARANCE = 76); v1.1:src/app.css:146 (scroll-padding-top is the only scroll-padding in src; same on main); v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/+layout.svelte:121-127 (`<main>` with px-4 py-6 only)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PomodoroTimer.svelte:120, layout/MobileBottomNav.svelte:176-181, layout/FocusModeNav.svelte:11, PWAUpdater.svelte:123/:147 (identical on main); v1.2:src/lib/components/PomodoroTimer.svelte:116 (the June 'bottom-6 right-6' pill)
- v1.1 capture measurements: `paged[*].pagesWithObscuredButtons` (desktop §1.2 pages 5/11 and 13/13, §3.1 4/17 and 6/17; phone §3.1 'Hluti 2 af 4 · Síða 2 af 9' and 'Hluti 3 af 4 · Síða 2 af 17', §1.3 'Síða 7 af 11'), pagesWithObscuredText (4/34, 11/28, 3/11) and recall.obscuring (tablet 27/348)
- v1.1:e2e/pagination.spec.ts:147-161 (allowedOverrun), :176 and :328 (where it is applied): exempt 32/34, 24/28 and 10/11 measured pages at phone and desktop, every obscured or below-fold page included
- v1.2 capture: prequestion__phone__light__initial-viewport.png and kvordun__desktop__light__seeded-viewport.png (pill over the card and the 2.1 'Ofmat' badge); b_cloze.toast phone (toast x 98-293 against pill x 290-366: a 3 px corner touch, toast z-50 above pill z-40); R4-18 (2.4.11 requires 'not entirely hidden')

**Fix.** (1) Define the contract once in app.css: a --chrome-bottom custom property for the height the bottom chrome needs (the corner controls and their gap, plus env(safe-area-inset-bottom) below lg, and the focus-mode bar's own height in focus mode). Set html { scroll-padding-bottom: var(--chrome-bottom) } beside the existing scroll-padding-top, so focus() and anchor scrolling stop above the chrome (WCAG technique C43 for 2.4.11), and give the book layout's `<main>` a matching padding-bottom, so the end of a section, the section nav and Kvörðun's last row can scroll clear. (2) Fix one stacking order: the tools button and the pill keep the two bottom corners; transient messages stack above --chrome-bottom, centred, never on the focus-mode bar's anchor (bottom-6 left-1/2). Keep the toast host minimal: one fixed stack in the layout, shared by the PWA banners and, at the P1.3 port, the cloze toast. (3) For the pager pick ONE of two shapes, not both: (a) the in-flow pager takes its reserve from --chrome-bottom instead of its own 76 px constant (custom properties compute to their source text unless registered with @property, so read a probe element's height or register the property); or (b) in paged mode a sticky pager bar docks the tools button and the idle timer (or hides the idle pill below lg and pads the bar's right end for the tools button) and owns the inset; a sticky bar also changes the 'nav below the viewport' condition in CODE-06. Do not lift the tools button and the pill above a sticky bar and drop BOTTOM_CLEARANCE: that moves the cover from the buttons onto the last lines of every full page. Either way keep the pager's reserve at today's 76 px: how much of the viewport a page may fill is [USER]'s open call 1, and if [USER] changes it, this one value is where the change goes. (4) On desktop, aligning the pager with the text column (UI-17) moves Næsta out of the pill's corner. (5) Tighten the e2e guard: exempt a page only when its single block is taller than the budget, and on such a page scroll to its end and check the buttons there; run the tightened guard on dde768e as a control, where it must fail. (6) At the P1 port, re-measure the pre-question card, the cloze toast and the Kvörðun badges on main's chrome (pill bottom-left below lg), not on v1.2's June pill, and keep the badges where they are.

**Acceptance test.** (1) getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).scrollPaddingBottom is a px value above 0; today it computes to 'auto'. (2) The tightened pagination guard fails on dde768e (control) and passes after the fix: with readingMode set to 'paged' in the test, on every page of the sections the e2e derives with sectionsContaining() at 375x667 and 1280x720, and of §1.2, §3.1 and §1.3 at 390x844, 768x1024 and 1280x800 in the measurement harness, elementFromPoint at 3 interior points of Fyrri and of Næsta returns the button (on a page whose single block is taller than the budget, after scrolling to that page's end). (3) Tabbing through every focusable element of a section page in both reading modes, no focused element has all 9 of its sample points covered by a fixed element, and the recall textarea and its two buttons, once focused, have none covered. (4) At the P1 port: at 390 and 1280 px the cloze toast's rect intersects neither the pill, the tools button nor the focus-mode bar; at arrival on a section that shows the pre-question, elementFromPoint at its button's centre returns the button; at maximum scroll on Kvörðun, elementFromPoint at the centre of the last row's badge returns the badge.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: six floating elements at the bottom — OfflineIndicator and StorageWarning are top banners (top: 0); the bottom set on main and v1.1 is four components (pill, tools button, focus-mode bar, PWA banners), five with v1.2's cloze toast.
- NOT: the PWA banners as full width at every size — full width only below md; from md they are max-w-md and anchored right.
- NOT: phone text covered on '4-6 pages per section' — it is 4 of 34 (§1.2), 11 of 28 (§3.1) and 3 of 11 (§1.3) pages, at 5-17 of 820-900 sample points each, mostly on oversized pages that scroll anyway.
- NOT: the cloze toast covered by, or overlapping, the pill and the tools button — its box touches the pill by 3 px at rounded corners and paints above it (z-50 over z-40), and it does not reach the tools button; 'overlapsTimer: true' is only a bounding-box test.
- NOT: v1.2's collision geometry as what a port gets — v1.2 keeps the June bottom-right pill (PomodoroTimer.svelte:116); main and v1.1 moved it bottom-left below lg, and the main-side figures were computed, not captured.
- NOT: 'causes mis-taps' as measured — it is inferred.
- NOT: a WCAG 2.4.11 (AA) failure — partial cover passes it; whether any part of Fyrri stays visible on the §1.3 phone page 'Síða 7 af 11' was not measured.
- NOT: 'CI stays green because allowedOverrun() exempts these pages' as shown — CI runs on sectionsContaining('&lt;p', 3) (§5.3, §15.1, §14.3 on this copy) at 375x667 and 1280x720, which were not measured; plausible, not shown.
- NOT: the exemption applied at pagination.spec.ts:323-327 — it is applied at :328.
- NOT: lifting the tools button and the pill above a sticky pager bar and dropping BOTTOM_CLEARANCE — that puts floating chrome over the last lines of every full page, and dropping the clearance re-tunes availableHeight(), which is [USER]'s open page-height call.
- NOT: moving Kvörðun badges to the start of the row — on main and v1.1 below lg the pill sits bottom-left, so a left-hand badge lands under it.
- NOT: testing the pre-question button after scrolling to the end of a section — it sits near the top; test it at arrival.

**Source findings:** UI3-07, UI1-05, UI2-17

### UI-13. The selection popup, where P1.3 adds 'Eyðukort', opens only on mouseup (pre-existing on main), so phone and keyboard readers will likely never reach the cloze button.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P1.3 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ All three verifiers: high. It is a WCAG 2.1.1 (Level A) failure, but on the annotation tool rather than the reading path, it is pre-existing, and P1.3 is not shipped. Raise it to critical if a real-device check shows that a touch long-press never opens the popup, because the cloze feature would then be unreachable on phones.

**Problem.** SelectionPopup opens only from a single onmouseup listener on the content container (TextHighlighter.svelte:449 on main and v1.1, whose TextHighlighter and SelectionPopup are identical; :520 on v1.2); no selectionchange, pointerup, touchend or keyup handler exists, and no keyboard shortcut acts on a selection. In Playwright at 390 px a programmatic selection followed by touchend and selectionchange, with no mouseup, did not open it; that a real long-press or handle drag never produces mouseup fits the Touch Events model (compatibility mouse events are sent only for taps) but was not verified under emulation or on a device. A keyboard (caret-browsing) selection has no way to open it, and once open it is role='dialog' yet moves no focus and is not modal; it renders after the article's ~76 focusables, reaching it took 61 Tabs in the capture, and Escape leaves focus on `<body>`. The gap already blocks highlights, notes and selection flashcards on phones, so it is not a P1.3 regression, but it decides whether P1.3 reaches its main audience: v1.2 adds 'Eyðukort' (an inline SVG, three lines with a gap) beside 'Minniskort', and with the labels hidden below 640 px ('hidden sm:inline') the two are adjacent icon-only buttons that each make a card. The popup's aria-label 'Valmoguleikar yfirstrokunar' looks misspelt (likely 'Valmöguleikar yfirstrikunar'; native-speaker check). Its positioning (the left clip at x=-108, the drift on scroll) is UI-28, and the cloze toast is UI-12 (placement) and UI-02 (contrast).

**Evidence.**

- main:src/lib/components/TextHighlighter.svelte:449 (onmouseup only; the same line on v1.1, and git diff main..v1.1 is empty for TextHighlighter and SelectionPopup); v1.2:src/lib/components/TextHighlighter.svelte:520; git grep for selectionchange, touchend and keyup on all three branches: none serves the popup
- v1.2 capture measurements b_cloze.touch ('phone light touchend+selectionchange only': selection 'losar varma', popupShown false; the same in dark)
- v1.2 capture keyboardAndAria 'SelectionPopup' (61 Tabs, focus not moved in, Escape to `<body>`); main:src/lib/components/SelectionPopup.svelte:67-74 (role=dialog and aria-label, no aria-modal, no focus management), :100/:111/:123 ('hidden sm:inline' labels)
- v1.2:src/lib/components/SelectionPopup.svelte:141-159 (the Eyðukort button, inline path at :154); b_cloze.popup phone: 'Búa til eyðukort með einum smelli' 32x44 between 'Búa til minniskort' and 'Fletta upp í orðasafni'
- R6 designImplications[14] (before claiming the feature for phone-first students, verify that a touch long-press opens the popup)

**Fix.** Fix it on main first: it also restores highlights, notes and selection flashcards on phones, and P1.3 inherits it at the port. (1) Trigger: one document 'selectionchange' listener, debounced about 250-300 ms (it fires continuously while the handles are dragged) and filtered to the content container, reusing handleMouseUp's checks (selection not collapsed, at least 3 characters, inside containerElement); close the popup when the selection collapses. It covers touch, keyboard (caret browsing) and mouse in one path; keep mouseup for an immediate desktop response. pointerup and keyup are not substitutes: pointerup fires when the touch ends, before any long-press selection exists, and keyup misses touch. (2) Make it a toolbar, not a dialog: role='toolbar' with an Icelandic name checked by a native speaker (e.g. 'Aðgerðir fyrir valinn texta'), arrow keys between its buttons, and Escape that closes it and returns focus to the content. Move focus into it only for a keyboard-made selection, never on a touch selectionchange. If a shortcut to reach it is added, route it through keyboardShortcuts (shortcutPreferences, keyBelongsElsewhere) and test it; avoid Alt+Shift, the default Windows input-language switch that students with Icelandic and English layouts use. (3) On coarse pointers keep the actions clear of the native selection menu: below the selection, or docked as a bottom bar above env(safe-area-inset-bottom) that clears main's bottom-left pill and bottom-right tools button (UI-12). (4) Keep short visible labels on phones (two rows if needed), so 'Minniskort' and 'Eyðukort' are told apart by words, using the app's existing term 'Minniskort'; draw the icons from the registry ('rectangle-ellipsis' for Eyðukort, CODE-29). (5) Correct the popup's aria-label after the native-speaker check, and with it the case in 'Yfirstrika með {label.toLowerCase()}' (SelectionPopup.svelte:86). (6) Before release, [USER] checks it on a real iPhone and an Android phone.

**Acceptance test.** (1) Playwright, context {viewport 390x844, isMobile: true, hasTouch: true}: make a selection inside a content paragraph with getSelection().setBaseAndExtent and fire no mouse event; the selection popup, located by role or a test id (not by today's misspelt aria-label, which the fix corrects), is visible within 500 ms. Today nothing opens. (2) Desktop, keyboard only: with a programmatic selection followed by a Shift keyup on the content (Playwright cannot toggle caret browsing), the popup opens and the keyboard path puts focus on its first button; ArrowRight moves along its buttons (to 'Eyðukort' after the P1.3 port), and Escape closes it with document.activeElement inside the article. (3) At 390 px the 'Minniskort' and 'Eyðukort' buttons each show a visible text label. (4) Manual, on a real iPhone and an Android phone: long-press a word, drag the handles, and the popup opens; a colour tap saves a highlight.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the 'Cannot read properties of undefined (reading clientX)' page error in the phone runs as a product bug — it comes from the harness's synthetic TouchEvent with no touch list; a real touchend always carries the lifted touch.
- NOT: as verified, that a long-press or handle drag never produces mouseup — no engine produced a long-press selection under emulation and no device was used; it is only consistent with the Touch Events model.
- NOT: pointerup as the touch trigger — it fires when the touch ends, before any long-press selection exists, so it gives mouse and pen parity only.
- NOT: mouseup plus keyup as the fix — keyup misses a touch long-press; one debounced selectionchange listener covers touch, keyboard and mouse.
- NOT: Alt+Shift+M as the shortcut — Alt+Shift is the default Windows input-language switch.
- NOT: moving focus into the popup whenever it opens — only for keyboard-made selections; a touch selectionchange fires while the reader drags the handles.
- NOT: 'Spjald' as the phone label for Minniskort — the app says 'Minniskort' everywhere.
- NOT: Minniskort and Eyðukort as look-alike glyphs — the glyphs differ (sparkles; three lines with a gap); the problem is two adjacent icon-only buttons that each make a card.
- NOT: clamping x to [8, innerWidth − popupWidth − 8] while translateX(−50%) stays — clamp the centre to [8 + w/2, innerWidth − 8 − w/2] or drop the translate (positioning is UI-28).

**Source findings:** CODE2-06, UI2-05, UI3-09

### UI-14. A one-tap cloze card is saved silently and permanently: no preview, no undo, and no UI anywhere to edit or delete a card.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P1.3 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.2, main, v1.1 · **Also in:** Code, Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ Both verifiers: high. The missing delete is pre-existing on main for every card, and it is what makes the cloze extraction defects (CODE-11) bite: broken cards stay in the study queue with no way out short of clearing the browser's site data.

**Problem.** On v1.2, handleCreateCloze (TextHighlighter.svelte:448-484) adds the card straight to the store (addDeck :466, addCardToDeck :477), clears the selection (:479-480) and shows a 2.5 s text-only toast, 'Eyðukort vistað í „Eyðukort“' (:537-545, a role='status' element inserted together with its text inside {#if}, which screen readers may not announce; not verified), so the student never sees the generated front and cannot undo it; on phones the trigger is a 32x44 icon-only button between 'Búa til minniskort' and 'Fletta upp í orðasafni', so a mis-tap is easy. No .svelte file on main, v1.1 or v1.2 calls removeCardFromDeck or removeDeck, although both exist (main flashcard.ts:216, :196); no branch has an updateCard, and /minniskort offers no edit or delete. 'Hreinsa gögn' on /greining resets only the analytics store (PED-14), so on all three branches a card, once made, is removed only by clearing the browser's site data; this is pre-existing on main for every card, manual ones included. With CODE-11's defect rate, the 'Eyðukort' deck would fill with broken cards the student cannot clean up. The cloze deck is also found by display name (:458), so a student's own deck called 'Eyðukort' receives the cards, and one deck serves every book because FlashcardDeck has no bookSlug.

**Evidence.**

- v1.2:src/lib/components/TextHighlighter.svelte:448-484 (no confirmation; deck matched by name at :458; 2500 ms timer at :483), :537-545 (text-only toast, role=status inside {#if clozeSaved})
- git grep removeCardFromDeck and removeDeck over `src/**/*.svelte` on main, v1.1 and v1.2: 0 callers each (positive control addCardToDeck: FlashcardModal.svelte on all three, TextHighlighter.svelte:477 on v1.2); main:src/lib/stores/flashcard.ts:196, :216 (the unused methods)
- main:src/routes/[bookSlug]/greining/+page.svelte:162-165 calls analyticsStore.clearAllData() only; clearAllStorage() (storageMigration.ts:97-102) has no importer; flashcardStore.reset() has no caller
- v1.2 capture b_cloze.popup phone ('Búa til eyðukort með einum smelli' 32x44 between 'Búa til minniskort' and 'Fletta upp í orðasafni'); v1.2 health mutation K4 (deleting addCardToDeck still shows the toast and no test fails)
- v1.2:src/lib/types/flashcard.ts:14-20 (FlashcardDeck has no bookSlug); the store key 'namsbokasafn:flashcards' is global

**Fix.** Keep the one-tap design and make it reversible; everything works in localStorage. (1) Ship a per-card delete in /minniskort on main now, since it serves every card: 'Eyða korti' with a confirm step and no timeout, through the existing removeCardFromDeck, dropping the card's studyRecords entry with it. (2) At the P1.3 port, turn the toast into an undoable snackbar: one always-mounted, empty role='status' region that receives only the text, including the generated front (e.g. 'Eyðukort vistað: „…“'), with the buttons outside the live region. 'Afturkalla' removes the card, and the deck if this tap created it and it is now empty; 'Breyta' opens FlashcardModal prefilled (front = cloze, back = selection), which needs an updateCard store method that no branch has, or a remove-and-recreate. Keep it visible 5-8 s or until used, pause the timer on hover and focus, and rely on the untimed delete in (1) as the WCAG 2.2.1 fallback. Place it in UI-12's toast stack, clear of main's bottom-left pill and bottom-right tools button; its white-on-amber contrast (2.92:1 light, 2.08:1 dark) is fixed by UI-02's accent tokens. (3) Identify the cloze deck by a stored kind flag (e.g. FlashcardDeck.kind = 'cloze') or a stored id, not by its display name; the validator checks decks only as an array, so it needs no change. CODE2-02's verifier suggested keeping a name match as a fallback for older cloze decks; that fallback is needed only if a profile can already hold a cloze deck made before the flag, and by this review's reading none can, because P1.3 has never been deployed (every deploy in CLAUDE.md's log is from main), while the fallback would bring back the collision with a student's own 'Eyðukort' deck. If [USER] knows of a tester profile that should keep its cards, migrate it once instead of adding a permanent fallback. Whether the cloze deck is per book (a bookSlug on the deck) can be settled during the port; with one published book it blocks nothing.

**Acceptance test.** (1) e2e on main: create a card, delete it in /minniskort through its confirm step and reload: the deck's card count is one lower and the card's studyRecords entry is gone. Today there is no delete control. (2) e2e on the port branch: select a phrase in a section, tap 'Eyðukort', then 'Afturkalla': localStorage 'namsbokasafn:flashcards' holds no new card and no empty auto-created deck; the snackbar's text contains the generated front, and its buttons sit outside the role='status' element. (3) Unit: with an existing student deck named 'Eyðukort' and no kind flag, a cloze card goes to a deck flagged as the cloze deck and the student's deck is unchanged. (4) 'Breyta' saves an edited front, and the card keeps its id and study record.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: 'Hreinsa gögn' as the way out — it resets only the analytics store, although its confirm text promises 'eyða öllum námsupplýsingum'; only clearing the browser's site data removes a card (PED-14 owns the button).
- NOT: the toast covering the timer pill — on v1.2 it touches the pill by 3 px and paints above it; on main the pill is bottom-left below lg, so a port would touch it on the left instead (placement is UI-12).
- NOT: 'Breyta' as a prefilled FlashcardModal alone — no branch has an updateCard, so add one or remove and recreate the card.
- NOT: the snackbar's buttons inside the role=status region — put them outside and announce only the text.

**Source findings:** CODE2-02, UI2-04

### UI-15. Below 640 px the /greining tabs, Kvörðun's included, have no accessible name, and the tablist has no arrow-key movement (pre-existing on main).

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P1.1 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ Both verifiers: high. Empty tab names are a WCAG 4.1.2 Level A failure for every phone user of /greining, live on main today; not critical because /greining is not the reading path, the tabs stay operable, and the fix is one class change plus a small keyboard action.

**Problem.** In AnalyticsTabs (main:src/lib/components/analytics/AnalyticsTabs.svelte:40-41, byte-identical on v1.1) each tab is a decorative `<Icon>`, aria-hidden when it has no label, plus a label hidden below 640 px with 'hidden sm:inline' (display:none), so on phones every tab's accessible name is empty: main's four tabs today, five once P1.1 adds Kvörðun. On v1.2 all five tabs measured 67x44 with an empty name, and axe reported button-name (critical) on 5 nodes at 390x844; v1.2's inline 'scale' SVG has no title, with the same result. The tablist sets role, aria-selected and aria-controls but has no roving tabindex and no arrow, Home or End keys (the APG tabs pattern); on v1.2 ArrowRight from 'Kvörðun' left focus and selection where they were. The defect is pre-existing on main and should be fixed there before P1.1 adds a fifth tab. CalibrationTab's own issues are separate: its matrix and headline colour (UI-18), status tones (UI-16), sort (CODE-26) and tab icon (CODE-29).

**Evidence.**

- main:src/lib/components/analytics/AnalyticsTabs.svelte:40-41 (decorative Icon plus 'hidden sm:inline'; no keydown handler); main:src/lib/components/Icon.svelte (aria-hidden when label is null); the v1.1 file is identical to main's
- v1.2 capture measurements c_kvordun.tabs 'empty phone' (accName '' for all 5 tabs, 67x44, labelVisible false); axe_scoped_shipped 'analytics-tablist phone light' (button-name, critical, 5 nodes); c_kvordun.keyboard (ArrowRight from Kvörðun leaves focus and selection on Kvörðun)
- v1.2:src/lib/components/analytics/AnalyticsTabs.svelte:26-61 (role=tablist/tab, aria-selected, aria-controls; no tabindex management, no keydown), :51-54 (inline 'scale' SVG with no title), :60 ('hidden sm:inline')
- main:src/lib/components/icons.ts:117 ('grid-2x2'); main:docs/design/icon-handback-2026-06.md:73-76 ('grid-2x2' reserved for the calibration matrix)

**Fix.** On main now, independent of P1.1: (1) replace 'hidden sm:inline' on the tab label with 'sr-only sm:not-sr-only' (both Tailwind 4 utilities), or set aria-label={tab.label} on the button, so the visible design is unchanged and every tab is named at every width. (2) Add the APG tabs keyboard through a small tabsKeyboard action: ArrowLeft and ArrowRight move and select (wrapping), Home and End go to the first and last tab, and only the selected tab has tabindex='0'. (3) At the P1.1 port the Kvörðun tab joins the same array as { id: 'kvordun', label: 'Kvörðun', icon: 'grid-2x2' } (CODE-29), which also settles the AnalyticsTabs conflict. (4) CalibrationTab's raw gray/white card palette (dark cards #1e2939 against the app's #16161c) matches its siblings on main, so port it as it is and move all of /greining to tokens (--bg-secondary, --border-color, --text-secondary) in a separate pass.

**Acceptance test.** On main at 390x844 in light and dark: axe button-name reports 0 nodes on /greining, and each tab's accessible name equals its label in the tabs array (today all are empty). With focus on the selected tab, ArrowRight selects and focuses the next tab in the array (the first after the last), Home and End reach the first and last, and exactly one tab, the selected one, has tabindex='0'. After the P1.1 port the same tests pass with five tabs, and icons.test.ts passes with 'grid-2x2' in use.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: CalibrationTab's raw Tailwind palette as a P1.1-specific defect — its sibling tabs on main use the same 'bg-white dark:bg-gray-800' (FlashcardStatsTab 3, ReadingPatternsTab 6, greining/+page.svelte 7), so the drift covers all of /greining.
- NOT: 'the house rule reserves amber for interaction' as the case against the accent '56%' — CLAUDE.md lists badges among the accent's uses; the case is a non-interactive statistic at 2.92:1, below the 3:1 large-text minimum (UI-18).
- NOT: registering a new 'scale' icon to settle the AnalyticsTabs conflict — the registry already reserves 'grid-2x2' for calibration.

**Source findings:** UI2-08, UI3-10

### UI-16. Status colours are raw Tailwind palette values, so the section toolbar, objective markers and rating scale fail AA in light mode, and Kvörðun would add more.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ The verifier's high. These are AA failures (1.4.3 for text, 1.4.11 for icon-only buttons) on page chrome and status indicators, pre-existing and live on main; the reading text and the pager are unaffected, so not critical. The amber 'Gott' rating text overlaps the critical accent-token item (UI-02).

**Problem.** app.css on main, v1.1 and v1.2 has no semantic status tokens (no --status, --success, --warning, --danger or --info), so status colour is raw Tailwind palette, and in the default light theme much of it fails AA on main as well as on v1.1, which restyles the section page around the new pager. On the section page the icon-only toolbar buttons (print, share, notes, bookmark) are gray-400 at 2.37:1 (1.4.11's 3:1 applies), the active bookmark yellow-500 at 1.74:1, 'Lesið' emerald-600 at 3.33:1, a completed objective's white check on green-500 2.22:1, and the reading time gray-500 4.41:1. In flashcards the rating scale, duplicated in /minniskort (:509-544) and ReviewPhase (:308-314), mixes hardcoded red, orange and green hex with the brand amber for 'Gott' (2.71:1) and fades the interval previews to 75 % (2.07-3.97:1). On v1.2, Kvörðun adds more raw palette (amber, emerald, blue) and one new light-mode failure, gray-500 helper text on the page background (CalibrationTab.svelte:110, :173, 4.41:1); its badges and tiles pass (4.52-8.11:1), and its amber-600 '45%' on the white card is 3.20:1, which passes as 24 px bold large text. There is also no token for the machine-translation status ('Vélþýtt') that R4 wants visible in paged mode.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:280-283 (text-gray-500), :305, :322-324, :337, :345-347 (gray-400 and yellow-500 icons), :364-367 ('Lesið'), :396-398 (green-500 check); the same classes on main at :270, :295, :314, :327, :336-337, :354 and :387
- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/minniskort/+page.svelte:226-262 (opacity-75 previews; 'Gott' at :247-251), :509-544 (rating hex); v1.1:src/lib/components/study/ReviewPhase.svelte:308-314 (the same), :352-398 (answer-box and assess-button hex); all present on main
- Contrast from Tailwind 4.3.3 theme values against #f7f4ef: gray-500 #6a7282 4.41, gray-400 #99a1af 2.37, yellow-500 #f0b100 1.74, emerald-600 #009966 3.33, white on green-500 #00c950 2.22; candidates emerald-700 #007a55 4.89 (light) and emerald-400 #00d492 9.29 on #16161c (dark)
- v1.2:src/lib/components/analytics/CalibrationTab.svelte:110, :173 (gray-500 helper text, 4.41), :130-131 ('45%' amber-600 on a white card, 3.20, large text), :141-157 and :196-200 (badges and tiles, 4.52-8.11)
- main:docs/design/design-coordination-2026-06.md:151 (F3 status chips), :167 (no `--status-*` tokens, no MT token); R4 designImplications[15] (MT status visible in paged mode)

**Fix.** Add semantic tokens next to the accent set in app.css, each with a strong tone for text and indicators and a soft fill, in light and dark: --success-strong (e.g. #007a55 light, 4.89:1 on the page; #00d492 dark, 9.29:1) with --success-soft; `--warning-*` in a hue distinct from the accent, so a warning such as 'Ofmat' does not read as a link; `--danger-*`; `--info-*` (blue stays semantic-only); `--status-mt-*` for 'Vélþýtt'; and --rating-again, --rating-hard, --rating-good and --rating-easy, none of which reuses the accent (they may use the blue data-viz range CLAUDE.md allows for rating scales). Then the icon-only toolbar buttons use --text-secondary (5.45:1) and UI-02's accent tone when active; the interval previews drop the opacity and use --text-secondary; one RatingButtons.svelte replaces the two copies in /minniskort and ReviewPhase (the same duplication as CODE-23), and ReviewPhase's answer-box and assess-button hex (:352-398) move to the same tokens. Verify the dark pairs as well as the light ones, because the dark rating backgrounds are rgba overlays. At the P1.1 port, CalibrationTab uses `--warning-*` for Ofmat, `--info-*` for Vanmat and `--success-*` for 'Í jafnvægi', and its gray-500 helper text moves to --text-secondary.

**Acceptance test.** (1) A token-contrast unit test (shared with UI-02) reads the token values from app.css and asserts that every `*-strong` tone is at least 4.5:1 against --bg-primary and --bg-secondary, and every on-fill pair at least 4.5:1, in both themes; it fails today because the tokens do not exist. (2) axe color-contrast reports 0 nodes in the section toolbar, the objectives list and the rating grid, in light and dark. (3) A scripted check finds each icon-only toolbar icon at least 3:1 against its button background. (4) grep finds no '#b91c1c', '#c2410c' or '#047857' in minniskort/+page.svelte or ReviewPhase.svelte (9 hits on v1.1 today). (5) After the P1.1 port, axe color-contrast reports 0 nodes on the Kvörðun panel in light and dark.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: Kvörðun's amber-600 headline failing at 2.92:1 — the '45%' sits on a white card, where it is 3.20:1 and passes as large text; 2.92 is amber-600 on the page background. The headline that fails at 2.92:1 is the accent '56%' (UI-18).
- NOT: Kvörðun's amber badges and tiles as breaking the house rule or reading as interactive — CLAUDE.md lists badges among the accent's uses, and they pass (4.52-8.11:1); the defect is the raw palette, not the hue.

**Source findings:** UI3-08

### UI-17. There is no Button or Switch primitive, so the paged reader's main action looks secondary and the hand-rolled switches fail WCAG 1.4.11 in both themes.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1, v1.2, main · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ The verifier raised it from medium to high. The AA failure unique to this issue is the 1.4.11 switches, in Settings, off the core reading path. The 3.65:1 label on 'Merkja kafla sem lesinn', which on its own would rate critical, is pre-existing on main and owned by finding UI1-08, so it does not lift this issue.

**Problem.** No branch has a Button or Switch component, so primary, secondary and ghost buttons and the switches are rebuilt in each component: about 37 white-on-amber sites (15 class strings and 22 same-block CSS rules on v1.1; UI-02), the ghost 'Sleppa', and Settings switches on a gray-300/gray-600 track (design-coordination F4, still open). On v1.1's most-used new surface this inverts the hierarchy: Næsta, which a paged reader presses about 30 times in §1.2 (34 pages) or §3.1 (28 pages), is a secondary button, a white fill with a 1.32:1 border (PagedReaderControls.svelte:549-589), while the emerald 'Merkja kafla sem lesinn' (white on emerald-600, 3.65:1, 18 px weight 500, not large text; pre-existing on main, see UI1-08) is the largest filled control on every page. The switches fail 1.4.11 in both themes: in light the off track is 1.34:1 against the dialog, its white knob 1.47:1 against the track, and the on state a white knob on #c78c20 at 2.92:1; in dark the off track (gray-600) is 2.38:1 against #16161c and the white knob on #e8a838 2.08:1. In-flow reading chrome also ignores the reading measure: the pager and the recall prompt span the 896 px article container while the text column is 560-608 px (Næsta at x=1157-1248 at 1280x800), and v1.2's pre-question card is 896 px wide. The nordic-clarity plan's §6.5 makes 'next' primary for section navigation; it applies to P0.4's page buttons by analogy.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:549-589 (both page buttons styled as secondary); v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:469-480 (the emerald button; main :442-446)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/SettingsModal.svelte:338-415 (three switches: bg-gray-300 dark:bg-gray-600 track, white knob); contrast: gray-300 #d1d5dc vs #f7f4ef 1.34, white vs #d1d5dc 1.47, white on #c78c20 2.92, gray-600 #4a5565 vs #16161c 2.38, white on #e8a838 2.08
- v1.1 capture layout[8], [9] and the 1280x800 arrival geometry (pager x 352-1248, first paragraph 520-1080, Næsta 1157-1248); contrast[7], [8] (pager border 1.32); v1.2 capture layout (896 px pre-question card against a 608 px column)
- git ls-tree on main, v1.1 and v1.2: no Button or Switch component; main:docs/design/design-coordination-2026-06.md:152 (F4); docs/plans/2026-02-20-nordic-clarity-redesign.md §3 Buttons and §6.5
- main:src/app.css:1630-1636 ((pointer: coarse) min-height 44px on button, a and [role=button], since January 2026)

**Fix.** Add two primitives once the accent tokens exist (UI-02, where the choice between dark ink on amber and white on a darker bronze re-skins every primary button and is [USER]'s): (1) src/lib/components/Button.svelte with variant 'primary' | 'secondary' | 'ghost', size 'md' | 'lg', and an `<a>` when href is given: primary = the accent fill with the on-accent ink and the hover token; secondary = --bg-secondary with a 1 px input-border token; ghost = --text-secondary with --bg-tertiary on hover. The global (pointer: coarse) 44 px min-height already covers height, so add only a min-width for icon-only variants. (2) Switch.svelte, with every state pair at 3:1 or more in both themes: an off track at least 3:1 against the dialog (e.g. #8f7c5c, 3.68:1 in light, with a 4.03:1 knob) and an on track with a knob colour chosen per theme (e.g. a white knob on #8a5d0e in light, 5.75:1; a #16161c knob on #e8a838 in dark, 8.66:1, where a white one is 2.08:1). (3) Make Næsta primary and Fyrri secondary. Demote 'Merkja kafla sem lesinn' to secondary, or hide it while the pager is working; following UI1-08, key that on pager readiness, not on the readingMode setting, because when the pager fails and falls back to scrolling that button is the only completion control. Hiding it is consistent with ruling 3 ('Næsta' on the last page finishes the section). (4) Wrap the pager, the recall prompt and, at the P1.2 port, the pre-question card in the same max-width as .reading-content (`var(--line-width-*`)), so the controls line up with the text column.

**Acceptance test.** (1) PagedReaderControls renders Fyrri and Næsta through Button (secondary and primary) and SettingsModal's three switches through Switch; grep for class strings holding both 'bg-[var(--accent-color)]' and 'text-white' outside Button.svelte returns 0 (15 on v1.1 today). (2) With readingMode set to 'paged' in the test, at 390x844 and 1280x800 the filled control with the largest area in the viewport is Næsta. (3) At 1280x800 the left and right edges of the pager and of the recall prompt are within 2 px of .reading-content's edges (not the first paragraph's, which sits 24 px inside). (4) In light and dark, each switch's knob is at least 3:1 against its track in both states, and the off track at least 3:1 against the dialog background.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: switches failing 1.4.11 in light mode only — dark fails too (off track 2.38:1 against #16161c; white knob on #e8a838 2.08:1).
- NOT: a white knob on --accent-strong as the Switch fix — in dark (#e8a838) that is 2.08:1; the knob needs a per-theme colour.
- NOT: giving every Button variant a 44 px coarse-pointer minimum (from the refuted UI3-15) — app.css already sets min-height: 44px on button, a and [role=button] under (pointer: coarse); a blanket min-width would also widen the phone selection popup past a 390 px viewport.
- NOT: §6.5 of the nordic-clarity plan as the spec for page navigation — it specifies section navigation; it applies to Næsta by analogy.
- NOT: an alignment test 'within 2 px of the first paragraph' — that contradicts the max-width fix by .reading-content's 24 px padding.
- NOT: hiding 'Merkja kafla sem lesinn' whenever readingMode is 'paged' — key it on pager readiness, or the pager's scroll fallback loses its only completion control (UI1-08).
- NOT: medium severity — the verifier rated it high.

**Source findings:** UI3-14

### UI-18. Kvörðun's prediction-against-outcome matrix has no axes at any width, its headline number uses the accent colour at 2.92:1, and its section rows show no sample size.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** P1.1 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.2 · **Also in:** Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ The verifier's high, backed by a measured WCAG 1.4.3 failure: the accent '56%', 24 px bold, is 2.92:1 on white against the 3:1 large-text minimum. Not critical because /greining is not the core reading path.

**Problem.** On v1.2's CalibrationTab the four outcome tiles (:140-157, 'grid-cols-2 sm:grid-cols-4') form a 1x4 strip from 640 px and a 2x2 grid below it, with no row or column headers at either width, so the prediction-against-outcome structure has to be read from each tile's text, and the two accurate cells are styled differently (emerald 'Kunni — og vissi það', grey 'Vissi af eyðunni'), so accuracy does not read as a diagonal. The headline '56%' (:125) is set in --accent-color: a non-interactive statistic at 2.92:1 on the white card in light mode, below the 3:1 minimum for 24 px bold text. Beside it the '45%' (:131) is amber-600 when overconfidencePct > 30 and gray-900 otherwise, so two similar ambers (#c78c20 and #e17100 in light, #e8a838 and #ffb900 in dark) carry different meanings and denominators. The per-section rows (:184-209) are text only, 'Sjálfsöryggi {row.confidencePct}% · Árangur í dæmum {row.successPct}%', with no sample size. P1.1 exists only on v1.2 and is to be ported onto v1.1; what the tab measures (PED-09, PED-10) and the row sort (CODE-26) are separate issues.

**Evidence.**

- v1.2:src/lib/components/analytics/CalibrationTab.svelte:140-157 ('grid-cols-2 sm:grid-cols-4', no header cells), :125 (text-[var(--accent-color)] '56%'), :131 (amber-600 only when > 30), :184-209 (text rows with no count)
- v1.2 capture kvordun__desktop__light__seeded-full.png (1x4 strip, no axes) and kvordun__phone__light__seeded-viewport.png (2x2, no axes)
- v1.2 capture measurements c_kvordun.contrast ('56%' #c78c20 on #ffffff 2.92:1, passes false; '45%' #e17100 3.2:1)
- main:docs/plans/2026-02-20-nordic-clarity-redesign.md:64-66 (semantic colours: warnings amber, success green, info blue); R2 designImplications[6] and R5 designImplications[12] (lead with the flagged cards, no grade-like percentage headline)

**Fix.** At the P1.1 port: (1) Render the matrix as a real `<table>` at every width, with row headers for the prediction ('Man það' / 'Man það ekki') and column headers for the outcome, worded to match PED-08's fix of what the rating records (the review's draft is 'Gekk vel' / 'Gekk illa' rather than 'Rétt' / 'Rangt'; native-speaker check), and the counts in --text-primary. Encode accuracy by position: give the two diagonal cells one shared treatment and tint the two off-diagonal cells semantically (UI-16's warning tone for ofmat, blue for vanmat; CLAUDE.md allows blue as a data colour), with a text label in every cell so colour is never the only cue. (2) Whatever headline survives PED-09's rework (R2 and R5 recommend leading with the flagged cards and a practice button rather than a grade-like percentage), render any number in it as non-interactive text in --text-primary, not --accent-color. (3) Section rows: two thin horizontal bars on one 0-100 scale for the two values, labelled as PED-10's rework decides, the values as text, and a sample size that counts attempts ('tilraunir') or distinct problem ids; the row's badge keeps its words. The row order is CODE-26's fix.

**Acceptance test.** Component test on the port branch with the review's seed: at 390 and 1280 px the matrix is a `<table>` in which getByRole('columnheader') and getByRole('rowheader') each count 2 (header text is not asserted, since PED-08 sets the wording); no element in the tab panel other than links and buttons has a computed colour equal to the computed --accent-color; axe color-contrast reports 0 nodes on the panel in light and dark; each section row contains its sample-size count. All four fail on v1.2 today (no table, the '56%' in the accent at 2.92:1, no counts).

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: 'two near-identical ambers' — they are similar, not near-identical: #c78c20 (gold) against #e17100 (orange) in light, #e8a838 against #ffb900 in dark.
- NOT: the '45%' as always amber — it is amber-600 only when overconfidencePct > 30 (:131), gray-900 otherwise, and on its white card it is 3.20:1, which passes as large text.
- NOT: 'amber is reserved for interaction' as the reason to move the headline — CLAUDE.md lists badges among the accent's uses; the reason is a non-interactive number at 2.92:1, below the 3:1 large-text minimum.
- NOT: an 'Æfa þessi spjöld' session as one new store method — currentCard (main flashcard.ts:403-407) looks up the queued id only inside currentDeck, deleted or cloze card ids must be filtered out, and reviewHistory can hold several entries per card (keep the latest prediction); that rework is PED-09's.
- NOT: '(n dæmi)' as the sample size — it counts attempts, not problems: say 'tilraunir' or count distinct problem ids.

**Source findings:** UI2-07

### UI-19. The module header is paged like any block, so a short section can open on its title alone and later pages show no title.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** Code, Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ All three verifiers: medium. A title-alone page costs an extra page turn and later pages lose the title below 1024 px, but axe page-has-heading-one is a best-practice rule (moderate), not a WCAG A/AA failure, and the document `<title>` still names the section. The arrival shortfall bundled here is medium too.

**Problem.** On v1.1, buildUnits (paginate.ts:155-175) keeps the article `<header>` (the h1; its static objectives div is hidden by ContentRenderer) as an ordinary intro block of unit 0, and KEEP_WITH_NEXT_SELECTOR is 'h2, h3, h4' (paginate.ts:30), so nothing keeps the title with the block after it: at the measured 427 px desktop budget a 389 px first block shares page 1 and a 390 px one strands the title. On §1.3, page 1 is the title alone, 38 px (4.8 % of the viewport) on desktop and 29 px (3.4 %) on the phone, because the next paragraph (455 / 808 px) exceeds the budget (427 / 514); on the tablet page 1 is the header plus that paragraph. In 3 of the 136 paged chemistry pages of the ② preview copy (14.3, 14.4, 16.3) `<main>` opens with a `<section>`, so the header is its own unit and 'Hluti 1 af N · Síða 1 af 1' is the title alone at every viewport whatever the budget; the e2e tolerates title-alone pages (pagination.spec.ts:409-410, an implementer's comment, not a ruling). From page 2 the header is display:none, so titleVisible is false on every later page in all 9 runs, and below 1024 px no page shows the section title (from 1024 px the sidebar highlights it; the breadcrumbs name only the chapter). Separately, the arrival scroll stops short of its 96 px target (containerTop 172 / 184 px on §1.3 desktop / phone and 217 px on tablet §3.1, leaving 84 / 96 / 129 px of the objectives box visible), because showPage() scrolls synchronously (PagedReaderControls.svelte:423, :431) before the {#if ready} pager nav renders at the await tick() on :432, so the page is short by the nav's height (80 / 85 / 80 px) at that moment.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/utils/paginate.ts:30 (KEEP_WITH_NEXT_SELECTOR 'h2, h3, h4'), :101-110 (heading carried with an oversized block), :155-175 (buildUnits keeps the header as an intro block)
- v1.1 capture measurements `paged[*`]: §1.3 pages[0].blocks ['header:38'] desktop and ['header:29'] phone, pages[1] 'p:455' / 'p:808'; titleVisible false on every page after the first in all 9 light runs; paginatorAvailableHeightEstimate 427 (desktop)
- Scan of the ② preview chemistry copy: `<main>` opens with a `<section>` in 14-3-hlutfallslegur-styrkur-syra-og-basa, 14-4-vatnsrof-salta and 16-3-annad-og-thridja-logmal-varmafraedinnar (3 of 136 paged pages)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:170-174, :211, :423, :431-432 (the scroll runs before the nav renders); measurements paged[14], [2], [7].arrival (containerTop 172 / 184 / 217 px)
- v1.1:docs/plans/2026-04-22-screen-vs-paper-reader-plan.md:172 (the position label 'in the section header'); R1 E25 (Mangen 2019: motivates a persistent position cue, does not test one)

**Fix.** (1) Keep the title with the content. Recommended, the verifiers' preferred fix: take the module header out of pagination and keep only its h1 visible on every page as a running title (it then counts once in gapAbove), optionally with the position label beside it, e.g. '1.3 Eðlis- og efnaeiginleikar · Hluti 1 af 1 · Síða 3 af 11', as plan:172 puts the label 'in the section header'. One change fixes §1.3, the three header-only units and the missing h1, and gives sighted readers orientation on later pages; it costs 29-38 px of each later page (58 px for §3.1's two-line title on a phone), which comes out of the page-height budget (open call 1), so design it together with UI1-09's running head and show it to [USER]. If [USER] would rather keep every page's full height for content, the no-cost alternative is: add 'header' to KEEP_WITH_NEXT_SELECTOR (it is matched only against pageable blocks, PagedReaderControls.svelte:131); fold a header-only intro into the first section's unit, with applyVisibility (:148-168) changed to also hide that unit's children outside its `<section>` wrapper, or the folded header shows on every other unit's pages; and on later pages hide the header with an sr-only class instead of [hidden], removing the class in restoreAll() before measure(), or it measures about 1 px on a re-split. Keep-with-next alone is partial: it makes §1.3's desktop page 1 overrun by about 66 px into the 76 px bottom reserve and does nothing for 14.3, 14.4 and 16.3. (2) Make the arrival scroll reach its target (this implements ruling 1; it does not re-litigate it): re-scroll after the await tick() that renders the pager nav, or in calibrate(). The re-scroll alone is not enough everywhere: in the final layout desktop §1.3 can reach the target, but phone §1.3 is still 3 px short and tablet §3.1 41 px short, so also reserve the missing height below the pager and attribution (e.g. on the outer page `<article>`, +page.svelte:231), never as a min-height on article.cnx-module or the content wrapper, which would push the pager down so 'Næsta' lands at the bottom of a short page. The header fix by itself makes §1.3 pass without fixing the timing, so test tablet §3.1 as well.

**Acceptance test.** Under either option: (1) A unit test of computePages()'s DOM classification on a fixture article (a 38 px `<header>` followed by a 455 px `<p>` at a 427 px budget) gives no page made of the header alone; today it does. (A pure paginate() call given keepWithNext: true already passes on today's code and cannot fail.) (2) buildUnits on header + main>section gives a first unit that is not the header alone. (3) e2e with readingMode set to 'paged' in the test: on every page of §1.3 and of a section whose `<main>` opens with a `<section>` (derived from the content, e.g. 14.3 on the ② copy), the h1 is in the accessibility tree and no page's visible blocks are only header, h2, h3 or h4. (4) After arrival on §1.3 at 1280x800 and 390x844 and on §3.1 at 768x1024 (the case the header fix does not cover), the content wrapper's top equals the arrival offset the test reads from PagedReaderControls (SCROLL_OFFSET; 96 px on dde768e) within 2 px, rather than a pinned number; on dde768e it is 172, 184 and 217 px.

**Decision for [USER].** Whether the section title stays visible on every page: a running title costs 29-58 px of each later page, which comes out of the page-height budget (open call 1); the alternative keeps the h1 for assistive technology only. The fix for title-alone pages and the arrival re-scroll need no decision.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the dde768e commit message as current evidence — it describes the title-alone page caused by the old page-1 budget, which that commit removed; the current evidence is the e2e comment and the §1.3 capture.
- NOT: the e2e tolerance at pagination.spec.ts:405-406 — it is the comment at :409-410.
- NOT: axe page-has-heading-one as a WCAG failure measured on every later page — it is a best-practice rule (moderate), measured only in the recall-prompt-open (last-page) state; the 'later pages' claim rests on titleVisible = false.
- NOT: 140-152 px of the objectives visible on arrival — the box's bottom edge is at 140 / 152 px, so 84 / 96 px of it shows below the 56 px sticky header.
- NOT: the arrival shortfall as caused by a too-short document or tied to title-only pages — it is mainly timing (the scroll runs before the pager nav renders; desktop §1.3's final layout would allow the target), and tablet §3.1, whose page 1 is the header plus a paragraph, stops short too.
- NOT: 'no page shows the section title' at every width — from 1024 px the sidebar highlights the current section.
- NOT: keep-with-next alone as a full fix — on §1.3 desktop the header and the 455 px paragraph then overrun by about 66 px into the bottom reserve, and it cannot help the three header-only units.
- NOT: folding a header-only intro into the first section's unit with applyVisibility unchanged — the header would stay visible on every other unit's pages.
- NOT: a unit test that feeds paginate() keepWithNext: true — it already passes on today's code; test computePages()'s classification of the `<header>`.
