- NOT: a min-height on article.cnx-module or the content wrapper for the arrival scroll — it pushes the pager down; reserve any space below the pager and attribution.
- NOT: Mangen 2019 as a test of a persistent position cue — it compared Kindle with print for a narrative text and the overall location effect was not significant; it motivates the cue, and plan:172 specifies it.

**Source findings:** CODE1-10, UI1-16, PED1-07

### UI-20. The end of a section is a dead end: the last 'Næsta' looks like a page turn, then sits disabled, and focus drops to `<body>` instead of moving on to the next section.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ The verifier lowered it from high to medium: after completion on §1.3 the section nav ('Næsti kafli 1.4 Mælingar') was inside the viewport in every captured after-save and after-skip shot, so what holds is a misleading last label, a disabled button left behind and no focus hand-off. The sharper dead end on an already-read section comes from the code and was not checked in a browser.

**Problem.** On v1.1 the last page's right-hand button keeps the visible label 'Næsta' and the aria-label 'Næsta síða' (PagedReaderControls.svelte:531-539), although next() there calls completeSection() (:225-245) and finishes the section (ruling 3). After the press it is disabled (:534) at opacity 0.4 (:579-582), and after 'Vista upprifjun' (the prompt closes 1200 ms later, RecallPrompt.svelte:33) or 'Sleppa' (:36-38) focus ends on `<body>`; no code moves it anywhere. In the captured flow the section nav still pointed onward: after completion on §1.3, 'Næsti kafli 1.4 Mælingar' was inside the viewport at desktop, tablet and phone sizes. The sharper dead end is on a section already marked read: +page.svelte:171-180 opens no prompt there, so the last press only disables the button and nothing scrolls, and on §1.3's desktop last page the pager was already 37 px below the fold before the press (from the code; not checked in a browser). PagedReaderControls receives no navigation data, so it cannot offer the next section itself.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:531-539 (constant 'Næsta' label, aria-label 'Næsta síða', disabled={current === flatPages.length - 1 && completionFired}), :225-245 (completeSection), :579-582 (opacity 0.4)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/RecallPrompt.svelte:33 (1200 ms auto-close), :36-38 ('Sleppa'); v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:171-180 (no prompt when the section was already read)
- v1.1 capture keyboardAndAria[8], [10] (focus on `<body>` after 'Vista upprifjun' and 'Sleppa'); the after-save and after-skip screenshots of §1.3 ('Næsti kafli 1.4 Mælingar' at about y 687-751 of 800 desktop, 680-736 of 844 phone, 911-975 of 1024 tablet)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/NavigationButtons.svelte:102-108 ('Næsti kafli')

**Fix.** Keep the button, its place and its behaviour: it still finishes the section, as ruling 3 requires; only what it says and what follows change. (1) On the last page label it for what it does, e.g. 'Ljúka kafla' with the registry 'check' icon (native-speaker check), with a matching aria-label. (2) Pass data.navigation.next into PagedReaderControls as a prop, and after completion replace the disabled button with a link 'Næsti kafli: 1.4 Mælingar' built from it ('Aftur í efnisyfirlit' when there is no next section). (3) Move focus to that link when the recall prompt closes, after 'Vista upprifjun' and after 'Sleppa', and straight after the press on an already-read section, where no prompt opens and the hand-off matters most. Don't rely on a link inside the prompt's saved state, which lasts only 1200 ms (whether that state should stay until dismissed is PED-19's question).

**Acceptance test.** e2e with readingMode set to 'paged' in the test, on a section whose next section is derived from the TOC: (1) on the last page the right-hand button's accessible name is the last-page label constant, not 'Næsta síða'; (2) after 'Vista upprifjun' and after 'Sleppa', document.activeElement is a link whose href is the next section's URL and which lies inside the viewport; (3) with the section pre-marked as read, pressing the last-page button moves focus to the same link, inside the viewport; (4) no disabled button remains in the pager after completion. All four fail on dde768e.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the section nav starting below the fold at 865 px (desktop) and 959 px (phone) on §1.3 — those figures are §1.2's arrival; on §1.3 the '1.4 Mælingar' link's top is at 767 / 765 px.
- NOT: 'nothing points to the next section' for the captured flow — after save or skip on §1.3, 'Næsti kafli 1.4 Mælingar' was inside the viewport at all three sizes.
- NOT: the already-read dead end as measured — it comes from the code (+page.svelte:171-180) and was not checked in a browser.
- NOT: a next-section link inside the recall prompt's saved state as the hand-off — that state closes after 1200 ms.
- NOT: high severity — the verifier rated it medium.

**Source findings:** UI1-11

### UI-21. The recall prompt opens below the pager, under the last page: on a tablet that page stays in view and the prompt's buttons sit below the fold.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P0.2 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1

_Severity note:_ The verifier's medium, down from the reviewer's high. The buttons are off-screen on one of the three measured viewports, the heading and the focused textarea are visible on all three, a scroll recovers the flow and the textarea is only partly covered, so WCAG 2.2 SC 2.4.11 (AA) is not failed. On the tablet the student can still read the page while being asked to recall it.

**Problem.** On v1.1 `RecallPrompt` renders after the `{#key}` block and after the pager, outside `PagedReaderControls` (section `+page.svelte:459-467`), so it opens under the page the student has just finished, and it can also open from the manual mark-read on any page (`markAsRead()`, :171-180). On the 768×1024 tablet the prompt's plain `focus()` (RecallPrompt.svelte:24-26) does not scroll, because the textarea's top edge is already in view: the last page stays on screen above a prompt that asks for recall 'án þess að fletta til baka', the textarea shows 63 px (y 961-1083 of 1024, with 27 of 348 sample points under fixed bottom chrome), and 'Sleppa' and 'Vista upprifjun' sit at y 1102-1138, off-screen. On the 390×844 phone and at 1280×800 `focus()` does scroll the window (scrollY 790 against 306 on arrival, and 881 against 254), leaving only the last page's caption above the prompt. At 1280×800 the prompt spans 896 px against the 560 px text column, so its 135-character instruction runs on one line, and a layout-viewport resize (rotation or split-screen, as in the 390×500 run) re-splits the pager behind the open prompt, from 11 to 12 pages (PagedReaderControls.svelte:440-455). The prompt is v1.1's (P0.2); main has none.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/kafli/[chapterSlug]/[sectionSlug]/+page.svelte:459-467 (the prompt after the `{#key}` block and the pager), :171-180 (`markAsRead()` opens it on a first completion, from any page)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/RecallPrompt.svelte:24-26 (plain `focus()`), :48-53 (`mt-8`, full article width, 250 ms fly)
- v1.1 measurements extras.recallObscured[1] (tablet: textarea y 961-1083 of 1024, 63 px visible, 27 of 348 sample points covered; buttons at y 1102-1138, off-screen) and [0], [2] (phone scrollY 790 against 306 on arrival; desktop 881 against 254)
- v1.1 measurements, desktop recall run: prompt region 896 px wide against the 560 px first paragraph; 390×500 run: 'Síða 12 af 12' behind the open prompt after an 11-page split (PagedReaderControls.svelte:440-455)
- v1.1:src/app.html:5 (viewport meta without `interactive-widget`, so an on-screen keyboard is expected to resize only the visual viewport; not verified on a device)

**Fix.** In paged mode, give the prompt the page's place. When it opens, hide the blocks of the current page (not only the last page's, since mark-read can open it from any page), scroll to the content top as a page turn does, and focus the textarea with `focus({ preventScroll: true })`; restore the page on close or on Fyrri. Ignore resize re-splits while the prompt is open, so the label stays stable. In scrolled mode the measured runs already had the prompt in view; `scrollIntoView({ block: 'start' })` with `scroll-margin-top` set to the header height plus 16 px is optional, and it keeps the end of the section out of sight, which fits 'án þess að fletta til baka'. Constrain the prompt and the pager to the reading measure (the `.reading-content` width) so the instruction wraps. Keeping the prompt clear of the timer pill and the FAB is UI-12's bottom-inset contract. The fix applies wherever the prompt opens, so it does not touch open call 2 (per section or per sub-section), and ruling 3 is unchanged: 'Næsta' on the last page still finishes the section, which is what opens the prompt on a first completion.

**Acceptance test.** E2E with `readingMode` set explicitly in each test, never taken from the launch default. (1) With 'paged', at 768×1024 and 390×844, finish a section with Næsta on its last page: while the prompt is open no block of that page is visible, and the textarea, 'Sleppa' and 'Vista upprifjun' lie between the header's bottom edge and `innerHeight`, with `document.elementFromPoint` at each centre returning that element (fails today on the tablet: buttons at y 1102-1138). (2) The same when the prompt is opened by the manual mark-read control on page 1, wherever that control remains. (3) Resizing to 390×500 with the prompt open leaves the pager label text unchanged (today it goes from 11 to 12 pages). (4) At 1280×800 the prompt is no wider than `.reading-content` (896 px against 560 today). (5) With 'scrolled', the textarea and both buttons are in view and uncovered once the prompt opens.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: severity high; the verifier set medium (buttons off-screen on one of three viewports, SC 2.4.11 not failed).
- NOT: that `focus()` does not scroll; it scrolled the window on the phone (scrollY 790 against 306) and at 1280×800 (881 against 254), and only on the tablet did it not.
- NOT: that the last page stays on screen on every device; it does fully only on the tablet, while on the phone and the desktop only its caption stays above the prompt.
- NOT: the 390×500 run as an on-screen-keyboard stand-in; it resizes the layout viewport (rotation, split-screen, a window resize), and with no `interactive-widget` in the viewport meta a real keyboard is expected to resize only the visual viewport, so the 11-to-12 re-split would not happen under it (not verified on a device).
- NOT: an instruction of about 130 characters; it is 135.

**Source findings:** UI1-10

### UI-22. With no pre-paint script, readers on the dark theme get a light first paint on every full page load (on main too), and P1.4 reads the OS preference once and freezes it into storage, as its spec says.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P1.4 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ All three verifiers gave medium. The flash is a visual defect on every full load for stored-dark readers (inferred, not timed), and the freeze follows the documented spec, so that half is a spec critique backed by R4 rather than a broken feature; no data loss and no AA failure.

**Problem.** No branch sets the theme before first paint: main's `src/app.html` has no theme script (its only inline script is the GoatCounter loader, :18-26), there are no hooks, and the `dark` class is added only by the settings store's subscribe (main settings.ts:107-118) and the root layout's `$effect` (main `+layout.svelte:23-27`), both after the bundle runs. A reader who stored 'dark' therefore gets the prerendered light page first on every full page load, on main and the live site today and on v1.1 too; this is inferred from the pre-hydration state (with JavaScript off and the OS dark the page paints a light body, rgb(247,244,239), on a Vite dev server) and was not timed on a production build. v1.1 and main never read `matchMedia` and default to light; v1.2's P1.4 reads `prefers-color-scheme` only when nothing is stored (settings.ts:108-112), and the store's first subscribe saves the whole state (:122-124), so a fresh OS-dark profile stores 'dark' as if chosen and stays dark after the OS switches to light (measured), and every OS-dark first visitor also gets the flash. That freeze is P1.4's documented behaviour ('prefers-color-scheme only when unset', plan :59; 'honored on first visit only … a saved theme still wins', design-coordination-2026-06.md:130-131), so following the OS until the reader chooses is a spec change, which R4 DI7 supports. No test reaches the new branch: jsdom has no `matchMedia` (mutants S2 and S3 survived) and no e2e emulates `colorScheme: 'dark'`.

**Evidence.**

- main:src/app.html:18-26 (the only inline script is the GoatCounter loader; no theme script, no `src/hooks*`); main:src/lib/stores/settings.ts:107-118 (subscribe saves the state and toggles `.dark` after the bundle runs); main:src/routes/+layout.svelte:23-27
- v1.2:src/lib/stores/settings.ts:108-112 (first-visit `matchMedia`), :122-124 (the first subscribe saves it); main:src/lib/stores/settings.ts:76 (validator `isOneOf('light', 'dark')`), :136-140 (binary `toggleTheme`)
- v1.2 measurements d_settings_typography.firstVisit (Vite dev server): a fresh OS-dark profile stores theme 'dark'; the same profile reloads with htmlClass 'dark' after the OS switches to light; with JavaScript off and the OS dark, bodyBg rgb(247,244,239) and no inline theme script
- docs/plans/2026-04-22-screen-vs-paper-reader-plan.md:59; main:docs/design/design-coordination-2026-06.md:130-131; health_v1.2 mutations S2-S4 (the `matchMedia` branch's true arm is uncovered)
- R4 DI7 (Ljóst / Dökkt / Fylgja kerfi, a live listener, light when the OS states no preference); R4-12 (Piepenbrock 2013, abstract, adults: dark text on light reads better); R4-13 (Legge 1985, abstract: polarity does not change reading rate for normal vision, and some low-vision readers do better in dark mode)

**Fix.** (1) Pre-paint script, which can land on main ahead of the port, together with CODE-05's step (4): an inline script of about eight lines at the top of `<head>` in `src/app.html` that reads 'namsbokasafn:settings' inside try/catch (both localStorage and `JSON.parse` can throw), applies the store's own theme validation, and sets the `dark` class and `color-scheme` on `<html>` before first paint, with light when nothing valid is stored. Inline scripts already run on the live site (the GoatCounter loader and SvelteKit's inline bootstrap; there is no `kit.csp`), so no CSP change is needed, but confirm the live header, because nginx-config-example.conf is not production. (2) When porting P1.4, replace first-visit seeding with 'follow the OS until the reader chooses', built on CODE-05's explicit-choice record (Option A's chosen marks or Option B's sparse storage) rather than a flag of its own: an unchosen theme resolves through `matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)')` with a live change listener and falls back to light when the OS states no preference (R4-12), and `setTheme` and `toggleTheme` (the header button and the 't' shortcut) count as explicit choices. A stored 'system' value touches the Theme type, the validator, `toggleTheme` (which must flip from the effective theme), the subscriber's class toggle, the cross-tab path and the header icon, so add a resolved-theme derived store for every `$theme === 'dark'` consumer, and extend the head script to resolve an unchosen theme with `matchMedia`. If [USER] wants the choice visible (decision (c)), Settings gets 'Ljóst / Dökkt / Fylgja kerfi' (R4 DI7). (3) Existing profiles: main has already stored theme 'light' in every profile that loaded the site, so 'persist only explicit choices' cannot be applied backwards; the migration follows [USER]'s choice in the decision note. (4) Tests: `settings.test.ts` with a stubbed `window.matchMedia`, and e2e under `colorScheme: 'dark'`. `settings.reset()` needs nothing for readers: nothing outside the tests calls it.

**Acceptance test.** Main, after step 1: the prerendered `build/index.html` contains the theme script, and in Playwright with `colorScheme: 'dark'`, a stored 'dark' theme and only the JavaScript blocked (`page.route('**/_app/immutable/{entry,chunks,nodes}/**', r => r.abort())`, so the CSS still loads), `document.documentElement` has the class `dark` at DOMContentLoaded and the body has the dark theme's background (fails today: no inline script). After the P1.4 port: unit tests with a stubbed `matchMedia` show that empty storage under OS dark gives a resolved theme of dark while the stored theme is 'system' or absent, never 'dark'; that flipping the stub to light with no explicit choice makes the theme follow without a reload; and that an explicit `setTheme('light')` or `toggleTheme()` survives a reload under OS dark. E2E: a fresh profile under `colorScheme: 'dark'` has `html.dark` before hydration (the same JS-blocked check), and `page.emulateMedia({ colorScheme: 'light' })` then removes it without a reload. A stored pre-change profile loads as [USER]'s migration choice says, and loading it twice changes nothing.

**Decision for [USER].** Three choices for [USER], none of them one of the four open calls. (a) Whether P1.4's documented 'follow the OS on first visit only' becomes 'follow the OS until the reader chooses' (R4 DI7 supports it). (b) How existing profiles are treated: main has stored theme 'light' in every profile that loaded the site, and a stored 'light' cannot be told from a chosen one, so either migrate a pre-change stored 'light' to follow the OS once (this overrides readers who picked light on purpose) or treat existing profiles as chosen and leave them on light, so that only new profiles follow the OS. It has the same shape as CODE-05's 'medium' to 'narrow' line-width choice. (c) Whether Settings shows the choice as 'Ljóst / Dökkt / Fylgja kerfi' (R4 DI7) or the header toggle stays the only control.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: that v1.1 freezes the OS preference; only v1.2 reads `matchMedia`, while v1.1 and main always default to light and share only the missing pre-paint script.
- NOT: the freeze as a bug against the spec; it is P1.4's documented behaviour (plan :59; design-coordination :130-131), so changing it is a spec change.
- NOT: a measured or timed flash on a production build; it is inferred from the pre-hydration state captured with JavaScript off on a Vite dev server.
- NOT: `settings.reset()` as a path readers hit; it returns light whatever the OS says, but nothing outside the tests calls it.
- NOT: the v1.2 persistence at settings.ts:118-123; the first-visit branch is :108-112, and the saving subscribe is :122, calling `safeSetItem` at :124.
- NOT: 'persist only explicit choices' as enough on its own; every existing profile already stores theme 'light' without having chosen it, so a migration rule ([USER]'s choice) and an explicit-choice record are needed.
- NOT: effort S; a 'system' value touches six places, plus the migration.

**Source findings:** CODE2-16, UI2-14, UI3-11

### UI-23. The reader-font classes only set a variable that the article reads and share their names with Tailwind's font utilities, so the settings preview never shows the chosen face and the chrome renders in the device's system serif (on main too).

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P1.4 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ All three verifiers gave medium (UI2-09 down from the reviewer's high): reading text does get the chosen face and no WCAG or learning impact was measured, but a reader cannot see a font before choosing it, and the system-serif chrome is a long-standing visual inconsistency on main.

**Problem.** On every branch the reader-font classes (`.font-serif`, `.font-sans`, `.font-opendyslexic` and v1.2's `.font-hyperlegible`) only set `--font-family-base`, and the only consumer of that variable is `article.cnx-module` (content.css:54 on v1.2). The Forskoðun preview paragraph and the font-card names set no font-family, so none shows its own face: with Atkinson selected the preview computes to 'Literata, Georgia, serif', OpenDyslexic's preview and label are Literata as well (on main too), and the Serif and Sans cards show Tailwind's default stacks instead of Literata and the reading sans ('Neue Helvetica W01', Helvetica, Arial). Because `font-serif` and `font-sans` are also Tailwind 4 utilities, the book layout's wrapper class puts all chrome (the pager label, Næsta, the recall prompt, flashcards, the sidebar) in Tailwind's ui-serif stack under the default 'serif' setting, rendered as Liberation Serif in the Linux capture, while the article is Literata and the brand Bricolage; OpenDyslexic (on main) and Atkinson (P1.4) have no Tailwind namesake, so choosing either drops the chrome to body Literata. The unlayered '.font-hyperlegible h1–h6' and '.font-opendyslexic h1–h6' rules lose to '.reading-content h1–h4' (same specificity, later in the file), so reading headings stay Bricolage and only chrome headings such as the dialog title, an `<h2 class="font-sans">`, change face. P1.4 copies main's pattern and adds a fourth branch to the same ternary in three places, design-coordination F1's agreed fix (chrome on the heading family) and its `--font-display` token never landed, and the native radios render in Chromium blue against the amber-accent rule.

**Evidence.**

- v1.2:src/app.css:294-308 (the four classes set only `--font-family-base`), :310-317 (`.font-hyperlegible` h1-h6, unlayered), :473-479 (`.reading-content` h1-h4 in Bricolage, later, wins); v1.2:static/styles/content.css:54 (the only consumer); main: app.css:280-299 and :446-450 (the same pattern)
- v1.2 measurements d_settings_typography: modal['preview light'] (class includes font-hyperlegible, fontFamily 'Literata, Georgia, serif'); fonts['atkinson desktop light'] (reading paragraph Atkinson, reading h1 Bricolage, breadcrumb, pager and body Literata)
- v1.1 measurements extras.fonts (CDP): the pager label, Næsta, the recall heading and flashcard strings declare 'ui-serif, Georgia, …' and render as Liberation Serif; article Literata; brand Bricolage; v1.1:node_modules/tailwindcss/theme.css:5 (Tailwind 4.3.3 `--font-serif`)
- v1.2:src/routes/[bookSlug]/+layout.svelte:83-89 and v1.2:src/lib/components/SettingsModal.svelte:259, :397 (the same ternary three times: the layout falls back to font-serif, the preview to font-sans); main: [bookSlug]/+layout.svelte:75-79, SettingsModal.svelte:248, :396; no e2e test references the class names
- main:docs/design/design-coordination-2026-06.md:31 (the chrome typeface is the design track's), :150 (F1: point chrome at the heading family, Bricolage), :166 (`--font-display` not in code); v1.2:src/lib/components/SettingsModal.svelte:251-257 (radio with no accent-color; the slider has it at :205)

**Fix.** (1) Rename the reader classes so they no longer collide with Tailwind (`reader-font-serif`, `reader-font-sans`, `reader-font-opendyslexic`, `reader-font-hyperlegible`) in app.css, the book layout and SettingsModal, still setting `--font-family-base` only, and replace the three ternaries with one `FONT_FAMILY_CLASS: Record<FontFamily, string>` exported next to the FontFamily type, so P1.4's Atkinson becomes one map entry. This can land on main first (it fixes OpenDyslexic's preview there) or as the first commit of the P1.4 port. (2) Give the Forskoðun paragraph and each font-card name `font-family: var(--font-family-base)`; each element carries its own option class, so each then shows its own face. Cheap additions: a one-line specimen in each card in its own face ('Þ ð æ ö · I l 1 · O 0'), the preview moved directly under the font list (the dialog blurs the page behind it), and `accent-color: var(--accent-color)` on the radios, as the slider already has. (3) The rename visibly moves every book-route control on the live site from the system serif to body Literata, so check it with screenshots across routes, not only in the modal. Where the chrome lands is the design track's call (design-coordination :31): F1 agreed Bricolage through a `--font-display` token in `@theme` (mapping `--font-sans` to it would also change 16 font-sans uses in 5 files and preflight's html default); either way a chosen web font, not the device's. (4) Two scope choices for [USER] (see the decision note): whether reading headings follow the reading font under OpenDyslexic and Atkinson (scoping the overrides as `.reader-font-x .reading-content :is(h1, …)` makes them outrank `.reading-content h1`, a behaviour change; otherwise delete the dead h1-h6 rules), and which study-text surfaces beyond `article.cnx-module` follow the reading font (flashcard faces, the recall textarea, glossary definitions; accessibility fonts are the code track's domain, design-coordination §1). No new font files, so no precache cost; P1.4's Atkinson licence and precache work is CODE-12 and CODE-30.

**Acceptance test.** Playwright, for each value the fontFamily validator allows: open Stillingar and choose it; the Forskoðun paragraph's computed font-family equals that of `article.cnx-module p` (fails today for every option: Atkinson and OpenDyslexic show Literata, Serif and Sans show Tailwind's stacks), and each card name's computed family starts with its own option's family. The pager label's and the dialog title's computed font-family is the same under every option, and CDP `CSS.getPlatformFontsForNode` on the pager label reports a web font rather than a system face (Liberation Serif today), whichever face the design track picks. The checked radio's computed accent-color equals `--accent-color`. Unit: `Object.keys(FONT_FAMILY_CLASS)` equals the validator's allowed values. `grep -nE "'font-(serif|sans)'" src/routes/[bookSlug]/+layout.svelte` returns nothing.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] decides, and none of it is one of the four open calls: (a) whether reading headings follow the reading font under OpenDyslexic and Atkinson; (b) which study-text surfaces beyond the article follow it (flashcards, the recall textarea, glossary definitions); (c) with the design track (design-coordination :31, F1), the chrome face the rename lands on: body Literata, which is what the rename alone gives, or Bricolage per F1.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: that P1.4 introduced the pattern; main already has the variable-only `.font-opendyslexic`, the same losing heading rule and the same ternaries, so OpenDyslexic's preview and label are broken on main and P1.4 adds a second case.
- NOT: the card-label values ('ui-serif, Georgia, …' for Serif, Atkinson and OpenDyslexic) as measured with Atkinson selected; they were captured with Serif checked, and with Atkinson selected the Atkinson and OpenDyslexic names inherit Literata.
- NOT: 'today ui-serif' for the Atkinson-selected label in the acceptance test; it is Literata.
- NOT: that the dialog title was in Bricolage before the override; it is an `<h2 class="font-sans">` in the system sans, and the h1-h6 overrides move it to Atkinson or OpenDyslexic.
- NOT: 'ui-sans-serif, system-ui, …' as the sans stack on main and v1.1; that is Tailwind 4.3.0's (v1.2 only), while main and v1.1 (Tailwind 4.3.3) resolve font-sans to '-apple-system, BlinkMacSystemFont, "Segoe UI", Roboto, …', still a system sans.
- NOT: New York, Georgia or a Noto serif as what students' devices show; that is an inference, only Liberation Serif was measured, and the sidebar link's rendered face was not captured.
- NOT: the chrome dropping to Literata as new in P1.4; choosing OpenDyslexic already does it on main.
- NOT: three identical ternaries; the layout falls back to font-serif and the preview to font-sans.
- NOT: severity high; the verifier set medium.
- NOT: an acceptance test that lets only `article.cnx-module p` follow the reading font; which study surfaces follow it is [USER]'s choice.

**Source findings:** CODE2-15, UI2-09, UI3-12

### UI-24. There is no automated accessibility gate (no axe scans, no token-contrast test), so v1.1's CI passed while this review's axe runs recorded contrast, aria-hidden-focus and heading failures.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ The verifier's medium: not a reader-facing defect in itself, but without a gate the contrast and focus fixes slated before release can regress unnoticed, and one WCAG 4.1.2 failure (aria-hidden-focus) is already live on main.

**Problem.** main, v1.1 and v1.2 have no axe dependency and no contrast test: `e2e/accessibility.spec.ts` (v1.1 :1-229) checks headings, lang, meta, aria-labels, landmarks and one reduced-motion case by hand. CI run 37191708681 on dde768e succeeded (e2e 111: 72 passed, 39 skipped) while the gather agents' axe-core 4.10.3 runs recorded color-contrast, aria-hidden-focus and page-has-heading-one on v1.1 surfaces; button-name (5 nodes, critical) was measured on v1.2's analytics tablist at 390 px, and the same pattern is present by code on v1.1 and main (AnalyticsTabs.svelte:40-41: an aria-hidden icon beside a `hidden sm:inline` label) but was not measured there. Of these, the WCAG-tagged rules are color-contrast (1.4.3), aria-hidden-focus and button-name (4.1.2); page-has-heading-one and the landmark rules are best-practice in axe 4.10.3, and aria-hidden-focus is already live on main (Sidebar.svelte:114). Without a gate, the token edits planned for contrast (UI-02) can regress silently. An axe gate would not catch everything this review found: focus falling to `<body>`, Escape not dismissing the recall prompt, the pager under the timer pill and segmented buttons without aria-pressed came from keyboard, layout and screenshot probes.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:package.json, and main and v1.2 likewise (no axe dependency); v1.1:e2e/accessibility.spec.ts:1-229 (hand checks only; the reduced-motion case at :96-116 covers the landing page)
- CI run 37191708681 on dde768e: security, lint-and-test and e2e all succeeded (e2e 111 tests: 72 passed, 39 skipped)
- ui-capture_v1.1 axe[0]-[6] (color-contrast, including 'Vista upprifjun' with text typed, 'Síðuskipt' and '.flashcard-hint'; aria-hidden-focus; page-has-heading-one); v1.2 measurements axe_scoped_shipped (button-name ×5 on the analytics tablist at 390 px, light and dark); main Sidebar.svelte:114 (aria-hidden-focus)
- axe-core 4.10.3 checks in jsdom: under `.include()` scoping, page-has-heading-one and both landmark rules come back inapplicable, and the label rule passes a placeholder-only textarea, which RecallPrompt's is (v1.1 RecallPrompt.svelte:68-75)
- v1.1:.github/workflows/ci.yml:131 (the blocking audit is `npm audit --audit-level=high --omit=dev`, so a devDependency does not touch it)

**Fix.** Add @axe-core/playwright as a devDependency (dev tree only; the blocking audit runs with `--omit=dev`). In `e2e/accessibility.spec.ts`, in light and dark at 390×844 and 1280×800: (a) `.include()`-scoped scans of the new surfaces, failing on color-contrast, button-name, label and aria-hidden-focus: the paged section page (pager, objectives, toolbar; set `readingMode` in the test), the open recall prompt with text typed (the 'Vista upprifjun' node exists only then), the Settings dialog, /greining's tablist (today's button-name failure) and, once ported, the pre-question, the selection toolbar and Kvörðun; (b) one unscoped pass per page for page-has-heading-one, or a direct assertion of exactly one visible h1, because scoped runs return that rule and the landmark rules inapplicable; disable the landmark rules there with `disableRules` until efni's fix for the nested `<main>` lands (UI3-17). Assert separately that RecallPrompt's textarea has a label other than its placeholder (a `<label>`, aria-label or aria-labelledby), which axe's label rule does not check. Write the /minniskort predict and rating scans after the main merge, on #249's approach, because on v1.1 a navigation race skips all six flashcard specs. Derive content-bearing fixtures (the objectives) from content, per the e2e-fixtures rule. Add a pure-Node token-contrast vitest (no new dependency) that reads the light and dark token values from app.css and asserts 4.5:1 for each text-on-background and on-accent pair. Run the new scans on dde768e first as a control: they must fail there.

**Acceptance test.** The gate is done when its control run on dde768e fails on the nodes this review recorded: the scoped scans on 'Vista upprifjun' (text typed) and 'Síðuskipt', the unscoped pass or h1 assertion on the surfaces where ui-capture_v1.1 recorded page-has-heading-one, and the textarea-label assertion (placeholder only); after the main merge, the /minniskort scan fails on '.flashcard-hint'. Each check then passes, in both themes at both widths, once the fix its failing rule points at has landed (contrast: UI-02; the textarea: a label; the /greining button-name, the h1 and Sidebar's aria-hidden-focus: their own issues), so a red scan after UI-02 alone is expected, not a broken gate. The token test passes on the fixed tokens and fails when one listed pair is put back below 4.5:1 (for example white on `--accent-color`, 2.92:1 in light).

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: that every failure in this review came from axe or contrast scripts; keyboard, layout and screenshot probes found many (focus to `<body>`, Escape not closing the recall prompt, the pager under the timer pill and FAB, segmented buttons without aria-pressed), which an axe gate would not catch either.
- NOT: button-name as measured on v1.1; it was measured on v1.2's analytics tablist at 390 px (5 nodes), and on v1.1 and main it is present by code (AnalyticsTabs.svelte:40-41) but unmeasured.
- NOT: page-has-heading-one and the landmark rules as WCAG failures; axe 4.10.3 tags them best-practice.
- NOT: failing on page-has-heading-one, or marking the landmark violations expected, inside `.include()`-scoped scans; scoped runs return those rules inapplicable.
- NOT: axe's label rule as the check for RecallPrompt's textarea; it passes a placeholder-only textarea.
- NOT: /minniskort scans written on v1.1; a navigation race skips all six flashcard specs there, so write them after the main merge (#249).

**Source findings:** UI3-16

### UI-25. Animations ignore prefers-reduced-motion across the site (on main too), including v1.1's new recall fly-in, and the documented 'full reset' does not exist.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ Medium, the highest a verifier gave (UI3-13), because the merged issue is the whole gap: looping keyframe animations and smooth scrolls with no reduced-motion path, a documented contract (design doc §3 and §8, design-coordination §2) and a P0.4 spec line (plan :210) that v1.1 does not meet. The individual 150-250 ms fades alone would be low, as UI1-23's and UI2-18's verifiers set them (WCAG 2.3.3 is AAA).

**Problem.** On main, v1.1 and v1.2 only two files in src have a prefers-reduced-motion rule (MobileBottomNav.svelte:307 and the landing page, routes/+page.svelte:1403 on v1.1), app.css has none, nothing uses svelte/motion's `prefersReducedMotion`, and the 'full prefers-reduced-motion reset' that design-coordination §2 lists (:74) was never built, although the design doc says every transition respects it (nordic-clarity :148). Svelte transitions run on the Web Animations API, which CSS cannot stop, so under emulated reduce the Settings dialog still scales and fades (200 ms and 150 ms) and the selection popup scales (150 ms), both already on main, as does FlashcardModal (fade 150 ms, scale 200 ms); v1.1 adds the recall prompt's 250 ms fly-in (RecallPrompt.svelte:52), the only new motion that runs. CSS `@keyframes` (the Skeleton shimmer, the Pomodoro pulse rings, the OfflineIndicator and StorageWarning slide-downs, spinners), the continue-prompt fly (section `+page.svelte:238`) and the smooth scrolls ('Halda áfram' at :157, and `aside { scroll-behavior: smooth }`) have no reduced-motion path either. v1.1's pager and predict buttons keep `transition: all 0.15s` hover colour fades, which WCAG 2.3.3 does not cover but P0.4's own spec does ('prefers-reduced-motion: reduce disables any transition', plan :210). P1's only new transition, the pre-question fly (v1.2 PreQuestionPrompt.svelte:37), never plays, because it sits inside the component's own `{#if objective}` while the page toggles the outer `{#if}`.

**Evidence.**

- `git grep -n prefers-reduced-motion <ref> -- src static/styles`: 2 hits on each of main, v1.1 and v1.2 (MobileBottomNav.svelte:307, routes/+page.svelte:1403 on v1.1); prefersReducedMotion, motion-reduce and motion-safe: 0 on every ref
- v1.1 measurements keyboard.reducedMotion (mediaMatches true: recall animation 250 ms running, Settings 200 ms, pager transition 'all 0.15s'); v1.2 measurements f_gaps.motion.reduce (popup 150 ms, Settings 200 + 150 ms) and f_gaps.prequestionIntercepted (0 animations under both reduce and no-preference)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/RecallPrompt.svelte:52; main: SettingsModal.svelte:128, :137, SelectionPopup.svelte:73, FlashcardModal.svelte:141, :146; v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:571; v1.2:src/lib/components/PreQuestionPrompt.svelte:32-37; a directive-anchored grep over `*.svelte`: 24 directives on main, 25 on v1.1 and 26 on v1.2, in 13 files that import svelte/transition on v1.1 (14 on v1.2)
- docs/plans/2026-02-20-nordic-clarity-redesign.md:148, :327; main:docs/design/design-coordination-2026-06.md:74; docs/plans/2026-04-22-screen-vs-paper-reader-plan.md:210
- v1.1:node_modules/svelte/src/motion/index.js:30 (`prefersReducedMotion`, Svelte 5.57.1; v1.2 has 5.56.3); v1.1:node_modules/svelte/src/internal/client/dom/elements/transitions.js:370 (no `element.animate()` when duration and delay are both 0)

**Fix.** Land it on main, where the gap already is; v1.1's recall fly-in and pager fades are covered once main is merged in. (1) In app.css, unlayered: `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { *, *::before, *::after { animation-duration: 0.01ms !important; animation-iteration-count: 1 !important; transition-duration: 0.01ms !important; scroll-behavior: auto !important; } }`. It reaches the CSS keyframes, the hover fades in component-scoped styles and aside's smooth scrolling. (2) Add `src/lib/utils/transitions.ts`, which re-exports fly, fade, scale and slide wrapped so that `prefersReducedMotion.current` (from svelte/motion) forces both duration and delay to 0, since Svelte skips `element.animate()` only when both are 0 (transitions.js:370). Switch the 13 files that import 'svelte/transition' (14 on v1.2) to it, and add an ESLint `no-restricted-imports` rule for 'svelte/transition' outside that file, so a new component cannot bring the gap back. (3) Add a `scrollBehavior()` helper that returns 'auto' under reduce, for the 3 `behavior: 'smooth'` calls. (4) When porting P1.2, delete the pre-question's dead transition rather than moving it to the parent. Extend the existing reduced-motion e2e (accessibility.spec.ts:96-116, landing page only). The other half of UI1-23, the chrome font, is UI-23.

**Acceptance test.** Extend `e2e/accessibility.spec.ts`. With `emulateMedia({ reducedMotion: 'reduce' })`, after opening the recall prompt (with `readingMode` set in the test), the Settings dialog, the selection popup and, once ported, the pre-question: `document.getAnimations().filter(a => (a.effect?.getTiming().duration ?? 0) > 1).length` is 0, and the pager button's computed transition-duration is at most 0.01 ms. Control: the same steps under 'no-preference' produce at least one animation longer than 1 ms. The reduce run fails today (recall 250 ms, Settings 200 ms, popup 150 ms). The duration filter holds whether or not a zero-length animation object is created. Lint: a file outside `src/lib/utils/transitions.ts` that imports 'svelte/transition' fails `npm run lint`.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: 'P1 motion' as the scope; the popup scale and the Settings fade and scale are already on main, v1.1's recall fly-in is the only new motion that runs, and P1's one new transition (the pre-question fly) is dead code.
- NOT: the pre-question fly as motion that ignores reduce; it never plays in either run.
- NOT: the Settings fade and scale as new in v1.1; they are on main (:128, :137).
- NOT: the selection popup's 150 ms scale as v1.2-only; it is on main and v1.1 too.
- NOT: the pager and predict `transition: all 0.15s` as motion under WCAG 2.3.3; they are hover colour fades, covered by the P0.4 spec (plan :210) and the design doc, not by WCAG.
- NOT: MobileBottomNav as the only reduced-motion rule in the repo; the landing page has a second (routes/+page.svelte:1403). It is the only one in the CSS a reader page loads.
- NOT: 25 directives or '25 imports' from the cited grep; that pattern returns 158 on v1.1 (141 on v1.2, 156 on main) because in: and out: also match margin: and join:. A directive-anchored grep over `*.svelte` gives 24, 25 and 26 directives (main, v1.1, v1.2) in 13 import sites on v1.1 (14 on v1.2).
- NOT: `el.getAnimations().length === 0` right after mount as the check; filter on duration above 1 ms, with a no-preference control.

**Source findings:** UI1-23, UI2-18, UI3-13

### UI-26. A cloze card's back shows the bare answer without its sentence, and the blank has no accessible name.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P1.3 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.2 · **Also in:** Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ The verifier's medium (confirmed): the card still works, but its back hides the context the answer should be checked against, and the blank is likely unreadable to a screen reader.

**Problem.** On v1.2's /minniskort a card's front or back is plain text in one `<p class="flashcard-card-text">` (minniskort/+page.svelte:196-205), so after 'Man það' a cloze card's back is only 'SVAR' / 'losar varma', and the learner cannot check the answer in its sentence. The blank is six underscores (cloze.ts:9) with no accessible name; screen readers are expected to read it as punctuation or skip it (not verified). The card type has no kind or MT field (flashcard.ts:5-12): the creation and fallback dialog shows the stored source raw ('Heimild: efnafraedi-2e > 05 > 5-1', FlashcardModal.svelte:183-187), no card links back to the text, and cards made from machine-translated sections carry no MT flag (in the content copy no section is reviewed: true, so every card comes from MT text). It is P1.3's; main and v1.1 have no cloze cards.

**Evidence.**

- v1.2:src/routes/[bookSlug]/minniskort/+page.svelte:196-205 (one `<p class="flashcard-card-text">` shows either the back or the front)
- v1.2 cloze-card screenshots (phone, light): the back after 'Man það' reads only 'SVAR' / 'losar varma'; the front reads 'Breyting sem `______` er kölluð útvermið ferli (e.'
- v1.2:src/lib/utils/cloze.ts:9 (BLANK, six underscores); v1.2:src/lib/types/flashcard.ts:5-12 (no kind, no MT field); v1.2:src/lib/components/FlashcardModal.svelte:183-187 ('Heimild: {source}'); v1.2:src/lib/components/TextHighlighter.svelte:474 (source stored as slugs)
- main:src/lib/stores/sectionRenameMigration.ts:9-38 (flashcards are not migrated); main:src/lib/utils/contentLoader.ts:213-221 (getSectionPath returns the file basename), :272-310 (findSectionBySlug also accepts the short form)
- R4 DI16 (cards from a section without reviewed: true carry and display a 'Vélþýtt' flag)

**Fix.** When porting P1.3: (1) store `kind: 'cloze'` on the card rather than detecting the underscores in its front. (2) Render a cloze card's back as the full sentence, the front with the blank filled by the answer, marked with `<mark>` in a non-accent tint plus bold. (3) Render the front's blank as an element with visually hidden text ('eyða') inside it, rather than role="img" on an inline span. (4) On the back, show the source as a link, 'Úr 5.1 Grundvallaratriði orku', resolved with findSectionBySlug (it accepts v1.2's short '5-1' and the long form) and built with getSectionPath, plus the existing 'Vélþýtt' label (as in PreviewBanner and R4 DI16) when the section is not reviewed. A stored section reference makes cards section-keyed records, so per the #258 rule give the flashcard store a `renameSections()` and a line in `migrateRenamedSections()`, as CODE-02 does for recall, or resolve the link through sectionRedirects when it is rendered. The quality of the card's text is CODE-11, and whether verbatim cloze should stay the card type is PED-23.

**Acceptance test.** Component test (with CODE-21's setup) or e2e: a card with kind 'cloze' renders its back as the front with the blank replaced by the answer inside `<mark>`; the front's blank has the accessible name 'eyða'; the back's link href equals getSectionPath() for the card's section (the long-form URL, derived in the test, not typed in) and resolves for a short '5-1' source; with that section reviewed: false in the TOC fixture the 'Vélþýtt' label is present, and with reviewed: true it is absent; after `migrateRenamedSections()` with an active rename row, the link points at the new slug. All of it fails on v1.2 today (no `<mark>`, no link, no MT label, no accessible name).

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the link as '/efnafraedi-2e/kafli/05/5-1/' built from the stored short slug; main's canonical path is the file basename (getSectionPath), so resolve the source through findSectionBySlug.
- NOT: detecting cloze cards by the underscores in the front; store `kind: 'cloze'`.
- NOT: a new 'Vélþýddur texti' label; reuse the existing 'Vélþýtt' wording.
- NOT: role="img" on an inline span for the blank; visually hidden text inside it is more robust.
- NOT: a source link without rename handling; flashcards are not in main's `migrateRenamedSections()`, so a rename would strand the link.

**Source findings:** UI2-15

### UI-27. When no cloze can be built, 'Eyðukort' silently opens the ordinary flashcard dialog instead, and a whole sentence followed by another is saved as a malformed card with no dialog.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P1.3 · **Stage:** when porting P1 · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.2, main

_Severity note:_ The verifier's medium: the fallback is confusing but recoverable, and the silent malformed card needs a specific selection (a whole non-final sentence with its end mark). That one-tap cards cannot be undone or deleted makes it worse, but that is a separate issue.

**Problem.** On v1.2 `buildClozeFront` returns null when the selection is not found in the block text (cloze.ts:25-26) or when fewer than 10 characters remain after blanking (:47-49); a whole sentence hits the second case when it is the block's last sentence or is selected without its end mark, and `handleCreateCloze` then opens the full 'Búa til minniskort' dialog (TextHighlighter.svelte:451-455) with no explanation and with `decks[0]` preset (FlashcardModal.svelte:30-35). A whole sentence selected with its end mark and followed by another sentence in the same block does not return null: the end-scan (cloze.ts:31-33) runs on into the next sentence, and a card whose front is '`______` Hversu mikinn hita gleypti vatnið í sig?' and whose back is the selected sentence is saved silently (probe). In both paths the popup stays open behind the dialog, because `handleCreateFlashcard` (TextHighlighter.svelte:395-403) never clears `selection` and the popup renders under `{#if selection}` (:525); main's `handleCreateFlashcard` has the same body, so 'Minniskort' leaves the popup behind its dialog on the live site too. The difference between 'Minniskort' and 'Eyðukort' is explained only in a title tooltip (SelectionPopup.svelte:147), and below 640 px both buttons are icon-only (:138, :157). Whether the popup opens on touch at all (TextHighlighter listens only for mouseup) is UI-13.

**Evidence.**

- v1.2:src/lib/utils/cloze.ts:25-26 and :47-49 (the two null returns), :31-33 (the end-scan that runs on into the next sentence)
- Probe of v1.2's cloze.ts (node 22.22.2): the last sentence with '?' gives null; the first sentence with '.' and another sentence after it gives '`______` Hversu mikinn hita gleypti vatnið í sig?'; the first sentence without '.' gives null; mid-sentence 'hituð' gives a normal cloze
- v1.2:src/lib/components/TextHighlighter.svelte:451-455 (the fallback), :395-403 (selection never cleared), :525 (popup under `{#if selection}`); main:src/lib/components/TextHighlighter.svelte:380-415 (the same `handleCreateFlashcard` body)
- v1.2 cloze-fallback screenshot (desktop, whole sentence selected): the dialog with the popup's end ('…ðukort / Orðasafn / x') still drawn beside it; v1.2:src/lib/components/FlashcardModal.svelte:30-35 (presets `decks[0]`)
- v1.2:src/lib/components/SelectionPopup.svelte:147 (title-only explanation), :138, :157 (icon-only below 640 px); R5 DI6 ('Explain once, at first encounter', written for P0.2, P0.3 and P1.2)

**Fix.** (1) Close the popup inside `handleCreateFlashcard` and `handleAddNote` themselves (set `selection = null` after copying the text and the range). That also fixes main's 'Minniskort' and 'Athugasemd' paths, so it can land on main before the port. (2) Guard `buildClozeFront`, as part of CODE-11's rework of it (`buildClozeFront(text, start, length)`): end the sentence at the selection's own end mark instead of scanning past it, and return null when the selection spans the whole sentence. (3) On the null path, open the dialog with a one-line notice at the top, for example 'Ekki tókst að búa til eyðukort úr þessu vali. Veldu orð eða orðasamband inni í setningu — eða skrifaðu kortið hér.' (draft copy for a native-speaker check). (4) Explain the two actions once, at first use, with the same per-feature 'seen' flag that PED-25 proposes (for Eyðukort, for example: 'Setningin verður spurning og valdi textinn svarið.'); R5's explain-once was written for the other interventions, so extending it to P1.3 is reasonable but untested. (5) Check touch reachability on a real phone first: if the popup never opens on touch, the touch path (UI-13) matters more than the tooltip wording.

**Acceptance test.** Unit (cloze.test.ts): a block's first sentence selected with its period and followed by another sentence gives null (today '`______` Hversu mikinn hita gleypti vatnið í sig?'); a mid-sentence term still gives a cloze. E2E (desktop, mouse selection): selecting a non-final whole sentence with its period and tapping Eyðukort saves no card and opens the dialog with the notice; selecting the block's last sentence does the same; after either, and after 'Minniskort' on main, no element with an aria-label starting 'Valm' (the popup's 'Valmoguleikar yfirstrokunar') remains in the DOM.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: that every whole-sentence selection returns null; only the block's last sentence or one selected without its end mark does, while one with its end mark and a sentence after it is saved as a malformed card.
- NOT: that the dialog presets the Eyðukort deck; it presets `decks[0]`, and Eyðukort was preset only because it was the capture's only deck.
- NOT: the popup's end beside the dialog as an outro, or as uncertain; the popup is left open because `selection` is never cleared, and it closes on the next outside mousedown or on Escape.
- NOT: a P1.3-only defect; main's 'Minniskort' leaves the popup behind its dialog too.
- NOT: the 'Eyðukort vistað' toast in the fallback screenshot as part of the fallback; it is the previous cloze's toast (2.5 s timeout).
- NOT: R5's 'Explain once, at first encounter' as evidence about P1.3; it was written for P0.2, P0.3 and P1.2.

**Source findings:** UI2-16

### UI-28. The selection popup clips at the screen edge (already on the live site), and P1.3's cloze toast fails contrast and overlaps the timer pill by 3 px on phones.

**Severity:** medium · **Feature:** P1.3 · **Stage:** before it reaches students · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ The verifier's medium: a confirmed WCAG 1.4.3 failure on the toast in both themes once P1.3 is ported, and a clipping defect that is live on main today; the pill overlap is 3 px and low priority.

**Problem.** SelectionPopup.svelte is byte-identical on main and v1.1 and clamps only the right side, assuming a 400 px popup (`x = min(x, innerWidth − 200)`, then `translateX(−50%)`, :31-34), never the left, so a selection near the left edge clips the popup on the live site. Measured on v1.2's phone, the popup (331 px wide with P1.3's Eyðukort button) sat at x = −108; without that button it would be about 295 px and still clip, and on desktop it is about 491 px before P1.3 (583 px after), already wider than the clamp assumes (the pre-P1.3 widths are computed from the measured buttons); on a phone the right clamp is over-conservative, since any centre at 190 px or more lands at left 25. P1.3 widens the popup by about 36 px on a phone and 92 px on desktop. Its 'Eyðukort vistað' toast (TextHighlighter.svelte:537-545) is white on `--accent-color` at 14 px weight 500, 2.92:1 in light and 2.08:1 in dark, failing WCAG 1.4.3 (4.5:1), and it is fixed at bottom-6 centre, where on a phone it overlaps the timer pill by 3 px (the toast ends at x 293, the pill starts at x 290) and leaves the pill and '25:00' visible. The toast is a role=status element inserted together with its text, which live-region guidance says may not be announced (no screen-reader output was captured).

**Evidence.**

- main and v1.1 src/lib/components/SelectionPopup.svelte:31-34 (right-only clamp), :70 (`translateX(−50%)`); `git diff --stat origin/main origin/feature/reader-v1.1` for that file is empty
- v1.2 measurements b_cloze.popup: 'phone light left-edge' x −108, clippedLeft true, width 331; 'desktop light' width 583
- v1.2 measurements b_cloze.contrast: cloze toast 2.92 (light) and 2.08 (dark); b_cloze.toast['phone light']: the toast ends at x 293, the timer pill starts at x 290 (w 76), y 776-820
- v1.2:src/lib/components/TextHighlighter.svelte:537-545 (toast markup); main: 14 uses of white text on `bg-[var(--accent-color)]` (for example FlashcardModal.svelte:268, DownloadBookButton.svelte:145, :267)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:68 (`BOTTOM_CLEARANCE = 76`, the pattern for keeping clear of the bottom controls)

**Fix.** (1) Fix the clamp on main now, not only in the port: a pure `clampPopupX(centerX, width, viewportWidth, margin = 8)` in src/lib/utils that returns the popup's left edge, fed by its measured width (`bind:clientWidth` after mount), with the popup rendered hidden until it is measured so it does not jump on the first frame. (2) Toast contrast: fix it once at the token level rather than on the toast alone, because white on `--accent-color` is the house pattern (14 uses on main): use the accent-foreground token of UI-02 (#1b2838 on #c78c20 gives 5.12:1, #16161c on #e8a838 gives 8.66:1), or, for the toast alone, `var(--text-primary)` on `var(--accent-light)` (13.87:1 light, 12.16:1 dark). (3) Render the toast's text into an always-mounted role=status container. (4) Low priority, under UI-12's bottom-inset contract: place the toast above the fixed bottom controls, as the pager reserves `BOTTOM_CLEARANCE`. The mouse-only trigger is UI-13. Steps 2-4 ride the P1.3 port.

**Acceptance test.** Unit: `clampPopupX(20, 331, 390) >= 8` and `clampPopupX(380, 331, 390) + 331 <= 382` (today's formula puts the first popup's left edge at −145.5). Playwright at 390×844 on main: a selection at the left edge gives a popup whose rect.x is at least 0, with and without P1.3's button. After the port, at 390×844: the toast's rect does not intersect the timer pill's rect, the role=status container exists before the toast text is inserted, and axe reports no color-contrast violation on the toast in light or dark (2.92 and 2.08 today).

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: that P1.3 causes the clipping; the clamp is byte-identical on main and v1.1, and the popup clips at the left edge without the Eyðukort button too. P1.3 only widens it, by about 36 px (phone) and 92 px (desktop).
- NOT: the pre-P1.3 widths (about 295 px and 491 px) as measured; they are computed from the measured buttons.
- NOT: that the toast covers the timer pill; it overlaps it by 3 px, and the pill and '25:00' stay visible.
- NOT: that the toast also overlaps the mobile-tools FAB; the rects do not support it.
- NOT: a measured right-side clip at 1280 px; none was measured, and the right-side analysis is from the code.
- NOT: the screen-reader miss as measured; it is standard live-region guidance, with no screen-reader output captured.

**Source findings:** CODE2-17

### UI-29. The 'Hluti N af M · Síða X af Y' label resets per unit, can read 'Hluti 1 af 1' and counts the intro and footnotes as parts, and the UI uses more than one word for 'section'.

**Severity:** low · **Feature:** P0.4 · **Stage:** after release · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** v1.1, main

_Severity note:_ The verifier's low, down from the reviewer's medium: the label is accurate and matches the spec's format (plan :172); the problems are clarity and wording, with no data loss or learning harm measured.

**Problem.** On v1.1 the pager label counts pages within a sub-section unit (`positionLabel()` at PagedReaderControls.svelte:192-196, used by the live region, and a second copy of the same format in the template at :525-527), so §1.2's 34 pages (units of 11, 13 and 10 on desktop and phone; 20 pages on the tablet) never appear as one count: page 11 reads 'Hluti 1 af 3 · Síða 11 af 11' with Næsta still enabled and the next 'Hluti 2 af 3 · Síða 1 af 13', so a reader may take 'Síða 11 af 11' for the end. A single-unit section reads 'Hluti 1 af 1 · Síða 1 af 11', and on §3.1 the header-plus-paragraph intro and the `<section class="footnotes">` after `</main>` become units 1 and 4 of 4 (paginate.ts:155-175). The label is accurate and is the spec's format (plan :172; since fix E19 the live region announces the same per-unit label), and KEEP-04 keeps a persistent 'Hluti … · Síða …' cue. Separately, the reader UI uses several words for one level: 'kafli' for a section ('Næsti kafli', 'Merkja kafla sem lesinn', v1.1's recall heading 'Kafli lesinn — rifjaðu upp') and for a chapter ('Kafli 1 › 1.3'), 'undirkafli' (aria-label 'Velja undirkafla') and 'eining' (the MT notice, PreviewBanner.svelte:20); all of these except v1.1's recall heading are also on main.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/PagedReaderControls.svelte:192-196 (`positionLabel()`, used by the live region) and :525-527 (the template's own copy of the format)
- v1.1 measurements paged[12] (§1.2: 34 pages in units of 11/13/10 on desktop and phone): pages[10] 'Hluti 1 af 3 · Síða 11 af 11', pages[11] 'Hluti 2 af 3 · Síða 1 af 13'; paged[14].pages[0] 'Hluti 1 af 1 · Síða 1 af 11'; tablet §1.2: 20 pages
- v1.1:src/lib/utils/paginate.ts:155-175 (the §3.1 intro and the footnotes after `</main>` become units of their own); v1.1:e2e/pagination.spec.ts:41, :281, :457-467 (match or parse 'Hluti')
- v1.1:src/lib/components/NavigationButtons.svelte:58, :61, :108, :126; PreviewBanner.svelte:20 ('eining'); RecallPrompt.svelte:62; plan 2026-04-22 :172 (label format), :208; manual-qa-2026-06.md:177, :231
- Label width at 390 px (13 px Liberation Serif): a slot of about 151 px; today's label 132.8 px; 'Síða 13 af 34 · Flokkun efnis' 151.6 px; across 295 sub-section h2s the median is 22 characters, 104 exceed 25 and the longest is 62. R1 E25 (Mangen 2019, abstract, 50 adults, a narrative text) supports a persistent position cue, not one format over another

**Fix.** Within the spec (plan :172, fix E19) and KEEP-04: (1) build the visible label and the live region from one function (the template calls `positionLabel()` instead of repeating the format), which KEEP-04 also asks for now; (2) drop the 'Hluti' part when a section has one unit; (3) leave the footnotes unit out of the count, or name it ('Neðanmálsgreinar') instead of 'Hluti 4 af 4'. Update the e2e assertions that match or parse 'Hluti' (pagination.spec.ts:41, :281, :457-467) so they cover single-unit sections. Any change of format is [USER]'s call in QA batch E, since it departs from plan :172 and from E19's per-unit announcement: an overall count with the sub-section heading ('Síða 13 af 34 · Flokkun efnis') would need a phone variant (the count alone, with the heading truncated or moved to a running head), because the 390 px slot is about 151 px and most headings would wrap the label onto two or three lines; whether the intro counts as a part belongs to the same call. Terminology is also [USER]'s: settle the Icelandic words for chapter, section and sub-section, then apply them in one sweep that also reaches the chapter page title (kafli/[chapterSlug]/+page.svelte:67), 'Veldu kafla' and '{n} kaflar' on the book home ([bookSlug]/+page.svelte:86, :141), Header's 'Til baka í kafla' (Header.svelte:70) and the shortcut descriptions (keyboardShortcuts.ts:46, :51), and update the QA doc item (:231). Steps 1-3 take about an hour; the sweep is larger.

**Acceptance test.** Unit test of the label function on constructed unit and page counts (no viewport, so no page budget is assumed): one unit gives no 'Hluti'; a footnotes unit is left out of the count or labelled by name; a multi-unit section keeps 'Hluti N af M · Síða X af Y'. E2E: on every page of a multi-unit section and on a single-unit section, the status region's text equals the visible label (pagination.spec.ts:271-281, with its 'Hluti' filter updated). After [USER]'s terminology ruling, a word-bounded grep (for example `\beining\b`, if that word is retired) finds the retired terms in no reader UI string; a plain grep would also hit Skilgreining and Leiðbeiningar.

**Decision for [USER].** [USER] decides, in QA batch E, and neither is one of the four open calls: (a) whether the label keeps the spec's per-unit format (plan :172, E19, kept by KEEP-04) or changes to an overall count, including whether the intro counts as a part; (b) the Icelandic words for chapter, section and sub-section in reader UI text, and whether 'eining' goes.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: that 'Síða 11 af 11' hides that the section is not done; the full label reads 'Hluti 1 af 3 · Síða 11 af 11' and Næsta stays enabled, so it is a possible misreading, not hidden information.
- NOT: 34 pages for §1.2 on every device; that is desktop and phone, and the tablet gives 20.
- NOT: that the QA doc expects a different announcement; :177 names the full label, and :231 (like plan :208) is loose wording.
- NOT: Mangen 2019 (E25) as support for an overall count; it supports a persistent position cue, and both formats are one.
- NOT: severity medium; the verifier set low.
- NOT: 'Síða 13 af 34 · Flokkun efnis' as a drop-in label; on a 390 px phone most headings wrap it, and it departs from plan :172 and E19.
- NOT: 'kafli' = section as the fix; that keeps 'kafli' for both section and chapter, the ambiguity named, and the word choice is [USER]'s.
- NOT: a terminology sweep of effort S; it also reaches the chapter page title, the book home, the Header and the shortcut descriptions.
- NOT: a plain grep for 'eining'; it matches Skilgreining and Leiðbeiningar.
- NOT: dropping 'Hluti' without touching the e2e; pagination.spec.ts:281 filters the status region by 'Hluti'.

**Source findings:** UI1-21

### UI-30. efni's module body is a second `<main>` inside the app's `<main>`; a non-landmark wrapper needs agreeing with efni, and vefur's paginator must follow it.

**Severity:** low · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** needs efni first · **Effort:** M · **Branches:** main, v1.1, v1.2 · **Also in:** Code

_Severity note:_ The verifier lowered the reviewer's medium: axe-core 4.10.3 tags landmark-main-is-top-level, landmark-no-duplicate-main and landmark-unique 'best-practice' only, with no WCAG success criterion, so this is a screen-reader UX defect (two 'main' landmarks), not an A/AA failure. It is pre-existing on main and on the live site, and paging works on today's markup.

**Problem.** Every section page renders efni's article.cnx-module > header + main inside vefur's own `<main id="main-content">` ([bookSlug]/+layout.svelte: v1.1 :121-128, main :113-120, v1.2 :123-130), so a screen reader's landmark list offers two 'main' regions, one nested in the other. The nested `<main>` is in 251 of 251 chapter files of the ② preview content, in efni main's renderer (the module emit at cnxml-render.js:1079/1117 and four rollup emitters) and on the live site, so it is pre-existing on main and neither branch introduced it. v1.1 now depends on the element: buildUnits looks through a child whose tagName is 'MAIN' (paginate.ts:155-160), and both e2e helpers in pagination.spec.ts (:84-89, :150-155) look through ':scope > main'; on main nothing reads it (content.css and print.css have no main selector, and app.css's print reset on main has nothing to reset on the inner element). The fix belongs in efni's markup; stripping or re-roling the element in vefur would be the content workaround CLAUDE.md forbids. Order matters, because CI syncs efni's main: if a non-landmark wrapper reached it before vefur accepts one, v1.1 would put the whole module body on one page.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/utils/paginate.ts:155-160 (buildUnits looks through 'MAIN'; comment :146-153); v1.1:e2e/pagination.spec.ts:84-89 and :150-155 (':scope > main'); v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/+layout.svelte:121-128 (`<main id="main-content">`; main :113-120)
- main-element count: 251 of 251 chapter HTML files of the ② preview hold exactly one nested `<main>`, as do this machine's copies of the three frozen books (edlisfraedi-2e 15/15, liffraedi-2e 19/19, orverufraedi 22/22); the live §1.1 content file has the same article.cnx-module > header + main skeleton
- efni main 98a5fe2ff: tools/cnxml-render.js emits `<main>` at :1079/:1117 (module) and :3193, :3432, :3528, :3754 (rollups), and regex-reads it at :3776 and :4256-4284; tools/validate-chapter.js:866-871; 10 render-golden fixtures; tools/**tests**/css-contract.test.js STRUCTURAL_CLASSES (:35-47)
- axe scan (v1.1 capture): every section-page state reports landmark-main-is-top-level, landmark-no-duplicate-main and landmark-unique (impact moderate) and the flashcard and /nam pages do not; axe-core 4.10.3 tags all three 'best-practice', with no WCAG criterion
- word search for main: 0 hits in static/styles/content.css and print.css (control: 7 for figure); src/app.css only in the print reset (v1.1 :1848, main :1811); main:CLAUDE.md: 'Avoid adding workarounds here that compensate for content problems'

**Fix.** Land vefur's half first, on v1.1 (or on main once v1.1 is merged); it changes nothing on today's content. (1) vefur: export one module-body selector from paginate.ts beside ATOMIC_SELECTOR, matching `<main>` and the agreed wrapper (cnx-body is the proposal; efni picks the name). buildUnits looks through any root child that matches it (el.matches), and both pagination.spec.ts helpers receive it through page.evaluate, as ATOMIC_SELECTOR already does (:9, :101), and find the body with Array.from(root.children).find((el) => el.matches(selector)) instead of ':scope > main' (prefixing ':scope > ' to a comma selector would match the class at any depth). Add a paginate.test.ts case for the wrapper beside the existing `<main>` cases (:126-160), and update the comments at paginate.ts:146-153 and PagedReaderControls.svelte:5. Keep the MAIN branch: the three frozen books stay live with `<main>` markup, and after the main merge a book downloaded for offline reading (offline-book:`<slug>`, which nothing expires) keeps its old pages. (2) efni: send a handoff note (see efniNote) asking for a non-landmark wrapper such as `<div class="cnx-body">`, to be merged to efni main only after step 1 has landed. (3) Nothing else in vefur: no content.css rule (the class is structural), and no stripping or re-roling of the inner `<main>` at render time.

**Acceptance test.** (1) vefur unit, fails today: in paginate.test.ts, a module whose body sits in `<div class="cnx-body">` (or the agreed wrapper) yields the same units as the identical fixture with `<main>`; today the div becomes one intro block. The existing `<main>` cases still pass, as the control. (2) vefur e2e, fails today: with page.route rewriting `<main>`…`</main>` to the agreed wrapper in the served chapter HTML (the device CODE-15 uses), 'every page of a section fits the viewport' passes; today the whole body lands on one page, and the wrapper does not match ATOMIC_SELECTOR, so the overrun is not allowed. (3) efni, before it merges: css-contract.test.js passes with VEFUR_CONTRACT=1, validate-chapter.js reports no 'content is too short' page, the 10 render-golden fixtures are regenerated, and the rendered chapters hold 0 files with `<main>` (251 of 251 in the ② preview today). (4) Once synced content is deployed: document.querySelectorAll('main').length is 1 on a section page (2 today). The member's axe check cannot run as written, because the repo has no axe dependency; the DOM count in (4) stands in for it.

**efni side.** Ask efni, through a handoff note (vefur does not edit efni), to emit the module body as a non-landmark wrapper (proposed `<div class="cnx-body">`; efni picks the name and tells vefur) at all five emit pairs in tools/cnxml-render.js: the module at :1079/:1117 and the rollups at :3193/:3229, :3432/:3479, :3528/:3620 and :3754/:3798. The rollups matter for the landmark, since they also render inside #main-content; only the module emit matters to the paginator. efni must also update its own regex consumers at :3776 and :4256-4284 and validate-chapter.js:866-871 (which would otherwise report every page as 'content is too short (0 chars)'), regenerate the 10 render-golden fixtures, and add the class to STRUCTURAL_CLASSES in css-contract.test.js with a vefur-hook comment like the one for 'check-knowledge-answer', because the contract fails for any emitted class with no content.css rule. Merge to efni main only after vefur's pager accepts both wrappers, since CI syncs efni main, and ideally fold the change into the next whole-book render rather than a render of its own. Readers see nothing until a chemistry sync, which is held.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: medium severity, or an A/AA failure: axe-core 4.10.3 tags the three landmark rules 'best-practice' only; 'moderate' is axe's impact rating.
- NOT: 'no other change in the meantime' in vefur: e2e/pagination.spec.ts:84-89 and :150-155 also look through ':scope > main' and must accept the new wrapper.
- NOT: effort S: efni emits `<main>` at five sites, regex-reads it at cnxml-render.js:3776 and :4256-4284 and in validate-chapter.js:866-871, and carries it in 10 render-golden fixtures.
- NOT: that efni's CSS contract test passes for the new class as it stands: it fails for any emitted class with no content.css rule, so the class goes into STRUCTURAL_CLASSES.
- NOT: a defect of either branch: the verifier found it on main and on the live site; v1.1 only adds a dependency on the element.

**Source findings:** UI3-17

### UI-31. P0.1's narrow measure reaches only new profiles, and nothing in Settings says that 'Þröngt' is now the default.

**Severity:** low · **Feature:** P0.1 · **Stage:** after release · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1 · **Also in:** Pedagogy

_Severity note:_ The verifier confirmed the reviewer's low. Nothing is broken: returning readers keep the width they stored, and the gaps are that nobody is told 'Þröngt' is now the default and that the setting does nothing on a phone. R1 grounds the measure in comfort and convention, not learning gains.

**Problem.** P0.1, new in v1.1, makes lineWidth 'narrow' (38rem) the default (settings.ts:72; main's is 'medium', settings.ts:67), measured on §1.2 at 16 px as 68.2 characters per line on the desktop and 36-37 on the phone. The settings store saves its full state on first load (v1.1 :118-122, the same on main at :107-111) and a valid stored key wins over the default (storeValidation.ts:45-58), so the new default reaches only profiles with nothing stored. A returning reader keeps whatever width they stored, which is main's 'Miðlungs' (52rem) for anyone who never changed it, and the store cannot tell that auto-save from a deliberate choice. Nothing in Settings says that 'Þröngt' is now the default: the Línubreidd group (SettingsModal.svelte:286-303) has no help text, unlike Lestrarstilling (:322-324). On the phone (390×844) the 318 px column is narrower than all three widths (608, 832 and 992 px), so tapping the setting there changes nothing. No test pins the new default: settings.test.ts has no lineWidth case, and the mutant 'narrow' → 'medium' survived.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/stores/settings.ts:72 (lineWidth 'narrow') and :118-122 (saves the full state on load); main:src/lib/stores/settings.ts:67 ('medium'); v1.1:src/lib/utils/storeValidation.ts:45-58 (a valid stored key wins)
- v1.1:src/lib/components/SettingsModal.svelte:55-59 ('Þröngt' / 'Miðlungs' / 'Breitt') and :286-303 (no help text), against Lestrarstilling's help text at :322-324
- measure (v1.1 capture, §1.2, 16 px Literata): 68.2 cpl in a 560 px column on the desktop, 36-37 cpl in a 318 px column on the phone; v1.1:src/app.css:23-25 (38/52/62rem)
- health_v1.1 mutant [39] (settings.ts:72 'narrow' → 'medium') survived; v1.1:src/lib/stores/settings.test.ts:32-48 has 0 lineWidth matches against 10 for fontSize
- R1 designImplications[13] (justify P0.1 by comfort, convention and accessibility, not learning gains) and R1 synthesis (speed favours long lines); v1.1:docs/manual-qa-2026-06.md:152-154 (a stored profile keeps its Línubreidd)

**Fix.** Add one help line under 'Línubreidd', styled like Lestrarstilling's (:322-324), in the verifier's neutral wording plus a phone clause. Draft copy, for [USER]'s native-speaker check: 'Þröngt er sjálfgefið: styttri línur, nær því sem er í prentaðri bók. Á mjóum skjá, t.d. í síma, breytir stillingin engu.' Render the option's name from the store's default (export the defaults, or a DEFAULT_LINE_WIDTH, from settings.ts and look its label up in lineWidths), so the line cannot name the wrong option; if the default ever moves, the pin test below fails and the words after the colon need rewriting. Do not say that most readers find narrow lines easier: R1 treats the measure as comfort and convention, and speed favours long lines. Pin lineWidth 'narrow' in settings.test.ts next to the existing defaults (:32-48); it is one test shared with PED-33 and CODE-05 (its test (1)), so write it once. Leave stored values alone in this change. Whether existing 'medium' profiles move to 'narrow' once is CODE-05's owner question, and the help line is the same either way. Recommendation for that question: leave them, since a stored 'medium' cannot be told apart from a deliberate choice and Batch D of the QA doc expects a stored width to survive. Out of scope here, though cheapest done in the same edit: the exposed selected state on these buttons (finding UI1-03). The em-based measure is PED-33.

**Acceptance test.** (1) e2e on a fresh profile, fails today: open Stillingar; the help text under 'Línubreidd' contains the label of the button the group exposes as selected (aria-pressed or aria-checked, whichever finding UI1-03's fix uses), which on a fresh profile is defaultSettings.lineWidth's option. Today the group has no help text. (2) Shared with PED-33 and CODE-05: a settings.test.ts case reads lineWidth 'narrow' on a fresh profile (P0.1's spec value, not an open call), so health_v1.1 mutant [39] ('narrow' → 'medium'), which survives today, is killed. (3) Guard that passes today and must keep passing: a stored lineWidth 'wide' still loads as 'wide'. The guard uses 'wide' so it holds whatever [USER] decides on CODE-05's one-time question about 'medium'.

**Not this (walked back in verification).**

- NOT: the reviewer's copy 'Þröngt (sjálfgefið): styttri línur sem flestum þykir þægilegra að lesa.': R1 does not show that most readers prefer narrow lines; it treats the measure as comfort and convention, and speed favours long lines.
- NOT: 'every current student keeps Miðlungs' (UI1-22's wording): as CODE-05's verification put it, only returning profiles that never changed their width store 'medium'; a profile that picked 'Þröngt' or 'Breitt' on main keeps that.

**Source findings:** UI1-22

## Keep

### KEEP-01. Keep the low-stakes stance: skippable prompts, no card dropped by a prediction, no completion rewards or peer comparison, and learning stores kept on the device; the page-view beacon must be decided before the data rule is written.

**Severity:** high · **Feature:** cross-cutting · **Stage:** keep · **Effort:** S · **Branches:** v1.1, v1.2, main · **Also in:** UI

_Severity note:_ The verifier kept high because the list guards against regressions with learning-harm evidence (R5-07, R2-15), not because anything is broken today.

**Problem.** Four properties hold on v1.1 and v1.2 and must survive the merge, the P1 port and any redesign: every prompt closes in one tap and gates nothing (v1.1's inline RecallPrompt has 'Sleppa'; v1.2's pre-question closes with one tap on 'Ég hugleiddi þetta — byrja að lesa' while the content renders regardless); both predict buttons reveal the answer, so 'Man það' never drops a card from practice; nothing awards badges, unlocks or ranks, and v1.1 replaced the section 'Vel gert!' celebration with retrieval and a short informational line; and no view compares a student with peers (v1.2's CalibrationTab reads only the student's own stores). One engagement score remains on main and v1.1, /greining's 0–100 'Samræmi', which gives points for streaks and section visits (PED4-18); it is not a completion reward. The claim that all learning data stay on the device is true of the stores (recall, predictions, ratings, highlights and analytics stay in localStorage) but not of the deployed reader: a build-time input (VITE_API_URL in the gitignored .env.production) makes every [bookSlug] route change POST {eventType, book, chapter, section} to ritstjorn.namsbokasafn.is/api/analytics/event, confirmed in the live chunk, the live CSP and a headless-Chromium probe. efni main's handler stores user-agent and referrer with each event (whether production stores rows was not tested), and the beacon is pre-existing on main, so the data rule cannot be written truthfully until [USER] decides on it.

**Evidence.**

- v1.1:src/lib/components/RecallPrompt.svelte:48-53 (role=region, not a dialog), :79-84 ('Sleppa'); v1.2:src/lib/components/PreQuestionPrompt.svelte:56 (one-tap dismiss)
- v1.1:src/routes/[bookSlug]/minniskort/+page.svelte:32-41, :207-219 and v1.1:src/lib/stores/flashcard.ts:281-300 (both predictions flip the card; predictedKnown is only recorded)
